# Term index

A reverse glossary: look up a word met in these documents or in the code to
learn what it means in vif, where it is explained, and where it lives. A word
with several meanings lists each sense on its own line, introduced by its area,
and **bold** names in a definition are entries of their own. Links point to the
section that explains the term; code locations name the package and the main
identifier. A definition that starts with "Future design" names something the
code does not have yet, described only by a design such as
[Generic kinetic analysis](generic-kinetic.md).

[0-9](#0-9) · [A](#a) · [B](#b) · [C](#c) · [D](#d) · [E](#e) · [F](#f) · [G](#g) · [H](#h) · [I](#i) · [J](#j) · [K](#k) · [L](#l) · [M](#m) · [N](#n) · [O](#o) · [P](#p) · [Q](#q) · [R](#r) · [S](#s) · [T](#t) · [U](#u) · [V](#v) · [W](#w) · [X](#x) · [Z](#z)

## 0-9

**256-color** (also: 256-colour, `-color 256`) — The palette color mode: cells may carry xterm-256 indices instead of RGB, finalization fills untouched backgrounds without occlusion dimming, and a last pass settles every cell on a text console's sixteen colors, but for a rectangle a renderer keeps out of it (`SetExact`). `-color auto` lets the terminal's capability choose between it and truecolor; a terminal that is not a text console (`Config.TextConsole` false), such as an emulator over ssh, gets it too, and its theme draws the sixteen entries while xterm's cube shows as it is. See also **truecolor**, **xterm**. [Rendering §6](rendering.md#6-finalization-and-color-modes) · `internal/render` `console.go`, `RenderBuffer.SetExact` · `internal/engine` `ConfigResource.TextConsole`

**30-session.yaml** — The fleet's session template: one Job and one Service per session. The router builds the same objects for each request (`tool/vif-router/manifest.go`), and `session.sh create` renders the file itself through `render-session.sh`. See also **template**. [Fleet deploy §9](kube-docker-deploy.md#9-the-fleet-objects-and-the-shared-volumes) · `deploy/k3s/30-session.yaml`

**7777** — The conventional game port: `network.DefaultConfig`'s listen address, the example in `-host`, `-serve`, `:host` and `:join` usage, the session pod's game port, and the router's front door (`vif://<site>:7777/<session>`); a fleet session's probe listens beside it on 7778. [Development §4](development.md#session) · [Fleet design §9](kubernetes-fleet.md#9-the-session-routes) · `internal/network` `DefaultConfig` · `tool/vif-router` `manifest.go`

## A

**accidental** (also: `acc`, `Step.Acc`, `StepPitch`, `PitchStep`) — A pattern step's semitones off its scale degree, at most two either way, so a note outside the key is written exactly and still resolves through the harmony and follows a chord: the **rip** writes one for a note off the key it heard, `take` for a key played outside it, and the grid marks it `#` or `b` after the degree. [Audio §7](audio.md#7-music-sequencer) · [Audio §11](audio.md#11-ripping-a-recording) · `pkg/audio` `Step`, `StepDef`, harmony.go

**action** (also: ActionFunc, delayed action) — five senses. See also **APM**.
- *FSM*: a named effect a state's `on_enter`, `on_update` or `on_exit`, or a transition, runs, with an optional guard and `delay_ms`; a delayed action counts down in region time and is dropped when its owning state exits. [FSM §8](fsm-and-configuration.md#8-actions-and-delayed-work) · [FSM reference: Actions](fsm-reference.md#actions) · `internal/fsm` `Action`, `ActionFunc`, `DelayedAction`
- *Input*: a canonical action name such as `fire_main`, `motion_right` or `toggle_auto_fire`, which keymaps bind and bot and script intents name. [Input §10](input-and-modes.md#10-keymap-override-files) · `internal/input` `ActionNames`, `KeyEntry`
- *Bots*: an FSM action of a bot graph (`Intent`, `Text`, `TypeGlyph` or `PointAt`) that queues intents, sent at the graph's `actions_per_second` (default 12). [Bots: Actions](bots.md#actions) · `internal/bot` `Driver`, `DefaultActionsPerSecond`
- *Scripts*: one `[[action]]` of an authored script, placed at a `(run, tick)` position and setting exactly one of `intent`, `text`, `command` or `event`. [Development: Authored headless scripts](development.md#authored-headless-scripts) · `internal/journal` `ScriptAction`
- *Config menu*: an action row runs the command it names and closes the menu. [Menu: Key binding and persistence](config-menu.md#key-binding-and-persistence) · `internal/mode` `commandAction`

**adaptation** — two senses. See also **route adaptation**, **cadence**.
- *Navigation*: route adaptation, the online choice among a gateway's candidate routes per route graph and eye subtype, weighted by the fitness of the actors that took each route. [AI and physics §2](ai-physics-and-evolution.md#2-online-route-adaptation) · `internal/system` `AdaptationSystem`
- *Multiplayer*: cadence adaptation, the per-link controller that sets each peer's correction cadence and keyframe interval from measured round trip, jitter, delivered bytes, saturation and demand. [D-24](domain-design.md#d-24--cadence-adapts-the-convergence-floor-does-not) · `pkg/linkpace` `Controller`

**admission** (also: admit) — six senses. See also **convergence floor**, **catch-up**, **APM**.
- *Joins*: per-address admission spends a dialling host's budget of `NetworkAdmitBurst` (6) joins per `NetworkAdmitWindow` (1 minute) before any capture or world is built; a holder's bots join in-process and spend none. [Services and networking §7.2](services-and-networking.md#72-join-identity-and-who-decides-it) · [Logging: sub="admit"](logging-and-diagnostics.md#subadmit) · `internal/network` `AdmissionLimiter`, `Coordinator.Admit` · `pkg/network` `Pipe`
- *Roster*: a mid-run join becomes a transport peer before the authority's capture is read, so no crossing falls between the capture and the stream (D-22). [D-22](domain-design.md#d-22--admit-before-capturing) · [Multi-player §5](multi-player.md#5-membership-topology-and-authority-continuity) · `internal/app` `membership.admitLateJoiner`
- *Link*: a join whose measured link cannot carry one whole world per convergence-floor window is refused; a link with no measurement yet is admitted. [D-24](domain-design.md#d-24--cadence-adapts-the-convergence-floor-does-not) · `pkg/linkpace` `Admit`; `internal/converge` `Corrections.AdmitLink`
- *Lifecycle*: whether an allocated session still gives a dial a roster slot; a draining or expired session refuses with `ErrSessionEnding`. [Runtime: The allocated session](runtime.md#the-allocated-session) · `internal/lifecycle` `Controller.Admit`
- *Peer links*: the gate that decides, by roster membership and without allocating anything, whether a peer link from a given participant may open. [Multi-player §5.3](multi-player.md#53-reachability-under--authority-migrate) · `internal/network` `PeerGate`
- *Audio*: the mixer admits an sfx under its caps, two per sound and 12 in all, stealing a voice when full. [Audio §5](audio.md#5-sounds-and-sound-effects) · `pkg/audio` `Mixer`

**allocated session** — A dedicated host (`-serve`) created on someone's behalf and bounded by `-first-join`, `-empty` and `-drain`; its lifecycle moves through waiting, occupied, vacant, draining and expired, and the process exits when it expires. See also **vif-router**. [Runtime: The allocated session](runtime.md#the-allocated-session) · `internal/lifecycle` `Controller`

**allocator** — two senses. See also **vif-router**.
- *Fleet*: the pod-allocating half of **vif-router**, the fleet's node-local service: it creates one Job and one Service per session, waits for readiness and returns join targets. [Deployment §11](kube-docker-deploy.md#11-the-router) · [Fleet §9](kubernetes-fleet.md#9-the-session-routes) · `tool/vif-router` `allocator`
- *Entities*: the per-domain entity ID counter `World.CreateEntity` draws from; a capture carries the Shared one, so an install re-issues the IDs the authority would (D-9). [D-9](domain-design.md#d-9--entity-identity-is-domain-local-and-deterministic) · [D-19](domain-design.md#d-19--every-future-affecting-shared-value-is-restorable) · `internal/engine` `World.CreateEntity`

**ambient domain** (also: WithDomain, WithOrigin) — The domain and origin `World.PushEvent` tags an event with when the call names neither, set around a call by `World.WithDomain` and `World.WithOrigin`; a system's declared profile never changes it silently (D-7). See also **domain**. [D-7](domain-design.md#d-7--domain-is-explicit-ambient-context) · `internal/engine` `World.WithDomain`, `World.WithOrigin`, `World.PushEvent`

**anchor** — seven senses.
- *Journal*: a journal anchor names a run's identity and position (seed, scenario and content identity, schema, RNG session, run and tick, geometry, slot); it is written when capture opens, after a reset and every 600 ticks, and replay builds its config from the first one and refuses a mismatch. [Logging: Record and anchor shapes](logging-and-diagnostics.md#record-and-anchor-shapes) · `internal/event` `JournalAnchor`; `internal/parameter` `AnchorIntervalTicks`; `internal/app` `ConfigFromAnchor`, `App.VerifyAnchor`
- *Joins*: a join anchor wraps a journal anchor as what one participant offers another so both reproduce the same session (seed, RNG session, tick rate, scenario digest, speed, map latch); it travels in the session offer and the handoff record. [Services and networking §6](services-and-networking.md#6-operator-session-surface) · `internal/event` `JoinAnchor`; `internal/network` `SessionOffer`, `HandoffRecord`
- *Entities*: the entity another is positioned against: a composite's header for its members, the host entity (such as a pylon header) a gateway spawns from and dies with, the entity a splash follows, and what a particle orbits (the cursor for dust, a spinning or charging Kraken for decay, a vortex's button for any of them). [ECS §6](ecs-and-events.md#6-composite-entities) · [Gameplay §8](gameplay.md#8-hostile-and-structural-actors) · `internal/component` `MemberComponent`, `GatewayComponent.AnchorEntity`, `SplashComponent.AnchorEntity`
- *Navigation*: a target anchor marks an entity as a navigation destination of a script target group. [Navigation §5](navigation-design.md#5-target-groups) · `internal/component` `TargetAnchorComponent`
- *Images*: the authored anchor offset a `.vifimg` asset carries, exposed on `PatternResult`; `WallSystem` does not consume it. [Content §8](content-assets-and-tools.md#8-image-and-pattern-pipeline) · `internal/pattern` `PatternResult`
- *Input*: the router's fire anchors, the last main and special fire times that auto-fire and held buttons share so their requests do not stack. [Input §1](input-and-modes.md#1-input-pipeline) · `internal/mode` `Router`
- *Clock*: `PausableClock`'s anchor, the rate segment (wall and game instants, scale, paused) game time is projected from; a rate change, pause, resume or paused step swaps in a fresh one, so game time neither jumps nor bursts to catch up. [Runtime §9](runtime.md#9-time-control) · `internal/engine` `PausableClock`

**APM** (also: actions per minute, music APM) — Admitted input gestures per minute, counted to drive the adaptive music: one per dispatch pass, diminishing for one key mashed, one per pointer reversal after six columns or forty columns swept one way, at most eight a second; macro playback and auto-fire add nothing. `GameState` publishes a 60-second APM and a five-second music APM. [Input §8](input-and-modes.md#8-apm-signal) · [Audio §8](audio.md#8-adaptive-game-conductor) · `internal/engine` `GameState.AdmitAction`, `GameState.MusicAPM`

**App** (also: app.App, composition root) — One wired runtime: its services, world, input, scheduler and presentation adapter, composed by `app.New` in a fixed order; play, headless, replay, script, server and bot-seat instances are each an App. [Runtime §2](runtime.md#2-composition-sequence) · [Package map §4](package-map.md#4-production-package-responsibilities) · `internal/app` `App`, `New`

**apply tick** (also: ApplyTick) — The tick at which a crossing applies: its producer's epoch plus the receive lead, never earlier than that producer's previous crossing, with crossings of one tick applied in `(ApplyTick, Source, Seq)` order. On time, every copy applies at it; a crossing that reaches the authority late is committed at the authority's next tick, or voided, and then only its producer keeps the stamped tick. See also **commit**, **barrier**. [Multi-player §3.1](multi-player.md#31-ordinary-and-barrier-bound-crossings) · [Domain model §4.1](domain-design.md#41-receive-lead) · `internal/event` `ScheduledWireFrame.ApplyTick`

**arc** — two senses.
- *Weapons*: the rod's charge-cap effect: each unique hit arcs once to the nearest other target within `ArcRange`, skipping targets the volley already struck. [Combat §4](combat.md#4-at-the-charge-cap) · `internal/component` `WeaponSpec.ArcRange`
- *Geometry*: a contiguous unblocked span of an ellipse; the weapon system spreads a cursor's orbs over the free arcs of their orbit. [AI and physics §6](ai-physics-and-evolution.md#6-numeric-model-and-determinism) · `pkg/vmath` `ArcSegment`, `FindUnblockedArcs`

**arch-check** — The Makefile target that reports any `pkg/` package importing `internal/`; it is diagnostic and separate from `make verify`. [Development §2](development.md#2-common-build-workflow) · [Package map §5](package-map.md#5-reusable-pkg-libraries) · `Makefile`

**archive** — two senses.
- *Genetics*: the fixed-capacity, descending set of scored candidates a `StreamingEngine` keeps, which makes elitism structural; `Checkpoint`/`Restore` carry it with the rest of the engine and `Snapshot`/`Inject` carry it alone. [AI and physics §3](ai-physics-and-evolution.md#3-genetic-library) · [Genetic README: StreamingEngine](../pkg/genetic/README.md#streamingengine) · `pkg/genetic` `StreamingEngine`
- *Release*: a published tarball: the wad archive (`make wad-archive`, the installed config tree unpacking as `vif/`) or a tag's source archive. [Packaging §2](packaging.md#2-release-surface) · [Development §2](development.md#2-common-build-workflow) · `Makefile`

**artifact** — two senses. See also **crossing**, **term**.
- *Multiplayer*: anything one instance sends that another applies: a crossing, an epoch, a capture or a correction; authoritative artifacts carry the authority term. [D-3](domain-design.md#d-3--cross-the-smallest-shared-outcome) · [Multi-player §1](multi-player.md#1-vocabulary-and-boundaries) · `internal/system` `barrierArtifact`
- *Builds*: a built binary, browser bundle or container image, as measured for size. [Platforms §3](multi-platform.md#3-reference-artifact-measurements) · `Makefile`

**ascimage** (also: .vifimg) — The image tool and library that converts raster images into terminal cells (quadrant or background mode), writes dual color-mode `.vifimg` assets, views them and exports one mode as ANSI. See also **dual-mode image**, **quadrant mode**. [Content §9](content-assets-and-tools.md#9-ascimage-tool) · [Content §8](content-assets-and-tools.md#8-image-and-pattern-pipeline) · `cmd/ascimage`, `pkg/ascimage`

**attack family** (also: CombatAttackType) — The attack type a hit resolves under (tracer, lightning, missile, pulse, burn and others); damage allowances inside a target's immunity window are kept per cursor and family. [Combat §5](combat.md#5-resolution-statuses-and-what-crosses) · `internal/component` `CombatAttackType`

**attack profile** (also: AttackProfile, attack matrix) — One entry of the dense `[attack][attacker][defender]` matrix that resolves a hit: damage type and value, effect mask, chained and charged follow-ups, an over-time status, and the collision profile and kinetic immunity a knockback applies. See also **collision profile**. [Combat §5](combat.md#5-resolution-statuses-and-what-crosses) · `internal/profile` `AttackProfile`, `Attack`

**audio** — Everything vif plays, sound effects and music together: the engine, its service and backend, and the speakers' channel mask, pause and telemetry. See also **sfx**, **music**, **sound**. [Audio](audio.md) · `pkg/audio`; `internal/system` `AudioSystem`

**audio backend** (also: -ab) — The external PCM player the audio engine pipes samples to: `pacat`, `pw-cat`, `aplay`, `sox`, `ffplay`, or `oss` on FreeBSD, tried in that order with failover; `-ab` forces one, or the synthetic `null` or `wav:<path>`. [Audio §3](audio.md#3-backend-detection-and-failover) · `pkg/audio` `BackendConfig`

**audio engine** (also: AudioEngine) — The thread-safe, pure-Go control surface of `pkg/audio`: sound and pattern registries, backend lifecycle and failover, and an ordered command channel to the mixer goroutine. [Audio §1](audio.md#1-layered-design) · `pkg/audio` `AudioEngine`

**AUR** (also: Arch User Repository) — The Arch Linux package repository the `vif` package, with an optional `vif-git`, targets. [Packaging §4](packaging.md#4-arch-aur)

**authority** (also: `-authority`) — three senses. See also **term**, **coordinator**, **succession**, **handoff**.
- *Multiplayer*: the peer authoring the Shared world and its corrections under the current term: the coordinator until a handoff moves authorship. Each instance holds its view of term, holder, succession chain and handoff record. [Multi-player §5.0](multi-player.md#50-who-listens-who-authors-who-can-be-reached) · [Multi-player §5.1](multi-player.md#51-succession) · `internal/converge` `Authority`
- *Policy*: `-authority host|migrate`: `migrate` hands authorship to the successor the succession chain designates, `host` ends the session with its authoring participant so every survivor forks. The default is `host` with `-serve` and `migrate` otherwise. [Runtime §1.0](runtime.md#10-the-four-session-flags) · `internal/app` `AuthorityHost`, `AuthorityMigrate`
- *Geometry*: map bounds are Shared authority (D-14), carried in every capture header; `Mode.OwnsGeometry` says whether this run's terminal decides them. [D-14](domain-design.md#d-14--map-bounds-are-shared-authority) · `internal/app` `Mode.OwnsGeometry`

**authority report** — two senses.
- *Succession*: a flooded loss notice `{Term, From, Lost}` that lets a survivor more than one link away start succession; it elects nobody. [Multi-player §5.1](multi-player.md#51-succession) · [Services and networking §8](services-and-networking.md#8-wire-frame-and-session-messages) · `internal/network` `AuthorityReport`, `MsgAuthorityReport`
- *Diagnostics*: the structured answer `Authority.State` gives to who is authoring (term, authority, local participant, migrations, fork, retained capture, roster and anchor), which tests read; `:session` shows the one-line `Authority.Summary` instead. [Telemetry reference: Session telemetry](telemetry-reference.md#session-telemetry) · `internal/converge` `AuthorityReport`, `Authority.State`

**auto-fire** (also: automatic fire, :auto) — Router-driven repeated firing that starts with both weapons; `a` in Normal mode or `:auto` cycles both, off and main only. It is operator state and admits no APM. [Input §7](input-and-modes.md#7-mouse-and-automatic-fire) · `internal/engine` `GameContext.AutoFire`

## B

**background** — three senses. See also **quadrant mode**.
- *FSM*: a background region (`background = true`) runs fully but stays out of the foreground summary the status bar shows. [FSM §3](fsm-and-configuration.md#3-root-schema) · `internal/fsm` `RegionConfig`
- *Rendering*: a terminal cell's background channel; finalization fills a cell no layer drew with the normal background, or with the deferred background overlay a renderer set. [Rendering §3](rendering.md#3-compositor-data-model) · [Rendering §6](rendering.md#6-finalization-and-color-modes) · `internal/render` `RenderBuffer`
- *Images*: background mode converts each sampled pixel to one terminal background cell. [Content §8](content-assets-and-tools.md#8-image-and-pattern-pipeline) · `pkg/ascimage` `ModeBackgroundOnly`

**barrier** (also: playout barrier, session barrier) — two senses. See also **barrier-bound crossing**, **playout lead**.
- *Schedule*: the network system's queue of own and received crossings, each waiting for its apply tick and released into that tick's body in `(ApplyTick, Source, Seq)` order, where they settle after the systems; it is the event queue's wire sink. [Domain model §4.1](domain-design.md#41-receive-lead) · [Multi-player §3.1](multi-player.md#31-ordinary-and-barrier-bound-crossings) · `internal/system` `NetworkSystem`, `barrierArtifact`; `internal/event` `WireSink`
- *Latch*: whether a run's crossings pass through the barrier, latched as session-shared so a replay of a session defers by the same lead. [D-14](domain-design.md#d-14--map-bounds-are-shared-authority) · `internal/engine` `World.SessionBarrier`

**barrier-bound crossing** (also: barrierBound) — A crossing that decides what the world is rather than what happens in it: participant joined or departed, game reset, swarm or quasar spawn, drain defeated, cursor defeat state, a hold. It applies at one agreed tick on every instance and closes its source's capture fence at production; it stays out of its producer's own replay suffix, while every instance retains its applied copy so a projection over an earlier capture re-applies it at its tick. See also **ordinary crossing**, **crossing fence**. [Multi-player §3.1](multi-player.md#31-ordinary-and-barrier-bound-crossings) · `internal/system` `barrierBound`


**behavior** — four senses. See also **behavior machine**, **behavior program**.
- *Composites*: the routing id that names which species system owns a composite's events (`BehaviorGold` through `BehaviorKraken`, and `BehaviorData` for every data species). [ECS §6](ecs-and-events.md#6-composite-entities) · `internal/component` `Behavior`, `HeaderComponent`
- *Particles*: `ParticleComponent.Behavior` selects the dust, decay or blossom rules a particle follows. [Gameplay §4](gameplay.md#4-glyph-production-and-decay) · `internal/component` `ParticleBehavior`
- *Input*: the class of a key-table entry, such as motion, operator, char-wait, action or system. [Input §3](input-and-modes.md#3-normal-mode-grammar) · `internal/input` `KeyBehavior`
- *Species*: the `Behavior` component a data species' header carries (program, machine instance, frame, stored target, `Shielded`), which `BehaviorSystem` steps through its behavior program. See also **data species**. [Behavior machines §6.4](machine-design.md#64-beyond-the-machine) · `internal/component` `BehaviorComponent` · `internal/system` `BehaviorSystem`

**behavior machine** (also: pkg/machine, machine.Graph) — The standard-library-only per-entity state-machine runtime: a `machine.Instance` for a component to carry, stepped by an immutable `machine.Graph` that a species system declares in typed Go or a behavior program compiles to. The swarm (`SwarmComponent.Machine`: chase, lock, charge, teleport, arrive, decelerate), the quasar (`QuasarComponent.Machine`: homing, charging, zapping) and each storm circle (`StormCircleComponent.Machine`: cooldown, active, one graph per colour) run on code graphs, every data species on its program's. [Behavior machines §1](machine-design.md#1-overview) · [§2](machine-design.md#2-runtime) · [§4](machine-design.md#4-species-graphs) · `pkg/machine` `Graph`, `Instance`; `internal/system` `SwarmSystem`, `QuasarSystem`, `StormSystem`, `BehaviorSystem`

**behavior program** (also: [behaviors], behavior/<name>.toml) — A species defined by files: a `[prototype]` body and a state machine in the FSM dialect's keys with the machine's additions, listed in the scenario's `[behaviors]` table and compiled once per run against the game vocabulary into a `machine.Graph`; at most `BehaviorProgramsMax` (32), taking species IDs 10 to 41 in sorted-name order. See also **data species**. [FSM reference: Behavior Programs](fsm-reference.md#behavior-programs) · [Behavior machines §6](machine-design.md#6-data-species) · `internal/behavior` `Compile` · `internal/system` `CompileBehaviors`

**blend mode** (also: BlendMode) — How a draw combines with the cell beneath it (replace, alpha, add, max, soft light, screen, overlay), with flags selecting the foreground and background channels. [Rendering §4](rendering.md#4-blend-modes) · `internal/render` `BlendMode`

**block** — two senses.
- *Content*: a content block is a unit of up to five corpus lines that the glyph system places as one spawn; plain text closes one at five lines or at a top-level indent shift, drops a trailing single line and leaves out lines under five characters, and TOML content declares its blocks, splitting longer ones. [Content §4](content-assets-and-tools.md#4-plain-text-parsing) · [Content §5](content-assets-and-tools.md#5-authored-toml-content) · `internal/core` `CodeBlock`
- *Walls*: a wall's block mask states what it stops: cursors, kinetic movers, particles or spawning. [Gameplay §9](gameplay.md#9-walls-maps-navigation-and-environment) · `internal/component` `WallBlockMask`

**block** (also: `Index.Blocks`, `blockOf`, plane) — The position package's word for a cell an occupant blocks for a mask (0 is any), the game's **wall**: the index keeps a plane of bits for each mask asked, a bit a cell, brought to the cell's shared occupants the engine names walls by their `Components.Wall` mask and box style whenever one is placed, moved, removed or remasked there, and a wall query answers from the **surface**s first, then the bit. The walls stay entities; the planes are derived, never captured. See also **spatial grid**, **wall log**. [Position §1](position-package.md#1-the-boundary) · [ECS §3](ecs-and-events.md#3-position-and-spatial-queries) · `pkg/position` `Index.Blocks`, `Index.BlocksArea`, `Index.BlockChangedAt`, `Index.BlockBits` · `internal/engine` `Position.wallOf`, `Position.WallChanged`

**blossom** (also: ParticleBlossom) — A particle, raised from the bottom edge by a wave or released from the red glyphs a positive tracer crosses, that raises a glyph's level up to bright and is spent on a red glyph; a blossom and a decay that meet annihilate. See also **decay**, **dust**. [Gameplay §4](gameplay.md#4-glyph-production-and-decay) · `internal/component` `ParticleBlossom`

**boot** (also: boot settle, boot chain) — A scenario's initial states' entries and every transition the events of that settle trigger, run before the clock starts: at load, at each reset and under the start hold a game waits in, where a joiner lands too. It spawns the cursor and sets the level up, drawn behind the start prompt; the game begins at the first `Tick`, which every shipped scenario's boot state leaves on. See also **hold**, **wait**. [FSM and configuration §5](fsm-and-configuration.md#5-tick-processing) · [FSM reference: Transitions](fsm-reference.md#transitions) · `internal/app` `App.holdFirstGame` · `internal/engine` `Scheduler.executeReset`

**boost** (also: BoostComponent) — A per-cursor timed reward started or extended by a correct character or a species kill credited to that cursor (a predicted kill only once confirmed); while active it doubles heat gain per character and blocks ordinary energy penalties, and a typing error ends it. It is owner-authored state (D-13). [Gameplay: Boost](gameplay.md#boost) · `internal/component` `BoostComponent`; `internal/system` `BoostSystem`

**boss stage** (also: `RaidClear`, `RaidBoss`, `boss` region, `raid_boss`, `Rush`) — The end of a vanguard cave's **tier**: the fourth **rank**'s third kill holds the raid; once no raider is left it strobes green, a **cave opening** to no rock comes in past the right edge, the rock and the drift at twice their pace (up to `CaveMaxSpeed` and `DriftMaxSpeed`), and as it fills the map the scroll and the drift ease to rest over a second and the **Kraken** rises at the right edge with 120 hit points in the first cave, thirty seconds of the gun on it, and twice the last one's in each after. A player's kill drops a missile charge, heat and two energy, doubles the **damage multiplier**, and lays the next cave behind a **cave mouth** in another stone art, a fifth faster in whole columns a second up to `CaveMaxSpeed`, its drift four times that up to `DriftMaxSpeed`, the rock and the drift rushing in from rest at twice that pace until the cave fills the map past its mouth (`EventCaveEntered`), then easing to its own, where the raid resumes and the climb starts over at the first rank. [Gameplay §10](gameplay.md#10-embedded-campaign-progression) · `wad/scenario/vanguard/scenario.toml` `RaidClear`, `RaidBoss`, `BossApproach`..`BossExit`

**bot** (also: -bot, :bot) — A participant driven by a graph instead of a terminal: it holds a roster slot and a cursor, unless the map places none, and its intents pass through the same mode router under the same session rules. See also **seat**, **graph**. [Bots](bots.md#bots) · [Bots: Flags](bots.md#flags) · `internal/bot` `Driver`, `Graph`

**boundary** — five senses. See also **position lattice**, **crossing fence**, **compared surface**.
- *Journal*: the third counter of the position lattice `(run, tick, boundary)`; a tick opens it at 0, its body, and it advances when a settle group closes, the body's own first, so the between-tick groups count from 1, where a run and a **written world** open. [Logging: Position lattice](logging-and-diagnostics.md#position-lattice) · `internal/event` `Stamp`, `EventQueue.NextBoundary`
- *Capture*: the line a capture draws through crossings: ordinary frames are judged by their source's crossing fence, barrier-bound frames by the capture tick. [Multi-player §3.2](multi-player.md#32-the-capture-boundary) · `pkg/network` `CrossingFences`
- *Poll*: the point in each tick where the network system drains the transport, so network goroutines never touch the world. [Services and networking §9](services-and-networking.md#9-poll-boundary-and-receive-lead) · `internal/system` `NetworkSystem`
- *Weapons*: a weapon belongs to its host's domain: a cursor's weapon crosses only its impacts, and a mounted one crosses nothing. [Combat §1](combat.md#1-the-boundary) · `internal/system` `WeaponSystem`
- *Replay comparison*: the status keys a replay is verified on, leaving out operator, presentation and session state. [Runtime: Replay comparison boundary](runtime.md#replay-comparison-boundary) · `internal/snapshot` `SimDeniedKey`

**braiding** — The maze generator's setting from 0 (a perfect maze, a tree) to 1 (no dead ends) that removes walls to add cycles. [AI and physics §7](ai-physics-and-evolution.md#7-maze-generation) · `pkg/maze` `Config.Braiding`

**breakpoint** (also: :d break) — A standing stop condition on an event dispatch, a region spawning, a state entered or a status comparison; it pauses the run each time it trips until cleared, survives `:new` and is refused in a live session. [Runtime §9](runtime.md#9-time-control) · [Input §9](input-and-modes.md#9-command-mode) · `internal/engine` `Breakpoint`, `BreakKind`

**browser route** (also: wss route, /vif/ws/<session>) — The public `wss://<site>/vif/ws/<id>` path: the site's nginx terminates TLS, the router checks the origin before the upgrade, then ends the WebSocket and places its payload as the front door places a dial, refusing in the stream, and splices it to the session pod's game port. [Fleet §9](kubernetes-fleet.md#9-the-session-routes) · [Platforms: The public route](multi-platform.md#the-public-route) · `tool/vif-router` `wsRouter`

**build profile** (also: build tag, build variant) — A compile-time capability set: the full native build, `vif_headless`, `vif_noaudio`, the js/wasm browser build, the desktop window in every Linux and FreeBSD build (`pkg/window`; `vif_nogui`, `make cli`, leaves it out and `vif_gui`, `make gui`, opens it by default) and the experimental Windows cross-build, with `novlog` orthogonal to all. [Platforms §1](multi-platform.md#1-current-build-profiles) · [Logging §15](logging-and-diagnostics.md#15-build-variants) · `Makefile`

**bullet** (also: BulletComponent, BulletSystem) — A straight-line projectile with contact damage, stopped by walls and bounds and ended by its lifetime: a cursor's turret bullets resolve against species, and a hostile one (a mounted turret's, such as the storm's red circle) strikes cursors and shields. [Combat §2](combat.md#2-layers) · `internal/component` `BulletComponent`; `internal/system` `BulletSystem`

**bundle** (also: MsgBundle) — One session message packing several frames for one peer: the raw epochs and owner syncs a guest sends up, or the committed copies and own epoch and syncs an authority or relay sends a link behind it. The port packs them and releases a link's when a drain ends, when the tick closes and before a frame of any other kind, so a link reads its frames in the order they were sent; a frame too large to share travels alone, as a correction chunk does. [Services and networking §8](services-and-networking.md#8-wire-frame-and-session-messages) · `pkg/network` `Bundle`, `Unbundle`, `SocketPort.Release`; `internal/network` `Bundles`; `internal/engine` `BundlingPort`

**burn** (also: StatusBurn, CombatAttackBurn) — The charged disruptor pulse's chained over-time status: `BurnDamage` every `BurnPeriod` for `BurnDuration`, credited to the last refresher; only a ward refuses it, and it spends no immunity window. [Combat §5](combat.md#5-resolution-statuses-and-what-crosses) · `internal/component` `StatusBurn`, `CombatAttackBurn`

**burst** — two senses. See also **ember**, **admission**.
- *Heat*: the heat burst emitted when overheat reaches its threshold; it flashes the meter and starts the ember phase. [Gameplay: Heat and ember](gameplay.md#heat-and-ember) · `internal/event` `EventHeatBurst`
- *Admission*: `NetworkAdmitBurst`, the joins one dialling address is admitted per `NetworkAdmitWindow`. [Services and networking §7.2](services-and-networking.md#72-join-identity-and-who-decides-it) · `internal/parameter` `NetworkAdmitBurst`

**bus** — two senses. See also **event class**.
- *Events*: `ClassBus` is the event class of a request a Player producer may raise to affect Shared state; such an event reaches the wire only when pushed through the crossing path (`World.PushCrossing`), the D-3 crossing. [D-10](domain-design.md#d-10--event-class-and-event-domain-answer-different-questions) · `internal/event` `ClassBus`
- *Audio*: a separate floating-point submix: the mixer renders music and sfx into their own buses, and a sound definition may route layers through named buses. [Audio §2](audio.md#2-stream-contract) · [Audio §5](audio.md#5-sounds-and-sound-effects) · `pkg/audio` `Mixer`

**button** — two senses. See also **input hook**, **echo**.
- *Gameplay*: one of a row of Shared on-map buttons a scenario lays out (as `simon` does), each bound to a key and a sound; a key or click presses it through a crossing that lights it a lead later. [Gameplay §10](gameplay.md#10-embedded-campaign-progression) · [Input §1](input-and-modes.md#1-input-pipeline) · `internal/component` `ButtonComponent`; `internal/system` `ButtonSystem`
- *Echo*: this instance's own latest press, lit and sounded at once before the crossing applies; it presents the gesture, not the verdict, and is never replicated. [D-18](domain-design.md#d-18--local-prediction-is-private) · `internal/engine` `ViewResource.ButtonEcho`

## C

**cadence** (also: correction cadence, keyframe interval) — How many ticks separate the authority's corrections to one peer, with a keyframe interval beside it; both adapt to the link within the convergence floor. See also **convergence floor**, **operating point**. [D-24](domain-design.md#d-24--cadence-adapts-the-convergence-floor-does-not) · [Multi-player §2](multi-player.md#2-current-contract) · `pkg/linkpace` `Plan`, `Controller`

**camera** (also: CameraSystem, FollowCamera) — The viewport's top-left over a map larger than the terminal; it follows the local cursor's cell with a dead zone and is local view state. [Rendering §2](rendering.md#2-render-context-and-coordinates) · `internal/engine` `ConfigResource.FollowCamera`; `internal/system` `CameraSystem`

**campaign** (also: embedded campaign, main campaign, `main`) — The main scenario every build embeds from `internal/asset/scenario/`, which `-s main` plays unless a configuration root supplies `scenario/main`. Only its background monitor region starts at boot, spawning and arming the cursor and opening `main`, which cycles gold sequences and decay waves and escalates without end through quasar, storm, Kraken and tower encounters. See also **encounter**, **monitor**, **scenario**. [Gameplay §10](gameplay.md#10-embedded-campaign-progression) · [FSM and configuration §2](fsm-and-configuration.md#2-scenario-resolution) · `internal/asset` `MainScenario` · `internal/paths` `MainScenarioName`

**capability** — two senses.
- *Services*: capability injection: after `InitAll` each service contributes one narrow capability (files, content, audio, network) to the world's resources, so systems never import services. [Services and networking §5](services-and-networking.md#5-capability-injection) · `internal/service` `ResourceContributor`
- *Builds*: a system's local build capability (`audio`); builds without it swap such systems for null sinks, and the manifest fingerprint ignores it. [Platforms §2](multi-platform.md#2-composition-boundary) · `internal/manifest` `SystemDef.Capability`

**capture** — six senses. See also **snapshot**, **written world**, **header**.
- *Shared world*: the Shared world at one tick (D-19): a header (run and tick, build, seed and scenario identity, map bounds, term and authority, one crossing fence per participant, integrity) and a body of Shared stores, allocator counters, RNG positions, declared system state, the compared status surface and FSM state. A join installs one and a keyframe carries one. [Multi-player §3.2](multi-player.md#32-the-capture-boundary) · [D-19](domain-design.md#d-19--every-future-affecting-shared-value-is-restorable) · `internal/snapshot` `SharedCapture`, `CaptureHeader`, `Schema`
- *Journal*: a `capture` record is a written world in a journal, and "captured" events are the ones the queue journals; `journal.Capture` is an in-memory journal sink for test harnesses. [Logging: Written worlds and notes](logging-and-diagnostics.md#written-worlds-and-notes) · [ECS: Replay capture](ecs-and-events.md#replay-capture) · `internal/event` `JournalCapture`; `internal/journal` `Capture`
- *Input*: key capture is the set of keys a scenario takes from play-mode parsing (`EventKeyCaptureRequest`); a captured key becomes a key press and release for the scenario, and `releases = true` asks a console keyboard for releases while it holds. [Input §1](input-and-modes.md#1-input-pipeline) · `internal/input` `KeyCapture`
- *FSM*: payload capture (`capture_vars`) copies event payload fields into FSM variables when a transition matches. [FSM: Payload capture](fsm-and-configuration.md#payload-capture) · `internal/fsm` `TransitionConfig.CaptureVars`
- *Runtime output*: with `-dev`, the process's stderr goes to a file so races, fatal errors and panics are kept as runtime reports. [Logging §10](logging-and-diagnostics.md#10-runtime-output-capture) · `internal/core` `CaptureStderr`
- *Profiler*: a profile capture is the CPU, heap or mutex profile or execution trace `:d cpu|heap|mutex|trace` writes to the log directory, whether or not the profiler is on; a timed one runs `[s]` seconds, `ProfCaptureDefault` (10 s) when omitted and at most `ProfCaptureMax` (5 min). See **vif-cpu**. [Logging §11](logging-and-diagnostics.md#11-profiler) · `internal/prof` `StartCPU`, `StartTrace`, `StartMutex`

**card** (also: telemetry card, OverlayCard) — One status group in the telemetry overlay or HUD, holding at most `OverlayCardMaxEntries` (15) metrics; `/` filters cards and Space pins one. See also **pin**, **telemetry**. [Telemetry reference: The registry](telemetry-reference.md#the-registry) · [Logging §6: the telemetry overlay](logging-and-diagnostics.md#integer-units) · `internal/core` `OverlayCard`; `internal/status` `SplitKey`

**carried accumulator** — A `time.Duration` field advanced by dt and reduced by its period, carrying the remainder; two fields, no type. The quasar's speed steps on one (`QuasarComponent.SpeedElapsed`, movement time only), as do the swarm's and a data species' pattern (`SwarmComponent.PatternElapsed`, `BehaviorComponent.PatternElapsed`, time not stunned) and a **cave**'s scroll, a column each period. See also **carried age**. [Behavior machines §3](machine-design.md#3-hosting-a-machine) · `internal/component` `QuasarComponent.SpeedElapsed`, `SwarmComponent.PatternElapsed`, `BehaviorComponent.PatternElapsed`

**carried age** — A `time.Duration` field its owner advances by dt and resets to zero when its period starts, so a clock travels in a capture with no instant in it and a receiver on another epoch reads it alike: a cursor's shield drain and ember decay (advanced only where the cursor is simulated) and a Wraith's wait (on every instance). A reset a tick's settle makes, after every owner has run, first counts on the next tick, so the period runs whole. See also **carried accumulator**. [Domain design D-19](domain-design.md#d-19--every-future-affecting-shared-value-is-restorable) · [Runtime §5](runtime.md#5-simulation-tick-steps) · `internal/component` `ShieldComponent.DrainElapsed`, `HeatComponent.EmberElapsed`, `WraithComponent.Waited`

**carrier** (also: snapshot carrier, declared system state) — A system's private Shared state that no component holds, saved and restored through `engine.SharedStateSaver` and declared by `SystemDef.Snapshot = "state"`; a capture carries one record per carrier, and a status cell the compared surface excludes but a Shared FSM reads must live in one (D-19). See also **SharedStateSaver**. [D-19](domain-design.md#d-19--every-future-affecting-shared-value-is-restorable) · `internal/engine` `SharedStateSaver`; `internal/manifest` `SystemDef.Snapshot`; `internal/snapshot` `SystemStateRecord`

**catch-up** — The ticks a mid-run joiner simulates after installing its capture to reach the session's tick; a gap past `NetworkJoinCatchUpTicks` (200), or one still wider than `NetworkJoinLagTicks` once caught up, refuses the join. [Multi-player §5](multi-player.md#5-membership-topology-and-authority-continuity) · [D-22](domain-design.md#d-22--admit-before-capturing) · `internal/parameter` `NetworkJoinCatchUpTicks`, `NetworkJoinLagTicks`

**causal player** (also: causal cursor, fuse_owner) — The one cursor a Shared trigger elects deterministically before it reads Player state (D-16); only that cursor's owner emits the resulting crossing. [D-16](domain-design.md#d-16--shared-triggers-elect-one-causal-player) · `internal/system` `FuseSystem`

**cave** (also: `pkg/cave`, `EventCaveStart`, `EventCaveStop`, `EventCavePace`) — A side-scrolling cave of ceiling and floor rock. `pkg/cave` gives each column's rows as a pure integer function of its index and seed, under rough-rock detail and closing in from a centred mouth or a **cave mouth** of open map: a snaking cave's gap and channel as value noise eased by smoothstep, the channel snaking between alternating sides, or a **jagged cave**'s walls reaching in by fractal noise of their own. On `EventCaveStart` the wall system lays the map's columns as a **rock field**, drawn in a visual or in the **stone art** preset `art` names, then scrolls it at a pace in columns a second, a **ramp** up to the soft cap `CaveMaxSpeed`, each whole column the pace carries a step that moves it and every Shared wall a column left and lays the column entering, as many a tick as it carries, the particles no anchor holds riding them in the **cave frame**; rock arriving on a cursor moves it a column left and a row toward the channel, a tick's steps carrying it on and its move going out once, a flying composite moves whole, and against the left edge is a **crush**. `EventCaveStop` brings the pace to rest and `EventCavePace` sets a new one, each at once or eased, and a **cave opening** widens it past the right edge. Its profile, pace, carried phase and offset ride the wall system's capture record, from which a start, a resize and a load lay the same field. See also **vanguard**, **wall**. [Gameplay §9](gameplay.md#9-walls-maps-navigation-and-environment) · `pkg/cave` `Config.At` · `internal/system` `WallSystem` · `internal/event` `CaveStartPayload`

**cave frame** (also: `CaveResource`, carry) — The frame a scrolling **cave** gives what rides it. The wall system leaves a tick's steps in `CaveResource`, and before it moves its particles the particle system carries every one no anchor holds as far left, the cell it last entered with it, so decay and blossom fall or rise down one column of the cave and dust wanders in its frame, entering a cell only by its own motion; one carried off the map is gone. It leaves the scroll's part-column and rate there too, and the rock's look, and the wall renderer carries the rock and the Shared walls on by them to where the scroll stands at a frame's moment, from the field's columns ahead past the edge; what hides behind them reads `Index.BlocksCarried`. See also **particle**, **rock field**. [Gameplay §9](gameplay.md#9-walls-maps-navigation-and-environment) · [Rendering §7](rendering.md#7-layer-inventory) · `internal/engine` `CaveResource` · `pkg/position` `Index.BlocksCarried` · `internal/system` `ParticleSystem`

**cave mouth** (also: `mouth`, `Config.Mouth`, `EventCaveEntered`) — The columns of open map, no rock at all, a **cave** starts with before its **ramp** closes in from none; without one the ramp closes in from a centred mouth of `max_gap` rows. Vanguard's is the map's width, so a game opens on open map and the cave comes in past the right edge. The wall system emits the Shared `EventCaveEntered` once the mouth and the ramp have scrolled past the left edge, the cave's own rock filling the map. Past the ramp the walls keep `min_wall` rows, a **jagged cave**'s median included. [Gameplay §9](gameplay.md#9-walls-maps-navigation-and-environment) · `pkg/cave` `Config.Mouth` · `internal/event` `CaveStartPayload`

**cave opening** (also: `EventCaveOpen`, `EventCaveOpened`, `cave.Opening`) — A **cave** widening for an encounter: from the first column past the map's right edge, so no rock in view changes, over `ramp` columns, the cave's own when 0, to `gap` open rows centred, at most all the walls leave, or no rock at all when 0, and held open from there; it applies after a **jagged cave**'s median, and a cave opens once. The wall system emits the Shared `EventCaveOpened` as the opening fills the map, the cue a boss encounter waits on. [Gameplay §9](gameplay.md#9-walls-maps-navigation-and-environment) · `pkg/cave` `Opening` · `internal/system` `WallSystem`

**cell** — six senses. See also **spatial grid**, **status**, **interest cell**, **coordinate space**, **maze**.
- *Spatial grid*: one 256-byte grid bucket holding up to 31 entities, Shared first, with the Player half capped at 12. [ECS §3](ecs-and-events.md#3-position-and-spatial-queries) · `pkg/position` `Cell`, `Grid`
- *Map*: the integer simulation coordinate of positions, walls and motion, a column and a row; a cell of a volume adds its layer. [Rendering §2](rendering.md#2-render-context-and-coordinates) · `pkg/vmath` `Point`, `Point3` · `internal/component` `PositionComponent`
- *Terminal*: one character cell of the render buffer: rune, foreground, background and attributes. [Rendering §3](rendering.md#3-compositor-data-model) · `internal/render` `RenderBuffer`
- *Status*: one typed atomic metric in the status registry; one asked for after `Freeze` is a detached cell, usable but absent from snapshots and counted in `stat.late`. [Logging: Freeze](logging-and-diagnostics.md#freeze) · `internal/status` `Registry`
- *Interest*: a peer's reported cursor cell, carried in its link report as a relevance hint for correction scheduling and never as simulation input. [Services and networking §8](services-and-networking.md#8-wire-frame-and-session-messages) · `pkg/linkpace` `Cell`
- *Maze*: one wall-or-passage cell of `pkg/maze`'s odd-sized grid; `EventMazeSpawnRequest` scales each to `cell_width` × `cell_height` map cells, so the maze spans the map divided by them. [AI and physics §7](ai-physics-and-evolution.md#7-maze-generation) · `internal/event` `MazeSpawnRequestPayload` · `internal/system` `WallSystem`

**cell aspect** (also: CellAspect, `vmath.Aspect`, RefAspect) — The width-over-height ratio of one cell, reported by the presentation and held in `ConfigResource.CellAspect`; a terminal's is the reference 0.5, every authored vertical extent scales by `Aspect.Y`, navigation costs come from `navigation.CostsFor`, and it joins the anchor as simulation identity. [Rendering §2.2](rendering.md#22-the-cell-aspect)

**chain** — five senses. See also **succession**.
- *Succession*: the succession chain, every participant that declared a port, in join order with its address, which survivors dial after a loss. [Multi-player §5.0](multi-player.md#50-who-listens-who-authors-who-can-be-reached) · [Multi-player §5.3](multi-player.md#53-reachability-under--authority-migrate) · `internal/network` `SuccessionChain`
- *Combat*: an attack chain is a follow-up attack an attack profile derives from a hit, such as the tracer's lightning or the charged pulse's burn; it is derived on every instance and never transported. [Combat §5](combat.md#5-resolution-statuses-and-what-crosses) · `internal/profile` `AttackProfile.Chain`
- *Audio*: a sound's processor chain of filters, envelopes, shaping and gain between a source and its bus. [Audio §5](audio.md#5-sounds-and-sound-effects) · `pkg/audio`
- *Seeds*: `MixChain` folds several values into one seed, one SplitMix64 round per value, so the result moves with every input and its order. [AI and physics §6](ai-physics-and-evolution.md#6-numeric-model-and-determinism) · `pkg/vmath` `MixChain`
- *Behavior machines*: an edge's candidate chain, the target states it tries in order, each by its guard and then its state's probe, with a fallback required when every candidate can fail. [Behavior machines §2.2](machine-design.md#22-graph) · [§2.3](machine-design.md#23-step) · `pkg/machine` `Edge`, `Target`

**charge** (also: charge cap, cap effect, `MaxCharges`) — two senses.
- *Weapons*: a weapon kind's charge count: zero means not owned, it only rises, and it scales the kind up to `MaxCharges`, where the kind keeps a cap effect until the loadout is lost. [Combat §4](combat.md#4-at-the-charge-cap) · [Gameplay §7](gameplay.md#7-weapons-and-attacks) · `internal/component` `WeaponComponent.Charges`, `WeaponSpec.MaxCharges`
- *Species*: a species' attack run or wind-up: the swarm's charge at a locked target, the Kraken's charge at the farthest cursor, and the quasar's charge before it zaps a cursor out of range. [Gameplay §8](gameplay.md#8-hostile-and-structural-actors) · `internal/component` `SwarmStateCharge`, `QuasarCharging`; `internal/system` `SwarmSystem`, `KrakenSystem`

**charge line** (also: `ChargeLine`, `ChargeLineRenderer`) — Two pulses from a body's centre to the centre of the footprint its charge lands on, over the end of its lock, so a player sees where a charge goes before it leaves: the swarm's lock, and a data species' state flagged `ChargeLine`. See also **teleport line**, **landing marker**. [Rendering §7](rendering.md#7-layer-inventory) · [FSM reference: Behavior Programs](fsm-reference.md#behavior-programs) · `internal/render/renderer` `ChargeLineRenderer` · `internal/component` `BehaviorComponent.ChargeLine`

**-check** — Resolves and validates `vif.toml`, the scenario, keymap, audio overrides, content and every bot graph, prints the result and exits without starting a game. [Development §4 Run](development.md#run) · [FSM §12](fsm-and-configuration.md#12-validation-rules-and-diagnostics) · `internal/resource` `Check`

**checkpoint** (also: replay checkpoint, WorldCopy) — A replay copy's whole state (world, queue, scheduler, system state, content cursor, clock) and world digest, used to step back and seek: the last `ReplayRing` (8) steps keep one each, and older ones sit every `ReplayCheckpointSteps` (100) steps, the spacing doubling past `ReplayCheckpoints` (32). [Runtime: Replay playback](runtime.md#replay-playback) · `internal/app` `checkpoint`; `internal/engine` `WorldCopy`

**chunk** — One transport frame of a capture, correction or scenario body, carrying a 28-byte header (run, tick, chunk index, chunk count, total length) before its payload; the receiver reassembles and refuses gaps and overruns. [Services and networking §8](services-and-networking.md#8-wire-frame-and-session-messages) · `pkg/network` `SnapshotAssembly`; `internal/parameter` `SnapshotCorrectionChunkBytes`

**claim** (also: claim token, `claimKeeper`, `MsgJoinClaims`, `RefusalReturning`, `CursorRecord`) — What a participant comes back to its place with: a random token its process keeps (a page in its tab's `sessionStorage`, a terminal for the process's life) whose digest its roster entry and cursor carry. A dial names its tokens when the coordinator asks, before a place is assigned, and one whose digest an away entry carries takes back the **Wraith**'s place, a full session's too, with the energy, heat, health and loadout its cursor last synced before it left. A claim whose place is still linked closes that link and is refused to retry in a moment. A bot its holder takes out of a session keeps its place in the one the holder opens under a new token over a copy of its record, so the claim it left under keeps that record for a return. [Multi-player §5.4](multi-player.md#54-wraiths) · [Services and networking §8](services-and-networking.md#8-wire-frame-and-session-messages) · `internal/app` `claimKeeper`, `claimKeeper.carry`, `membership.claimedLocked` · `internal/network` `MsgJoinClaims`, `ClaimDigest` · `internal/event` `CursorRecord` · `internal/system` `CursorSystem.reclaim`

**client budget** (also: -client-joins, -client-creates) — The router's per-address limits: joins per address over both proxied routes (`-client-joins`), keyed by the dialler's address on the front door and by the header `-client-address-header` names on the browser route, and sessions created per address (`-client-creates`, one per first-join window; a refused or full-fleet request spends none); an IPv6 address counts by its /64; with no header named, the API and browser route charge none. See also **admission**. [Fleet §4](kubernetes-fleet.md#4-security-posture) · `tool/vif-router`

**clip** (also: playfield clip) — The compositor bound that confines simulation-space layers and the cursors to the playfield, set per layer by its render priority; screen-space layers stay unclipped. See also **playfield**. [Rendering §2.1](rendering.md#21-the-playfield-clip) · `internal/render` `RenderPriority.ClipsToPlayfield`, `RenderBuffer.SetClip`

**clock** (also: PausableClock, ManualClock) — The time source a run advances under: `PausableClock` for interactive play and dedicated hosts, `ManualClock` for headless, replay and script runs, whose caller steps it with `App.Tick`. See also **time control**, **SimTime**. [Runtime §3](runtime.md#3-runtime-clocks-and-cadence) · `internal/engine` `PausableClock`, `ManualClock`

**collision profile** (also: CollisionProfile) — The impulse parameters one contact applies (mass ratio, impulse range, angle variance, additive or override mode, offset influence), kept as tables per impactor and target. [AI and physics §5](ai-physics-and-evolution.md#5-physics-primitives) · `pkg/vmath/physics` `CollisionProfile`, `ApplyCollision`; `internal/profile`

**column layout** (also: columns, `SharedWorldEncodeStoreColumns`, `snapshot_columns_gen.go`) — The form a keyframe's and a delta's store rows take on the wire: a row count, the entities as zigzag deltas in capture order, then each leaf field of the component over every row, integers as varints, floats as their bits, strings and times behind a length, a slice behind its length plus one so nil travels as nil; generated per component beside the stores by `gen-manifest`, so a row's entropy, its entity, stops repeating with every field and a td keyframe deflates to a twentieth of its JSON rows. The manifest's rows, shards and journals stay JSON. [Netcode §4](netcode-design.md#a-columnar-keyframe-codec) · [Netcode §3.7](netcode-design.md#37-readings-for-the-last-batch) · `internal/engine` `snapshot_columns.go`

**combat** (also: CombatComponent, CombatSystem) — The resolution of attacks on combat entities through the attack-profile matrix: hit points, damage immunity per cursor and family, kinetic immunity shared by every attacker, knockback, stun and one status slot per kind. See also **attack profile**, **attack family**. [Combat](combat.md#combat-and-weapons) · [Combat §5](combat.md#5-resolution-statuses-and-what-crosses) · `internal/component` `CombatComponent`; `internal/system` `CombatSystem`

**command** (also: ex command, command mode) — A `:` command typed in command mode or issued by a script, run by the mode router under the world lock; commands that would desynchronise Shared state are refused in a live session. See also **command frame**, **operator**. [Input §9](input-and-modes.md#9-command-mode) · `internal/mode` `ExecuteCommand`, `CommandNames`

**command frame** (also: WireBatch, production epoch) — One participant's production epoch on the wire, in the binary envelope: its encoded crossings (event as its registry index, domain, payload in the generated codec, sequence), each with an apply tick, named by source, run and producing tick; a receiver in another run refuses it. A guest sends it raw to the authority, which commits it and relays the committed copy. See also **wire frame**, **epoch**. [Multi-player §3.5](multi-player.md#35-command-frames-the-commit-and-pacing) · `internal/event` `WireBatch`, `ScheduledWireFrame`

**commit** — five senses.
- *Crossings*: the authority's decision on each guest crossing: apply at its stamped tick when on time, at the authority's next tick when late, or void it past `NetworkCommitLateTicks` (20). Other instances apply another participant's crossings only as committed. [Multi-player §3.5](multi-player.md#35-command-frames-the-commit-and-pacing) · [Domain model §4.1](domain-design.md#41-receive-lead) · `internal/system` `NetworkSystem`
- *Installs*: a staged install's commit projects the staged capture to the live tick and writes what moves onto the live world between ticks, under the world lock, measuring the correction magnitude. [D-23](domain-design.md#d-23--the-host-world-is-the-correction) · `internal/app` `StagedInstall.Commit`
- *FSM*: a transition commits the target path and resets leaf time after the exit actions and delayed-action cleanup and before the transition and enter actions; the region's trigger mask is rebuilt on every commit. [FSM §7](fsm-and-configuration.md#7-transition-semantics) · `internal/fsm` `Machine`
- *Positions*: a position batch's commit places its batched additions, refusing a cell any entity occupies; `CommitShared` refuses only Shared occupancy, so a Player glyph cannot veto a Shared placement. [ECS §3](ecs-and-events.md#3-position-and-spatial-queries) · `internal/engine` `PositionBatch.Commit`, `PositionBatch.CommitShared`
- *Tick*: a tick's commit increments the tick count and APM and stores its counters while the world is stable, before the tick's production epoch closes; the periodic status snapshot reads them once the world lock is released. [Runtime §5](runtime.md#5-simulation-tick-steps) · `internal/engine` `Scheduler.processTick`

**compared surface** (also: comparison surface, SharedKey) — The status keys and world lines two instances (`SnapshotShared`) or a run and its replay (`SnapshotSimulation`) are compared on; a key outside it is not compared. [Domain model §5](domain-design.md#5-telemetry-and-snapshot-views) · [Runtime: Replay comparison boundary](runtime.md#replay-comparison-boundary) · `internal/snapshot` `SharedKey`, `SimDeniedKey`; `internal/app` `App.SnapshotShared`, `App.SnapshotSimulation`

**component** (also: ECS component, Store[T]) — two senses. See also **update.sh**.
- *ECS*: A plain-data struct attached to an entity and held in one typed sparse-set store per type (Position's also keeps the spatial index), with its logic in the owning system. Each is declared once in the manifest with the entity domain it may attach to, from which its store, mask bit and capture code are generated; `engine.Component` is the world's generated struct of those stores. [ECS §4](ecs-and-events.md#4-component-catalog) · [ECS §2](ecs-and-events.md#2-entities-and-typed-stores) · `internal/component`; `internal/manifest` `Components`; `internal/engine` `Component`
- *Deployment*: one of the node parts `deploy/update.sh` brings current by name, in dependency order: `filter`, `objects`, `wad`, `image`, `router` and `logwisp`; a current one is skipped. [Deploying the session fleet](kube-docker-deploy.md#deploying-the-session-fleet) · `deploy/update.sh`

**composite** (also: composite entity, CompositeType) — A multi-entity actor of one header entity and its member entities; its type says where damage lives: on the header (unit), on each member (ablative) or nowhere (container). See also **header**, **member**. [ECS §6](ecs-and-events.md#6-composite-entities) · `internal/component` `HeaderComponent`, `MemberComponent`, `CompositeType`

**composite passability** (also: CompositePassability) — A grid of bits, one a header, set where a composite's whole footprint runs off the map or over a wall, derived from the walls' bits a row of words at a time, a changed wall cell deriving again only the header rows its footprints reach, and used as the blocked test for its flow fields and route graphs. [Navigation §3](navigation-design.md#3-passability-tiers-and-wall-changes) · `pkg/navigation` `CompositePassability`

**conductor** (also: adaptive game conductor) — The music system's adaptive control of the sequencer: it maps music APM to an intensity tier under hysteresis (a hold before rising, one tier per hold when falling) and a target tempo (`100 + 100·√(APM/480)` BPM) slewed and applied on the beat, keeping game concepts out of `pkg/audio`. [Audio §8](audio.md#8-adaptive-game-conductor) · `internal/system` `MusicSystem`; `internal/parameter` `TierTracker`

**config field** (also: ConfigIntCompare, ConfigBoolCompare, ConfigToVar) — A `ConfigResource` field an FSM script may read or compare: `map_width`, `map_height`, `viewport_width`, `viewport_height`, `camera_x`, `camera_y`, `color_mode` and `crop_on_resize`; the first read of a terminal-local one in a shared session logs a D-14 warning. [FSM reference: Config Fields](fsm-reference.md#config-fields) · [D-14](domain-design.md#d-14--map-bounds-are-shared-authority) · `internal/engine` `ConfigIntAccessor`, `ConfigBoolAccessor`

**config menu** (also: menu, configuration menu, :menu, :config, :g, Ctrl-G, F10, status bar hold) — An in-run overlay of six pages (Game, Scenarios, Multiplayer, Settings, Developer, Launch options), each in titled sections, of settings, actions, text boxes and forms that run the same commands as the command line; its Game page pauses and restarts the game, its Scenarios page marks an installed scenario and starts it; `/` searches every page's rows in place; what `vif.toml`'s `[play]` holds is written back as it changes, the rest changes only the current run, and the keymap is never rewritten; Ctrl-G, F10, `:menu` or a left press held on the status bar for `MenuHoldDuration` opens it on every host, a finger included, and a press selects and acts on its rows. The pages, sections and rows are one tree of `configNode`, each of a kind (group, toggle, choice, step, pick, action, form, info) and keyed by its path of keys from the root (`settings/#Sound/sfx`), which the `tui` rows, a search's rows and a host's ops all name; Android and the browser draw the tree with their own widgets (see **menu snapshot**). [Menu](config-menu.md#menu) · [Menu: Navigation](config-menu.md#navigation) · [Menu: Android and the browser](config-menu.md#android-and-the-browser) · [Input §7](input-and-modes.md#7-mouse-and-automatic-fire) · [Native menu §3](native-menu.md#3-the-tree) · `internal/mode` `configPages`, `configNode`, `configNodeFor`, `applyConfigNode`, `configMenuView`, `Router.onStatusBar`, `Router.pointConfigMenu`

**config root** (also: configuration root, -config-dir, user root, XDG_CONFIG_HOME) — One categorized directory tree searched for scenarios, keymaps, audio, content, images and bot graphs, in priority order: `-config-dir`, the user root (`vif` under Go's `os.UserConfigDir`, which follows `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` on Linux), the checkout's `wad/`, then the system roots (`VIF_SYSTEM_ROOTS` replaces them). [Filesystem layout §3](filesystem-layout.md#3-resolution-policy) · `internal/paths` `ConfigRoots`, `FindFile`

**ConfigResource** (also: Config resource) — The world resource holding map, viewport and camera geometry, crop policy, the scenario's map floor, color mode and console palette, with the map-to-viewport conversions. [ECS §5](ecs-and-events.md#5-singleton-resources) · `internal/engine` `ConfigResource`, `ConfigResource.MapOffset`, `ConfigResource.ViewportToMap`

**confirmed kill** (also: EventSpeciesKillConfirmed) — A local event raised once an authoritative world proves a kill this instance predicted as a guest; loot and boost reward a predicted kill only from it. See also **prediction ledger**. [Multi-player §3.4](multi-player.md#34-predicted-derivations) · `internal/event` `EventSpeciesKillConfirmed`

**console palette** (also: ConsolePalette) — The text console's sixteen colors (Linux's live palette, FreeBSD vt's default or VGA) that every cell settles on in 256-color mode. [Rendering §6](rendering.md#6-finalization-and-color-modes) · `internal/engine` `ConsolePalette`

**console-guard** (also: console guard) — The watchdog the terminal module runs, a second copy of the vif binary, before a Linux or FreeBSD console's keyboard first goes raw for a capture with `releases`: it restores the keyboard and the tty if vif dies where no handler of its own runs (`kill -9`, the OOM killer, a Go fatal error). It runs the `init` of every package initialized before the terminal module's, so none may have side effects. See also **key report**, **core.Go**. [Development §10](development.md#10-crash-and-runtime-capture) · [Input §1](input-and-modes.md#1-input-pipeline) · `github.com/lixenwraith/terminal` `KeySourceConsole`

**contact damage** (also: profile.Contact) — What a species touching a cursor costs it, applied through `strikeCursor` only on a cursor this instance simulates. See also **cursor damage**. [Combat §5](combat.md#5-resolution-statuses-and-what-crosses) · `internal/profile` `Contact`

**control kind** (also: ControlKind, ControlLocal, ControlRemote) — Whether this instance drives a cursor through its own mode router (`ControlLocal`) or mirrors one driven elsewhere (`ControlRemote`); a bot is local on its own instance and remote everywhere else. [Gameplay §2](gameplay.md#2-cursor-and-player-state) · [D-2](domain-design.md#d-2--one-instance-simulates-each-player) · `internal/component` `ControlKind`

**converge** (also: internal/converge, converge.Instance) — The package holding one instance's half of the authority protocol: term and authorship, succession and peer links, per-link cadence, the selective manifest exchange, the install and the playout buffer. `converge.Instance` is all it asks of a run. [Package map §4](package-map.md#4-production-package-responsibilities) · [Logging: sub="converge"](logging-and-diagnostics.md#subconverge) · `internal/converge` `Instance`, `Corrections`, `Authority`

**convergence floor** (also: floor window, SnapshotFloorKeyframeTicks) — The fixed bound of `SnapshotFloorKeyframeTicks` (60 ticks, 3 s) within which every participant must receive a whole world or prove it holds the authority's; cadence times keyframe interval never exceeds it. A link that cannot carry it is refused at admission or reported as `snapshot.cadence_floor_breached`. [D-24](domain-design.md#d-24--cadence-adapts-the-convergence-floor-does-not) · [Multi-player §2](multi-player.md#2-current-contract) · `internal/parameter` `SnapshotFloorKeyframeTicks`

**coordinate space** — One of the three coordinate systems a frame uses: map cells (authoritative simulation positions), viewport (the visible map area after camera crop or centring) and screen (with UI margins, addressing the render buffer). [Rendering §2](rendering.md#2-render-context-and-coordinates) · `internal/render` `RenderContext.MapToScreen`

**coordinator** — two senses. See also **authority**, **participant**.
- *Session*: the participant authoring the session and running its roster: identity 1 (`CoordinatorParticipant`), the instance started with `-host` or `-serve`, or a solo run that opened a session with `:host` or by seating a bot, until a handoff moves the term to a successor; a run that goes on in a session of its own with `:leave` coordinates it under the identity it had. It alone crosses arrivals, departures and resets. A dedicated host's coordinator holds `NoPlayerSlot` and drives no cursor, so participant counts leave it out. [Multi-player §5.0](multi-player.md#50-who-listens-who-authors-who-can-be-reached) · [Domain model §4.4](domain-design.md#44-membership-and-session-control) · `internal/engine` `CoordinatorParticipant`, `SessionState.Coordinator`, `World.IsSessionCoordinator`
- *Handshake*: the host side of the join handshake that `HostAcceptor` runs per dial: the hooks `Admit`, `Assign`, `Release`, `Report` and `Scenario`, and the session `Name`. [Services and networking §7.2](services-and-networking.md#72-join-identity-and-who-decides-it) · `internal/network` `Coordinator`, `HostAcceptor`

**core.Go** (also: crash handler) — The launcher application goroutines start through: it recovers a panic, runs the logging crash hook (which flushes the flight recorder first), restores the terminal, prints the stack and exits. [Development §10](development.md#10-crash-and-runtime-capture) · `internal/core` `Go`, `SetCrashHook`

**cornering brake** (also: TurnSeverity, NavCorneringBrake, NavCorneringBase) — A steering drag, `TurnSeverity` × `NavCorneringBrake` (0.8), that grows as an actor's heading turns from its target and acts at any distance and any heading, since the severity is `NavCorneringBase` (3) minus the turn's cosine; it stops the orbit a homing pull alone sustains, and drains, swarms, quasars, eyes, snake heads and loot apply it. [AI and physics §5](ai-physics-and-evolution.md#5-physics-primitives) · `pkg/vmath/physics` `TurnSeverity`; `internal/parameter` `NavCorneringBrake`, `NavCorneringBase`

**corridor field** (also: CorridorField) — A route's flow field over its corridor alone: the corridor's cells with their distance and step, searched on the graph's grid with everything outside the corridor a wall and kept sorted by cell, so a route costs its corridor rather than the map. [Navigation §8](navigation-design.md#8-route-graphs) · `pkg/navigation` `CorridorField`

**corpus** (also: content corpus, `-f`, `-config-content`, `:content`) — The immutable typing text loaded once at startup from `.txt` and `.toml` sources (printable ASCII, 4 MiB per file, 32 MiB in all), with rejected files kept by name and reason, served as code blocks. See also **block**, **cursor**. [Content §3](content-assets-and-tools.md#3-corpus-model-and-limits) · `internal/content` `Corpus`

**correction** (also: correction pipeline) — The authority's Shared world sent to each guest on that link's cadence: a manifest root first, answered hash-only, by selective repair, or with a keyframe. The guest stages it, projects it if behind and commits it between ticks; nothing acknowledges or retransmits it, and the newest supersedes. See also **manifest**, **selective repair**, **hash-only**, **projection**. [Netcode design §2](netcode-design.md#2-the-correction-pipeline) · [D-23](domain-design.md#d-23--the-host-world-is-the-correction) · `internal/converge` `Corrections`

**correction magnitude** (also: WorldDifference) — How far an install moved this instance's world (disagreeing component cells, the Shared entities behind them, and the largest placement shift), leaving out owner-authored cells; it is telemetry, not a verdict. [D-23](domain-design.md#d-23--the-host-world-is-the-correction) · [Netcode design §2](netcode-design.md#2-the-correction-pipeline) · `internal/engine` `WorldDifference`, `SharedWorldDifference`

**corrector** (also: correction glue, correctionRun) — The type in `internal/app` that is the authority protocol's view of a run: it captures, verifies, stages, projects and installs the shared world over the world and the capture encoder, and reads the run through `correctionRun` alone (its position, authority stamp, session anchor, assigned participant, barrier and journal, and the staging world the run builds); `App` forwards `CaptureShared`, `InstallShared`, `StageShared` and `VerifyCapture` to it. See also **converge**, **staging world**. [Package map §4](package-map.md#4-production-package-responsibilities) · `internal/app` `corrector`, `instance`

**count** (also: vim count) — A numeric prefix that repeats a motion or operator, capped at 9999; operator and motion counts multiply. [Input §3](input-and-modes.md#3-normal-mode-grammar) · `internal/input` `Machine`

**crop on resize** (also: CropOnResize, crop_on_resize) — Whether a terminal resize crops the map to the new size. It rides the join anchor and is written only by level setup and reset; while a session is shared a resize changes only the local view. [D-14](domain-design.md#d-14--map-bounds-are-shared-authority) · [Rendering §2.1](rendering.md#21-the-playfield-clip) · `internal/engine` `ConfigResource.CropOnResize`

**crossing** — two senses. See also **barrier-bound crossing**, **ordinary crossing**, **CrossingID**, **crossing fence**, **crossover**.
- *Multiplayer*: the smallest artifact by which a player mechanic decides a Shared outcome (D-3), such as a cursor's destination cell or an explosion's geometry: a `ClassBus` event pushed through `World.PushCrossing` (or the Stamped direct combat request tagged Shared), stamped with a crossing identity and an **apply tick**, and applied by its producer at the stamped tick and by every other instance at the tick the authority commits. Arrivals, departures and resets cross too, from the coordinator alone; derived follow-ups do not cross again (D-5). One acting as another participant's cursor is refused wherever it applies (`crossingActors`). [D-3](domain-design.md#d-3--cross-the-smallest-shared-outcome) · [Multi-player §3.1](multi-player.md#31-ordinary-and-barrier-bound-crossings) · [Domain model §4.4](domain-design.md#44-membership-and-session-control) · `internal/engine` `World.PushCrossing` · `internal/system` `crossingActors`
- *Energy*: an energy zero crossing is energy reaching zero or changing sign, which clears a cursor's loot weapons and keeps the gun; unrelated to the D-3 sense. [Combat §3](combat.md#3-drivers) · `internal/event` `EventEnergyCrossedZero`

**crossing fence** (also: fence, CrossingFence) — One participant's ordinary-crossing boundary `{Source, Seq}`: the sequence through which a world already contains that participant's crossings. A capture header carries one per participant, and an install drops ordinary frames at or below their source's fence and keeps the rest. [Multi-player §3.2](multi-player.md#32-the-capture-boundary) · `pkg/network` `CrossingFence`, `CrossingFences`

**CrossingID** (also: crossing identity) — A crossing's own identity (producing participant and its wire sequence), stamped before encoding. Values a receiver cannot re-derive, such as knockback spread, are seeded from it, and events derived from the crossing inherit it. [D-3](domain-design.md#d-3--cross-the-smallest-shared-outcome) · `internal/event` `CrossingID`, `StampCrossing`

**crossover** (also: combiner) — The genetic operator that recombines parent genotypes, uniformly or at N points. [AI and physics §3](ai-physics-and-evolution.md#3-genetic-library) · [Genetic README: Operators](../pkg/genetic/README.md#operators) · `pkg/genetic` `UniformCombiner`, `NPointCombiner`

**crush** (also: `EventCursorCrushed`) — Cave rock arriving on a cursor it finds no room for, pressed against the map's left edge: the wall system emits the Shared `EventCursorCrushed` and the cursor stays where the rock pressed it, and `MetaSystem` latches its slot defeated, so a scenario's defeat reset follows; a cursor at the edge with no rock arriving takes nothing. `vanguard` halts its rock and drift under a red strobe, then starts over. See also **cave**. [Gameplay §9](gameplay.md#9-walls-maps-navigation-and-environment) · `internal/system` `WallSystem` · `internal/event` `EventCursorCrushed`

**cue** (also: AtPermille) — A timed machine state's hook at a permille of its entry's duration, run once per entry in the step that reaches that point; a retry's new period runs it again. [Behavior machines §2.2](machine-design.md#22-graph) · [§2.3](machine-design.md#23-step) · `pkg/machine` `Cue`

**cursor** — five senses. See also **control kind**, **cursor prediction**, **roster**.
- *Gameplay*: a player's entity on the map: text position, collision point, weapon origin and camera subject. It is Shared and carries roster slot, control kind, peer and holder, with owner-authored energy, heat, shield, boost and weapon state beside it; an **off-map cursor** carries the slot, control, peer and holder alone. [Gameplay §2](gameplay.md#2-cursor-and-player-state) · `internal/component` `CursorComponent`; `internal/system` `CursorSystem`
- *Rendering*: the local cursor is the one this instance drives; a peer cursor is another participant's, drawn in its slot's color. [Rendering: Local and peer cursors](rendering.md#local-and-peer-cursors) · `internal/render/renderer` `CursorRenderer`, `PeerCursorRenderer`
- *Content*: where the corpus walk stands, sequential within a source with random hops between sources; its state rides replay checkpoints. [Content §6](content-assets-and-tools.md#6-block-selection) · `internal/content` `Cursor`, `CursorState`
- *Replay*: where a replay driver stands in its stream, so a driver restored from a checkpoint resumes there. [Runtime: Replay playback](runtime.md#replay-playback) · `internal/journal` `Cursor`, `ReplayDriver.Resume`
- *Editing*: the text cursor of the insert, search and command-line editors. [Input §5](input-and-modes.md#5-insert-search-and-command-text-editing) · `internal/mode`

**cursor damage** (also: CursorDamage, HostedDamage) — What a hit on a cursor costs: energy through an active shield, heat without one. A mounted weapon's hit costs its `HostedDamage`. See also **contact damage**. [Combat §5](combat.md#5-resolution-statuses-and-what-crosses) · `internal/component` `CursorDamage`, `WeaponSpec.HostedDamage`

**cursor kinetics** (also: owner-authored kinetics) — Future design: the cursor as a kinetic body, preferably with its owner combining input and force and crossing canonical state. [Generic kinetic §9](generic-kinetic.md#9-cursor-as-a-future-kinetic-entity)

**cursor prediction** (also: local prediction) — A private bounded FIFO, at most `MaxPredictedCursorCells` (64), of cells this participant requested and Shared simulation has not yet announced; input, camera, rendering and weapons read it through `World.CursorCell`, and Shared systems do not (D-18); this participant's own placements settle it as they land.
