[D-18](domain-design.md#d-18--local-prediction-is-private) · `internal/engine` `World.CursorCell`, `World.PushCursorMove`, `PlayerResource.Predict`

**cursor scope** (also: CursorScopePayload) — An event payload naming the cursor an effect belongs to: entity zero means session-wide, and a named cursor is acted on only by the instance simulating it. [FSM §8](fsm-and-configuration.md#8-actions-and-delayed-work) · `internal/event` `CursorScopePayload`

**cycle damage multiplier** (also: energy.damage_multiplier) — The session-wide factor on energy penalties that each storm kill in the campaign, and each Kraken of a vanguard **boss stage**, doubles. See **damage multiplier**. [Gameplay: Energy](gameplay.md#energy) · `internal/system` `MetaSystem`; `internal/event` `EventCycleDamageMultiplierIncrease`

## D

**-d** (also: -config-embedded) — Startup flag that skips scenario and content discovery and runs the embedded scenario and corpus, ignoring `vif.toml`'s scenario and content paths; it refuses `-s` and `-f`, and keymap, audio and bot-graph overrides still resolve as usual. [Filesystem layout §3](filesystem-layout.md#3-resolution-policy) · [Development: Configuration](development.md#configuration) · `cmd/vif` `configFlags` · `internal/resource` `Options.Embedded`

**D-1** (also: Reads follow ownership) — Domain rule: a Player-domain system may read Shared state, but a Shared-domain system reads Shared state only, except through the D-12 geometry and D-13 owner-authored seams; cross-domain access is declared and checked, not inferred. [Domain design D-1](domain-design.md#d-1--reads-follow-ownership) · `internal/engine` `auditEntityDomain` · `internal/system` `TestSystemDomainProfiles`

**D-10** (also: Event class and event domain answer different questions) — Domain rule: an event type's class (`Local`, `Shared`, `Bus`, `Stamped`) decides whether journals hold it (`event.Replicated`) and whether peers receive it (`event.OnWire`); its domain says whose world it acts on. [Domain design D-10](domain-design.md#d-10--event-class-and-event-domain-answer-different-questions) · `internal/event` `EventClass`, `Replicated`, `OnWire`

**D-11** (also: The host is exact; guests converge) — Domain rule: the host owns the canonical Shared event order, entity creation order, RNG progress and component values; a guest is its last installed host world plus a bounded local prediction suffix, and may differ between corrections. [Domain design D-11](domain-design.md#d-11--the-host-is-exact-guests-converge) · `internal/converge` `Corrections`

**D-12** (also: Shared geometry may inspect both domains) — Domain rule: a Shared system that claims cells (spawn footprint clearing, composite sweep, wall push-out) may enumerate both domains, but its outcome depends only on the cells and protection masks, and its emission is split by domain. [Domain design D-12](domain-design.md#d-12--shared-geometry-may-inspect-both-domains) · `internal/system` `cellSweep` · `internal/event` `EmitDeath`

**D-13** (also: Owner-authored Shared values have one writer) — Domain rule: a cursor's energy, heat, shield, boost, weapon and combat stores and its `CursorViewComponent` and `PingComponent` are written by its owner alone; all but ping travel on the owner-state sync, and all stay out of the manifest hash, the correction magnitude and selective repair. [Domain design D-13](domain-design.md#d-13--owner-authored-shared-values-have-one-writer) · `internal/system` `NetworkSystem.sendCursorState`

**D-14** (also: Map bounds are Shared authority) — Domain rule: `MapWidth` and `MapHeight` are Shared state carried in every capture header and `CropOnResize` rides the join anchor; while `World.SessionShared` is true a terminal resize changes only the local viewport and camera. [Domain design D-14](domain-design.md#d-14--map-bounds-are-shared-authority) · `internal/engine` `World.SessionShared`

**D-15** (also: Classification is declared once) — Domain rule: system domain profiles, required and optional dependencies and snapshot participation are declared once in `internal/manifest/definition.go` and generated into runtime tables. [Domain design D-15](domain-design.md#d-15--classification-is-declared-once) · `internal/manifest` `SystemDef`

**D-16** (also: Shared triggers elect one causal player) — Domain rule: a Shared trigger selects one causal cursor deterministically before reading any Player state, and only that cursor's owner emits the crossing; the main scenario captures the drain-defeat crossing's cursor as `fuse_owner`. [Domain design D-16](domain-design.md#d-16--shared-triggers-elect-one-causal-player) · `internal/system` `DrainSystem.crossDefeated` · `internal/event` `DrainDefeatedPayload`

**D-17** (also: Throttle phase is state) — Domain rule: a throttled Shared derivation carries its inputs and its recompute phase in the capture; navigation saves its counters, last targets and route-rebuild budget and re-derives fields and graphs on install. [Domain design D-17](domain-design.md#d-17--throttle-phase-is-state) · `internal/system` `NavigationSystem`

**D-18** (also: Local prediction is private) — Domain rule: input is reflected at once through a private local cursor prediction, a FIFO of requested cells that input, camera, rendering and weapons read through `World.CursorCell` and Shared systems never do; an authoritative move it did not produce clears it and snaps. [Domain design D-18](domain-design.md#d-18--local-prediction-is-private) · `internal/engine` `World.CursorCell`, `World.PushCursorMove`

**D-19** (also: Every future-affecting Shared value is restorable) — Domain rule: any value that can change a future Shared outcome is a component on a Shared entity, system state carried through `engine.SharedStateSaver`, or state the install re-derives from captured inputs. [Domain design D-19](domain-design.md#d-19--every-future-affecting-shared-value-is-restorable) · `internal/engine` `SharedStateSaver`

**D-2** (also: One instance simulates each player) — Domain rule: only the instance that owns a cursor simulates its weapons, projectiles, drains, nuggets and player effects, and remote Player-domain state does not exist locally. [Domain design D-2](domain-design.md#d-2--one-instance-simulates-each-player) · `internal/engine` `World.SimulatesLocally`, `World.ResolveOwnedCursor`

**D-20** (also: Shared FSM regions move on the authority's transitions) — Domain rule: every FSM region is Shared state, moved in a live session by the authority's transitions alone, each a **transition crossing** applied at one tick everywhere, so a guard may read what the authority alone holds and a `Local` event still cannot steer a region. [Domain design D-20](domain-design.md#d-20--shared-fsm-regions-move-on-the-authoritys-transitions) · `internal/app` `TestFSMTriggersAreReplicated`

**D-21** (also: Simulation time is tick-derived) — Domain rule: `engine.SimTime(tick, interval)` measured from `engine.SimEpoch` is the only simulation instant; wall time paces ticks and presentation but never selects a Shared outcome. [Domain design D-21](domain-design.md#d-21--simulation-time-is-tick-derived) · `internal/engine` `SimTime`, `SimEpoch`

**D-22** (also: Admit before capturing) — Domain rule: a running join becomes a transport peer and buffers traffic before the authority's capture is read, so no event falls between capture and wire; admission refuses a transfer gap beyond the lead or a link that cannot carry a keyframe inside the convergence floor. [Domain design D-22](domain-design.md#d-22--admit-before-capturing) · `internal/app` `membership.admitLateJoiner`

**D-23** (also: The host world is the correction) — Domain rule: the authority publishes its `SharedCapture` on a cadence through a selective manifest exchange, and a guest resolves it into a staging world and commits between ticks, projecting a capture behind its clock rather than moving the clock back. [Domain design D-23](domain-design.md#d-23--the-host-world-is-the-correction) · [Netcode design §2](netcode-design.md#2-the-correction-pipeline) · `internal/converge` `Corrections`

**D-24** (also: Cadence adapts; the convergence floor does not) — Domain rule: each link's cadence and keyframe interval adapt to measured capacity and demand, but every participant receives a whole world, or proves it holds one, within `SnapshotFloorKeyframeTicks` (60); a link that cannot is refused at admission or reported floor-breached. [Domain design D-24](domain-design.md#d-24--cadence-adapts-the-convergence-floor-does-not) · `pkg/linkpace` `Admit` · `internal/converge` `Corrections.AdmitLink`

**D-3** (also: Cross the smallest shared outcome) — Domain rule: a player mechanic that affects Shared state emits the smallest artifact that fully determines the outcome, a crossing with its own `CrossingID`, applied everywhere at one agreed tick; one that decides what the world is (arrival, departure, reset, swarm and quasar spawns, defeats) is barrier-bound. [Domain design D-3](domain-design.md#d-3--cross-the-smallest-shared-outcome) · [Multiplayer §3.1](multi-player.md#31-ordinary-and-barrier-bound-crossings) · `internal/engine` `World.PushCrossing` · `internal/event` `CrossingID`

**D-4** (also: Wire payloads name Shared entities only) — Domain rule: a replicated payload, or a component on a Shared entity, may name only Shared entities; Player emitters are reduced to values such as cell, velocity, radius, amount and owner cursor. [Domain design D-4](domain-design.md#d-4--wire-payloads-name-shared-entities-only) · `internal/event` payload.go

**D-5** (also: Derived events are not transported) — Domain rule: an event produced deterministically from a replicated event is re-derived on every instance and never sent; explosion geometry crosses, the per-target combat requests derived from it do not. See also **derived event**. [Domain design D-5](domain-design.md#d-5--derived-events-are-not-transported) · `internal/event` `Derived`, `OnWire`

**D-6** (also: Presentation and personal effects are Player-domain) — Domain rule: lightning, flashes, particles, orbs, projectiles, loot and other presentation are Player-domain and never decide a Shared outcome; a cursor scope narrows a local effect to one participant, and entity zero means session-wide. [Domain design D-6](domain-design.md#d-6--presentation-and-personal-effects-are-player-domain) · `internal/event` `CursorScopePayload`

**D-7** (also: Domain is explicit ambient context) — Domain rule: `World.WithDomain`, `PushEventDomain`, `PushLocal` and `PushCrossing` stamp the producer or target domain explicitly, and a system's declared profile never silently changes the ambient tag. [Domain design D-7](domain-design.md#d-7--domain-is-explicit-ambient-context) · `internal/engine` `World.WithDomain`

**D-8** (also: RNG streams are domain-separated and restorable) — Domain rule: every RNG stream derives from (session root, domain, label), a capture stores Shared stream positions rather than seeds, and simulation never seeds from wall time; a Shared draw must happen at the same tick and count on every instance. [Domain design D-8](domain-design.md#d-8--rng-streams-are-domain-separated-and-restorable) · `internal/engine` `RandResource.Stream`, `RandResource.SaveStreams`

**D-9** (also: Entity identity is domain-local and deterministic) — Domain rule: `CreateEntity` keeps one counter per domain, so Shared creation order must match everywhere, and a crossing that allocates Shared identity is barrier-bound. [Domain design D-9](domain-design.md#d-9--entity-identity-is-domain-local-and-deterministic) · `internal/engine` `World.CreateEntity`

**D-n** (also: domain rule) — One of the numbered multi-instance domain rules D-1 to D-24 that define simulation ownership, transport, correction, admission and authority continuity, cited by code comments and tests. Not the fleet decisions **D1–D6**, which have no hyphen. [Domain design §2](domain-design.md#2-domain-rules)

**D1–D6** (also: fleet decisions, D1, D2, D3, D4, D5, D6) — The six numbered decisions of the fleet deployment; not the domain rules **D-n**, which carry a hyphen. [Fleet deploy §0](kube-docker-deploy.md#0-decisions)
- *D1, one node, no registry*: session images are imported straight into the node's K3s containerd, and every session container runs with `imagePullPolicy: IfNotPresent`. [Fleet deploy §8](kube-docker-deploy.md#8-the-session-image) · `deploy/guest/update-vif-image.sh`
- *D2, Docker is a build tool, not a runtime*: Docker is started only for a session-image or LogWisp build and disabled afterwards, because its iptables rules share a table with K3s's. [Fleet deploy §4](kube-docker-deploy.md#4-docker-for-builds-only) · `deploy/guest/update-vif-image.sh`
- *D3, cluster commands run through `sudo kubectl`*: K3s's kubeconfig stays root-readable (mode 0640), never copied to a login user. [Fleet deploy §5](kube-docker-deploy.md#5-k3s)
- *D4, the vif JSON line is the log contract*: log records originate in `internal/vlog`, every hop preserves those bytes, and nothing reads a Kubernetes pod log. [Fleet deploy §10](kube-docker-deploy.md#10-logwisp-the-node-log-reader) · `internal/vlog`
- *D5, the router runs on the node*: it needs the K3s API and node-local access to the pod probes; port 6443 never leaves the node. [Fleet deploy §11](kube-docker-deploy.md#11-the-router) · `tool/vif-router`
- *D6, the session transports are unauthenticated*: anyone who can reach a forwarded port or the browser route can join; the forwarded surface and application limits such as the per-address admission budget are the controls. [Fleet design §4](kubernetes-fleet.md#4-security-posture) · `internal/network` `AdmissionLimiter`

**damage multiplier** (also: cycle damage multiplier, energy.damage_multiplier) — The session-wide factor on energy penalties, in practice shield drains: it doubles on each `EventCycleDamageMultiplierIncrease` (every storm kill in the campaign, every Kraken of a vanguard **boss stage**) and returns to one on a game reset or `EventCycleDamageMultiplierReset`; `MetaSystem` carries it in the capture (D-19), since an installed FSM state never reruns the on_enter action that raised it. [Gameplay: Energy](gameplay.md#energy) · [Domain design D-19](domain-design.md#d-19--every-future-affecting-shared-value-is-restorable) · `internal/system` `MetaSystem`

**damage type** (also: CombatDamageType, direct, area, over-time) — The resolver a combat profile picks for a hit: direct and area take hit points, while over-time leaves a status that ticks from the profile's figures. [Combat §5](combat.md#5-resolution-statuses-and-what-crosses) · `internal/component` `CombatDamageType`

**dark** (also: GlyphDark) — The lowest of a glyph's three levels (dark, normal, bright): it drifts at a quarter of the drift speed, special fire converts it to dust when it is green or of the cursor's polarity (blue, or red at negative energy), while a dust-all request or a special-fire blast that covers it flashes it away, and decay turns it into the next color's bright glyph, or flashes it away when it is red. [Gameplay §4](gameplay.md#4-glyph-production-and-decay) · [Gameplay §7](gameplay.md#7-weapons-and-attacks) · `internal/component` `GlyphLevel`

**data species** (also: SpeciesDataFirst, SpeciesMax) — A species a scenario's behavior program defines, run by `BehaviorSystem`: IDs `SpeciesDataFirst` (10) to `SpeciesMax` less one (41), one per program in sorted-name order, so a scenario's guard names it (`PayloadSpeciesIn`, resolved at load). The per-kind tables indexed by species are sized `[SpeciesMax]` and leave its row zero; its contact, drop table, footprint, mass, hit points and look come from its prototype. Vanguard's thirty-two raider types are data species: one attack cycle as programs over one macro. See also **behavior program**, **prototype**. [Behavior machines §6.4](machine-design.md#64-beyond-the-machine) · [FSM reference: PayloadSpeciesIn](fsm-reference.md#payloadspeciesin) · `internal/component` `SpeciesDataFirst`, `SpeciesMax`, `BehaviorPrototype` · `internal/manifest` `RegisterFSMComponents`

**dead letter** (also: dead, event.dead) — A dispatched event that no active FSM region consumed and no system handler is registered for; it is counted in `event.dead`, per type in `event.dead_by_type` and in a `pass` record's `dead` field, and usually means an event is emitted that nothing handles or a system stopped registering its handler. See also **dropped**, **dispatch**. [Logging `sub="event"`](logging-and-diagnostics.md#subevent) · [Telemetry reference](telemetry-reference.md#engine-event-and-process-telemetry) · `internal/engine` `Scheduler` · `internal/event` `EventQueue.RecordDispatch`

**dead zone** (also: DeadZone) — two senses.
- *Homing*: `HomingProfile.DeadZone`, the radius at which a homing entity snaps to its target (0.5 cells when unset). [AI and physics §5](ai-physics-and-evolution.md#5-physics-primitives) · `pkg/vmath/physics` `HomingProfile`
- *Camera*: the inner part of the viewport, inside `CameraDeadZoneMarginX` and `CameraDeadZoneMarginY` of its edges, where cursor movement does not scroll the following camera; the narrower `CameraPointerMarginX` and `CameraPointerMarginY` apply while the pointer placed the cursor. `internal/engine` `ConfigResource.FollowCamera` · `internal/parameter` `CameraDeadZoneMarginX`

**deadline** — three senses.
- *Timer*: `TimerDeadline`, the `TimerComponent` slot whose expiry its owner acts on (missile flight, materialize, gold sequence, swarm life, button light); unlike a lifetime it stays expired until the owner re-arms or stops it. [ECS and events: Timers](ecs-and-events.md#timers) · `internal/component` `TimerSlot`
- *Fleet*: the session Job's one-hour `activeDeadlineSeconds`, which ends a session however occupied it is. [Fleet design §2](kubernetes-fleet.md#2-runtime-contract) · `tool/vif-router` manifest.go
- *Link*: `NetworkLinkDeadAfter` (5 s), the `Config.ReadTimeout` within which every frame of a stream past its offer must arrive; the far end answers each link probe on receipt, so only a link that stopped misses it, and its close starts a departure or a succession. [Services §10](services-and-networking.md#10-timeouts-limits-and-security) · `internal/parameter` `NetworkLinkDeadAfter` · `pkg/network` `Config.ReadTimeout`

**death** (also: death request, EventDeathBatch, DeathComponent, EmitDeath) — The event path that destroys entities with effects: `event.EmitDeath` pushes an `EventDeathBatch` split into domain-pure batches, and `DeathSystem` destroys each after its protection checks and publishes the effect once per batch; `DeathComponent` tags an entity it destroys silently. [ECS and events §7](ecs-and-events.md#7-destruction-and-protection) · `internal/event` `EmitDeath` · `internal/system` `DeathSystem`

**debug** (also: `:debug`, `:d`) — four senses.
- *Command*: `:debug` (`:d`) opens the profiler report, toggles the profiler, writes a CPU, heap or mutex profile or an execution trace, and sets standing breakpoints with `:d break`. [Input and modes §9](input-and-modes.md#9-command-mode) · [Logging §11](logging-and-diagnostics.md#11-profiler) · `internal/mode` commands.go
- *Log level*: the `vlog` level between trace and info, for per-pass detail such as dispatch summaries, input intents and FSM transitions; the default when logging is on without `-lv`. [Logging §3](logging-and-diagnostics.md#3-levels) · `internal/vlog` `LevelDebug`
- *Event origin*: `OriginDebug`, events from a harness or out-of-band control such as `App.Region` and `-script` events. [Logging §9](logging-and-diagnostics.md#9-replay-journal) · `internal/event` `OriginDebug`
- *Debug view*: the visibility-gated flow-field or route-graph overlay for one target group that `:flow` and `:graph` toggle. [Rendering §9](rendering.md#9-ui-and-debug-projections) · `internal/render/renderer` `FlowFieldDebugRenderer`

**decay** — two senses.
- *Particle*: `ParticleDecay`, which a wave (`EventParticleWave`, `:decay`) drops from the top edge; it lowers the level of each Player glyph it enters, destroys nuggets, annihilates with blossom and orbits a spinning or charging Kraken or a vortex's button. [Gameplay §4](gameplay.md#4-glyph-production-and-decay) · `internal/component` `ParticleDecay` · `internal/system` `ParticleSystem`
- *Audio*: the `decay` stage of a sound's processor chain, an attack followed by an exponential fall with time constant `tau`. [Audio §5](audio.md#5-sounds-and-sound-effects) · `pkg/audio` sound_render.go, generator.go

**dedicated host** (also: -serve, ModeServer, server) — A run that authors a session with no terminal, renderer, audio or cursor of its own, on the real clock; its coordinator holds `parameter.NoPlayerSlot`, so it is a peer but not a participant, its `-players` counts guests, it starts on its first guest, and it parks its clock while the roster is empty. See also **drain**. [Runtime §1.2](runtime.md#12-the-dedicated-host) · [Development: A dedicated host](development.md#a-dedicated-host) · `internal/app` `ModeServer`, `Mode.Serves`

**defeat** (also: EventCursorDefeatState, session.any_defeated, session.all_defeated, EventDrainDefeated) — two senses.
- *Cursor*: a cursor at zero energy and zero heat is defeated: its owner crosses the defeat state barrier-bound once every crossing it made by the close of that tick has applied on it, never when what those paid back undid it, `MetaSystem` latches `session.any_defeated` and `session.all_defeated`, and the shipped scenarios' monitor region answers with a game reset; the level setup that rebuilds the world clears the latch. [Gameplay §10](gameplay.md#10-embedded-campaign-progression) · [FSM reference: Player Metrics](fsm-reference.md#player-metrics) · [Multiplayer §3.1](multi-player.md#31-ordinary-and-barrier-bound-crossings) · `internal/system` `MetaSystem` · `internal/event` `EventCursorDefeatState`
- *Drain*: `EventDrainDefeated`, the barrier-bound crossing a personal drain's death sends with its owner cursor; it advances the shared kill progression (`kills.drain`) a region gates its spawns on and names the causal cursor (D-16). [Multiplayer §3.1](multi-player.md#31-ordinary-and-barrier-bound-crossings) · [Domain design D-16](domain-design.md#d-16--shared-triggers-elect-one-causal-player) · `internal/system` `DrainSystem.crossDefeated` · `internal/event` `DrainDefeatedPayload`

**defender** — The target side of the combat profile matrix `[attack][attacker][defender]`, indexed by combat entity type; `combat.{damage,absorbed}_defender_<type>` count the damage dealt to, or refused by immunity on, each defender type. [Combat §5](combat.md#5-resolution-statuses-and-what-crosses) · [Telemetry reference: Key families](telemetry-reference.md#key-families) · `internal/profile` `AttackProfile`, `Attack`

**deflate** (also: flate, VIFS envelope) — four senses.
- *Link stream*: from the join reply on, each direction of a link is a sequence of deflate runs at `NetworkStreamLevel` (7), each frame compressed against every run before it, and of raw frames between them for the bodies the snapshot codec deflated already (`Traits.Precompressed`). [Services and networking §8](services-and-networking.md#8-wire-frame-and-session-messages) · `pkg/network` `Stream`
- *Snapshot envelope*: a correction delta or shard set travels in a 10-byte envelope (`VIFS` magic, version, codec, plain size) followed by deflate data at level 6, which is the level the wire gets since such a body passes the link stream raw, its plain size bounded by `MaxSnapshotPlainBytes`; a capture, as a keyframe or a join's, travels in the same envelope as sections (codec 2): a count, then each part's name, plain and deflated lengths and its own deflate, each store in its column layout, those of `SnapshotSectionBlobBytes` or more as parts the encoder keeps deflated while their rows hold, the smaller together in one part and the rest as one JSON object; a delta is one part, its JSON behind a length and then its world delta in columns; a manifest and a request use the plain codec and leave compression to the link stream. [Services and networking §8](services-and-networking.md#8-wire-frame-and-session-messages) · [Domain design D-23](domain-design.md#d-23--the-host-world-is-the-correction) · `internal/snapshot` codec.go
- *Scenario transfer*: `MsgScenarioBody` carries a scenario's length-prefixed canonical form as one deflate stream, so its near-identical files share a window; the digest covers the bytes deflate was given. [Services and networking §7.2](services-and-networking.md#72-join-identity-and-who-decides-it) · `internal/resource` `Scenario.MarshalCompressed`
- *Image file*: a `.vifimg` stores its fixed-width cell stream deflated; the reader also accepts legacy uncompressed files. [Content and tools §8](content-assets-and-tools.md#8-image-and-pattern-pipeline) · `pkg/ascimage` `WriteDualMode`

**delayed action** (also: delay_ms, DelayedAction) — An FSM action with `delay_ms`, counted down in region time while its owning state stays on the active path and cleared when that state exits; its guard is checked when it is scheduled, and a capture stores its compiled action identity so it resumes after a join or correction. [FSM reference: Delayed Actions](fsm-reference.md#delayed-actions) · [FSM configuration §8](fsm-and-configuration.md#8-actions-and-delayed-work) · `internal/fsm` `DelayedAction`

**delivery** (also: WeaponDelivery) — The mechanism a weapon kind discharges through (lightning, missile, pulse, bullet, ray or tracer), one column of `component.WeaponSpecs` shared by cursor loadouts and mounts. [Combat §2](combat.md#2-layers) · `internal/component` `WeaponDelivery`

**delta** — four senses.
- *Correction*: `SharedCaptureDelta`, the correction the host publishes between keyframes: a capture expressed against the last keyframe it sent (`BaselineTick`) by differencing only the component stores, as a `SharedWorldDelta` of per-store changed rows, removed rows and order, with streams, system records, status and FSM carried whole; a receiver holding another baseline refuses it. See also **dense-store order**. [Services and networking §9](services-and-networking.md#9-poll-boundary-and-receive-lead) · [Telemetry reference: Snapshot and correction](telemetry-reference.md#snapshot-and-correction) · `internal/snapshot` `SharedCaptureDelta`, `DiffCapture` · `internal/engine` `SharedWorldDelta`, `StoreDelta`
- *Journal*: `WrittenDelta`, a world this instance wrote (a join or a correction) expressed against the one it held just before, carried in a journal `capture` record. [Logging: Written worlds and notes](logging-and-diagnostics.md#written-worlds-and-notes) · `internal/snapshot` `WrittenDelta`
- *Energy*: an energy delta class (reward, penalty, passive, spend) decides how a change moves energy toward or across zero. [Gameplay: Energy](gameplay.md#energy) · `internal/component` `EnergyDeltaType`
- *Time*: `DeltaTime` (dt), the fixed simulated duration of one tick, a function of the tick interval only. [Domain design D-21](domain-design.md#d-21--simulation-time-is-tick-derived) · `internal/engine` `TimeResource`

**demand** (also: Demand) — What a participant wants from its correction cadence, as opposed to what its link can carry: drift (its correction magnitude rising above its recent level), relevance (its share of what the next correction moves against the session mean) and idleness place its plan inside the feasible range. [Domain design D-24](domain-design.md#d-24--cadence-adapts-the-convergence-floor-does-not) · [Multiplayer §2](multi-player.md#2-current-contract) · `pkg/linkpace` `Demand`

**dense-store order** (also: dense slot) — The order of entities in a component store's dense slices, which swap removal makes depend on history; a correction delta carries it so a rebuild reproduces the capture byte for byte, and two equal worlds that differ only in it match by manifest root but not by capture integrity. [ECS and events §2](ecs-and-events.md#2-entities-and-typed-stores) · [Domain design D-23](domain-design.md#d-23--the-host-world-is-the-correction) · `internal/engine` `Store`

**departure** (also: EventParticipantDeparted) — A participant leaving the session: a direct neighbour that sees its link close floods a notice, and the coordinator crosses the one barrier-bound departure, so every instance turns its cursor and its bots' **Wraith** at the same tick, or removes them for a **dismissal**; an instance with no link left drops cursors locally instead, as a run that leaves with `:leave` drops everyone it leaves behind. The host removes one with `:player drop <slot>`. [Domain design §4.4](domain-design.md#44-membership-and-session-control) · [Multiplayer §5](multi-player.md#5-membership-topology-and-authority-continuity) · [Multiplayer §5.2](multi-player.md#52-the-roster-after-a-loss) · `internal/system` `NetworkSystem.announceDeparture` · `internal/event` `EventParticipantDeparted`

**dependency** (also: Requires, Optional) — A system's declared `Requires` (cannot function without) and `Optional` (absence only degrades it) lists in `SystemDef`; `World.InitSystems` runs every `Init` in dependency order, at startup and on a game reset, and fails startup on an unknown name or a cycle, `-check` and `World.AllowSystemDisable` refuse a disable that leaves an enabled system without a required dependency, and `World.ApplySystems` keeps such a dependency on against a region's list, while tick order comes from priority. [FSM configuration §10](fsm-and-configuration.md#10-region-lifecycle-and-system-policy) · [ECS and events §1](ecs-and-events.md#1-world-ownership) · `internal/manifest` `SystemDef` · `internal/engine` `World.InitSystems`, `World.AllowSystemDisable`

**derivation** (also: predicted derivation, `event.Derivation`) — two senses. See also **derived event**.
- *Simulation*: a Shared derivation is a value or event every instance computes identically from Shared state, such as a flow field or a re-derived event; a throttled one carries its inputs and recompute phase in the capture. See **throttle phase**. [Domain design D-17](domain-design.md#d-17--throttle-phase-is-state)
- *Prediction*: `event.Derivation` names the edge-triggered event types a predicting instance can raise more than once for one fact, because a correction can restore the Shared state they read; only `EventSpeciesKilled` is one, and a Shared-domain push of it while `World.PredictsShared` holds is stamped `PhasePredicted` and entered in the prediction ledger. Not `event.Derived`, which keeps a re-derived payload off the wire. See also **prediction ledger**, **event phase**. [Multi-player §3.4](multi-player.md#34-predicted-derivations) · `internal/event` `Derivation`

**derived event** (also: Derived, IsDerived) — An event every instance produces deterministically from a replicated one, such as the per-target combat requests from crossed explosion geometry or a combat chain follow-up; it is re-derived rather than sent, and a payload whose `event.Derived` method `IsDerived` reports true stays off the wire. [Domain design D-5](domain-design.md#d-5--derived-events-are-not-transported) · [Domain design D-10](domain-design.md#d-10--event-class-and-event-domain-answer-different-questions) · `internal/event` `Derived`, `OnWire`

**desync** — The network badge qualifier `desync t`: pacing's verdict that this instance is t ticks out of step with the authority's epochs (`network.stale`, with `network.pace_late_ticks`), beyond what a trim answers quietly: ahead past `NetworkPaceBandTicks`, so committed crossings reach it late, or behind by a step, so its lead grows. The margin pacing holds on purpose (`network.lag_ticks`) never qualifies. See also **pacing**, **playout lead**. [Services and networking §9](services-and-networking.md#9-poll-boundary-and-receive-lead) · [Telemetry reference: Network](telemetry-reference.md#network) · `internal/system` `NetworkSystem.publishStaleness` · `internal/render/renderer` status_bar.go

**detached cell** — What a status-registry `Get` of an unknown key returns after `Registry.Freeze`: a usable cell that appears in no snapshot or recorder window and increments `stat.late`, so a non-zero `stat.late` is a regression. [Logging: Freeze](logging-and-diagnostics.md#freeze) · [Development §9](development.md#9-status-and-snapshots) · `internal/status` `Registry.Freeze`

**determinism** — The property the domain model rests on: one build stepping the same Shared state through the same replicated events at the same ticks reaches the same result on every instance. So derived events are re-derived rather than sent (D-5), Shared ids come from per-domain counters (D-9), RNG streams are labelled and restorable (D-8), simulation time is tick-derived (D-21), and code iterating a map sorts before a write, a draw or a push. It is no recovery protocol and no cross-platform lockstep: a lost, late or doubled crossing, a local leak into Shared state or a float difference still diverges a world, and the authority's corrections repair it. A driven run makes the narrower bit-exact claim. See also **driven run**, **correction**. [AI and physics §6](ai-physics-and-evolution.md#6-numeric-model-and-determinism) · [Multiplayer §10.2](multi-player.md#102-why-determinism-alone-was-not-enough) · `pkg/vmath` `FastRand` · `internal/engine` `RandResource.Stream`

**-dev** — Startup flag that redirects fd 2 to `vif-stderr-<timestamp>.log` before the alternate screen and polls it every `DevDrainInterval` (500 ms), logging each complete block as a `sub="race"` pointer record; on by default for race builds such as `make dev`, off with `-dev=false`. See also **drain**. [Logging §10](logging-and-diagnostics.md#10-runtime-output-capture) · [Development §10](development.md#10-crash-and-runtime-capture) · `internal/core` `StartStderrDrain`, `DrainStderr`

**device** (also: OriginDevice) — The event origin for this machine's own: the audio mute, and its operator's pause and overlay requests; such events are never journaled and stay out of the event tallies a replay compares. [Logging §9](logging-and-diagnostics.md#9-replay-journal) · [ECS and events §8](ecs-and-events.md#8-event-transport) · `internal/event` `OriginDevice`

**dialect** (also: FSM dialect, dialect kernel) — two senses.
- *FSM*: the TOML state-machine language of states, transitions, guards and actions that scenarios and bot graphs share, each vocabulary with its own actions and guards. [FSM reference](fsm-reference.md#fsm-configuration-reference) · [Bots: Document](bots.md#document) · `internal/fsm` · `internal/bot`
- *Kernel*: the dialect kernel: the comparison `Op`, the strict typed decode of an argument table that names an unknown key or a mistyped value by its path, the names an enum field takes, the `And`, `Or` and `Not` guard tree, and the vocabulary entries `-schema` lists; `fsm/std`, bot graphs and the behavior compiler use it. See also **payload names**. [Behavior machines §5](machine-design.md#5-dialect-kernel) · `internal/fsm/dialect` `Op`, `Decode`, `Names`, `Tree`, `Entry`

**digest** — three senses, one retired. See also **divergence**, **desync**.
- *State digest*: retired; the runtime parity probe that hashed the compared Shared surface every six ticks. Parity is the correction exchange's, and a repair names its sections in `snapshot.repair_sections`. [Domain design §4.3](domain-design.md#43-runtime-parity) · [Netcode design](netcode-design.mdceive-lead) · `internal/engine` `SharedStateDigest` · `internal/snapshot` `SharedDigest` · `internal/system` `NetworkSystem.sendStateDigest`
- *Journal digest*: a `digest` record written every `DigestIntervalTicks` (20) with positions, kinetics, combat and entities hashes from `snapshot.DigestWorld`; a replay reports a divergence at the first one it does not reproduce. [Logging: Written worlds and notes](logging-and-diagnostics.md#written-worlds-and-notes) · [Runtime: Replay playback](runtime.md#replay-playback) · `internal/event` `JournalDigest` · `internal/parameter` `DigestIntervalTicks` · `internal/snapshot` `DigestWorld`
- *Scenario digest*: the hex SHA-256 of a scenario's canonical form, the only scenario field a join compares; a joiner whose roots lack it asks the coordinator for the scenario. [Services and networking §7.2](services-and-networking.md#72-join-identity-and-who-decides-it) · [FSM configuration §2](fsm-and-configuration.md#2-scenario-resolution) · `internal/resource` `Scenario.Digest`

**dim** (also: DimRenderer, occlusion dim) — two senses.
- *Pause*: the post-process that scales every mask category except UI while the game is paused, or in 256-color mode, where a console shows a halved color as mostly black, grays them instead. [Rendering §5](rendering.md#5-semantic-masks-and-post-processing) · `internal/render/renderer` `DimRenderer`
- *Occlusion*: the truecolor finalizer dims the background under runes whose masks match the occlusion set (glyphs and transients) by `OcclusionDimFactor`. [Rendering §6](rendering.md#6-finalization-and-color-modes) · `internal/parameter/visual` `OcclusionDimMask`

**direct route** (also: direct_target, DirectTarget) — A fleet session reached on its own Kubernetes NodePort (`vif://<site>:<nodeport>/<id>`, from the router's 31700–31709 pool) instead of the front door; the pod itself refuses a bad dial by name, roster and per-address budget. [Fleet design §9](kubernetes-fleet.md#9-the-session-routes) · [Fleet design §8](kubernetes-fleet.md#8-decisions) · `tool/vif-router` `buildService`, `session.DirectTarget`

**disabled_systems** (also: only_systems, enabled_systems, DisableSystem) — three senses.
- *Root*: `[systems] disabled_systems` names, by runtime name, the systems the baseline leaves off; `only_systems` is its inverse and the two are exclusive. `ReconcileSystems` applies the baseline after FSM initialization, a reset and a capture import, and whenever the running regions change. [FSM configuration §3](fsm-and-configuration.md#3-root-schema) · [FSM configuration §10](fsm-and-configuration.md#10-region-lifecycle-and-system-policy) · `internal/fsm` `SystemsConfig`
- *Region*: while a region runs (neither paused nor ending), its `enabled_systems` are on and its `disabled_systems` off unless a running system requires them; its `only_systems` turns off every toggled system that no running region names, the union of the running regions' lists standing in for the baseline. Pausing or ending the region hands its systems back. [FSM configuration §10](fsm-and-configuration.md#10-region-lifecycle-and-system-policy) · `internal/fsm` `RegionConfig`, `Machine.SystemWanted` · `internal/engine` `World.ApplySystems`
- *Action*: `DisableSystem` turns one system off from an FSM action; it and `:system` are refused by `World.AllowSystemDisable` while a running system requires the target, and a system no running region decides keeps what they set until its wanted state changes. [FSM reference: System Control](fsm-reference.md#system-control) · `internal/engine` `World.AllowSystemDisable`

**dismissal** (also: `ParticipantDepartedPayload.Dismissed`) — A **departure** that removes a cursor and its bots for good and returns the identity and slot to the pool: the host's `:player drop`, a holder dropping its seat, an abandoned relayed join, an instance with no link dropping what it cannot reach, and the authority's own when a **Wraith** has waited `WraithTimeout` or gives up its place to a newcomer. A notice dismisses only a seat. [Multi-player §5.4](multi-player.md#54-wraiths) · `internal/event` `ParticipantDepartedPayload` · `internal/system` `NetworkSystem.expireWraiths`

**dispatch** (also: dispatchOnePass) — Delivery of queued events by the single consumer under the world lock: each event goes to the dispatch tap, then the FSM, then the registered system handlers, and a settle repeats passes up to `EventLoopIterations` (16) so follow-ups run within it. See also **settle**, **dead letter**. [ECS and events §8](ecs-and-events.md#8-event-transport) · [Runtime §6](runtime.md#6-event-loop-concurrency) · [Logging `sub="dispatch"`](logging-and-diagnostics.md#subdispatch) · `internal/engine` `Scheduler` · `internal/event` `EventQueue`

**dispatch tap** — The one observer `Scheduler.SetDispatchTap` installs, which sees every dispatched event before the FSM and system handlers while its payload is still the producer's; a driven App's bot or a test harness owns it. See also **tap** for the FSM transition tap and the `tap` log scope. [ECS and events §8](ecs-and-events.md#8-event-transport) · [Runtime: Bot seats](runtime.md#bot-seats) · `internal/engine` `Scheduler.SetDispatchTap`

**disruptor** (also: WeaponDisruptor, pulse) — The pulse weapon: a stun pulse on its orb's cell that fires only with a target inside; at the charge cap the pulse carries `Charged` and its chain burns every hit entity, and a mounted one strikes every cursor inside it, as storm's green circle does. [Combat §2](combat.md#2-layers) · [Combat §4](combat.md#4-at-the-charge-cap) · `internal/component` `WeaponDisruptor`

**divergence harness** (also: `VIF_DIVERGENCE`, `VIF_DIVERGENCE_SHAPE`, `RepairRows`, `repair rows`) — The in-repo instrument for a netcode divergence: a host and a guest fight over a mesh link shaped both ways with latency, jitter spikes and loss, the guest paced from its real trim and step, a storm raised at one tick on both, and every repair the guest applies logged with the rows it moved; deterministic run to run. `TestARelayedTopologyConvergesUnderShape` fights a host, a relay and a person behind it the same way and asserts their convergence and the authority's one link. [Netcode design §3.5](netcode-design.md#35-the-divergence-harness) · `internal/app` `TestADivergenceOverAShapedLinkNamesItsRows`, `TestARelayedTopologyConvergesUnderShape` · `internal/snapshot` `RepairRows`

**divergence** (also: Divergence, replay diverged) — The first journal digest a replay does not reproduce: `journal.Divergence` names its tick, the state classes that differ and the last digest that matched, the status bar shows `diverged by tick <n>`, and `-replay <file> -headless` exits non-zero on it. See also **digest**. [Runtime: Replay playback](runtime.md#replay-playback) · [Logging: Written worlds and notes](logging-and-diagnostics.md#written-worlds-and-notes) · `internal/journal` `Divergence`

**diversity** (also: DiversityFunc, PoolStats.Diversity, GeneSpread) — A genetic engine's optional population-spread measure: the `DiversityFunc` installed with `SetDiversity` runs once per generation under the engine mutex and is published as `PoolStats.Diversity`; the built-in `GeneSpread` is the mean per-gene standard deviation as a fraction of each gene's range. [Genetic library: Diversity](../pkg/genetic/README.md#diversity) · `pkg/genetic` `DiversityFunc`, `GeneSpread`

**domain** (also: core.Domain, Shared, Player, position.Scope) — eight senses. See also **D-n**, **domain audit**, **domain profile**.
- *Replication*: `core.Domain`, held in an entity's top 8 bits: `DomainShared` (0) is authoritative on the host and predicted on guests, `DomainPlayer` belongs to the local participant and is never reconstructed elsewhere; it says who simulates an entity, not the network role. [Domain design §1](domain-design.md#1-domains-and-identity) · [Multiplayer §1](multi-player.md#1-vocabulary-and-boundaries) · `internal/core` `Domain`, `Entity.Domain`
- *System*: a system's domain profile, `shared`, `player` or `dual`, declared in `SystemDef.Domain`. [Domain design D-15](domain-design.md#d-15--classification-is-declared-once) · `internal/engine` `SystemDomain`
- *Event*: `GameEvent.Domain`, the producer or target domain, ambient from `World.WithDomain` or explicit through `PushLocal`, `PushCrossing` and `PushEventDomain`; the replication filter reads it, a Stamped type's class depends on it, and the dispatcher never routes on it. [ECS and events §8](ecs-and-events.md#8-event-transport) · [Domain design D-7](domain-design.md#d-7--domain-is-explicit-ambient-context) · [Domain design D-10](domain-design.md#d-10--event-class-and-event-domain-answer-different-questions) · `internal/event` `GameEvent` · `internal/engine` `World.WithDomain`
- *Component*: the domain a component may attach to, declared in `ComponentDef.Domain` and generated into the `componentDomains` audit table. [ECS and events §4](ecs-and-events.md#4-component-catalog) · `internal/manifest` `ComponentDef` · `internal/engine` component_domain_gen.go
- *Spatial query*: a `position.Scope` (`ScopeShared`, `ScopePrivate`, `ScopeAll`) selects which half of a partitioned spatial-grid cell a query visits, an entity's class being its domain; not the `vlog.Scope` log mask. [ECS and events §3](ecs-and-events.md#3-position-and-spatial-queries) · [Domain design §3](domain-design.md#3-spatial-and-entity-classification) · `pkg/position` `Scope` · `internal/engine` `EntityClass`
- *Log scope*: the `domain` sub and scope (letter `o`), which carries domain-audit records. [Logging `sub="domain"`](logging-and-diagnostics.md#subdomain) · `internal/vlog` `ScopeDomain`
- *Telemetry*: a status key's first segment (`engine`, `combat`, `network`, `snapshot`...), the metric family `SplitKey` groups by; it gives each family's `buf_*` marks a `<domain>.buffers` group and splits the wide families into sub-groups; unrelated to the replication domain. [Logging: Key convention](logging-and-diagnostics.md#key-convention) · `internal/status` `SplitKey`
- *Hash separator*: a fixed prefix (row, page, section, root, capture integrity) that keeps snapshot hashes of different kinds from colliding; unrelated to the replication domain. `internal/snapshot` manifest.go, encoder.go

**domain audit** (also: SetDomainAudit, PinDomainAudit, DomainViolations) — The runtime check that warns when a component attaches to an entity of the wrong domain (per `componentDomains`) or a Shared-profile system writes a Player entity (D-1); it never blocks the write, runs while the `domain` log scope is on at debug or pinned for a soak, and counts and retains what it finds. [Logging `sub="domain"`](logging-and-diagnostics.md#subdomain) · [Domain design §6](domain-design.md#6-verification-model) · `internal/engine` `auditComponentDomain`, `auditEntityDomain`

**domain profile** (also: system profile, system domain, SystemDomain, SystemProfile, SystemShared, SystemPlayer, SystemDual) — A system's declared replication role in `SystemDef.Domain`: `shared` reads and writes Shared state only, `player` reads Shared state and writes this participant's Player entities and owner-authored cursor components, and `dual` resolves the domain per request or target, by ambient stamping (D-7) or claimed geometry (D-12). `TestSystemDomainProfiles` checks each against the RNG streams, entity domains and component stores its system's file touches. [Domain design D-15](domain-design.md#d-15--classification-is-declared-once) · [Development §5](development.md#5-validation-and-tests) · `internal/engine` `SystemDomain`, `SystemProfile` · `internal/system` `TestSystemDomainProfiles`

**door** (also: `-relay` door, `ChainEntry.Door`, `RelayDoor`) — Where a participant admits people who cannot reach the host, as the world reaches it: its `-relay` address, completed by the coordinator from the relay's own connection when it names no host, or the link `vif-router` publishes for it. A relay tells the authority its door on its own link once it is open (`MsgRelayJoin`, step `RelayDoor`, unanswered), and the chain carries it beside the port, so every participant holds every door, `:session` names them and a run with no path to its authority rejoins at one before a port. A door alone lists its participant without making it a candidate. See also **relay**, **succession**, **vif-router**. [Multiplayer §5.6](multi-player.md#56-relay-doors-and-the-router) · `internal/network` `ChainEntry`, `RelayDoor` · `internal/converge` `Reach.NoteDoor` · `internal/app` `membership.rejoinThrough`

**drag** (also: overspeed drag) — A velocity-opposing deceleration: `HomingProfile.Drag` slows a homing entity running above its cruise speed, and `ApplyLinearDrag` and `ApplyQuadraticDrag` are the general steering forms. [AI and physics §5](ai-physics-and-evolution.md#5-physics-primitives) · `pkg/vmath/physics` `HomingProfile`, `ApplyLinearDrag`

**drain** (also: DrainComponent, SpeciesDrain, -drain, draining) — nine senses.
- *Species*: the basic hostile species, a per-participant Player-domain population of `ceil(heat × 10 / 100)`, at most 10, that materializes, chases its cursor, drains shield energy, removes heat on unshielded contact, destroys the Player glyphs it crosses (normal and bright ones leaving dust), breaks the gold sequence it crosses and is absorbed into a swarm or quasar it crosses. [Gameplay §8](gameplay.md#8-hostile-and-structural-actors) · `internal/component` `DrainComponent` · `internal/system` `DrainSystem`
- *Energy*: the shield's cost: a hostile hit or contact through an active shield drains energy (`CursorDamage.EnergyDrain`) instead of heat, and an active shield passively drains 1% of current energy per second, bypassing boost and ember. [Gameplay: Shield](gameplay.md#shield) · `internal/system` `ShieldSystem` · `internal/component` `CursorDamage`
- *Combat effect*: `CombatEffectEnergyDrain`, the lightning profiles' effect: a fixed energy reward to the attacking cursor, growing it in its current polarity, and the zap drawn from its origin. [Combat §5](combat.md#5-resolution-statuses-and-what-crosses) · [Gameplay: Energy](gameplay.md#energy) · `internal/component` `CombatEffectEnergyDrain`
- *Lifecycle*: on a termination signal a `-serve` host enters `PhaseDraining`: readiness goes false, dials are refused with `ErrSessionEnding`, and it exits when the roster empties or `-drain` elapses (zero exits at once); a second signal exits at once. [Runtime: The allocated session](runtime.md#the-allocated-session) · [Fleet design §2](kubernetes-fleet.md#2-runtime-contract) · `internal/lifecycle` `Controller.Drain`, `PhaseDraining`
- *Fleet*: `deploy/k3s/session.sh drain` deletes every session Job and Service and clears the fleet's log files, so the fleet is empty before an update or a reboot; `--force` abandons a pod that will not terminate. [Runbook: Empty the fleet](../deploy/runbook.md#empty-the-fleet) · [Fleet deploy §14](kube-docker-deploy.md#14-the-reboot-gate) · `deploy/k3s/session.sh`
- *Transport*: `NetworkPort.Drain`, the per-tick poll that moves a transport's buffered inbound notifications into the network system; `DrainOffTick` reads between ticks without counting as one. [Services and networking §9](services-and-networking.md#9-poll-boundary-and-receive-lead) · `internal/engine` `NetworkPort`, `OffTickDrainPort`
- *Link backlog*: a correction body leaves a chunk at a time while the link's unacknowledged bytes drain within `SnapshotBulkQueue`, and a link that cannot deliver a whole world within `SnapshotKeyframeDrain` (500 ms) meets the floor with proofs instead. [Netcode design §2](netcode-design.md#2-the-correction-pipeline) · `internal/parameter` `SnapshotKeyframeDrain` · `internal/converge` `Corrections`
- *Route adaptation*: a gateway's despawn marks its adaptation entry draining, and after `RouteDrainTimeout` (60 s), which gives in-flight actors time to finish, the entry, its route graph and buffered outcomes are removed. [AI and physics §2](ai-physics-and-evolution.md#2-online-route-adaptation) · `internal/engine` `AdaptationResource.MarkDraining`
- *Stderr capture*: under `-dev`, the poll that turns each complete block written to the captured fd 2 into one `sub="race"` pointer record every `DevDrainInterval` (500 ms), once more at shutdown. See also **-dev**. [Logging §10](logging-and-diagnostics.md#10-runtime-output-capture) · `internal/core` `StartStderrDrain`, `DrainStderr`

**drift** — four senses. See also **digest**, **demand**.
- *Glyph drift*: a timed parallax movement of every loose Player glyph along a direction (`EventDriftStart`), each level at its `DriftLayerFactor` fraction of the speed (bright 1, normal 0.5, dark 0.25), advanced by simulated time with per-level sub-cell phases and no RNG, at once or eased along a **ramp** from where it stands, and an eased `EventDriftCancel` brings it to rest before it ends; new lines come on across an upwind edge as their layer makes room (**entering line**), as **scenery** when the start sets `scenery`, and sooner in proportion while it outpaces `DriftFillSpeed`. See also **parallax**. [Gameplay §9](gameplay.md#9-walls-maps-navigation-and-environment) · [Gameplay §4](gameplay.md#4-glyph-production-and-decay) · `internal/system` `EnvironmentSystem` · `internal/parameter` `DriftLayerFactor`, `DriftFillSpeed`
- *Repair drift*: `snapshot.repair_sections`, the sections the last repair this instance applied moved, and at debug the rows (`repair rows`). [Domain design §4.3](domain-design.md#43-runtime-parity) · [Telemetry reference: Snapshot and correction](telemetry-reference.md#snapshot-and-correction) · `internal/converge` `applyRepair`
- *Correction drift*: the linkpace demand's `Drift`, how far a participant's correction magnitude stands above its own recent level; past `UrgentDrift` it counts as urgent. [Domain design D-24](domain-design.md#d-24--cadence-adapts-the-convergence-floor-does-not) · `pkg/linkpace` `Demand`, `Bounds`
- *Target drift*: a flow-field target moving; past the recompute cooldown the field falls due once a target drifts the Manhattan `NavFlowDirtyDistance` (5 cells), or after `NavFlowSettleTicks` (20) for a smaller drift. [Navigation §4](navigation-design.md#4-throttle-phase-and-laziness) · `pkg/navigation` `FlowFieldCache`

**driven run** (also: driven App, Mode.Driven, ManualClock) — A headless, replay or script run whose `ManualClock` only `App.Tick` advances, with no scheduler or event goroutine; a solo one is a pure function of seed, resolved config and injected event groups for one build. [Runtime §1](runtime.md#1-process-modes) · [Runtime §3](runtime.md#3-runtime-clocks-and-cadence) · `internal/app` `Mode.Driven` · `internal/engine` `ManualClock`

**driver** — two senses.
- *Run driver*: what advances a driven App a tick at a time and injects its events: `journal.ReplayDriver` from a journal, `journal.ScriptDriver` from an authored script, `journal.FuzzDriver` a seeded action sequence for soak and parity tests, and `bot.Driver`, which plays one bot graph. [Runtime §1](runtime.md#1-process-modes) · [Runtime §4](runtime.md#4-play-playback-and-authored-script-loops) · `internal/journal` `ReplayDriver`, `ScriptDriver`, `FuzzDriver` · `internal/bot` `Driver`
- *Combat*: a system that fires weapons: `weapon` for cursor loadouts (Player) and `mount` for weapons hosted on Shared entities. [Combat §3](combat.md#3-drivers) · `internal/system` `WeaponSystem`, `MountSystem`

**drop table** (also: `[drops]`, `DropTable`, `drops`, killer) — A species' loot roll, its ordered **drop tiers**, rolled for each simulated cursor when a kill settles. Each code species has one in `profile.Drops`, which a scenario's `[drops]` table of its name replaces, and a data species rolls the one its program's `drops` names; `killer = "player"` rolls it only for a kill a cursor dealt. `LootSystem` resolves every species' table at `Init`. Vanguard's raiders all name `raider`, which drops a `U`, energy and heat on streaks all 32 share. See also **loot kind**. [FSM reference: Loot](fsm-reference.md#loot) · [Gameplay: Species loot](gameplay.md#species-loot) · `internal/component` `DropTable`, `DropKiller` · `internal/profile` `Drops`, `DropTableNamed` · `internal/behavior` `compileLoot`

**drop tier** (also: DropTier, pity) — One roll of a **drop table**, rolled for each simulated cursor when a kill settles (a predicted one only once confirmed) and dropping at most one of its entries: a unique tier skips weapon loot the cursor holds at full charge (a gun on an **upgrade path** at its last level) and any loot it has on the map and, when it skips them all, adds their fallback counts to each later non-unique tier, a roll that misses adding none; and pity improves repeated misses, an entry's chance growing by as much again for each roll in a row it misses, on streaks a table keeps for each cursor until the game starts over, every species rolling it sharing them. [Gameplay: Species loot](gameplay.md#species-loot) · `internal/component` `DropTier`, `DropEntry`

**dropped** — two senses. See also **dead letter**, **departure**, **drop tier**.
- *Event queue*: an event the full queue overwrote before dispatch; it counts in `event.dropped`, logs `queue overflow` and triggers a flight-recorder flush. [Logging: Triggers](logging-and-diagnostics.md#triggers) · [ECS and events §8](ecs-and-events.md#8-event-transport) · `internal/event` `EventQueue.Dropped` · `internal/status` `TrigDrop`
- *Transport*: a transport loss, an inbound notification a full poll buffer discarded or an outbound frame a peer's send queue refused, counted in `network.transport_lost_in` and `network.transport_lost_out`. [Services and networking §9](services-and-networking.md#9-poll-boundary-and-receive-lead) · [Logging `sub="net"`](logging-and-diagnostics.md#subnet) · `internal/system` `NetworkSystem.publishTransportLoss`

**drum kit** (also: drums.toml) — The sequencer's four percussion instruments (kick, snare, hihat, clap), each bound by name to the pre-rendered variants of a sound in the `drums.toml` bank, so a drum voice plays a cached sound and a user sound of the same name replaces the piece. [Audio §7](audio.md#7-music-sequencer) · [Audio §5](audio.md#5-sounds-and-sound-effects) · `pkg/audio` drumkit.go

**dual** (also: SystemDual, dual-domain) — The domain profile of a system that resolves the domain per request or target, by ambient stamping or claimed geometry, and draws from the target domain's RNG stream; eight systems are dual, among them `network`, `combat`, `death` and `environment`. See **domain profile**. [Domain design D-15](domain-design.md#d-15--classification-is-declared-once) · [Domain design D-8](domain-design.md#d-8--rng-streams-are-domain-separated-and-restorable) · `internal/engine` `SystemDual`

**dual-mode image** (also: .vifimg, DualModeImage) — An image converted once into both truecolor and xterm-256 cells (rune, colors, transparency) in quadrant or background render mode, with an authored anchor offset, stored deflated as a `.vifimg` file that the pattern loader turns into wall cells. [Content and tools §8](content-assets-and-tools.md#8-image-and-pattern-pipeline) · [Content and tools §9](content-assets-and-tools.md#9-ascimage-tool) · `pkg/ascimage` `DualModeImage`, `ConvertImageDual`

**dummy** (also: Target Dummy, `wad/scenario/dummy`) — A shipped solo scenario in `wad/scenario`: a weapon range whose cursor starts at the map's centre with no weapon and every loot kind lying still to its left, each lying back three seconds after its pickup under a countdown, and a pylon of one hit point a member to its right that drops nothing. A green `+` and a red `-` pad behind it add or take a hit point while the cursor stands on one, again every three seconds under a countdown, a dummy shot down rises on its spot once the cursor has walked onto it and back out of its shield's reach, and energy at 0 brings an energy drop homing from the dummy's centre. See also **event drop**, **:w**. [Gameplay §10](gameplay.md#10-embedded-campaign-progression) · `wad/scenario/dummy`

**dust** (also: ParticleDust, :dust) — The particle a glyph becomes when a drain crosses it at normal or bright level, on a dust-all request (`:dust`, a quasar encounter's gold round, a gold sequence destroyed in `main`, two storm circles colliding) or under special fire; it orbits the local cursor while that cursor's energy is nonzero, nudges drains, lives 2, 4 or 8 s by its glyph's level, and special fire consumes it into explosion centres. [Gameplay §4](gameplay.md#4-glyph-production-and-decay) · [Gameplay §7](gameplay.md#7-weapons-and-attacks) · `internal/component` `ParticleDust` · `internal/system` `ParticleSystem`

## E

**each** — A TOML macro invocation key on an array element: it names parameters bound to arrays of one length, and the macro expands once per zipped element, each result spliced into the array or appended as one element; the music bank's note lines are written this way. [TOML macros §4](toml-macros.md#4-invocations) · `toml/macro`

**echo** — two senses.
- *Input*: the journaled local event (`EventInputPressEcho`, `EventInputReleaseEcho`) that lights and sounds a captured key's button on the pressing instance at once, a playout lead before the crossing applies on every instance (D-18). [Input §1](input-and-modes.md#1-input-pipeline) · [Domain design D-18](domain-design.md#d-18--local-prediction-is-private) · `internal/engine` `World.PushInputPress`
- *Networking*: `MsgLinkEcho`, the answer to a link probe: the probe's bytes untouched plus the answering end's received bytes, `LinkReport` and clock, from which the prober times round trip, backlog and delivery rate. Neither probe nor echo reaches the game. [Services §8](services-and-networking.md#8-wire-frame-and-session-messages) · `pkg/network` `MsgLinkProbe`, `MsgLinkEcho`

**ECS** (also: entity-component-system) — The simulation's data model: entities are 64-bit handles, components are plain data in generated typed stores, and systems hold the logic and run in priority order on one `World` behind the world lock. See also **entity**. [ECS §1](ecs-and-events.md#1-world-ownership) · [Architecture §5](architecture.md#5-data-oriented-ecs) · `internal/engine` `World` · `internal/component` · `internal/system`

**edge** — four senses. See also **flood**.
- *Deployment*: the site configuration in front of the node: the public edge forwards only the game's TCP ports, without rewriting source addresses, and the site's edge (nginx in the reference) terminates TLS and publishes only the router's API, log and browser routes. [Deploy §2](kube-docker-deploy.md#2-the-public-edge) · [Deploy §12](kube-docker-deploy.md#12-the-sites-edge) · `deploy/website/vif.nginx.example`
- *Mesh*: the arrival edge, the link an epoch came in on; a relay passes each newly admitted committed epoch down to the participants behind it but that link, and a raw one up. [Domain design §4.2](domain-design.md#42-mesh-relay) · `internal/system` `NetworkSystem.forwardDown`
- *Prediction*: an edge-triggered derivation (only `EventSpeciesKilled`) is a Shared event a predicting guest can raise more than once for one fact, because a correction can restore what it announced; the guest holds it in a ledger until an install proves it, so its Player-domain reward is paid once. Level-triggered holds are re-derived from the imported state instead. [Multi-player §3.4](multi-player.md#34-predicted-derivations) · [Domain design D-19](domain-design.md#d-19--every-future-affecting-shared-value-is-restorable) · `internal/event` `Derivation`
- *Behavior machines*: a `pkg/machine` transition with a guard that selects it, an edge action between exit and enter, and ordered candidate targets; the first edge of a phase whose guard passes owns the phase. Global edges carry a `From` mask and run before suspension, so stun can cancel an attack. [Behavior machines §2.2](machine-design.md#22-graph) · [§2.3](machine-design.md#23-step) · `pkg/machine` `Edge`

**embedded assets** (also: -d, -config-embedded) — Everything a build needs to play without a filesystem (fallback scenario, tutorial corpus, keymap, settings, bot graphs, sound and music banks, version stamp), embedded by `internal/asset`, the only package with an `embed` directive for shipped data; `-d` forces the embedded scenario and content. [Content §7](content-assets-and-tools.md#7-embedded-assets) · [Filesystem layout §6](filesystem-layout.md#6-package-ownership-and-wasm) · `internal/asset`

**ember** (also: EmberActive) — The heat phase a heat burst starts at the overheat threshold: while active it decays heat periodically, blocks ordinary energy penalties and collects nuggets within its ellipse, and it is drawn as a field around the cursor. A scenario lights or puts it out with `EventCursorArmRequest`, and an ember burning its heat out crosses `EventEmberSpent`. See also **heat**. [Gameplay §5](gameplay.md#heat-and-ember) · `internal/component` `HeatComponent.EmberActive` · `internal/render/renderer` `EmberPainter` · `internal/event` `EventEmberSpent`

**:emit** (also: :e, :event) — Command that constructs and publishes any registered event, with an optional inline TOML payload decoded through the generated registry; the `Event` prefix is optional, and it is refused in a live session. [Input §9](input-and-modes.md#9-command-mode) · `internal/mode` `handleEmitCommand`

**EmitEvent** (also: payload_vars) — The FSM action that publishes a named event: its name and payload are compiled at load, and `payload_vars` injects FSM variables into payload fields at run time. An unguarded, immediate `ClassLocal` one in `on_enter` or `on_exit` marked `reconcile = true` is released and reacquired across a captured machine's install. [FSM reference: Event Emission](fsm-reference.md#event-emission) · [FSM reference: Reconciled Local Lifecycle](fsm-reference.md#reconciled-local-lifecycle) · `internal/fsm/std` `compileEmitEvent`, `ApplyPayloadVars`

**emitter** (also: WeaponEmitter) — The ray weapon kind: on a cursor, a timed ray from the cursor through its orb to the first wall, fired at an assigned target and sweeping as the orb orbits; at the charge cap it stays lit for as long as main fire is held, target or not; mounted on a Shared host, it warns, fires and rests along a locked ray. See also **hold**. [Combat §2](combat.md#2-layers) · [Combat §4](combat.md#4-at-the-charge-cap) · [Gameplay §7](gameplay.md#7-weapons-and-attacks) · `internal/component` `WeaponEmitter` · `internal/system` `WeaponSystem`, `MountSystem`

**-empty** (also: empty grace) — Allocated-session flag, `-serve` only: end the run this long after the last guest leaves, which is also the window in which a dropped guest reclaims its slot; the session parks over the same world meanwhile, and 0 keeps it open. See also **-first-join**. [Runtime: The allocated session](runtime.md#the-allocated-session) · [Runtime: An empty session parks](runtime.md#an-empty-session-parks) · `internal/lifecycle` `PhaseVacant`

**encounter** (also: escalation) — A gameplay stage, such as the quasar, storm, Kraken or tower defense, that a scenario's FSM regions open, pause `main` for and close from TOML; the embedded campaign escalates 3 storms → Kraken → 3 storms → Kraken → tower. [Gameplay §10](gameplay.md#10-embedded-campaign-progression) · [Architecture §6](architecture.md#6-data-driven-encounter-control) · `internal/asset/scenario`

**endpoint** — two senses.
- *Session address*: a parsed dial or bind target: `[tcp://|vif://]host:port[/name]`, a `ws(s)://` route, or an `http(s)://` site asked to allocate a session. Every flag and command that takes an address reads it through `ParseEndpoint`. [Services §6](services-and-networking.md#6-operator-session-surface) · `pkg/network` `Endpoint`, `ParseEndpoint`
- *Supervision*: the supervised run's endpoint, the HTTP server `-probe` binds, with `/health` (a liveness code, readiness and session fields in the body) and `/metrics` (the status registry in Prometheus format). [Services §12](services-and-networking.md#12-the-supervised-runs-endpoint) · `internal/probe` `Server`

**energy** (also: EnergyComponent, polarity) — A signed, owner-authored cursor resource (D-13): its sign sets attack polarity, any nonzero value raises the shield, and it changes by reward, penalty, passive or spend deltas, or by a typed glyph's signed, heat-scaled change, which can cross zero. [Gameplay §5](gameplay.md#energy) · [Domain design D-13](domain-design.md#d-13--owner-authored-shared-values-have-one-writer) · `internal/component` `EnergyComponent`, `EnergyDeltaType`

**engine** — four senses.
- *Simulation*: `internal/engine`, the ECS core: `World`, typed stores, positions and the spatial grid, resources, `GameContext`, clocks, time control and the scheduler. [Package map §4](package-map.md#4-production-package-responsibilities) · `internal/engine`
- *Audio*: `pkg/audio.AudioEngine`, the thread-safe audio control surface over the sound and pattern registries, mixer, sequencer and backend failover. [Audio §1](audio.md#1-layered-design) · `pkg/audio` `AudioEngine`
- *Genetics*: `genetic.Engine` or `StreamingEngine`. See **genetic engine**. [AI §3](ai-physics-and-evolution.md#3-genetic-library) · `pkg/genetic`
- *Telemetry*: the `engine.*` status keys (ticks, fps, APM, speed, breakpoint). [Telemetry reference: Engine, event and process telemetry](telemetry-reference.md#engine-event-and-process-telemetry)

**enraged** (also: IsEnraged) — A `CombatComponent` flag marking a species' protected phase: a drain below its HP threshold, a quasar charging or zapping, a swarm in its attack cycle, a snake head its living body shields, a data species in a state flagged `Enraged`. Kinetic attacks and soft-collision pushes skip an enraged target, two enraged drains fuse into a swarm, and stun clears it. See also **fusion**. [AI and physics §5](ai-physics-and-evolution.md#5-physics-primitives) · [Gameplay §8](gameplay.md#8-hostile-and-structural-actors) · `internal/component` `CombatComponent.IsEnraged`

**entrance** (also: `entrance`, `BehaviorEntrance`) — How a data species comes onto the map when a scenario asks for it: at once, or, its prototype naming `entrance = "materialize"`, through a Shared materialize over its footprint, lasting the prototype's `entrance_ms` or else the materialize's own second, that the body lands from as it ends, beside any rock that came over it meanwhile. A materialize in flight counts in `behavior.count` and ends with its program's cancel; vanguard's raiders come on this way. See also **materialize**. [Behavior machines §4.4](machine-design.md#44-spawns-that-ride-their-materializes-and-spirits) · [FSM reference: Behavior Programs](fsm-reference.md#behavior-programs) · `internal/component` `BehaviorEntrance` · `internal/system` `BehaviorSystem`

**entering line** (also: `EntryResource`, `EntryLine`, `DriftResource.Step`) — A line of new text coming onto the map across an upwind edge while glyphs drift. `GlyphSystem` holds its runes still to place and the cell the next waits at, as its layer stood before the tick's step; each tick it moves the line on by the step its layer took (`DriftResource.Step`) and places what that brought onto the map, a rune at a time across a side and a whole row across an end, starting as far past the edge as the layer last stepped. The glyph renderer draws the waiting runes on from there by the tick's step and the layer's carry, so text comes on at the edge as it leaves the other; a line the drift no longer carries in is dropped. See also **drift**, **parallax**. [Gameplay §4](gameplay.md#4-glyph-production-and-decay) · [Rendering §7](rendering.md#7-layer-inventory) · `internal/system` `GlyphSystem` · `internal/engine` `EntryResource`, `EntryLine`

**entity** (also: core.Entity) — A `uint64` handle laid out `[domain:8][id:56]`; ids come from one counter per domain, so one Shared creation order numbers Shared entities identically on every instance, and the zero Entity means none. [Domain design §1](domain-design.md#1-domains-and-identity) · [Domain design D-9](domain-design.md#d-9--entity-identity-is-domain-local-and-deterministic) · [ECS §2](ecs-and-events.md#2-entities-and-typed-stores) · `internal/core` `Entity`, `MakeEntity`

**envelope** — two senses.
- *Log record*: the fixed keys around a JSON log record's open `fields` payload: `time`, `level`, `sub`, `run` and `tick`, plus `trace` on trace records; `vif-log` relies on `sub`, `run` and `tick`, and a recorder sidecar carries the same envelope as the session log. See also **log**. [Logging §2](logging-and-diagnostics.md#2-record-shape) · `internal/vlog` `buildLogger`
- *Snapshot*: the 10-byte `VIFS` envelope (magic, version, codec, plain size) in front of a capture, correction or shard set; see **deflate**. [Services and networking §8](services-and-networking.md#8-wire-frame-and-session-messages) · `internal/snapshot` codec.go

**environment** (also: EnvironmentSystem) — The dual-domain system that owns map-wide gameplay effects: wind, which pushes drains and Shared species from one Shared random stream, and glyph drift, which moves every instance's loose glyphs by simulated time with no RNG; both travel in a capture as declared system state. [Gameplay §9](gameplay.md#9-walls-maps-navigation-and-environment) · [Generic kinetic §6](generic-kinetic.md#6-wind-as-the-first-environment-effect) · `internal/system` `EnvironmentSystem`

**epoch** (also: epoch window) — two senses.
- *Multiplayer*: a production epoch, one source's closed tick of crossing production, sent as one `WireBatch` (named by `Source` and `ProducedTick`) even when empty. A per-source window of `NetworkEpochWindow` (64) epochs admits each once, so mesh duplicates and reordering are harmless. The committed copy returns to its producer, which reads it for its age; a producer with nobody behind it (`WireBatch.Leaf`) gets its epoch's copy without its frames and its sync's not at all. [Domain design §4.1](domain-design.md#41-receive-lead) · [§4.2](domain-design.md#42-mesh-relay) · [Multi-player §3.5](multi-player.md#35-command-frames-the-commit-and-pacing) · `internal/event` `WireBatch`
- *Simulation*: `engine.SimEpoch` (2000-01-01 UTC), the origin from which `SimTime(tick, interval)` measures every simulation instant (D-21); its value is not session identity, since a capture carries ages and a shared reader takes its phase from game time since it. [Domain design D-21](domain-design.md#d-21--simulation-time-is-tick-derived) · `internal/engine` `SimEpoch`, `SimTime`

**event** (also: GameEvent, EventType) — two senses. See also **event class**, **event queue**.
- *Game*: A typed `event.GameEvent` pushed to the world's queue and offered to the HFSM, then to the registered system handlers; its `Origin`, `Domain` and `Phase` mark provenance, replication and prediction, and the dispatcher routes on none of them. `internal/event/type.go` declares each type with its payload and replication class, and generation builds the registry from it. [ECS §8](ecs-and-events.md#8-event-transport) · [ECS §9](ecs-and-events.md#9-event-catalog-and-payload-contracts) · `internal/event` `GameEvent`, `EventType`
- *Music*: a pattern track's event, one note trigger setting step position, velocity, scale degree, octave, **accidental**, duration and probability, at most 512 per track. [Audio §7](audio.md#7-music-sequencer) · `pkg/audio` `Step`, `StepDef`

**event class** (also: EventClass, Local, Shared, Bus, Stamped) — An event type's declared replication class: Local never replicates, Shared is re-derived and journaled everywhere but never sent, Bus is a D-3 crossing, and Stamped is decided per event by its domain tag. `event.Replicated` says which records every journal must hold; `event.OnWire` is the narrower set a peer must receive. See also **event domain**. [Domain design D-10](domain-design.md#d-10--event-class-and-event-domain-answer-different-questions) · [ECS §9](ecs-and-events.md#9-event-catalog-and-payload-contracts) · `internal/event` `EventClass`, `ClassOf`

**event drop** (also: EventLootSpawnRequest, LootSpawnRequestPayload, still drop) — Loot a scenario raises rather than a species roll: one drop of the named kind at a cell for every cursor the instance simulates, owned and homing like any drop unless `still` keeps it at its cell; `return_ms` takes one collected off the map and brings it back to its cell that long after, a countdown standing by the cell meanwhile (`LootComponent.Still`, `Return`, `Away`). The campaign's tower victory drops a gun this way. See also **loot**, **upgrade path**. [Gameplay: Species loot](gameplay.md#species-loot) · `internal/event` `EventLootSpawnRequest` · `internal/system` `LootSystem`

**event domain** (also: GameEvent.Domain) — The domain an event's replication filter reads: the producer's ambient domain (`World.WithDomain`), one given explicitly through `PushLocal`, `PushCrossing` or `PushEventDomain`, or for a Stamped type the domain its producer resolved. It answers a different question than the event class. [ECS §8](ecs-and-events.md#8-event-transport) · [Domain design D-7](domain-design.md#d-7--domain-is-explicit-ambient-context) · [D-10](domain-design.md#d-10--event-class-and-event-domain-answer-different-questions) · `internal/event` `GameEvent`

**event loop** — The event goroutine of `ModePlay` and `ModeServer`: woken by a push (`EventQueue.Wake`), it takes the world lock, by `TryLock` retried every `EventLoopInterval` (4 ms) with backoff, and runs one whole settle of what was pushed between simulation ticks. Driven Apps settle on the caller's goroutine instead. [Runtime §6](runtime.md#6-event-loop-concurrency) · [ECS §8](ecs-and-events.md#8-event-transport) · `internal/engine` `Scheduler`

**event origin** (also: Origin, OriginInput) — Who produced an event: system, input, macro, command, network, debug, session or device. The dispatcher ignores it; every origin but system and device is journaled, as is an own crossing whatever its origin, and only input-origin dispatches count toward APM. [ECS §8](ecs-and-events.md#8-event-transport) · [Input §8](input-and-modes.md#8-apm-signal) · `internal/event` `Origin`

**event phase** (also: EventPhase, PhasePredicted) — Marks a record derived from Shared state as settled or predicted: a predicting guest stamps a Shared death it derives `PhasePredicted` and holds it in a ledger, and the player-domain rewards behind it wait for `EventSpeciesKillConfirmed`. `event.Derivation` names the edge-triggered types this applies to. [Multi-player §3.4](multi-player.md#34-predicted-derivations) · `internal/event` `EventPhase`, `Derivation`

**event queue** (also: EventQueue, MPSC ring) — The world's bounded, lock-free multi-producer, single-consumer ring of `EventQueueSize` (2,048) events: each publish takes its slot as `Seq`, overflow overwrites the oldest and counts a drop, an `OnWire` event is first offered to the wire sink (the playout barrier), and a journaled one is recorded at the queue's replay position. [ECS §8](ecs-and-events.md#8-event-transport) · [Runtime §6](runtime.md#6-event-loop-concurrency) · `internal/event` `EventQueue`

**eviction** — three senses.
- *Multiplayer*: the authority drops a participant whose own epochs, after a first window of grace, reach it late at least `-slow-late` times a second of game time over `-slow-window` on a link carrying at least `-slow-bytes`, or whose link sends more than `NetworkIngressBytesPerSecond` for itself, each bot it holds and each person the authority has no link to; its bots go with it, and `-slow-window 0` turns both off. Counted in `network.evicted`. [Multi-player §3.5](multi-player.md#35-command-frames-the-commit-and-pacing) · `internal/converge` `SlowPolicy`, `Authority.driveEviction`, `Authority.driveIngress`
- *Event queue*: a producer that overruns the ring evicts the oldest unread events, counted as dropped and triggering a flight-recorder flush. [Runtime §6](runtime.md#6-event-loop-concurrency) · `internal/event` `EventQueue`
- *Genetics*: a streaming engine's pending evaluation is evicted only when the eight table slots from its id's home slot all hold newer evaluations, so `PendingCapacity` bounds the evaluations in flight. [AI §3](ai-physics-and-evolution.md#3-genetic-library) · `pkg/genetic` `StreamingConfig.PendingCapacity`
