**EXP3** — The name the route-adaptation code gives its weight update (`applyEXP3`); the update is Hedge, since rewards are not importance-weighted. See **Hedge**. [AI §2](ai-physics-and-evolution.md#2-online-route-adaptation) · `internal/system` `AdaptationSystem.applyEXP3`

**explosion** (also: explosion centre, ExplosionSystem) — An area attack given by centre, radius and attack: missiles and disruptor pulses burst into one, and special fire turns dust into one explosion centre per occupied cell. Player targets resolve locally, and `ExplosionSystem` resolves Shared targets from the crossed geometry (D-3). [Gameplay §7](gameplay.md#7-weapons-and-attacks) · [Domain design D-3](domain-design.md#d-3--cross-the-smallest-shared-outcome) · `internal/system` `ExplosionSystem`

**external-force accumulator** (also: KineticForceSource) — Future design: a per-tick, fixed-order channel of forces and impulses from separate sources, cleared once by the owning integrator, so wind adds to a body's own acceleration instead of overwriting it. [Generic kinetic §8](generic-kinetic.md#8-feasible-pseudo-system-design)

**eye** (also: EyeComponent, EyeSystem) — A 5×3 composite navigation attacker that gateways spawn into a target group: it homes along route graphs, self-destructs on contact, and its type, one of seven, is evolved by the genetic engine. [Gameplay §8](gameplay.md#8-hostile-and-structural-actors) · [AI §4](ai-physics-and-evolution.md#4-game-genetic-adapter) · `internal/system` `EyeSystem` · `internal/component` `EyeType`

## F

**fadeout** (also: FadeoutComponent) — A short-lived Player-domain effect that fades a cell's background, and optionally its character, over its lifetime timer. See also **flash**. [ECS §4](ecs-and-events.md#4-component-catalog) · [Rendering §7](rendering.md#7-layer-inventory) · `internal/component` `FadeoutComponent` · `internal/system` `FadeoutSystem`

**fallback** — four senses.
- *Correction*: the keyframe fallback: when a selective repair would be wider than the keyframe it stands in for, or cannot be served, the authority sends a whole compressed keyframe instead, counted in `snapshot.keyframe_fallbacks`. [Netcode design §2](netcode-design.md#2-the-correction-pipeline) · `internal/converge` `Corrections.sendKeyframeTo`
- *Content*: the embedded scenario, corpus and settings a run uses when no configuration root supplies its own. See **embedded assets**. [Filesystem layout §3](filesystem-layout.md#3-resolution-policy) · [Content §7](content-assets-and-tools.md#7-embedded-assets) · `internal/asset`
- *Loot*: a unique drop tier that drops nothing adds the fallback counts of the entries it rolled, or of every entry if it skipped them all, to each later non-unique tier's drop. [Gameplay §6](gameplay.md#species-loot) · `internal/component` `DropEntry.FallbackCount`
- *Behavior machines*: what a `pkg/machine` edge applies when no candidate commits: `Retry(d)` re-arms the state in place and reports `CauseRetry`, and `Hold` leaves it as it is, so an expired state tries its timeout again next step. [Behavior machines §2.3](machine-design.md#23-step) · `pkg/machine` `Fallback`, `Retry`, `Hold`

**FastRand** (also: DeriveSeed, NewSeededRand) — The deterministic xorshift64 generator in `pkg/vmath`, seeded through the SplitMix64 finalizer and resumable from its `State`. `DeriveSeed` makes a labelled stream seed from a root seed, and `RandResource.Stream` gives each system a domain-separated stream that a capture restores by position (D-8). [AI §6](ai-physics-and-evolution.md#6-numeric-model-and-determinism) · [Domain design D-8](domain-design.md#d-8--rng-streams-are-domain-separated-and-restorable) · `pkg/vmath` `FastRand`, `DeriveSeed` · `internal/engine` `RandResource.Stream`

**fidelity** (also: `Fidelity`, `RipFidelity`, `RipSound`, `Ripper.Sound`) — How much a render sounds like the recording it stands for, judged on the recording's own frames rather than on its **rip**: both heard through one front end into a `RipSound` (the frames, the log-mel bands and the beats), paired bar by bar on each one's beats, the cosine of their pitch salience, of their mel bands and of their onset strength per beat averaged into a total, each one where they sound alike. Soundlab's `refine` judges the voices and the steps by it, the rip-back reported beside it, and `song <name> score` prints both. [Audio §11](audio.md#11-ripping-a-recording) · [Rip design §2](rip-design.md#2-stages) · `pkg/audio` fidelity.go; `internal/soundlab` `songJudge`

**FileService** (also: FileSource, FileResource) — The service that owns runtime access to external files. It contributes only a narrow categorized `Open` capability (`engine.FileResource`) that opens a regular file by logical name under `<root>/<category>/` in each configured root and never a host path, so systems never discover roots or open host paths. [Services §5](services-and-networking.md#5-capability-injection) · [Filesystem layout §6](filesystem-layout.md#6-package-ownership-and-wasm) · `internal/service` `FileService`

**field refresh** (also: `field_refresh_ticks`, `[navigation]`, held flow) — How often a reader out of sight reads its flow field: every `field_refresh_ticks` of the scenario's `[navigation]`, every tick when unset. Between reads a navigator holds its `FlowX`, `FlowY` and a drop its `LootComponent.FlowX`, `FlowY`, component state a capture carries, and one that went out of sight since the last read homes on its target; navigation's `refresh_ticks` is the phase (D-17). See also **flow field**, **throttle phase**. [Navigation §4](navigation-design.md#4-throttle-phase-and-laziness) · `internal/system` `NavigationSystem.refreshTicks` · `internal/engine` `ConfigResource.NavRefreshTicks`, `PursuitResource.Fresh`

**fill** (also: phrase fill, SetAutoFill) — In the music sequencer, slot 2's seeded substitute pattern, drawn from fill-role patterns of the same group and tier, on the last bar of each eight-bar phrase and restored on the downbeat; it is on by default, and `SetAutoFill(false)` turns it off. [Audio §7](audio.md#7-music-sequencer) · `pkg/audio` `AudioEngine.SetAutoFill`

**finalizer** (also: finalization) — The render buffer's end-of-frame passes, run outside the world lock: fill untouched backgrounds, dim in truecolor the background under glyph and transient runes, repaint the void, give every cell a glyph, and in 256-color mode settle each cell on one of the console's sixteen colors. [Rendering §6](rendering.md#6-finalization-and-color-modes) · `internal/render` `RenderBuffer`

**fingerprint** — two senses.
- *Build identity*: the simulation fingerprint, `manifest.Fingerprint()`, a hash of the component and system lists (names, domains, snapshot obligations, dependencies, order) and not of renderers or audio. A coordinator refuses a joiner whose `simulation` field differs. [Services §7.2](services-and-networking.md#72-join-identity-and-who-decides-it) · [Package map §6](package-map.md#6-generated-assembly) · `internal/manifest` `Fingerprint`
- *Corpus*: the loaded corpus's file, block and line counts, carried in the journal anchor and the capture header; a replay verifies it, and a join does not, because the corpus is player domain. [Logging: Record and anchor shapes](logging-and-diagnostics.md#record-and-anchor-shapes) · `internal/event` `JournalAnchor` · `internal/snapshot` `CaptureHeader`

**-first-join** (also: first-guest window) — Allocated-session flag, `-serve` only: end the run if no guest connects within this long of the listener binding; 0 waits forever. See also **-empty**. [Runtime: The allocated session](runtime.md#the-allocated-session) · `internal/lifecycle` `PhaseWaiting`

**fitness** — three senses.
- *Bots*: the weighted sum of events a bot's own steps dispatched (+1 per correct character, +5 per kill, +25 per gold sequence, −1 per error, −100 for defeat); it compares graphs, not players. [Bots: Fitness and comparing graphs](bots.md#fitness-and-comparing-graphs) · `internal/bot` `Stats.Fitness`
- *Route adaptation*: progress × efficiency of a graph-routed actor at death, which Hedge folds into its route's weight. [AI §2](ai-physics-and-evolution.md#2-online-route-adaptation) · `internal/system` `AdaptationSystem`
- *Genetics*: a genotype's score reported to its population; for the eye, closest approach plus saturating damage. [AI §4](ai-physics-and-evolution.md#4-game-genetic-adapter) · `internal/system` `GeneticSystem` · `pkg/genetic/fitness`

**flash** (also: FlashComponent) — A short-lived Player-domain effect that shows one character briefly at a cell, as when a dark glyph flashes away. See also **fadeout**. [Gameplay §4](gameplay.md#4-glyph-production-and-decay) · [Rendering §7](rendering.md#7-layer-inventory) · `internal/component` `FlashComponent` · `internal/system` `FlashSystem`

**fleet** (also: session fleet) — The K3s deployment of dedicated sessions: one `vif -serve` pod, Job and Service per session, at most ten at once, which `vif-router` creates on a website's request and which end themselves when empty. [Fleet §1](kubernetes-fleet.md#1-what-is-deployed) · [Deploy §1](kube-docker-deploy.md#1-the-shape) · `deploy/k3s` · `tool/vif-router`

**flight recorder** (also: -lr, :log rec, vif-rec) — An in-memory ring of the last `RecorderDepthTicks` (200, set by `-lr`) ticks of the whole status registry, installed when a session log runs, sampled every tick and written only when a trigger fires (dropped event, lock hold, race, crash, manual, break, or an opt-in FSM transition), to the session log or a standalone `vif-rec-<timestamp>.jsonl`. [Logging §8](logging-and-diagnostics.md#8-flight-recorder) · `internal/status` `Recorder`, `Registry.Trigger`

**flood** (also: mesh flood, hop count) — How succession records and departure notices spread: each goes to every neighbour except the link it arrived on, deduplicated by content. Committed epochs, owner state and correction bodies no longer flood: they go down the tree to the participants behind each node, with the per-source epoch and sequence windows, the per-slot sync sequence and the chunk assembly ending a copy that arrives twice and `NetworkRelayHopLimit` on `WireBatch.Hops` as a backstop; a raw epoch travels only up. See also **upstream**. [Domain design §4.2](domain-design.md#42-mesh-relay) · [Services §9](services-and-networking.md#9-poll-boundary-and-receive-lead) · [Multi-player §5.1](multi-player.md#51-succession) · `internal/system` `NetworkSystem.announceDeparture` · `internal/converge` `Authority.flood`

**floor** — two senses.
- *Multiplayer*: short for the convergence floor: every participant receives a whole world, or proves with a hash-only answer that it holds the authority's, within `SnapshotFloorKeyframeTicks`; cadence adapts and the floor does not. See **convergence floor**. [Domain design D-24](domain-design.md#d-24--cadence-adapts-the-convergence-floor-does-not) · [Netcode design §2](netcode-design.md#2-the-correction-pipeline) · `internal/parameter` `SnapshotFloorKeyframeTicks`
- *Scenario*: the map floor, a scenario's `[map] min_width` and `min_height`, under every map size the run takes on resize, reset and level setup. [FSM §3](fsm-and-configuration.md#3-root-schema) · `internal/resource` `Scenario.MapMinWidth`

**flow field** (also: FlowField, FlowFieldCache, :flow) — A weighted reverse Dijkstra from a target group's cells (horizontal 10, vertical 20, diagonal 22) that stores each reachable cell's downhill direction, so many actors share one route to the nearest target. `FlowFieldCache` computes a group's field lazily at its first reader and throttles recomputation, a phase that is Shared state (D-17); `:flow [group]` draws it. [Navigation §2](navigation-design.md#2-flow-fields) · [Navigation §4](navigation-design.md#4-throttle-phase-and-laziness) · `pkg/navigation` `FlowField`, `FlowFieldCache`

**flush** — three senses.
- *Diagnostics*: a flight recorder flush writes the held window to the log; any goroutine may request one (`Registry.Trigger`, `:log rec flush`), but only the tick goroutine performs it, at most once per `depth/4` ticks. [Logging §8](logging-and-diagnostics.md#8-flight-recorder) · `internal/status` `Registry.Trigger`
- *Rendering*: the terminal flush that sends a finalized frame's cells to the terminal, outside the world lock. [Rendering §1](rendering.md#1-frame-pipeline) · `internal/render` `RenderOrchestrator`
- *Multiplayer*: the epoch flush at the end of a tick body, which closes the tick's production epoch and hands it to the transport. See **epoch**. [Runtime §5](runtime.md#5-simulation-tick-steps) · `internal/event` `EventQueue.FlushWire`, `WireBatch`

**footprint** (also: `navigation.Footprint`, passability tier) — The cells a composite occupies relative to its header, and the header's offset from their top-left. `CompositePassability` tests a whole footprint at every candidate header position, so a navigation path guarantees clearance, and the direct-path test flies the footprint from its header along the line of sight (`HasAreaLineOfSight`); navigation keeps one such tier for the eye's 5×3, which every body it holds around the same header rides, and one for each other footprint a navigator declares or a route graph is requested for, while one needs it. The Kraken's footprint demolishes walls and strikes the cursors it touches. [Navigation §3](navigation-design.md#3-passability-tiers-and-wall-changes) · [Gameplay §8](gameplay.md#8-hostile-and-structural-actors) · `pkg/navigation` `Footprint`, `CompositePassability` · `internal/system` `NavigationSystem.syncTiers`

**force** (also: impulse, override, constraint) — One of four kinetic operations kept distinct: a force (acceleration) changes velocity in proportion to `dt`, an impulse is an immediate velocity delta, an override replaces velocity, and a constraint changes position or velocity to satisfy bounds, walls, formation or state rules. See also **external-force accumulator**. [AI and physics §5](ai-physics-and-evolution.md#5-physics-primitives) · [Generic kinetic: Impulses, mass, immunity, and stun](generic-kinetic.md#impulses-mass-immunity-and-stun) · `pkg/vmath/physics` `Kinetic`

**fork** (also: local fork, Host lost) — A survivor that no handoff record reaches by the last candidate's turn on the succession list, one that no participant carried to its authority for a walk down the whole list, or any survivor under `-authority host` once its **redial** ends, gives its session up. A person's run then goes on in a session of its own as **:leave** takes it, every other player's place kept as a **Wraith** and a hold the lost host set kept, with `:host` and `:join` open at once; a run with no loop, a script's or a bot's, continues alone from the last authoritative state as an explicit local fork, with `network.fork` set and a persistent `Host lost` badge, and refuses a higher term met later. [Multi-player §5.1](multi-player.md#51-succession) · [Multi-player §5.5](multi-player.md#55-leaving-a-session) · `internal/converge` `Authority.giveUp`, `Instance.Forked` · `internal/app` `membership.forked`

**frame** — seven senses. See also **wire frame**, **command frame**.
- *Rendering*: one composited screen: the orchestrator draws the layers under the world lock every third of a tick, finalizes and flushes outside it, and a `frameReady` handshake keeps the simulation at most one update ahead. [Rendering §1](rendering.md#1-frame-pipeline) · [Runtime §3](runtime.md#3-runtime-clocks-and-cadence) · `internal/render` `RenderOrchestrator`
- *Layout*: `FrameRect` is the game area with its row and column gutters; the part outside the playfield is the void. [Rendering §2.1](rendering.md#21-the-playfield-clip) · `internal/render` `RenderContext.FrameRect`
- *Wire*: one session message on a link, a 12-byte header and up to 65,535 payload bytes. [Services §8](services-and-networking.md#8-wire-frame-and-session-messages) · `pkg/network` `HeaderSize`
- *WebSocket*: an RFC 6455 frame: `pkg/websocket` sends each `Write` as one binary frame and reads payload across frames and messages as one byte stream, so a session message need not align with a frame. [Multi-platform: What a browser joins with](multi-platform.md#what-a-browser-joins-with) · `pkg/websocket` `Conn`
- *Crossing*: one encoded crossing (`event.WireFrame`) inside a source's epoch, scheduled at an apply tick; an ordinary frame is judged by its source's fence, a barrier-bound one by the capture tick. [Multi-player §3.1](multi-player.md#31-ordinary-and-barrier-bound-crossings) · `internal/event` `WireFrame`, `ScheduledWireFrame`
- *Audio*: a PCM frame, one stereo pair of signed 16-bit samples (4 bytes) at 44.1 kHz, 441 per 10 ms of buffer. [Audio §2](audio.md#2-stream-contract) · `pkg/audio` `AudioBytesPerFrame`
- *Animation*: an eye's animation frame, `EyeComponent.FrameIndex`, which advances each time `FrameRemaining` runs out and wraps at its type's frame count. `internal/component` `EyeComponent` · `internal/system` `EyeSystem`

**free mouse** (also: :free, :f, :mouse free) — The operator preference, on by default, under which bare pointer motion moves the cursor; it survives both reset forms. [Input §7](input-and-modes.md#7-mouse-and-automatic-fire) · [Input §9](input-and-modes.md#9-command-mode) · `internal/engine` `GameContext.MouseFreeMode`

**freeze** — four senses.
- *Status*: `Registry.Freeze`, called once by `Scheduler.Prepare` after `World.Seal` and before the first tick, closes the status registry to new keys, builds the group index and lays out the flight recorder ring; a key registered later is a detached cell counted in `stat.late`. [Logging: Freeze](logging-and-diagnostics.md#freeze) · `internal/status` `Registry.Freeze`
- *Audio*: the sound registry freezes its name-to-ID map at engine start, so a later file may replace a sound but not add a name. [Audio §5](audio.md#5-sounds-and-sound-effects) · `pkg/audio` `RegisterSound`
- *Browser*: a frozen tab, whose Worker the browser has stopped; the session drops it after `NetworkLinkDeadAfter`, and on return the Worker redials its place. See **hidden tab**. [Multi-platform: A hidden tab](multi-platform.md#a-hidden-tab)
- *Session*: the hold every survivor takes when it sees its authority go, a hold request of origin `session` (`TimeControl.HoldAs(HoldFreeze)`). No host set it, so the successor ends it as its group resumes and a fork as it goes on; a hold the lost host set passes to the successor, or to a person's fork in the session it goes on in, whose `:pause` ends it. [Multiplayer §5.1](multi-player.md#51-succession) · `internal/engine` `HoldFreeze`, `TimeControl.HoldAs`, `TimeControl.Holds`

**front door** (also: -route-listen, :7777) — The router's single native TCP entry for every fleet session (`vif://<site>:7777/<id>`): it reads one `MsgSessionRoute` frame, resolves the session's ready pod, re-sends the name and splices the two sockets, refusing a bad dial before it dials anything. [Fleet §9](kubernetes-fleet.md#9-the-session-routes) · [Services §8](services-and-networking.md#8-wire-frame-and-session-messages) · `tool/vif-router` `tcpRouter`

**front instance** — The App the process presents or exists for: your own player, or the watched or headless bot, as opposed to the bot seats beside it. Only it journals under `-j` and owns the signals, terminal, audio and probe. See also **seat**. [Bots: Recording and replaying](bots.md#recording-and-replaying) · [Runtime §3](runtime.md#3-runtime-clocks-and-cadence) · `internal/app`

**FSM** (also: HFSM, state machine) — five senses.
- *Scenario*: the generic hierarchical state machine, `fsm.Machine[T]`, with parallel regions, machine-global variables and delayed actions, that runs a scenario's encounters from TOML, and every scenario region is Shared state (D-20); bot graphs run the same machine over a bot's mind. [FSM §1](fsm-and-configuration.md#1-boundary-and-data-flow) · [FSM reference](fsm-reference.md#fsm-configuration-reference) · `internal/fsm` `Machine`
- *Input*: `input.Machine`, the key parser that turns terminal events into `Intent` values and keeps only parsing context. [Input §1](input-and-modes.md#1-input-pipeline) · `internal/input` `Machine`
- *Species*: the per-entity behavior state of the swarm, the quasar, the storm circles and the data species, behavior machines. See also **behavior machine**. [Behavior machines §4](machine-design.md#4-species-graphs) · `internal/system`
- *Session lifetime*: `internal/lifecycle`, a pure state machine over an injected clock that turns roster observations into a phase (waiting, occupied, vacant, draining, expired). [Runtime: The allocated session](runtime.md#the-allocated-session) · `internal/lifecycle` `Controller`
- *Behavior machines*: `pkg/machine`, the standard-library-only runtime of timed per-entity machines with edges, cues and a suspend predicate; the swarm, the quasar, the storm circles and every behavior program run on it. [Behavior machines §2](machine-design.md#2-runtime) · `pkg/machine`

**FSM bridge** (also: fsm_bridge, RegisterFSMComponents) — `internal/manifest/fsm_bridge.go`, which binds the FSM standard library to the ECS world by filling `std.Host[*engine.World]` (events, systems, status, config); `RegisterFSMComponents` installs it on a machine. See also **host**. [FSM §1](fsm-and-configuration.md#1-boundary-and-data-flow) · `internal/manifest` `RegisterFSMComponents`

**FSM variable** (also: SetVar, IncrementVar) — A named signed 64-bit integer shared by every region of one machine: it persists across transitions and region spawn or terminate, is cleared on init or reset, and is replaced by an installed capture. [FSM reference: Variables](fsm-reference.md#variables-1) · [FSM reference: variable actions](fsm-reference.md#variables) · `internal/fsm` `Machine.SetVar`

**fusion** (also: fuse, FuseSystem) — Drains merging into a larger species: `EventFuseQuasarRequest` fuses the elected cursor's drains into a quasar, and two enraged drains fuse into a swarm. `FuseSystem` plays the Player-domain transition, which holds the spawn until it ends: a drain pair's drains fly in as spirits, the first naming the swarm its arrival lands, and a quasar fusion plays a materialize naming the quasar; the causal participant crosses one `EventDrainDefeated` per consumed drain as it fuses and the geometry-only spawn request as the spirits arrive or the materialize ends (D-3). [Gameplay §8](gameplay.md#8-hostile-and-structural-actors) · [Domain design D-3](domain-design.md#d-3--cross-the-smallest-shared-outcome) · [FSM reference: Behavior Programs](fsm-reference.md#behavior-programs) · `internal/system` `FuseSystem`

**fuzzer** — two senses.
- *Soak*: `journal.FuzzDriver`, a seeded action sequence (intents, ticks, commands, resets, region operations, resizes, level setups) drawn from its own stream, so two drivers from one seed step in lockstep; the soak tests in `internal/app` run it over seed sweeps tiered by `VIF_SOAK`. [Development §5](development.md#5-validation-and-tests) · `internal/journal` `FuzzDriver`, `RunFuzz`
- *Go fuzzing*: native `Fuzz*` test targets that check code against a reference or an invariant, such as the flow field against a heap search, or the join and peer-link handshakes against whatever a stranger sends. [Navigation §2](navigation-design.md#2-flow-fields) · [Session fleet §4](kubernetes-fleet.md#4-security-posture) · `pkg/navigation` `FuzzComputeMatchesReference` · `internal/network` `FuzzAJoinHandshakeHoldsOnlyTheIdentityItAdmits`, `FuzzAPeerLinkOpensOnlyForAnAdmittedParticipant`

## G

**game area** (also: GameAreaRect) — The whole viewport in screen space; the part a smaller map does not cover is the margin, and the frame (`FrameRect`) adds the row gutter beside it and the column gutter below it. See also **gutter**, **playfield**. [Rendering §2.1](rendering.md#21-the-playfield-clip) · `internal/render` `RenderContext.GameAreaRect`

**game reset** (also: new, :new, :n, :new!, :n!, EventGameResetRequest) — A new game on the same App, without rebuilding it: entities, `GameState`, kill tallies and statistics start over, the roster's assignments are kept and rearmed, FSM regions restart from their boot states, and the journal run and the RNG session advance. `:new` (`:n`) asks for one, and a scenario's FSM may raise one, as the campaign does on a defeat; in a live session only the coordinator may ask, and the request crosses to apply at one tick everywhere, while an FSM-raised reset is re-derived on every instance. `:new!` also clears the issuer's HUD, pins and breakpoints and returns its free mouse, auto-fire and rate to their startup values, and `:n <scenario>` instead rebuilds the run on another scenario, a **session restart** in a session. [Runtime §10](runtime.md#10-reset-and-operator-state) · [Input §9](input-and-modes.md#9-command-mode) · `internal/engine` `Scheduler.executeReset` · `internal/event` `EventGameResetRequest` · `internal/system` `MetaSystem`

**game time** (also: time.game_elapsed_ms) — Simulation time, a function of completed ticks and the tick interval measured from `SimEpoch`, never wall time; FSM guards read it as `time.game_elapsed_ms`, which a reset zeroes. [Domain design D-21](domain-design.md#d-21--simulation-time-is-tick-derived) · [FSM reference: Game Time](fsm-reference.md#game-time) · `internal/engine` `SimTime`, `TimeResource.GameTime`, `TimeResource.GameElapsed`

**GameContext** (also: ctx) — The per-instance handle that ties a `World` to its runtime surface: game state, time control, key table, logger, session controller and the operator's atomics (free mouse, auto-fire, HUD), plus the push family (`PushEvent`, `PushLocal`, `PushCrossing`). [Runtime §2](runtime.md#2-composition-sequence) · [ECS §5](ecs-and-events.md#5-singleton-resources) · `internal/engine` `GameContext`

**GameState** — The per-instance holder of the game mode, tick count and APM signal: lock-free atomics plus a small lock over the APM history, which the scheduler fills as it admits gestures; a game reset clears it. [Input §8](input-and-modes.md#8-apm-signal) · [Runtime §7](runtime.md#7-lock-and-ownership-matrix) · `internal/engine` `GameState`

**gate** — eight senses.
- *Session*: the start gate releases a lobby's guests into the world (`MsgStart`, answered by `MsgReady` once installed) and waits only on guests still linked; every later guest arrives through the mid-run gate, which `membership.midRunGate` serialises. [Runtime §1.0](runtime.md#10-the-four-session-flags) · [Runtime: The allocated session](runtime.md#the-allocated-session) · `internal/network` `MsgStart`, `MsgReady`
- *Authority*: the term gate, `Authority.admit`, which a correction, manifest, request or shard set passes before it is acted on: one from a term this instance has left is ignored (`network.term_stale`), and one from a term no handoff record granted is refused (`network.term_refused`). See **term**. [Services §8: The authority term and the succession](services-and-networking.md#the-authority-term-and-the-succession) · `internal/converge` `Authority.admit`
- *Ownership*: the D-2 admission gates `World.SimulatesLocally` and `World.ResolveOwnedCursor`, which keep Player-domain mechanics and owner-authored writes on the cursor's owner. [Domain design D-2](domain-design.md#d-2--one-instance-simulates-each-player) · `internal/engine` `World.SimulatesLocally`
- *Input*: the router's press gate: a captured key presses only in Normal, Visual and Insert, never while input is suspended and never from macro playback. [Input §1](input-and-modes.md#1-input-pipeline) · `internal/mode` `Router.pressInput`
- *Telemetry*: a `GroupGate` (always, sentinel, activity) decides whether a status group appears in a view, snapshot or recorder window. [Telemetry reference](telemetry-reference.md#the-registry) · `internal/status` `GroupGate`
- *Deployment*: the reboot gate, the drain, reboot and recheck procedure a node passes before it serves again. [Deploy §14](kube-docker-deploy.md#14-the-reboot-gate) · `deploy/k3s/session.sh`
- *Development*: the checks a change passes: `go build ./...`, `gofmt -l`, `go test` on the touched packages, a build of any constrained target it touches (wasm, `vif_headless`), and regeneration when an event or manifest definition changed. [Agent rules: Gates](../AGENTS.md#gates)
- *Testing*: the netem gate, an opt-in Linux test that shapes loopback with `tc netem` to check that corrections stay bounded and recover. See **netem gate**. [Development §5](development.md#5-validation-and-tests) · `internal/app` (netem_test.go)

**gateway** (also: GatewayComponent, GatewaySystem) — A timed spawner anchored to an entity: it emits eye or snake spawn requests carrying target group, route and genetic metadata at an interval that can accelerate down to a minimum, and despawns when its anchor dies; it is not a species. [Gameplay §8](gameplay.md#8-hostile-and-structural-actors) · [AI §4](ai-physics-and-evolution.md#4-game-genetic-adapter) · `internal/system` `GatewaySystem` · `internal/component` `GatewayComponent`

**generated code** (also: _gen.go, gen-manifest, make generate) — two senses.
- *Manifest*: the `_gen.go` files `go generate ./internal/manifest/...` (`make generate`) writes with `internal/gen-manifest` from `internal/manifest/definition.go`, the event declarations and input enums: typed stores, domain tables, capture and page code, system and renderer builders, the event registry and input strings. They are committed and never edited by hand. [Development §3](development.md#3-generated-code) · [Package map §6](package-map.md#6-generated-assembly) · `internal/gen-manifest`
- *Help*: the `-h` table, `doc/vif.6` and the shell completions, all rendered from `helpSections`; `TestGeneratedFilesAreTheHelpTable` fails on drift, and `VIF_WRITE_GENERATED=1` rewrites them. [Packaging §1](packaging.md#1-shared-guidelines) · [Docs: Source-of-truth map](README.md#source-of-truth-map) · `cmd/vif` `helpSections`

**generation** — four senses.
- *Genetics*: one step of evolution: `Engine` runs synchronous generations, and a `StreamingEngine` advances its generation by outcome count. [AI §3](ai-physics-and-evolution.md#3-genetic-library) · `pkg/genetic` `Engine` · `internal/component` `GeneticStats.Generation`
- *Multiplayer*: the session generation, another name for the authority term. See **term**. [Multi-player §1](multi-player.md#1-vocabulary-and-boundaries) · `internal/network` `AuthorityTerm`
- *Navigation*: one generation per passability tier, the walls and each composite passability, each moving when its tier changes, so a field or route graph stamped with it when computed is what a recompute would give; a flow field's generation stamps likewise mark its cells current without clearing the arrays. [Navigation §3](navigation-design.md#3-passability-tiers-and-wall-changes) · [Navigation §2](navigation-design.md#2-flow-fields) · `internal/system` `NavigationSystem.applyWalls`
- *Status*: the status registry's combined registration counter, which invalidates its cached group index; after `Freeze` the tick path makes no generation probe. [Logging: Freeze](logging-and-diagnostics.md#freeze) · `internal/status` `Registry.Gen`

**generic species system** (also: BehaviorSystem) — The context system that runs every data species from its compiled behavior program and owns one manifest component, `Behavior`, over the scaffold every composite species shares: spawn, hit points, lifetime, contacts and integrity. [Behavior machines §6.4](machine-design.md#64-beyond-the-machine) · `internal/system` `BehaviorSystem`

**genetic engine** (also: GA, pkg/genetic, StreamingEngine) — `pkg/genetic`, a standard-library evolution library with a synchronous generational `Engine` and a caller-driven steady-state `StreamingEngine` whose evaluations complete asynchronously by `EvalID` and whose state checkpoints and restores exactly. `GeneticSystem` evolves eye types with it and carries its checkpoint in the capture (D-19). [AI §3](ai-physics-and-evolution.md#3-genetic-library) · [AI §4](ai-physics-and-evolution.md#4-game-genetic-adapter) · `pkg/genetic` `StreamingEngine`, `Engine`

**genetic registry** (also: TrackedSpecies, EventGeneticRegisterSpecies) — `pkg/genetic/registry`, which holds up to 256 species populations in atomic slots, each a `TrackedSpecies` owning a streaming engine and its fitness and tracking pipeline, so sampling and stats take no lock. The FSM registers a species with `EventGeneticRegisterSpecies`, and a game reset deregisters every one. [AI §3](ai-physics-and-evolution.md#3-genetic-library) · [AI §4](ai-physics-and-evolution.md#4-game-genetic-adapter) · `pkg/genetic/registry` `Registry`, `TrackedSpecies`

**genotype** (also: GenotypeComponent, gene, GeneBounds) — The gene vector an evaluated actor carries with its evaluation ID; for the eye, one gene in [0, 1] that `parameter.GeneBounds.Bin` decodes into one of seven eye types. A gateway samples it, or a scout, at spawn, and the actor's fitness is reported when it dies. [AI §4](ai-physics-and-evolution.md#4-game-genetic-adapter) · `internal/component` `GenotypeComponent` · `internal/parameter` `GeneBounds`

**gesture** — One counted action in the APM signal: a dispatch pass of input-origin events, with a key repeated within 500 ms counted only on its 1st, 2nd, 4th, 8th... press and the pointer counted on a reversal after six columns or forty columns swept one way, so a drift reads low and jitter adds nothing; macros, auto-fire and input while paused count nothing. [Input §8](input-and-modes.md#8-apm-signal) · `internal/engine` `GameState.AdmitAction`

**GHCR** — GitHub Container Registry, where nightly CI publishes the `vif_headless` session image as `ghcr.io/lixenwraith/vif:nightly` and `sha-<commit>`. [Packaging §2](packaging.md#2-release-surface) · [Development §6](development.md#6-ci-workflows) · `.github/workflows/release.yml`

**glyph** (also: GlyphComponent, GlyphType, GlyphLevel, glyph atlas) — three senses. See also **top glyph**.
- *Font*: the font atlas, `internal/asset/font/mono.toml`, every rune the code draws as a 12x24 bitmap, which `pkg/glyph` parses and its `Raster` draws a frame of cells with into RGBA: the reference drawer of a graphical host, which a drawer in another runtime (Android's `CellDrawer.OnCanvas`) matches pixel for pixel from the same atlas as one texture, taking frames as the cells changed since the one it holds; the browser page takes the texture's runes and rasterizes them from a font at its own cell size. [Content §7](content-assets-and-tools.md#7-embedded-assets) · [Multi-platform §8](multi-platform.md#8-adding-another-renderer-or-android-host) · `pkg/glyph` `Atlas`, `Raster`, `Colors`, `Texture`, `Frames`, `Probe`; `android/game` `Atlas`, `Frame`; `tool/font-atlas`
- *Gameplay*: a character entity, typeable unless it is **scenery**: a rune with a type (green, blue, red, white, gold) and a level (dark, normal, bright). `GlyphSystem` places blue and green corpus text as Player-domain entities, red comes from decay, gold marks gold-sequence members, and a glyph's color and the typist's heat set its energy. [Gameplay §4](gameplay.md#4-glyph-production-and-decay) · [Gameplay §3](gameplay.md#3-typing-rules) · `internal/component` `GlyphComponent`
- *Rendering*: the rune a render-buffer cell draws; finalization leaves every cell with one, a space where none was drawn. [Rendering §6](rendering.md#6-finalization-and-color-modes) · `internal/render` `RenderBuffer`

**:god** (also: :demon) — Development grant on the invoking cursor: max heat, high positive (`:god`) or negative (`:demon`) energy, and every weapon. [Input §9](input-and-modes.md#9-command-mode) · `internal/mode` `handleGodCommand`

**gold sequence** (also: gold, GoldSystem) — A protected, bright, 10-character Shared composite typed left to right within `GoldDuration` (10 s); it ends completed, timed out or destroyed (any species body covering a member destroys it), completion credits the cursor that typed the most members, and the FSM decides the reward; a spawn request while one runs is answered with it, which is how the campaign's quasar and `main` adopt each other's sequence. `Shift-Tab` jumps to it for 10% of energy. [Gameplay §6](gameplay.md#gold-sequences) · `internal/system` `GoldSystem`

**graph** (also: route graph, bot graph, `:graph`) — five senses.
- *Bots*: a bot graph, one self-contained TOML file in the FSM dialect that drives one bot through intents; tables a bot cannot use are refused at load. [Bots: Writing a graph](bots.md#writing-a-graph) · `internal/bot` `Graph`, `ParseGraph`
- *Navigation*: a route graph, several distinct near-optimal corridors from a gateway to its target, built by repeated penalised Dijkstra, among which route adaptation chooses; `:graph [group]` draws it. [Navigation §8](navigation-design.md#8-route-graphs) · `pkg/navigation` `RouteGraph`, `RouteSolver`
- *FSM*: the validated, immutable state hierarchy and transitions a loaded scenario compiles to, which the machine steps. [FSM §1](fsm-and-configuration.md#1-boundary-and-data-flow) · [FSM §4](fsm-and-configuration.md#4-states-and-hierarchy) · `internal/fsm`
- *Architecture*: the dependency graph of services, which `core.TopoSort` orders, and of systems (`Requires`, `Optional`), which the same sort checks for missing names and cycles. [Services §2](services-and-networking.md#2-dependency-ordering-and-rollback) · `internal/core` `TopoSort`
- *Behavior machines*: `machine.Graph`, an immutable per-species state graph that `machine.New` declares and `Build` validates once. [Behavior machines §2.2](machine-design.md#22-graph) · `pkg/machine` `Graph`, `Builder`

**grayout** (also: EventGrayoutStart) — A post-process that desaturates glyph layers while held, sparing guidance, fields, transients, composites, health bars and UI. `EventGrayoutStart` and `EventGrayoutEnd` raise and lift it, scoped to one cursor's instance when the payload names one, as the quasar encounter does for the cursor it fused from. [Rendering §5](rendering.md#5-semantic-masks-and-post-processing) · [FSM: Installing a captured machine](fsm-and-configuration.md#installing-a-captured-machine) · `internal/render/renderer` `GrayoutRenderer`

**grid** — five senses.
- *Spatial*: the spatial grid, the position index every entity's cell is registered in. See **spatial grid**. [ECS §3](ecs-and-events.md#3-position-and-spatial-queries) · `pkg/position` `Grid`
- *Navigation*: the wall, or passability, grid that flow fields and route graphs are computed over; it moves only where the **wall log** says a wall turned and is named by a generation. [Navigation §3](navigation-design.md#3-passability-tiers-and-wall-changes) · `internal/system` `NavigationSystem`
- *Ping*: the ping grid, guide lines every five cells across the visible map from the cursor, flashed by `Esc` in Normal mode. [Input §2](input-and-modes.md#2-modes) · `internal/component` `PingComponent`
- *Physics*: grid traversal: `GridTraverser` walks every cell a segment crosses (supercover DDA) without allocating, and `Line` walks a segment's Bresenham cells, the line of sight, ray, bolt and charge and teleport line walks, as a value kept in registers. [AI §6](ai-physics-and-evolution.md#6-numeric-model-and-determinism) · `pkg/vmath` `GridTraverser`, `Line`
- *Maze*: a generated maze's `[][]bool` grid of walls and passages. [AI §7](ai-physics-and-evolution.md#7-maze-generation) · `pkg/maze` `Result`

**group** (also: target group, status group, pattern group) — four senses.
- *Navigation*: a target group, a set of up to eight navigation targets sharing one flow field: group 0 is the cursor roster, script groups 1–15 hold `TargetAnchor` entities, a followed entity, a fixed cell or a roster mirror, and each cursor on the map gets a private group, which the snake heads locked on it and the drops it owns steer by (`PursuitResource`). [Navigation §5](navigation-design.md#5-target-groups) · `internal/engine` `TargetResource`
- *Telemetry*: a status group, the bounded semantic group `status.SplitKey` maps a dotted key to; one group is one overlay card and one log record (`msg`) of at most 15 metrics, shown or hidden by its `GroupGate`. [Logging: Key convention](logging-and-diagnostics.md#key-convention) · `internal/status` `SplitKey`
- *Replay*: a settle group, one non-empty explicit settle of injected or queued events, which advances the journal's `boundary` counter; replay keeps group boundaries. [Logging: Position lattice](logging-and-diagnostics.md#position-lattice) · `internal/journal` `SameReplayGroup`
- *Music*: a pattern group, the patterns whose `groups` name it, a style anchored by its bass patterns (the shipped bank has rolling, offbeat, breaks, pump and trance); the sequencer draws rhythm, melody and bass from one group, keeps the bass through the tiers it covers, draws the member played least along the melody's motif ladder, and moves to another group after four phrases. [Audio §7](audio.md#7-music-sequencer) · `pkg/audio` `Pattern.Groups`, `RoleBass`

**guard** — three senses.
- *FSM*: a predicate on a transition or action: a static guard by name, or a factory guard compiled at load from its arguments, decoded strictly into a typed struct (`VarCompare`, `StatusIntCompare`, `Payload*`), combined with `And`, `Or` and `Not`. Guards in a Shared region read only what every instance holds (D-20). [FSM §9](fsm-and-configuration.md#9-guards) · [FSM reference: Guards](fsm-reference.md#guards) · `internal/fsm` `GuardFunc`, `Machine.RegisterGuard`
- *Bots*: a bot graph's guards over what its own instance shows (`HasTarget`, `OnTarget`, `InMode`, `Chance`, `Idle`, `Own`); each refuses an argument it does not read. [Bots: Guards](bots.md#guards) · `internal/bot`
- *Behavior machines*: a behavior program's predicate (`TargetDistance`, `LineOfSight`, `HitWall`, `Chance` and the rest), read-only over the instance, on a `pkg/machine` edge, candidate or suspend, combined with `And`, `Or` and `Not`. [Behavior machines §6.2](machine-design.md#62-vocabulary) · [FSM reference: Behavior Programs](fsm-reference.md#behavior-programs) · `internal/behavior` `Vocabulary.Predicate`

**guest** (also: game guest, joiner, -join) — three senses.
- *Multiplayer*: a non-authoring participant that runs the same Shared simulation as a predictor and adopts the authority's corrections; it is not a thin renderer, and it relays for its own bots. [Multi-player §1](multi-player.md#1-vocabulary-and-boundaries) · [Domain design D-11](domain-design.md#d-11--the-host-is-exact-guests-converge) · `internal/converge` `Corrections`
- *Session lifetime*: the count of roster entries other than the coordinator, a session's own bots included, which the lifecycle controller and the `/health` body read. [Runtime: The allocated session](runtime.md#the-allocated-session) · [Services §12](services-and-networking.md#12-the-supervised-runs-endpoint) · `internal/app` `membership.guestCount` · `internal/lifecycle` `State.Guests`
- *Deployment*: the fleet node itself, run as a guest of the host machine; `deploy/guest/` holds the node artifacts it is commissioned with and the helpers that replace one component at a time. [Deploying the session fleet](kube-docker-deploy.md#deploying-the-session-fleet) · `deploy/guest`

**gun** (also: WeaponGun, aim, `EventGunAimRequest`) — The innate cursor weapon: under main fire it sends one tracer along each cardinal of its aim, all four unless a scenario's `EventGunAimRequest` narrows them; a tracer deals direct damage on contact and chains into rod lightning, and charges set its lanes: one the centre, two the side lanes, three all three; a gun drop, the campaign's tower reward, adds one. A scenario can put it on an **upgrade path** instead. See also **weapon**, **lane**. [Combat §2](combat.md#2-layers) · [Gameplay §7](gameplay.md#7-weapons-and-attacks) · `internal/component` `WeaponGun`, `WeaponComponent.Aim`, `GunPath` · `internal/system` `WeaponSystem`

**gutter** (also: indicator gutters) — The relative row and column indicator strips beside and below the game area, which number only the rows and columns the playfield covers and paint the rest with the void color; `FrameRect` is the game area with its gutters. See also **game area**, **playfield**, **void**. [Rendering §2.1](rendering.md#21-the-playfield-clip) · [Rendering §9](rendering.md#9-ui-and-debug-projections) · `internal/render` `RenderContext.FrameRect`

## H

**handoff** (also: HandoffRecord, MsgAuthorityHandoff) — The record under which a successor takes authorship, and only authorship, at a new term: it carries the term, the new authority, its predecessor, the closed roster with slots and holders, the join anchor, the succession chain and the successor's evidence tick. A receiver refuses one that does not validate against its own players and chain; the lost predecessor and the bots may differ, a seat's crossing being its holder's word. See also **succession**, **term**. [Multi-player §5.1](multi-player.md#51-succession) · [Services §8: The authority term and the succession](services-and-networking.md#the-authority-term-and-the-succession) · `internal/network` `HandoffRecord`, `HandoffRecord.Validate`

**handshake** — three senses.
- *Join*: the plain exchange that opens a session link (the route name, empty for none, the claims, the coordinator's preview, the joiner's readiness and seat, the offer and anchor, the joiner's reply with its identity report); it is the admission, runs on its own goroutine, and `MaxHandshakes` bounds how many are in flight. [Services §7](services-and-networking.md#7-transport-roles-and-lifecycle) · [Services §7.1](services-and-networking.md#71-admission) · `internal/network` `HostAcceptor`, `JoinerReport`
- *WebSocket*: the HTTP upgrade a browser route or a `ws://` listener answers before the stream starts. [Fleet §9](kubernetes-fleet.md#9-the-session-routes) · `pkg/websocket`
- *Rendering*: the frame handshake: the main loop hands the scheduler one `frameReady` token per completed render, so the simulation runs at most one update ahead of the screen. [Runtime §3](runtime.md#3-runtime-clocks-and-cadence) · `internal/engine` `Scheduler`

**harmony** — In the music sequencer, the current root note, scale and chord progression through which pattern scale degrees resolve at trigger time. [Audio §7](audio.md#7-music-sequencer) · `pkg/audio` `harmony.go`

**hash-only** (also: hash-only correction, HashOnly, in place) — A correction exchange in which the receiver's manifest root equals the authority's: no state moves, the authority header is installed unless it is behind the clock, and the answer proves the receiver holds the authority's world, which also meets the convergence floor. Counted in `snapshot.corrections_hash_only`; a receiver holding the manifest as its tick closes indexes its live rows in place and captures nothing for it (`snapshot.manifests_in_place`, `Encoder.BuildLive`). [Netcode design §2](netcode-design.md#2-the-correction-pipeline) · [Domain design D-24](domain-design.md#d-24--cadence-adapts-the-convergence-floor-does-not) · `internal/converge` `Corrections.answerManifest`

**header** — four senses.
- *ECS*: a composite's header, the invisible, protected entity (the phantom head) carrying `HeaderComponent` (behavior, composite type, member entries with offsets) that its members point back to and that owns the composite's lifecycle. [ECS §6](ecs-and-events.md#6-composite-entities) · `internal/component` `HeaderComponent`
- *Multiplayer*: the capture header leading a Shared capture: schema, run, tick, seed, session, scenario digest, corpus fingerprint, map bounds, term, authority and per-participant crossing fences. A hash-only correction installs it alone. [Multi-player §3.2](multi-player.md#32-the-capture-boundary) · [Fleet §5](kubernetes-fleet.md#5-late-crossing-ordering) · `internal/snapshot` `CaptureHeader`
- *Networking*: the fixed 12-byte big-endian header (`HeaderSize`) before every session message: type, flags, sequence, ack and a 16-bit payload length. [Services §8](services-and-networking.md#8-wire-frame-and-session-messages) · `pkg/network` `HeaderSize`, `MaxPayloadSize`
- *Fleet*: the client-address header, the HTTP header the site's edge overwrites with the player's address (`-client-address-header`, `VIF_ROUTER_CLIENT_ADDRESS_HEADER`); it keys the router's per-player join and creation budgets, and with none named those routes charge none. [Deploy §12](kube-docker-deploy.md#12-the-sites-edge) · [Fleet §4](kubernetes-fleet.md#4-security-posture) · `tool/vif-router` `ClientAddressHeader`

**headless** (also: -headless, ModeHeadless, vif_headless) — two senses.
- *Runtime*: `app.ModeHeadless`, a run with no presentation or audio on a `ManualClock` its caller ticks: a harness, an authored `-script`, bots under `-bot -headless`, or `-r <journal> -headless` verifying a journal's digests unattended. [Runtime §1](runtime.md#1-process-modes) · [Runtime: Authored headless scripts](runtime.md#authored-headless-scripts) · `internal/app` `ModeHeadless`
- *Build*: the `vif_headless` build tag (`make headless`, `bin/vif-headless`), which omits renderers and audio; it is the dedicated-server profile the session image runs. [Multi-platform §1](multi-platform.md#1-current-build-profiles) · [Multi-platform: What headless still carries](multi-platform.md#what-headless-still-carries) · `Makefile` `headless`

**heartbeat** — The logger's `PROC` record every 60 s with its processed and dropped record counts. [Logging §13](logging-and-diagnostics.md#13-sink-policy) · `internal/vlog`

**heat** (also: HeatComponent, overheat) — Owner-authored typing momentum on a cursor, clamped 0–100: a correct key adds one (two under boost), an error removes 10, and additions past the cap accumulate as overheat until a burst starts the ember phase. Heat sets the local drain population and scales glyph energy. See also **ember**. [Gameplay §5](gameplay.md#heat-and-ember) · [Gameplay §3](gameplay.md#3-typing-rules) · `internal/component` `HeatComponent` · `internal/system` `HeatSystem`

**Hedge** (also: EXP3, applyEXP3, multiplicative weights) — The route-adaptation update: each observed route's weight is multiplied by `exp(rate × mean fitness)`, weights are normalised with a 0.5% floor, and routes are sampled 90% by weight and 10% as uniform scouts into the pool spawns pop. The code calls it `applyEXP3`, but rewards are not importance-weighted. [AI §2](ai-physics-and-evolution.md#2-online-route-adaptation) · `internal/system` `AdaptationSystem.applyEXP3`

**helper file** (also: `helperFiles`, unattributed file) — A file of `internal/system` that declares no system and takes the world it works on: `blast.go`, `interaction.go`, `speakers.go`, `sweep.go`, `targeting.go` and `telemetry.go`. Their functions and the timer slot's `armTimer` and `timerRunning` are the only ones in `internal/system` a system may call outside its own file; `helperFiles` pins the set, and `TestHelperFilesArePinned` fails when a file joins or leaves it. See also **timer**, **system**. [Package map §2](package-map.md#2-simulation-package-topology) · [ECS and events §10](ecs-and-events.md#10-adding-ecs-behavior-safely) · `internal/system` `helperFiles`, `TestHelperFilesArePinned`

**HFSM** — Hierarchical finite state machine, the scenario FSM. See **FSM**. [FSM §1](fsm-and-configuration.md#1-boundary-and-data-flow) · `internal/fsm`

**hidden tab** (also: pageHidden, frozen tab) — A browser page the user has hidden: the WASM build draws nothing but keeps ticking, answering probes and reading its socket in the Worker, and repaints once on return. A joined page hidden five minutes (`LEAVE_HIDDEN_MS`) ends its Worker, so the session sees it leave, and reloads when shown again, rejoining with the tab's claim. A tab the browser freezes stops the Worker, so the session drops it after `NetworkLinkDeadAfter` (5 s); on return the Worker redials its place, or the page reloads if frozen past the five minutes. [Multi-platform: A hidden tab](multi-platform.md#a-hidden-tab) · `internal/app` `pageHidden`

**hierarchy-map** (also: `tool/hierarchy-map`) — The terminal tool that selects Go source subsets as LLM context, indexed by `@lixen:` annotations, local imports and symbol-level coupling. [Content §10](content-assets-and-tools.md#10-other-repository-tools) · [Package map §9](package-map.md#9-commands-tools-and-sandboxes) · `tool/hierarchy-map`

**high-water mark** (also: _hwm) — A status metric holding the largest value reached since the last reset, kept by a compare-and-swap that only moves up; `<domain>.buf_<name>_hwm` is the peak length a reused slice reached. Marks clear on `Init`, once per game, and are left out of the cross-instance comparison. [Logging: High-water marks](logging-and-diagnostics.md#high-water-marks) · `internal/system` `newBufferTelemetry`

**hold** — nine senses. See also **park**, **reconcile**, **tap**, **pause**, **wait**.
- *Simulation*: the pause `:pause` (`:p`), the `p` key or Game → Pause starts and only another ends, so no mode change ends it, and the status bar shows `PAUSED` (`TimeControl.SetHeld`, `GameContext.Hold`). In a live session only the host may: its `EventGameHold` crosses, every participant stops after the same tick, a joiner lands held (`SessionOffer.Held`) with the crossings still scheduled (`NetworkSystem.PendingAfter`), and the host's next epoch resumes each guest (`NetworkResource.OnAuthorityAhead`); a lost host's hold passes to its successor, or to a person's fork. A reproduction applies it without stopping. [Runtime §8](runtime.md#8-pause-semantics) · [Multi-player §3.6](multi-player.md#36-holding-a-session) · `internal/engine` `TimeControl.Held`, `World.Reproduces`; `internal/system` `MetaSystem`
- *Start*: the start hold, a new game a person plays standing at its first tick until one press the held game would not take, which does nothing else (`HoldStart`): at launch, on `:n`, Restart game, Start and a map change, never on a scenario's own reset (`GameResetPayload.Wait`). Android's start screen's tap and the page's press are its launch press; in a session the host's press starts everyone, a joiner arriving meanwhile waits too (`SessionOffer.Start`), and a guest reads "Waiting for the host to start". [Input §2](input-and-modes.md#2-modes) · [Multi-player §3.6](multi-player.md#36-holding-a-session) · `internal/engine` `HoldStart`, `GameContext.HoldAs`; `internal/mode` `Router.TakeStart`; `internal/app` `App.holdFirstGame`, `App.takeStart`
- *Input*: a key hold is a captured key's or a button's press the router keeps until its release crosses, from the slot that pressed; leaving the capture, play stopping, focus or the report's source going, and the key pressing again end it as `lost`. A bot holds a key for the ticks its graph names or until its `release`. [Input §1](input-and-modes.md#1-input-pipeline) · [Bots: Actions](bots.md#actions) · `internal/mode` `Router.endHolds` · `internal/bot` `Driver`
- *Multiplayer*: the correction hold: a correction or manifest for a tick this instance has not reached, within `NetworkHoldTicks`, is held until that tick closes, the newest replacing the one waiting. See **playout buffer**. [Netcode design §2.1](netcode-design.md#21-the-playout-buffer) · `internal/converge` `Corrections` · `internal/parameter` `NetworkHoldTicks`
- *FSM*: a persistent instance-local effect a Shared region keeps for its lifetime, such as a grayout or drain pause: a `reconcile = true` entry acquires it, the matching exit releases it, and a capture install re-derives it. [FSM reference: Reconciled Local Lifecycle](fsm-reference.md#reconciled-local-lifecycle) · [Domain design D-19](domain-design.md#d-19--every-future-affecting-shared-value-is-restorable) · `internal/fsm` `Machine.ImportReconciled`
- *Combat*: the ray hold: at the charge cap every main-fire request renews `RayHold` on the emitter's ray, so it stays lit while main fire is held. [Combat §4](combat.md#4-at-the-charge-cap) · `internal/parameter` `RayHold`
- *Diagnostics*: a world-lock hold; while hold sampling is on (the `lock` scope at debug, or a running flight recorder), one longer than `LockHoldWarn` (20 ms of wall time, 200 ms in a race build) is logged as `long hold` under `sub="lock"` with the holder's trace and triggers a flight-recorder flush (`lock.hold`). [Logging: sub="lock"](logging-and-diagnostics.md#sublock) · `internal/engine` `LockHoldWarn`
- *Fleet*: the router's per-session count of connections it carries over the front door and the browser route together, bounded by `-route-max`. [Fleet §9](kubernetes-fleet.md#9-the-session-routes) · `tool/vif-router` `holds`
- *Behavior machines*: `Hold`, a `pkg/machine` fallback that leaves the state as it is, so an expired one tries its timeout again on the next step. See **fallback**. [Behavior machines §2.3](machine-design.md#23-step) · `pkg/machine` `Hold`

**holder** — two senses.
- *Bots*: the participant whose process holds a bot (`RosterEntry.Holder`, `CursorComponent.Holder`; zero for an independent player): the bot's raw artifacts reach the authority over its holder's link, it is never a succession candidate, and it leaves with its holder. [Bots: Ownership and leaving](bots.md#ownership-and-leaving) · [Multi-player §5](multi-player.md#5-membership-topology-and-authority-continuity) · `internal/network` `RosterEntry` · `internal/component` `CursorComponent`
- *Authority*: `Authority.Holder()`, the participant currently authoring under the held term. [Multi-player §5.0](multi-player.md#50-who-listens-who-authors-who-can-be-reached) · `internal/converge` `Authority.Holder`

**homing** (also: HomingProfile, cornering brake) — Steering toward a target at a cruise speed with acceleration, overspeed drag, arrival slowing and a dead-zone snap, tuned per actor by a `HomingProfile` (drains, loot, missiles, each homing species, and a data species by its program's `[prototype.physics]`); a cornering brake damps the turn so an actor cannot orbit its target. [AI §5](ai-physics-and-evolution.md#5-physics-primitives) · [FSM reference: Behavior Programs](fsm-reference.md#behavior-programs) · `pkg/vmath/physics` `HomingProfile`, `ApplyHomingScaled` · `internal/profile` `DrainHoming`, `BehaviorHoming`

**host** (also: `-host`, `:host`) — nine senses. See also **dedicated host**, **coordinator**.
- *Host model*: `app.Host`, the adapters a platform supplies to a run: its `Presentation` and its speakers' writer, the build's own where nil, so build tags guard constructors and the simulation sees none of it. [Build profiles §8](multi-platform.md#8-adding-another-renderer-or-android-host) · `internal/app` `Host`
- *Multiplayer*: the game host, the participant currently authoring the canonical Shared world, session identity, roster and correction stream: the coordinator until a handoff moves authorship; `SessionRole` reports it as `RoleHost`. [Multi-player §1](multi-player.md#1-vocabulary-and-boundaries) · [Domain design D-11](domain-design.md#d-11--the-host-is-exact-guests-converge) · `internal/network` `SessionRole` · `pkg/network` `RoleHost`
- *Session flag*: `-host <addr>` binds a port and plays from its first tick with a local cursor and terminal, and `:host <addr>` opens the game already running as a session; contrast `-serve`. [Runtime §1.0](runtime.md#10-the-four-session-flags) · [Services §6](services-and-networking.md#6-operator-session-surface) · `internal/app` `App.BeginHosting`
- *Authority policy*: `-authority host`, the default with `-serve`, keeps authorship on the starting machine, so losing it leaves each survivor a local fork; contrast `migrate`. [Multi-player §5.0](multi-player.md#50-who-listens-who-authors-who-can-be-reached) · [§5.1](multi-player.md#51-succession) · `internal/app` `AuthorityHost`
- *FSM*: `std.Host[T]`, the capability adapter through which the standard actions and guards reach their embedder: the world through the FSM bridge, or a bot's mind. [FSM §1](fsm-and-configuration.md#1-boundary-and-data-flow) · `internal/fsm/std` `Host`
- *Combat*: a mount's host, the Shared entity (a species, a structure or an emitter) that carries a mounted weapon, such as storm's red circle. [Combat §3](combat.md#3-drivers) · `internal/component` `MountComponent`
- *Browser*: the WASM host, the launcher page in `web/` that runs the browser build in a Worker and draws its cell frames on a canvas (`pkg/browser`). [Development: WASM host](development.md#wasm-host) · `web/terminal.js`
- *Deployment*: the machine the production node runs on as a guest, which owns routing and packet filtering; it and the site's nginx are site configuration kept outside this repository. See **edge**. [Deploying the session fleet](kube-docker-deploy.md#deploying-the-session-fleet) · [Deploy §2](kube-docker-deploy.md#2-the-public-edge)
- *Behavior machines*: the species system that steps a `pkg/machine` graph, refilling its context per entity and writing its state's flags to components: `SwarmSystem`, `QuasarSystem`, `StormSystem` and `BehaviorSystem`. [Behavior machines §3](machine-design.md#3-hosting-a-machine) · `internal/system` `SwarmSystem`, `QuasarSystem`, `StormSystem`, `BehaviorSystem`

**HTTP resource provider** (also: content-addressed bundle) — Future design: a provider that fetches the canonical scenario container by digest over HTTPS, holds it to the bounds and digest check a transferred scenario meets, and exposes it the same way, for a client with no config root and no peer. [Multi-platform §7](multi-platform.md#7-external-maps-and-assets)

**HUD** (also: :hud, telemetry HUD, pinned cards) — The pinned telemetry cards drawn live from the viewport's top-left in columns, toggled by `:hud` or `:t hud` and turned on by pinning a card; a `hidden` line names cards that do not fit, and it has no focus mode. [Input §9](input-and-modes.md#9-command-mode) · [Logging §6](logging-and-diagnostics.md#6-status-registry-and-periodic-snapshots) · `internal/render/renderer` `PinnedStatsRenderer` · `internal/engine` `GameContext.OverlayHUD`

## I

**identity** (also: `PeerIdentity`, join identity, participant identity, crossing identity) — four senses. See also **participant**, **fingerprint**, **join**.
- *Networking*: Join identity, `network.PeerIdentity`, is what a joiner turns out to be, in two halves: the build half (protocol, simulation fingerprint, capture and journal schemas, tick interval), which the dialer checks against the preview before it builds a world, and the session half (seed, session, scenario digest), filled from the anchor by `SessionFrom`. The coordinator checks both before the joiner can affect session state; a mismatch names both builds (`Build`, never compared) and one field, each side in its own terms, and is a refusal of kind `RefusalIdentity`, which no retry helps. A dedicated host's `/health` names its build the same way, which each row of the router's session list carries (`state.build`). [Services and networking §7.2](services-and-networking.md#72-join-identity-and-who-decides-it) · [Domain model §4.5](domain-design.md#45-join-and-stream-framing) · `internal/network` `PeerIdentity`, `PeerIdentity.SessionFrom`, `PeerIdentity.Verify`
- *Multiplayer*: Participant identity is an admitted session identity in 1..MaxPlayers+1, where zero means no participant; `link.PeerID` names both the participant and the link to it. [Multiplayer §1](multi-player.md#1-vocabulary-and-boundaries) · `pkg/network` `PeerID`
- *ECS*: Entity identity is domain-local and deterministic: each domain has its own allocator counter, so Shared creation order must match on every instance. [Domain model D-9](domain-design.md#d-9--entity-identity-is-domain-local-and-deterministic) · `internal/core` `Entity` · `internal/engine` `World.CreateEntity`
- *Crossing*: a crossing's own identity, `event.CrossingID` (producer and wire sequence, zero for none), seeds the outcomes derived from it on every copy whatever tick it lands on. See **CrossingID**. [Domain model D-3](domain-design.md#d-3--cross-the-smallest-shared-outcome) · `internal/event` `CrossingID`

**image** — two senses.
- *Build and deploy*: The session image is the `scratch` container image holding one static `vif_headless` binary, built from `deploy/docker/Dockerfile` by `make image` or `deploy/update.sh image`; the fleet imports it into K3s pinned. [Fleet deploy §8](kube-docker-deploy.md#8-the-session-image) · [Development §2](development.md#2-common-build-workflow) · `deploy/docker/Dockerfile`
- *Content*: An image asset is a raster that `ascimage` converts into terminal cells and saves as a dual-mode `.vifimg` holding truecolor and 256-color data; an `EventWallPatternSpawnRequest` spawns one as a composite wall. See also **dual-mode image**, **pattern**. [Content and tools §8](content-assets-and-tools.md#8-image-and-pattern-pipeline) · [Content and tools §9](content-assets-and-tools.md#9-ascimage-tool) · `pkg/ascimage` · `cmd/ascimage`

**immunity window** (also: damage immunity, kinetic immunity, `DamageImmunitySpent`, `RemainingKineticImmunity`) — A target's timed windows against repeated hits, kept on `CombatComponent`. Within the damage window (`RemainingDamageImmunity`) each cursor has one allowance per attack family; the kinetic window (`RemainingKineticImmunity`), opened by an accepted knockback or a soft-collision push, covers every attacker: while it runs only a knockback stronger than the one that opened it lands, resetting it, and it marks the target as displaced, suppressing soft-collision impulses and usually homing; `KineticStrength` records the opening impulse, zero for a window a species or a soft push opened, which refuses every knockback. [Combat §5](combat.md#5-resolution-statuses-and-what-crosses) · [AI and physics §5](ai-physics-and-evolution.md#5-physics-primitives) · `internal/component` `CombatComponent`

**impulse** (also: `ApplyImpulse`, `ImpulseMode`) — An immediate change to a kinetic body's velocity, either added to it (`ImpulseAdditive`) or replacing it (`ImpulseOverride`); it is kept distinct from a force, which acts in proportion to `dt`, and from a constraint. See also **force**, **immunity window**, **mass**. [Generic kinetic: Impulses, mass, immunity, and stun](generic-kinetic.md#impulses-mass-immunity-and-stun) · [AI and physics §5](ai-physics-and-evolution.md#5-physics-primitives) · `pkg/vmath/physics` `ApplyImpulse`, `ImpulseMode`

**Inbound** (also: `link.Inbound`) — One transport notification (connect, disconnect or message) that `SocketPort` appends to its bounded receive channel; `NetworkSystem` drains them at tick open, and a full channel counts a drop rather than blocking the socket. See also **poll boundary**, **ingress**. [Services and networking §9](services-and-networking.md#9-poll-boundary-and-receive-lead) · `pkg/network` `Inbound`, `InboundKind`

**index** — two senses.
- *Multiplayer*: The correction index is the manifest a correction leads with: a hash root and the partition, which a receiver whose root differs answers with the page hashes of every section, so only differing pages are sent. Indexing a capture is building that manifest. See also **manifest**, **page**, **section**. [Netcode design §2](netcode-design.md#2-the-correction-pipeline) · [Services and networking §8](services-and-networking.md#8-wire-frame-and-session-messages) · `internal/snapshot` `Manifest`, `CorrectionManifest`
- *ECS*: The spatial index is the fixed-capacity cell grid `engine.Position` keeps in step with every position write, so movement cannot update one without the other. See also **spatial grid**. [ECS §3](ecs-and-events.md#3-position-and-spatial-queries) · [ECS §2](ecs-and-events.md#2-entities-and-typed-stores) · `internal/engine` `Position` · `pkg/position` `Index`

**ingress** (also: ingress budget) — two senses. See also **Inbound**.
- *Raw artifacts*: The link on which the authority accepts a participant's raw artifacts: the participant's own link, else its holder's for a bot, else (a person behind a relay) whichever link carried them. [Domain model §4.2](domain-design.md#42-mesh-relay) · [Multiplayer §3.5](multi-player.md#35-command-frames-the-commit-and-pacing) · `internal/system` `NetworkSystem.ingress`
- *Budget*: The ingress budget evicts a link, with the bots it holds, once what it sends over `-slow-window` passes `NetworkIngressBytesPerSecond` for itself, each bot it holds and each person the authority has no link to. [Multiplayer §3.5](multi-player.md#35-command-frames-the-commit-and-pacing) · `internal/converge` `Authority.driveIngress` · `internal/parameter` `NetworkIngressBytesPerSecond`

**Init** — two senses.
- *ECS*: `System.Init` restores a system's state and telemetry to its starting values; `World.InitSystems` runs it in dependency order once at startup and again on a game reset, and a test constructing a system standalone calls it. [Telemetry reference: System telemetry](telemetry-reference.md#system-telemetry) · `internal/engine` `System`
- *Services*: `Service.Init` acquires and configures a service's resources without starting goroutines; `Start` follows once the whole application is assembled. See also **lifecycle**. [Services and networking §1](services-and-networking.md#1-service-lifecycle-contract) · `internal/service` `Service`

**input hook** (also: press, `EventInputPressed`, `EventInputReleased`, `PushInputPress`) — A scenario's one route for player input: a typed captured key, a left or right press, a finger or a wheel notch on that key's button, or a bot or script `press` action crosses as `EventInputPressed {slot, key, source, tick}` (the presser's roster slot; source 0 a key, 1 and 2 the left and right buttons, 3 and 4 the wheel, 5 a finger; the `engine.ticks` it was made at) so the Shared FSM sees it on every instance, with a local echo that lights and sounds the button at once. Every source counts only in Normal, Visual or Insert and never from macro playback, and every press crosses one `EventInputReleased`, its own release or a `lost` one. See also **capture**, **key**, **echo**, **hold**, **key report**, **input horizon**. [Input §1](input-and-modes.md#1-input-pipeline) · `internal/engine` `World.PushInputPress`

**input horizon** (also: `engine.input_horizon`, `NetworkApplyHorizonTicks`) — How many ticks after the one it was made at an input can still reach the Shared FSM: none solo, once the run is shared the longest **playout lead** plus the late commit (40 ticks). The scheduler publishes it before each tick and machine entry, so a scenario judging a press by its `tick` waits that long before calling a miss. See also **input hook**, **barrier**. [Input §1](input-and-modes.md#1-input-pipeline) · [FSM reference: PayloadIntCompareVar](fsm-reference.md#payloadintcomparevar) · `internal/engine` `Scheduler` · `internal/parameter` `NetworkApplyHorizonTicks`

**Insert mode** (also: `ModeInsert`, `i`) — The typing mode `i` enters: each printable key the scenario has not captured becomes a local `EventCharacterTyped` at the cursor for the typing rules to judge, arrow, Home, End and Page keys still move, Tab, Shift-Tab and Enter keep their Normal actions, Delete removes the current glyph, and Space and Backspace delete forward and back while moving. Simulation continues, and Escape returns to Normal. See also **typing**, **mode**. [Input §2](input-and-modes.md#2-modes) · [Input §5](input-and-modes.md#5-insert-search-and-command-text-editing) · `internal/core` `ModeInsert` · `internal/mode` `Router`

**install** (also: `InstallSharedWorld`, `ReconcileSharedWorld`, `make install`, `make install-config`) — three senses. See also **capture**, **projection**, **staging world**.
- *Multiplayer*: Writing a received capture into the live world: `InstallSharedWorld` replaces the Shared half wholesale for a join, `ReconcileSharedWorld` writes only what differs for a correction, and a staged install's commit compares the staging world's stores against the live ones in place and writes the same (`ReconcileSharedFrom`). Player entities are untouched, the receiver's own owner-authored values survive, and a capture behind the clock is projected forward first, so the clock never moves backward. [Multiplayer §3.3](multi-player.md#33-projection) · [Multiplayer §3.2](multi-player.md#32-the-capture-boundary) · `internal/engine` `World.InstallSharedWorld`, `World.ReconcileSharedWorld`
- *FSM*: Installing a captured machine places each region's named state, dwell and pause flag, the variables and the delayed actions without running ordinary entry actions; `Machine.Import` is the staging form and `ImportReconciled` the live one. [FSM: Installing a captured machine](fsm-and-configuration.md#installing-a-captured-machine) · `internal/fsm` `Machine.Import`, `Machine.ImportReconciled`
- *Build and deploy*: `make install` puts the binary, licence, manual, desktop entry and icons, completion and docs under `PREFIX` and the wad, bot graphs, keymap and `vif.toml` under `$(SYSCONFDIR)/vif`; `make install-config` copies the same tree into the user config root (`VIF_CONFIG_DIR`); both write the keymap and `vif.toml` only when absent. [Filesystem layout §4](filesystem-layout.md#4-installation) · [Development §2](development.md#2-common-build-workflow) · `Makefile`

**instance** — four senses.
- *Multiplayer*: One running copy of the simulation, an `app.App` with its own world, under one participant identity. A process can hold several: each bot seat is its own headless instance, and a received capture resolves in a staging-world `App`. [Runtime: Bot seats](runtime.md#bot-seats) · [Multiplayer §1](multi-player.md#1-vocabulary-and-boundaries) · `internal/app` `App`
- *Diagnostics*: `fields.instance` names the runtime a log record came from when a process runs more than one: `seat N` for a bot seat, `staging` for the staging world. [Logging §2](logging-and-diagnostics.md#2-record-shape) · `internal/vlog` `Log`
- *Code*: `converge.Instance` is the run the convergence protocol authors or follows; `bot.Instance` is the participant surface a bot driver plays. `internal/converge` `Instance` · `internal/bot` `Instance`
- *Behavior machines*: `machine.Instance[S]`, a state byte plus relative `Elapsed` and per-entry `Duration`, plain integers that capture, compare and install like any component field. [Behavior machines §2.1](machine-design.md#21-instance) · `pkg/machine` `Instance`

**integer units** (also: `IntUnit`, `FormatInt`) — The convention that an integer status metric's key suffix carries its unit: `.timer`, `.duration`, `.elapsed`, `.remaining` and `_ns` are nanoseconds, `_us` microseconds, `_ms` milliseconds, anything else a count. Displays resolve values through `FormatInt`; the log stores the raw integer. [Logging: Integer units](logging-and-diagnostics.md#integer-units) · `internal/status` `IntUnit`, `FormatInt`

**IntegrateWithBounce** (also: `IntegrateWithBounceStats`, swept bounce) — The semi-implicit integrator for bodies that rebound from walls: it applies acceleration once, then moves one axis at a time in substeps of about 0.45 cells (at most 20), restoring the axis and reflecting velocity scaled by the given restitution on a wall or map bound, the bounds a body's first and last cells, both inclusive. [Generic kinetic §2](generic-kinetic.md#2-current-representation-and-units) · [AI and physics §5](ai-physics-and-evolution.md#5-physics-primitives) · `pkg/vmath/physics` `IntegrateWithBounce`, `IntegrateWithBounceStats`

**integrity** — two senses.
- *Multiplayer*: A capture's integrity hash, sealed into its header over the header, parts and store rows, proves a whole body or a delta as it is resolved and a journal's written world on replay; a selective repair is proved by the manifest root instead. Two equal worlds can differ in integrity when their dense-store order differs. [Domain model D-23](domain-design.md#d-23--the-host-world-is-the-correction) · [Logging: Written worlds and notes](logging-and-diagnostics.md#written-worlds-and-notes) · `internal/snapshot` `Encoder.Seal`, `Encoder.Verify`
- *ECS*: A composite integrity breach is the event `CompositeSystem` raises when a composite loses a member it did not destroy, so the owning species system reacts instead of keeping broken backlinks. [ECS §6](ecs-and-events.md#6-composite-entities) · `internal/event` `EventCompositeIntegrityBreach`

**intent** (also: `input.Intent`, `IntentType`) — A plain data value for one parsed action (type, motion, operator, count, character, pointer cell, command text, macro flag) that the input parser emits and `mode.Router` applies under the world lock. Scripts, bots and macros inject the same intents a terminal produces. [Input §1](input-and-modes.md#1-input-pipeline) · [Architecture §7](architecture.md#7-input-as-a-semantic-boundary) · `internal/input` `Intent`, `IntentType`

**interest** (also: interest cell, `Sample.Interest`) — The cursor cell a participant reports in its link echo's `LinkReport`. The authority uses it only to score how much a correction moves near that participant, which decides when to send; it never enters the simulation. See also **relevance**, **link**. [Services and networking §8](services-and-networking.md#8-wire-frame-and-session-messages) · [Domain model D-24](domain-design.md#d-24--cadence-adapts-the-convergence-floor-does-not) · `pkg/network` `LinkReport` · `pkg/linkpace` `Sample.Interest` · `internal/converge` (relevance.go)

**internal transition** (also: `internal = true`) — A targetless FSM transition that consumes its trigger and runs its actions without leaving the state or resetting `TimeInState`. Declared on a composite state, it fires while any descendant is active. [FSM reference: Internal Transitions](fsm-reference.md#internal-transitions) · `internal/fsm`

**invocation** (also: macro invocation) — In a TOML document, any table with a `macro` key: the loader replaces it with that macro's body, binding its other keys as typed arguments, splicing an array result into the array it stands in, and expanding once per element of the zipped arrays `each` names. See also **macro**, **TOML macro**. [TOML macros §4](toml-macros.md#4-invocations) · `toml/macro` `ExpandDocument`

## J

**jagged cave** (also: `octaves`, `Config.Octaves`, `reach`, `Config.Reach`) — A **cave** whose walls are fractal: a band of the widest gap, `max_gap` or all the walls leave, snakes as a channel does, and the ceiling and floor each reach into it by `octaves` of value noise from the wavelength halving down, stretched and squared so a wall keeps near its side and now and then reaches far in, at most `reach` rows when that is set; walls reaching in together share what the min gap leaves. Each wall's edge is the median of the five columns around it, so no bump or notch of two columns or fewer stands, and both walls still leave the min gap. [Gameplay §9](gameplay.md#9-walls-maps-navigation-and-environment) · `pkg/cave` `Config.jagged`, `Config.fractal`

**Job** (also: session Job) — The Kubernetes Job the router creates for each session, with a config-check init container, no retries (`backoffLimit` 0) and a one-hour `activeDeadlineSeconds`, in the shape of the `30-session.yaml` template. See also **vif-router**, **manifest**. [Fleet deploy §1](kube-docker-deploy.md#1-the-shape) · [Fleet deploy §9](kube-docker-deploy.md#9-the-fleet-objects-and-the-shared-volumes) · `tool/vif-router` `buildJob`

**join** (also: `-join`, `:join`, `SessionOffer`, `JoinerReport`, join lag) — three senses. See also **offer**, **mid-run join**, **join refusal**, **anchor**, **identity**, **guest**.
