- *Networking*: The handshake by which a dialer becomes a participant: the coordinator's **preview**, a scenario transfer when the joiner lacks the scenario, the joiner's seat within its **ready period**, the `SessionOffer` assigning its place, the joiner's reply reporting its identity, terminal geometry and declared address (`JoinerReport`), which the coordinator verifies, then the start gate and the capture that follows it. [Services and networking §7.2](services-and-networking.md#72-join-identity-and-who-decides-it) · [Domain model §4.5](domain-design.md#45-join-and-stream-framing) · `internal/network` `SessionOffer`, `JoinerReport`
- *Input*: `-join <addr>` starts a run as a guest; `:join <target>` replaces a running solo game with the session at the target once the host answers; either is seated at its player's press ("Click or press a key to join"), unless the wait is off. [Runtime §1.0](runtime.md#10-the-four-session-flags) · [Input §9](input-and-modes.md#9-command-mode) · `internal/mode` (commands.go)
- *Telemetry*: Join lag, `network.join_lag_ticks`, is how many ticks a joiner still stood behind the session once its catch-up closed; above `NetworkJoinLagTicks` the join is refused. [Telemetry reference: Network](telemetry-reference.md#network) · `internal/system` `NetworkSystem.ReportJoinLag`

**join refusal** (also: `network.Refusal`, `RefusalCode`, `RetryOf`, `ErrSessionStarting`, `ErrSessionEnding`, `ErrSessionHandoff`) — A host's typed answer to a dial it will not admit, written back as a `MsgJoinReply` carrying its kind and reason rather than by closing the stream. One table indexed by kind gives each kind's retry and the advice the player reads after the reason: starting (between the lobby and the mid-run gate), handoff (an election is open), behind, yielding (a full session's longest-waiting **Wraith** is giving up its place) returning (a **claim**'s place is still linked or coming back on another dial) and unpressed (a seat asked for past its **ready period**, which a press dials again for) pass in a moment, and are all a seat or a first join retries; full and the join budget may pass later; ending (draining or expired), another build or session, and an unknown session never do, and a rejoin stops on them. `cmd/vif` exits 76 on any refusal, and the browser shows its reason as it is. [Runtime: The allocated session](runtime.md#the-allocated-session) · [Services and networking §8](services-and-networking.md#8-wire-frame-and-session-messages) · `internal/network` `Refusal`, `RefusalCode`, `RetryOf` · `internal/app` `ErrSessionStarting`, `ErrSessionEnding`, `ErrSessionHandoff`

**journal** (also: replay journal, `-j`, `:journal`, `vif-jrn-*.jsonl`) — The replay journal: a JSONL stream of anchors, journaled event records, written worlds, digests and an end record, each stamped on the position lattice, from which a run is replayed or verified. `-j` records one from the run's start; `:journal start [slot]` opens one where the run stands, this instance's own or a bot seat's, and `:journal stop [slot]` closes it, any number of times. See also **anchor**, **opening**, **written world**, **jseq**, **note**, **position lattice**. [Logging §9](logging-and-diagnostics.md#9-replay-journal) · [Logging: Opening mid-run](logging-and-diagnostics.md#opening-mid-run) · [Logging: Record and anchor shapes](logging-and-diagnostics.md#record-and-anchor-shapes) · `internal/journal` `Recorder` · `internal/event` `JournalRecord`, `JournalSchema`

**jseq** (also: `cseq`, `jrun`, `jtick`) — Journal record counters: `jseq` numbers records densely, so a gap means exactly one lost record; `cseq` orders written worlds that share a `jseq`; `jrun`, `jtick` and `boundary` give a record's place on the position lattice. [Logging: Record and anchor shapes](logging-and-diagnostics.md#record-and-anchor-shapes) · [Logging: Written worlds and notes](logging-and-diagnostics.md#written-worlds-and-notes) · `internal/event` `JournalRecord.JSeq`, `JournalCapture.CSeq`

**jump** — two senses.
- *Multiplayer*: A correction further ahead of the clock than `NetworkHoldTicks`, adopted at its own tick instead of waiting, superseding any held one and counted in `snapshot.corrections_jumped`. See also **hold**, **playout buffer**. [Netcode design §2.1](netcode-design.md#21-the-playout-buffer) · `internal/converge` `Corrections`
- *Gameplay*: Moving the cursor at once: `Tab` to its nugget (1% energy), `Shift-Tab` to the first remaining gold member (10%), both as Spend-class energy deltas, and `g` + direction + color to a glyph of that color. [Gameplay §6](gameplay.md#6-collectibles) · [Input: Prefixes, editing, and modes](input-and-modes.md#prefixes-editing-and-modes) · `internal/system` `NuggetSystem`, `GoldSystem`

## K

**K3s** — The single-node Kubernetes distribution the session fleet runs on; the router creates each session's Job and Service through its API, and `deploy/k3s` holds the static objects. See also **Job**, **vif-router**. [Fleet deploy §5](kube-docker-deploy.md#5-k3s) · [Fleet design §1](kubernetes-fleet.md#1-what-is-deployed) · `deploy/k3s`

**kernel** (also: dialect kernel) — The shared dialect kernel, one implementation of strict argument decoding, operator parsing and guard trees for `fsm/std`, bot graphs and behavior programs. See also **dialect**. [Behavior machines §5](machine-design.md#5-dialect-kernel) · `internal/fsm/dialect`

**key** — four senses. See also **keymap**, **capture**.
- *Telemetry*: A status key is a dotted metric name; `SplitKey` maps it to a group, which becomes a status record's `msg`, and per-slot metrics live under `player.<n>.*`. See also **integer units**. [Logging: Key convention](logging-and-diagnostics.md#key-convention) · [Telemetry reference: Key families](telemetry-reference.md#key-families) · `internal/status` `SplitKey`, `PlayerKey`
- *Input*: A captured key is a printable key a scenario takes away from the play grammar with `EventKeyCaptureRequest`; pressing it in Normal, Visual or Insert becomes `IntentKeyPress` and crosses as `EventInputPressed`. See also **input hook**. [Input §1](input-and-modes.md#1-input-pipeline) · `internal/engine` `GameContext.KeyCapture`
- *Rendering*: The key cell is the fixed `StatusKeyWidth` cells after the status bar's mode label, showing a pending key sequence or else the last one completed. [Rendering §9](rendering.md#9-ui-and-debug-projections) · [Input §3](input-and-modes.md#3-normal-mode-grammar) · `internal/parameter` `StatusKeyWidth`
- *Diagnostics*: A log record's follow key is its second string field by convention (`region` on FSM records, `ev` on dispatch records), which a viewer's follow-value navigation walks. [Logging §2](logging-and-diagnostics.md#2-record-shape)

**key report** (also: `EventInputReport`, `SetKeyReport`, key releases, host key report) — Whether a slot's keyboard reports releases or presses alone. The router asks the presentation for releases from a terminal's key event protocol or a graphical host's own key events (the desktop window, the page, Android: `hostkey.Report`) for the whole run, and from a Linux or FreeBSD console's keyboard while a capture asks with `releases = true`; a bot's instance answers releases itself (`App.ReportReleases`), as the bot lets go of each key it presses. While keys are captured each slot crosses `EventInputReport {slot, releases}`. See also **hold**, **tap**. [Input §1](input-and-modes.md#1-input-pipeline) · [Multi-platform §8](multi-platform.md#8-adding-another-renderer-or-android-host) · `internal/mode` `Router.syncKeyReport` · `pkg/hostkey` `Report` · `github.com/lixenwraith/terminal` `SetKeyReport`

**key family** (also: `killed_by_player`, `killed_by_lifecycle`, `cursor_rejects`, `disabled_rejects`, `protected_rejects`) — A pattern of status keys several systems publish under their own `<d>.` prefix, such as lifecycle counts (`spawned`, `despawned`, `killed_by_player`, `killed_by_lifecycle`, `spawn_failures`) from `newLifecycleTelemetry`, where a kill is the player's when its killer resolves to a roster cursor; bounce counts (`wall_collisions`, `boundary_reflections`, `physics_steps`) from `newBounceTelemetry`; dropped requests (`cursor_rejects`, `disabled_rejects`) from `newRejectionTelemetry`; protection-mask refusals (`protected_rejects`, `protected_player_rejects`); and buffer peaks (`buf_<name>_hwm`) from `newBufferTelemetry`. See also **key**, **high-water mark**. [Telemetry reference: Key families](telemetry-reference.md#key-families) · `internal/system` `newLifecycleTelemetry`, `newBounceTelemetry`, `newRejectionTelemetry`, `newBufferTelemetry`

**keyframe** (also: `CorrectionKeyframe`, whole world) — A self-sufficient whole capture, sent as sections whose large stores go as the deflate the encoder kept while their rows held (see **deflate**), to a peer every keyframe interval, on request, or in place of a repair wider than itself. The convergence floor bounds the ticks between two, and one is skipped while every peer has proved the authority's world within half the floor. See also **cadence**, **convergence floor**, **correction**. [Netcode design §2](netcode-design.md#2-the-correction-pipeline) · [Domain model D-24](domain-design.md#d-24--cadence-adapts-the-convergence-floor-does-not) · `internal/converge` `Corrections.KeyframeAt` · `internal/snapshot` `CorrectionKeyframe`, `Encoder.EncodeCapture`

**keymap** (also: `KeyTable`, `keymap.toml`, `-k`, key bindings) — The binding of keys to canonical action names, by section (`[normal]`, `[normal_keys]`, `[operator]`, `[prefix_g]`, `[overlay]`, `[overlay_keys]`, `[text_keys]`): the embedded `keymap.toml` with a sparse override merged over it, where `"none"` unbinds a default. A key names one action in a section and an action can have several keys (`ctrl_g` and `f10` both name `config_menu`); help lists them from the merged table. [Input §10](input-and-modes.md#10-keymap-override-files) · [Input §4](input-and-modes.md#4-default-bindings) · `internal/input` `KeyTable`, `KeyTable.Bindings`


**kill** (also: kill credit, `kills.*`, `LastDamagedBy`) — three senses. See also **confirmed kill**, **prediction ledger**.
- *Gameplay*: Kill credit is the last cursor to damage a target (`CombatComponent.LastDamagedBy`), which `EventSpeciesKilled` carries; boost and loot reward a settled kill at once and a predicted one only once confirmed. [Gameplay: Boost](gameplay.md#boost) · `internal/component` `CombatComponent.LastDamagedBy`
- *Telemetry*: Kill tallies, `kills.<species>`, `kills.total` and `kills.uncredited`, are `MetaSystem`'s game totals, carried as declared Shared state and cleared by a reset; scenarios read them with `StatusToVar` to count since an escalation. [Telemetry reference: Player, session and progression](telemetry-reference.md#player-session-and-progression) · [Gameplay §10](gameplay.md#10-embedded-campaign-progression) · `internal/system` `MetaSystem`
- *Multiplayer*: A predicted kill is a Shared death a guest derived ahead of the authority, stamped `PhasePredicted` and held until an authoritative world proves it and it is raised once as `EventSpeciesKillConfirmed`. [Multiplayer §3.4](multi-player.md#34-predicted-derivations) · `internal/event` `EventSpeciesKillConfirmed`

**kinetic** (also: `Kinetic`, `KineticComponent`) — A float64 point mass with sub-cell position (cells), velocity (cells/s) and acceleration (cells/s²), embedded in `KineticComponent` and projected onto the integer grid with `PointAt`. It records no mass, footprint, wall mask or whether its state is authoritative. See also **impulse**, **mass**. [Generic kinetic §2](generic-kinetic.md#2-current-representation-and-units) · [AI and physics §6](ai-physics-and-evolution.md#6-numeric-model-and-determinism) · `pkg/vmath/physics` `Kinetic` · `internal/component` `KineticComponent`

**KineticSystem** (also: `kinetic.go` pseudo-system) — Future design: one registered system integrating every kinetic body, unsafe while each species system owns steps on both sides of integration; the feasible form is an unregistered `internal/system/kinetic.go` pseudo-system of motion adapters that their callers schedule. [Generic kinetic §7](generic-kinetic.md#7-why-a-registered-kineticsystem-is-not-yet-safe) · [Generic kinetic §8](generic-kinetic.md#8-feasible-pseudo-system-design)

**knockback** — The impulse an accepted hit gives its target's `Kinetic` through the collision profile its attack family names for that defender; only the tracer, shield and explosion families carry one, and a pylon or a Kraken takes none. A direct hit pushes along the attacker's velocity and an area hit radially from its origin; a hit on a composite member lands offset on the header, except that a direct hit moves a member with a `Kinetic` of its own, such as a snake body segment. It lands while the target's kinetic window is closed, or when it is stronger than the knockback that opened it, never on an enraged target, a data species with a mass of its own weighed by it (`profile.Reweigh`), and it opens or resets that window against every attacker; on a Shared target a crossing seeds the spread from its `CrossingID`, so every instance pushes the same way. See also **immunity window**, **attack profile**, **CrossingID**. [Combat §5](combat.md#5-resolution-statuses-and-what-crosses) · [D-3](domain-design.md#d-3--cross-the-smallest-shared-outcome) · `internal/system` `CombatSystem` · `internal/profile` `collisionSet`

**Kraken** — two senses.
- *Gameplay*: An eight-tentacle Shared boss whose footprint covers hundreds of cells; it waits, lashes, or spins and charges the farthest cursor, demolishing walls and striking the cursors it touches. It rises with `KrakenInitialHP` hit points or the spawn request's `hp`, which its health bar spans. [Gameplay §8](gameplay.md#8-hostile-and-structural-actors) · `internal/system` `KrakenSystem` · `internal/component` `KrakenComponent`
- *FSM*: The campaign's `kraken` region, which spawns a Kraken on a full-map maze after three storm kills, and vanguard's `boss` region, which raises one at each **boss stage**. [Gameplay §10](gameplay.md#10-embedded-campaign-progression) · `internal/asset/scenario/kraken.toml` · `wad/scenario/vanguard/scenario.toml`

**Kraken shape** (also: KrakenShapeResource, KrakenSample, tentacle samples) — The discs along the Kraken's eight tentacles, one every `KrakenSampleStep` from root to tip and sparser near the root (`KrakenSampleStrides`), which both its footprint and its shading stamp. The Kraken system builds them once a tick into the `KrakenShape` resource with the pose they came from, and the kraken renderer reuses them for the same pose; derived state, so snapshots never carry it. [ECS §5](ecs-and-events.md#5-singleton-resources) · [Rendering §7](rendering.md#7-layer-inventory) · `internal/component` `KrakenComponent.AppendTentacleSamples` · `internal/engine` `KrakenShapeResource`

## L

**landing marker** (also: `Landing`, `LandingRenderer`) — One blinking cell, white, red and pink, at the centre of the footprint a charge or a teleport lands on: the swarm's from its lock through its charge or teleport, a data species' while its state is flagged `Landing`. The lines show an attack's path; the marker shows where to keep clear. [Rendering §7](rendering.md#7-layer-inventory) · [Behavior machines §6.5](machine-design.md#65-a-programs-look) · `internal/render/renderer` `LandingRenderer`

**lane** — A firing line. The gun's lanes are parallel rows or columns one apart, set per direction by its charges: one fires the centre lane, two the side lanes, three (its cap) all three; on an **upgrade path** each level sets them per cardinal instead. A ray mount's `Lane` instead fixes its ray along one of eight octant directions from its host, so the mount cycles as a level obstacle whether or not a cursor is in range. [Combat §4](combat.md#4-at-the-charge-cap) · [Combat §3](combat.md#3-drivers) · `internal/system` `WeaponSystem`, `MountSystem`

**latch** — three senses. See also **D-14**, **defeat**.
- *Session*: The session latch, `World.LatchSession`, moves the session state the simulation reads (`engine.SessionState`: live, participant, authority, seats-only, peer count) only between ticks, so no predicted phase shifts inside a tick; each change is journaled as a note. [Multiplayer §3.4](multi-player.md#34-predicted-derivations) · [Logging: Written worlds and notes](logging-and-diagnostics.md#written-worlds-and-notes) · `internal/engine` `World.LatchSession`
- *Map*: The map latch is a session's Shared map bounds with `CropOnResize`, installed from the join anchor before the FSM boots; while `World.SessionShared()` is true a terminal resize changes only the local viewport and camera. [Domain model D-14](domain-design.md#d-14--map-bounds-are-shared-authority) · `internal/engine` `World.SessionShared`
- *Gameplay*: The defeat latch is `MetaSystem`'s per-slot record of cursor defeat from the barrier-bound `EventCursorDefeatState`, folded into `session.any_defeated` and `session.all_defeated`; a level setup that clears entities without `preserve_loot` clears it. [Multiplayer §3.1](multi-player.md#31-ordinary-and-barrier-bound-crossings) · `internal/system` `MetaSystem`

**late** — three senses. See also **commit**, **void**, **playout lead**, **mid-run join**.
- *Multiplayer*: A late crossing reaches the authority after its stamped apply tick; the authority commits it at its own next tick, or as void past `NetworkCommitLateTicks`, while its producer keeps the stamped tick. [Multiplayer §3.5](multi-player.md#35-command-frames-the-commit-and-pacing) · [Fleet design §5](kubernetes-fleet.md#5-late-crossing-ordering) · `internal/parameter` `NetworkCommitLateTicks`
- *Multiplayer*: The slow policy evicts a participant whose own epochs reached the authority late at least `-slow-late` times a second of game time over `-slow-window` while its link carried at least `-slow-bytes` a second. [Multiplayer §3.5](multi-player.md#35-command-frames-the-commit-and-pacing) · `internal/converge` `SlowPolicy`, `Authority.driveEviction`
- *Telemetry*: `stat.late` counts status keys first asked for after `Registry.Freeze`; each gets a detached cell no snapshot sees, so a non-zero value is a regression. [Logging: Freeze](logging-and-diagnostics.md#freeze) · [Telemetry reference: The registry](telemetry-reference.md#the-registry) · `internal/status` `Registry.Freeze`

**launch arguments** (also: `VIF_ARGS`, `window.VIF_ARGS`, `arg` query) — The browser build's argument vector: `window.VIF_ARGS`, set before the terminal script loads, then repeated `arg` query parameters (at most 64 arguments of at most 1,024 characters) become the WASM program's argv, which can select embedded behavior but never a filesystem path; `data-wasm`, `data-worker` and `data-exec` on the script element name the launcher's files when a deployment publishes them under other names. [Multi-platform §6](multi-platform.md#6-browser-launch-arguments) · [Development: WASM host](development.md#wasm-host) · `web/terminal.js` `launchArguments`

**layer** — four senses.
- *Rendering*: A render layer is a renderer, or a group of them, drawn at its `RenderPriority`; layers stack in ascending priority from background guidance to debug and telemetry. [Rendering §7](rendering.md#7-layer-inventory) · `internal/render` `RenderPriority`
- *Gameplay*: A drift layer is all loose player glyphs of one level, moving as a rigid group at that level's fraction of the drift speed (`DriftLayerFactor`) so text scrolls in parallax; where layers share a cell, the nearest draws on top and typing acts on the nearest typeable glyph, while typeable text draws over **scenery** of any layer. See also **drift**, **parallax**. [Gameplay §9](gameplay.md#9-walls-maps-navigation-and-environment) · `internal/system` `EnvironmentSystem` · `internal/parameter` `DriftLayerFactor`
- *Audio*: A sound layer is one source through one processor chain inside a `SoundDef`, mixed into a bus or the master. [Audio §5](audio.md#5-sounds-and-sound-effects) · `pkg/audio` `Layer`
- *Volume*: a slice of the position index at one Z, a `Point3`'s layer; a query taking a column and a row answers for the first layer, one taking a `Point3` (`BlocksAt`, `BlocksBox`, `EntitiesIn`, `HasLineOfSightBetween`) for any. See also **spatial grid**. [Position §3](position-package.md#3-the-volume) · `pkg/position` `Grid.Depth`, `Index.BlocksAt`

**lead** (also: own lead, `ownLead`) — A participant's **playout lead**: how many ticks after production its crossings are stamped to apply.

**leaf** (also: `-no-advertise`) — two senses.
- *Multiplayer*: A participant that declares no address (`-no-advertise`, a failed bind, `-authority host`, or a join through a relay without `-listen`): it plays normally and is never a candidate. See also **reach**, **succession**. [Multiplayer §5.3](multi-player.md#53-reachability-under--authority-migrate) · [Runtime §1.0](runtime.md#10-the-four-session-flags) · `internal/converge` `Reach`
- *FSM*: The active leaf is the deepest active state of a region; transitions are searched from it toward the root, and `on_exit` runs leaf to root. [FSM §4](fsm-and-configuration.md#4-states-and-hierarchy) · [FSM §6](fsm-and-configuration.md#6-event-processing) · `internal/fsm`

**:leave** — Takes a run out of its session into one of its own, the world as it stands, as the same participant under the next term: its bots leave first, their places kept as Wraiths in the session left and in the one it opens, there under claims of their own, and are seated again to take them back, what the left session sent or still had scheduled is dropped, those left behind are removed locally, and the old port closes outside the world lock, a dial at it meanwhile refused as the session ending. Those left behind see an ordinary loss. A person's **fork** takes the same path, keeping every place. A solo run, one whose session holds only its own bots, and a server refuse it by its session role. See also **session role**, **departure**. [Multiplayer §5.5](multi-player.md#55-leaving-a-session) · [Input §9](input-and-modes.md#9-command-mode) · `internal/app` `membership.leaveSession` · `internal/converge` `Corrections.Leave` · `internal/system` `NetworkSystem.LeaveSession`

**level** (also: `-lv`, log level) — three senses.
- *Diagnostics*: A log level (`trace`, `debug`, `info`, `warn`, `error`) is the process-wide threshold below which records are dropped, independent of scope; `ERROR` bypasses the scope mask. Set with `-lv` or `:log level`. [Logging §3](logging-and-diagnostics.md#3-levels) · `internal/vlog`
- *Gameplay*: A glyph's level (dark, normal or bright) sets its brightness, its drift layer and its dust lifetime; decay lowers it and blossom raises it. [Gameplay §4](gameplay.md#4-glyph-production-and-decay) · `internal/component` `GlyphLevel`
- *Gameplay*: Level setup, `EventLevelSetup`, is the Shared event that sizes the map (never below the scenario's `[map]` floor), sets `crop_on_resize`, and may clear entities, with `preserve_loot` keeping uncollected rewards. [Gameplay §9](gameplay.md#9-walls-maps-navigation-and-environment) · [FSM §11](fsm-and-configuration.md#11-authoring-pattern) · `internal/engine` `World.SetupLevel`

**lifecycle** (also: lifecycle phase, lifecycle policy, `lifecycle.Phase`) — four senses.
- *Build and deploy*: An allocated session's lifecycle is the `internal/lifecycle` state machine behind `-first-join`, `-empty` and `-drain`, with the phases waiting, occupied, vacant, draining and expired; draining and expired sessions refuse dials. See also **park**. [Runtime: The allocated session](runtime.md#the-allocated-session) · `internal/lifecycle` `Controller`, `Phase`, `Policy`
- *FSM*: Lifecycle hooks are a state's `on_enter`, `on_exit` and `on_update` actions; region lifecycle is spawning, pausing, resuming and terminating a region. See also **on_enter**, **region**. [FSM reference: Lifecycle Hooks](fsm-reference.md#lifecycle-hooks) · [FSM §10](fsm-and-configuration.md#10-region-lifecycle-and-system-policy) · `internal/fsm`
- *FSM*: Reconciled local lifecycle replays only `reconcile = true` `ClassLocal` entry and exit events for the state paths a live install crosses. See also **reconcile**. [FSM reference: Reconciled Local Lifecycle](fsm-reference.md#reconciled-local-lifecycle) · `internal/fsm` `Machine.ImportReconciled`
- *Services*: The service lifecycle is `Init`, resource binding, `Start` and `Stop`, run in dependency order by the service hub. [Services and networking §1](services-and-networking.md#1-service-lifecycle-contract) · `internal/service` `Service`, `Hub`

**lightning** (also: `LightningSystem`, zap) — The rod's delivery and the gun's chained follow-up: a Player-domain bolt to each unique nearest target, whose energy-drain effect rewards the attacker in its current polarity; a mounted rod zaps the aimed cursor. See also **delivery**, **weapon**. [Combat §2](combat.md#2-layers) · [Gameplay §7](gameplay.md#7-weapons-and-attacks) · `internal/system` `LightningSystem`

**link** (also: `LinkReport`, `MsgLinkProbe`, `MsgLinkEcho`) — three senses. See also **peer**, **linkpace**, **interest**, **echo**.
- *Networking*: A stream between this instance and one direct neighbour, addressed by the participant identity on its far end. [Multiplayer §1](multi-player.md#1-vocabulary-and-boundaries) · `pkg/network` `PeerID`
- *Networking*: The link probe is a probe and echo pair (`MsgLinkProbe`, `MsgLinkEcho`) sent on every link to time the round trip and delivery rate and, answered on receipt, to keep a live link inside its read deadline; the echo carries the far end's `LinkReport` (tick, lag, correction magnitude, cursor cell), a scheduling hint that never reaches the game. [Services and networking §8](services-and-networking.md#8-wire-frame-and-session-messages) · `pkg/network` `LinkReport`
- *Build and deploy*: A session link is the address a player joins by, naming the session as well as where to dial: `vif://<host>:<port>/<id>` or `wss://<site>/vif/ws/<id>`, either of which joins from any client, which looks a link it cannot take up on the site that issued it. [Fleet deploy §1](kube-docker-deploy.md#1-the-shape) · [Fleet §9](kubernetes-fleet.md#9-the-session-routes) · [Multiplayer §5.0](multi-player.md#50-who-listens-who-authors-who-can-be-reached) · `pkg/network` `ParseEndpoint` · `internal/network` `PreviewSession`

**link capacity** (also: `CapacityBps`, priced, `SnapshotLinkCapacityTicks`) — The rate a link delivered while it was the limit, which the corrector prices that link's plan on after the link takes its frames again, so a plan that fits stays instead of creeping back to nominal and queueing the link; read anew whenever the link holds the frames, and doubled every `SnapshotLinkCapacityTicks` (5 s) a constrained plan goes without that, so a link that improved is offered more. [Netcode §3.5](netcode-design.md#35-the-divergence-harness) · [Telemetry reference: Snapshot and correction](telemetry-reference.md#snapshot-and-correction) · `internal/converge` `peerPublisher.priced`

**linkpace** (also: `pkg/linkpace`, `Controller`, `Bounds`) — The standard-library package that estimates a link (round trip, jitter, delivery rate, saturation) and plans each peer's correction cadence and keyframe interval within declared `Bounds`, admitting or refusing a link against the convergence floor; nothing it measures enters the simulation. See also **operating point**, **cadence**. [Package map §5](package-map.md#5-reusable-pkg-libraries) · [Domain model D-24](domain-design.md#d-24--cadence-adapts-the-convergence-floor-does-not) · `pkg/linkpace` `Controller`, `Plan`, `Admit`

**listen** (also: `-listen`, listener) — `-listen <addr>` names the port a guest binds and declares in a migrate session; the default is the host's own port, falling back to an OS-assigned one when it is taken. A host listens on its `-host`, `-serve` or `:host` address. See also **reach**, **leaf**. [Runtime §1.0](runtime.md#10-the-four-session-flags) · [Multiplayer §5.3](multi-player.md#53-reachability-under--authority-migrate) · `internal/converge` `Reach`

**live and ready** (also: `Live`, `Ready`, readiness) — The two answers of a dedicated host's `/health`: `live` (the run should keep running) is the only thing the HTTP code carries, and `ready` (a dial would be admitted now) is a body field the router reads. See also **probe**. [Services and networking §12](services-and-networking.md#12-the-supervised-runs-endpoint) · `internal/probe` `Snapshot`

**loadout** — A cursor's held weapon kinds with their charges and cooldowns, owned by `WeaponSystem`; main fire fires every ready kind, and an energy zero crossing clears all but the gun. See also **weapon**, **main fire**, **orb**. [Combat §3](combat.md#3-drivers) · [Gameplay §7](gameplay.md#7-weapons-and-attacks) · `internal/system` `WeaponSystem.fireLoadout` · `internal/component` `WeaponComponent`

**lobby** (also: startup lobby) — A host's wait before tick zero, kept only where no person plays at the process: it admits guests until its quorum (the first guest or the scenario's `min_players`, or the whole `-players` party for a scripted or bot-driven host), then the start gate releases everyone; a dedicated host without `-size` also takes its first person's geometry, or a bot's 120x40 when it seats bots of its own. See also **gate**, **mid-run join**. [Runtime §1.2](runtime.md#12-the-dedicated-host) · [Runtime §1.0](runtime.md#10-the-four-session-flags) · `internal/app` `membership.lobbyQuorum`, `membership.adoptLobbyGeometry`

**local** — four senses.
- *Multiplayer*: `ClassLocal` is the event class that is never replicated; a Local event cannot steer a Shared FSM region. See also **event class**. [Domain model D-10](domain-design.md#d-10--event-class-and-event-domain-answer-different-questions) · [Domain model D-20](domain-design.md#d-20--shared-fsm-regions-move-on-the-authoritys-transitions) · `internal/event` `ClassLocal`
- *Gameplay*: The local cursor is the roster cursor this instance drives: it receives input, anchors the camera and UI, and has its own renderer apart from peer cursors. In a session it is the one its identity owns, and a joiner has none until its arrival or an install brings it. See also **cursor**. [Rendering: Local and peer cursors](rendering.md#local-and-peer-cursors) · [Gameplay §2](gameplay.md#2-cursor-and-player-state) · `internal/engine` `PlayerResource.LocalSlot`, `World.LocalCursor`
- *Multiplayer*: `ControlLocal` marks a cursor driven through this instance's router and `ControlRemote` a peer's; control is re-derived from `PeerID` and hashed as zero. See also **control kind**. [Domain model D-13](domain-design.md#d-13--owner-authored-shared-values-have-one-writer) · `internal/component` `ControlKind`
- *Diagnostics*: In a log record describing both ends of a link, `local` names this end and `peer` the far one. [Logging: Naming a peer, counting participants](logging-and-diagnostics.md#naming-a-peer-counting-participants)

**LocalStateSaver** (also: SaveLocal, LoadLocal, StateCopier) — The interface a system holding state outside the stores implements so a copy of it continues as its source would: player-domain state, pending work and timers, as bytes that a journal's opening and a replay checkpoint carry; the system is taken whole, so a restore skips its `LoadShared`. `StateCopier` is the same for state that does not encode, navigation's caches and the transport, which a checkpoint keeps in memory and a replay re-derives. See also **opening**, **checkpoint**. [Logging: Opening mid-run](logging-and-diagnostics.md#opening-mid-run) · `internal/engine` `LocalStateSaver` `StateCopier`

**log** (also: `-l`, `:log`, session log) — The structured session log: one JSON record per line with `time`, `level`, `sub`, `run`, `tick` and `fields`, enabled by `-l` or any flag that implies it (`-lv`, `-ls`, `-lt`, `-lr`, `-log-session-id`, `-log-stdout`) and controlled at run time by `:log`. See also **level**, **scope**, **msg**, **vlog**. [Logging §2](logging-and-diagnostics.md#2-record-shape) · [Logging: Startup flags](logging-and-diagnostics.md#startup-flags) · `internal/vlog`

**log tmpfs** (also: `/var/log/vif-fleet`) — The node's 256 MiB RAM-backed mount that fleet sessions write `<session-id>.jsonl` to through a node-affine volume; a cleanup timer keeps each session's two newest rotations. See also **LogWisp**. [Fleet deploy §7](kube-docker-deploy.md#7-the-nodes-log-tmpfs) · `deploy/guest/vif-fleet-log-cleanup.py`

**LogWisp** — The node-local service that reads the fleet's `/var/log/vif-fleet/*.jsonl` session logs and serves them as Server-Sent Events on loopback; the router reverse-proxies those bytes at `/vif/api/logs` without parsing them. See also **log tmpfs**. [Fleet deploy §10](kube-docker-deploy.md#10-logwisp-the-node-log-reader) · [Fleet design §1](kubernetes-fleet.md#1-what-is-deployed) · `deploy/logwisp/aggregator.toml` · `tool/vif-router` (log_proxy.go)

**logo** (also: lixen mark, `logoCells`) — The lixen mark of `lixen-logo.svg`, a square ringed and crossed straight and diagonally by black strokes into eight colored wedges, which the about window and the start screen draw from its shape at any size rather than from a bitmap: in truecolor each cell takes the quadrant glyph and two colors that best fit its four anti-aliased quadrants; on a text console, whose fonts have no quadrants, a whole block in the console entry most of the cell shows, with gray strokes so the frame reads on the console's black. [Rendering §9](rendering.md#9-ui-and-debug-projections) · `internal/render/renderer` `logoCells` · `internal/parameter/visual` `RgbLogoWedges`, `LogoWedges256` · `pkg/ascimage` `FitQuadrant`

**look** (also: `component.Look`, `LookCell`, `visual.CompileLook`, `lookPainter`, console pair) — A composite's frames cell by cell: each cell a glyph, an entry of the look's fg and bg palettes and its bold or dim attribute, plus the colour of a glyph without an entry and an optional console pair (`fg256`, `bg256`) that a 256-colour host draws every glyph and background in, showing a hit by reversing the glyph. A data species' prototype authors it as rows of glyphs, hex palette entries and attributes, and the eye's types the same way; `visual.CompileLook` compiles both and `lookPainter` draws both. Every glyph is ASCII or one every text console font carries, since a look has no console frames of its own. See also **prototype**, **data species**. [FSM reference: Behavior Programs](fsm-reference.md#behavior-programs) · [Behavior machines §6.5](machine-design.md#65-a-programs-look) · `internal/component` `Look` · `internal/parameter/visual` `CompileLook`, `EyeTypeVisuals` · `internal/render/renderer` `lookPainter`

**loot** (also: `LootSystem`, drop) — Player-domain pickups, each of a **loot kind**: weapon charges, heat and energy, rolled for each cursor this instance simulates when a kill settles (a predicted one only once confirmed), from its species' **drop table** with pity, or placed by a scenario's event drop; a drop homes on, and is collected only by, the cursor it belongs to. See also **drop tier**, **event drop**, **confirmed kill**. [Gameplay: Species loot](gameplay.md#species-loot) · `internal/system` `LootSystem` · `internal/profile` (loot.go)

**loot kind** (also: `[loot]`, `LootKind`, `LootSet`, `LootType`, `LootMax`) — What a drop is: its look, a rune with its colour and its shield's glow, and its reward, one charge of a weapon or an amount of heat or energy. The code's eight (`LootSpecs`, `visual.LootVisuals`) take loot types 0 to 7, and a scenario's `[loot]` up to 32 more from `LootCount` in sorted-name order, which payloads name as they name the code's; the compiled set rides in `Resources.Loot`, and a drop carries its kind's look. Vanguard's `U` is one. See also **drop table**, **payload names**. [FSM reference: Loot](fsm-reference.md#loot) · `internal/component` `LootKind`, `LootSet`, `LootComponent.Look` · `internal/engine` `Resources.Loot` · `internal/behavior` `compileLoot`

**LUT** (also: lookup table) — `pkg/vmath`'s precomputed tables for sine, cosine and atan2 (1,024 entries) and exponential decay (256 entries), which replace transcendental calls in game math. [AI and physics §6](ai-physics-and-evolution.md#6-numeric-model-and-determinism) · `pkg/vmath` `Sin`, `Cos`, `Atan2`, `ExpDecay`, `LUTSize`

## M

**machine** — three senses. See also **FSM**, **behavior machine**, **MachineState**.
- *FSM*: `fsm.Machine` is the hierarchical state machine with parallel regions, guards, actions, delayed actions and variables that runs a scenario as the world-level director. [FSM §4](fsm-and-configuration.md#4-states-and-hierarchy) · `internal/fsm` `Machine`, `NewMachine`
- *Input*: `input.Machine` is the key parser: it holds only grammar state (count, character, operator and register waits, the `g` prefix, marker color) and emits `Intent` values. See also **intent**. [Input §3](input-and-modes.md#3-normal-mode-grammar) · `internal/input` `Machine`
- *Behavior machines*: a behavior machine is a per-entity runtime for species state, an instance a component carries plus a graph a species system builds in Go or a behavior program compiles to. [Behavior machines §1](machine-design.md#1-overview) · `pkg/machine` `Graph`

**MachineState** (also: `ExportFSM`, `ImportFSM`) — The FSM's capture form: each region's active state by name, time in state and pause flag, the variables, and pending delayed actions by compiled action ID. States travel by name, so a name the build lacks refuses the import. See also **install**. [FSM: Installing a captured machine](fsm-and-configuration.md#installing-a-captured-machine) · `internal/fsm` `MachineState`, `Machine.Export` · `internal/engine` `Scheduler.ExportFSM`, `Scheduler.ImportFSM`

**macro** — two senses. See also **TOML macro**, **invocation**.
- *Input*: A vim-style macro records parsed intents into a register `a`–`z` (`qa` … `q`) and plays them back (`@a`, `3@a`, `@@a` indefinitely, `@@@` every register), one intent per register per playback interval; playback presses no captured key. [Input §6](input-and-modes.md#6-macros) · `internal/mode` `MacroManager`
- *Content*: A TOML macro is a `[macros.<Name>]` table with typed parameters, defaults and a body, expanded wherever an invocation names it with `${name}` references substituted; a scenario entry, its `macro_files` and a bot graph define them. [TOML macros §2](toml-macros.md#2-definitions) · [TOML macros §3](toml-macros.md#3-references) · `toml/macro` `ExpandDocument`

**main fire** (also: `fire_main`, special fire, `fire_special`) — two senses. See also **loadout**, **auto-fire**.
- *Gameplay*: Main fire (`fire_main`, Enter, a left click) fires every owned, ready kind of the cursor's loadout, the innate gun included; it has its own cooldown, which the gun shares, and each other kind keeps its own. [Gameplay §7](gameplay.md#7-weapons-and-attacks) · [Combat §3](combat.md#3-drivers) · `internal/system` `WeaponSystem.fireLoadout`
- *Gameplay*: Special fire (`fire_special`, Backspace, Space or a right press) needs at least 1 heat; it converts dark glyphs into dust and detonates the dust as explosion centres, spending heat from overheat first. [Gameplay §7](gameplay.md#7-weapons-and-attacks) · `internal/system` `ParticleSystem` · `internal/event` `EventFireSpecialRequest`

**manifest** — three senses. See also **fingerprint**, **index**, **page**, **section**, **root**.
- *Architecture*: `internal/manifest` is the hand-written registry of components, systems and renderers (domains, dependencies, snapshot obligations, priorities), from which `make generate` builds stores, builders, the shared-world capture and the event registry; `manifest.Fingerprint` hashes its component and system lists, never the renderers. [Package map §6](package-map.md#6-generated-assembly) · `internal/manifest` `Components`, `Systems`, `Renderers`
- *Multiplayer*: The correction manifest is a versioned hash index over a capture (a root over section hashes, each over pages of row hashes) that leads a correction; an equal root ends the exchange hash-only, and a difference descends to the differing pages. [Netcode design §2](netcode-design.md#2-the-correction-pipeline) · [Services and networking §8](services-and-networking.md#8-wire-frame-and-session-messages) · `internal/snapshot` `CorrectionManifest`, `BuildManifest`
- *Build and deploy*: Kubernetes manifests: the static `deploy/k3s/*.yaml` objects and the per-session Job and Service the router builds in the shape of `30-session.yaml`. See also **Job**. [Fleet deploy §9](kube-docker-deploy.md#9-the-fleet-objects-and-the-shared-volumes) · `tool/vif-router` `buildJob`, `buildService`

**map** (also: map bounds, `MapWidth`, `MapHeight`) — two senses. See also **latch**, **viewport**, **floor**.
- *Simulation*: The logical simulation grid, sized independently of the terminal and never below the scenario's `[map]` floor; in a session its bounds are Shared state latched for every participant. [Domain model D-14](domain-design.md#d-14--map-bounds-are-shared-authority) · [Gameplay §9](gameplay.md#9-walls-maps-navigation-and-environment) · `internal/engine` `ConfigResource.MapWidth`, `ConfigResource.MapHeight`
- *Rendering*: Map coordinates are the authoritative cell space, which `MapToViewport` and `MapToScreen` convert to viewport and screen coordinates. [Rendering §2](rendering.md#2-render-context-and-coordinates) · `internal/render` `RenderContext.MapToViewport`, `RenderContext.MapToScreen`

**marker** (also: `MarkerComponent`, motion marker) — two senses.
- *Rendering*: A marker is a Shared, request-created area indicator (`EventMarkerSpawnRequest`), drawn as a filled rectangle or an inversion by the marker renderer. [Rendering §7](rendering.md#7-layer-inventory) · `internal/component` `MarkerComponent` · `internal/system` `MarkerSystem`
- *Input*: Motion markers are Player-domain inverted markers on the first glyph in each direction from the local cursor; `g` + direction shows colored ones before a color jump. See also **motion**. [Input: Prefixes, editing, and modes](input-and-modes.md#prefixes-editing-and-modes) · [Domain model D-6](domain-design.md#d-6--presentation-and-personal-effects-are-player-domain) · `internal/system` `MotionMarkerSystem`

**mask** — six senses.
- *Rendering*: A semantic write mask tags the cells a renderer draws (`MaskGlyph`, `MaskField`, `MaskUI` and others), so later passes such as grayout, dim and the finalizer can select earlier layers. [Rendering §5](rendering.md#5-semantic-masks-and-post-processing) · `internal/parameter/visual` (mask.go)
- *Gameplay*: Gameplay bit masks: a wall's `WallBlockMask` (what it stops: the cursor, kinetic movers, particles, spawning), an entity's protection mask (`ProtectionFlags`, with `ProtectAll` for every mechanic), and an attack profile's `CombatEffectMask` (energy drain, kinetic, stun). See also **protection**, **wall**. [Gameplay §9](gameplay.md#9-walls-maps-navigation-and-environment) · [Gameplay §2](gameplay.md#2-cursor-and-player-state) · `internal/component` `WallBlockMask`, `ProtectionFlags`, `CombatEffectMask`
- *Diagnostics*: A log scope mask selects which subsystems log, one letter per scope, set with `-ls` or `:log scope`. See also **scope**. [Logging §4](logging-and-diagnostics.md#4-scopes) · [Logging: Recommended masks](logging-and-diagnostics.md#recommended-masks) · `internal/vlog` `ParseScopes`
- *Audio*: The mute mask is the composed sfx and music mute bits that `Ctrl-S` cycles. See also **mute**. [Audio §6](audio.md#6-mixer-buses-mute-and-pause) · `internal/system` `AudioSystem` · `internal/parameter` `AudioMaskCycle`
- *FSM*: A region's trigger mask is the set of event types its active path names, so an event no active state reacts to is rejected with one bit test. [FSM §6](fsm-and-configuration.md#6-event-processing) · `internal/fsm`
- *ECS*: An entity's component mask records which stores hold it; destroying the entity removes every store the mask names. [ECS §2](ecs-and-events.md#2-entities-and-typed-stores) · `internal/engine` `World.AddComponentMask`

**mass** (also: `profile.Mass`, mass ratio) — An entity's relative collision mass (a single-cell entity is 1.0), used by collision profiles through a clamped impactor-to-target ratio and by wind; `Kinetic` itself carries no mass. See also **impulse**, **collision profile**. [Generic kinetic §2](generic-kinetic.md#2-current-representation-and-units) · [Generic kinetic: Impulses, mass, immunity, and stun](generic-kinetic.md#impulses-mass-immunity-and-stun) · `internal/profile` `Mass` (mass.go)

**materialize** (also: `MaterializeSystem`, beacon) — The converging-beam animation that announces a spawn before the entity appears, ending in `EventMaterializeComplete`; its domain is the requester's, and it lasts its request's `duration_ms`, its style's when that is 0. A materialize whose `Species` names one holds that spawn until it ends: a quasar fusion's quasar, a blue storm circle's swarm, a data species' entrance. A nugget beacon converges shorter beams on an existing nugget and spawns nothing. See also **entrance**, **teleport line**. [Domain model D-6](domain-design.md#d-6--presentation-and-personal-effects-are-player-domain) · [Gameplay: Nuggets](gameplay.md#nuggets) · `internal/system` `MaterializeSystem` · `internal/component` `SpawnType`

**max FPS** (also: `:fps`, `[play] max_fps`, frame rate, `tmux_frame_cap`, `MultiplexerFrameInterval`) — The most frames a run draws a second: auto (0) draws three a tick, or 24 a second inside tmux or screen, whose client blocking drops a terminal's output past eight bytes a cell, unless `[presentation] tmux_frame_cap` is false; a player's rate from 10 to 120 takes the place of either, held to no faster than the run's rate and no slower than a frame a tick. Set by `:fps`, Display → Max FPS or `vif.toml`, and written back. [Rendering §1](rendering.md#1-frame-pipeline) · [Input §9](input-and-modes.md#9-command-mode) · `internal/app` `App.frameInterval`, `frameFloor` · `internal/engine` `GameContext.MaxFPS`

**menu snapshot** (also: native menu, host menu, `menuPresentation`, `MenuOp`, `MenuSnapshot`) — The configuration menu as a host drawing it with its own widgets takes it: one JSON tree of every node with its kind, id, live label, value, choices, mark, confirm flag, disabled reason or form fields, with the title, the status line, a failed submit's error and a search's matches, built every frame the menu is open and handed over when its bytes changed, nil once it closed; the host answers with ops by node id (`set`, `step`, `activate`, `submit`, `search`, `close`), which the router applies as it applies a key. `menuPresentation` is the optional seam a `Presentation` offers for it; Android's view and the page's host do. The contract (`MenuKinds`, `MenuOps`, `MenuInputs`) is held to each host's source by a test. [Native menu §4](native-menu.md#4-snapshot) · [Native menu §5](native-menu.md#5-hooks) · [Native menu §6](native-menu.md#6-host-seam) · [Native menu §8](native-menu.md#8-browser) · `internal/mode` `Router.MenuSnapshot`, `Router.MenuOp`, `MenuOp`, `MenuKinds` · `internal/app` `menuPresentation`, `App.pushMenu`, `App.menuOp` · `internal/render` `RenderContext.NativeMenu` · `android/game` `Menu`, `Listener` · `android/app` `MenuDialog` · `pkg/browser` `Host.Menu`, `Host.MenuOp`, `HasMenu` · `cmd/vif` `menuHost` · `web/terminal.js` `applyMenu`

**MaxPlayers** (also: `NoPlayerSlot`) — The ceiling on participants (cursors), 16. Roster slots run 0..MaxPlayers-1 and session identities 1..MaxPlayers+1, because a dedicated host's coordinator is a peer that takes an identity and drives no cursor, holding `NoPlayerSlot` (0xFF). See also **slot**, **participant**. [Domain model §1](domain-design.md#1-domains-and-identity) · [Multiplayer §1](multi-player.md#1-vocabulary-and-boundaries) · `internal/parameter` `MaxPlayers`, `NoPlayerSlot`

**maze** (also: `pkg/maze`, `EventMazeSpawnRequest`) — A recursive-backtracker maze with optional rooms, braiding and open borders. On `EventMazeSpawnRequest` the wall system scales it by cell size into one wall batch, drawing from its own Shared RNG stream carried in the capture. [AI and physics §7](ai-physics-and-evolution.md#7-maze-generation) · [Gameplay §9](gameplay.md#9-walls-maps-navigation-and-environment) · `pkg/maze` `Generate`, `Config` · `internal/system` `WallSystem`

**member** (also: `MemberComponent`, composite member) — A child entity of a composite that carries `MemberComponent` pointing back to its header and is listed with its offset in the header's member entries; members are glyphs or hitboxes, and under an ablative composite take damage themselves. See also **composite**, **header**. [ECS §6](ecs-and-events.md#6-composite-entities) · `internal/component` `MemberComponent`, `HeaderComponent`

**membership** (also: membershipRun) — The type in `internal/app` that is a run's place in its session: hosting and joining, the roster and the offer, the seats it holds, the claims it comes back with, the relay desk and the authority it follows or holds; it is the half of `converge.Instance` the protocol moves, composed with `corrector` into `instance`, and reads the run through `membershipRun` alone (position, pacing, the lobby it shows on, the journal and anchor it writes a session into, the vacancy a dedicated host resumes from, and the run that replaces this one); `App` forwards its session API. See also **corrector**, **session**. [Package map §4](package-map.md#4-production-package-responsibilities) · `internal/app` `membership`

**mesh** — two senses. See also **relay**.
- *Multiplayer*: The session topology of direct links, a tree rooted at the authority: raw epochs go up each participant's one link toward it, committed epochs, owner state and correction bodies go down to the participants behind each node, and authority records flood. The shipped CLI builds a star, a guest seating bots or relaying people adds a level, and in a migrate session every participant but a seat also holds a peer link to the first succession candidate. See also **upstream**. [Domain model §4.2](domain-design.md#42-mesh-relay) · [Multiplayer §5](multi-player.md#5-membership-topology-and-authority-continuity) · `internal/system` `NetworkSystem.forwardDown`, `sendUp` · `internal/parameter` `NetworkRelayHopLimit`
- *Networking*: `link.Mesh` is the in-process multi-participant test transport with no wall clock; a `LinkShape` gives each link deterministic latency, loss and a byte budget per tick. [Services and networking §11](services-and-networking.md#11-verification-and-manual-run) · `pkg/network` `Mesh`, `MeshPort`, `LinkShape`

**MessageType** (also: `Msg*`, message type) — The one-byte type that opens every session frame's 12-byte header: control and links (session route, peer connect, disconnect), coordination (join offer and reply, start and ready, relay join and admit), gameplay (`MsgEvent`, `MsgStateSync`, `MsgBundle`, the parity digest), authority state (`MsgStateSnapshot`, `MsgStateCorrection`, the manifest, request and shard exchange), succession (`MsgAuthorityReport`, `MsgAuthorityHandoff`, `MsgPeerList`), scenario transfer and the link probe; reserved codes are never sent, and an unknown type counts as a drop. See also **wire frame**. [Services and networking §8](services-and-networking.md#8-wire-frame-and-session-messages) · `pkg/network` `MessageType`, `Kinds` · `internal/network` `Msg*`

**MetaSystem** (also: meta system, `ContextSystems`) — A context system, built by `internal/app` with the `GameContext`: it handles game reset (advancing the journal run), pause, speed and step, key capture, level setup, resize and the help, about and telemetry overlays, publishes context telemetry, and carries kill tallies, defeat latches and the damage multiplier as declared Shared state. See also **game reset**, **generic species system**. [Runtime §10](runtime.md#10-reset-and-operator-state) · [Package map §6](package-map.md#6-generated-assembly) · `internal/system` `MetaSystem` · `internal/manifest` `ContextSystems`

**mid-run join** (also: mid-run gate, reconnect, `admitLateJoiner`) — A join or reconnect into a running session through the mid-run gate every host arms once its clock runs. The joiner is admitted to transport before the capture is read (D-22), installs a recent keyframe and catches up a bounded gap; a gap past `NetworkJoinCatchUpTicks`, or still wider than `NetworkJoinLagTicks` afterwards, is refused. See also **gate**, **join**, **lobby**. [Domain model D-22](domain-design.md#d-22--admit-before-capturing) · [Multiplayer §5](multi-player.md#5-membership-topology-and-authority-continuity) · `internal/app` `membership.admitLateJoiner`

**migrate** (also: `-authority migrate`, `Migrating`) — The authority policy under which, when the authority is lost, every survivor walks the same succession list without exchanging anything, each candidate taking a term of its own in its turn. It is the default except under `-serve`, and the status bar shows `Migrating` while a succession is open. See also **succession**, **authority**, **leaf**. [Multiplayer §5.1](multi-player.md#51-succession) · [Multiplayer §5.3](multi-player.md#53-reachability-under--authority-migrate) · `internal/app` `AuthorityMigrate`

**missile** (also: Launcher, `WeaponLauncher`, `MissileSystem`) — The Launcher weapon's delivery: a homing projectile that sweeps its path for wall and combatant impact and blasts into explosion geometry, which crosses; a mounted launcher fires a slower hostile missile that homes on cursors. See also **loadout**, **mount**. [Combat §2](combat.md#2-layers) · [Combat §5](combat.md#5-resolution-statuses-and-what-crosses) · `internal/system` `MissileSystem` · `internal/component` `MissileComponent`

**mixer** (also: `audio.Mixer`) — The single goroutine that owns every voice, the sequencer and the buses; each pass it drains commands, renders music and sfx, ducks music under sfx, applies the pause ramp and writes one PCM buffer. See also **bus**, **sequencer**. [Audio §2](audio.md#2-stream-contract) · [Audio §4](audio.md#4-concurrency-and-command-ownership) · `pkg/audio` `Mixer`

**modal voice** (also: partial bank, waveModal, inharmonicity, coupled pair) — A tonal voice that is a string as its partials: one decaying phasor per partial at `k·f0` stretched by the string's inharmonicity, each at the amplitude the strike position, the spectral tilt and the velocity's hammer low-pass leave it and each decaying faster the higher it sits, the lowest doubled by a detuned second string whose slower aftersound makes the double decay. The piano and the bass are modal voices; the pluck is a delay line. [Audio §7](audio.md#7-music-sequencer) · `pkg/audio` `voiceSpec`, `TonalVoice.excite`, `TonalVoice.modal`

**mode** (also: process mode, interaction mode, `GameMode`, `ModePlay`, `ModeReplay`, `ModeScript`) — eight senses. See also **Normal mode**, **Insert mode**, **Search mode**, **visual mode**, **blend mode**, **free mouse**, **auto-fire**.
- *Input*: The interaction mode, `core.GameMode`, owned by `mode.Router`: Normal, Visual, Insert, Search, Command or Overlay. Normal, Visual, Insert and Search are play modes, held by `GameState` and journaled; Command and Overlay are the operator's (`GameMode.Operator`), open over the play mode on the context, never journaled and left by a reset. They pause a single-instance game but not a live session; `input.InputMode` is the parser's copy. [Input §2](input-and-modes.md#2-modes) · `internal/core` `GameMode`, `GameMode.Operator` · `internal/engine` `GameContext.SetPlayMode` · `internal/input` `InputMode`
- *Architecture*: The process mode, `app.Mode` (`ModePlay`, `ModeHeadless`, `ModeReplay`, `ModeScript`, `ModeServer`), chooses services, clock and goroutines through predicates such as `Presents`, `Driven` and `Serves`; it is separate from the build profile. See also **build profile**. [Runtime §1](runtime.md#1-process-modes) · `internal/app` `Mode`
- *Rendering*: Color mode is truecolor or 256-color output (`-color auto|256|true`), which picks the finalizer and palettes. [Rendering §6](rendering.md#6-finalization-and-color-modes) · `internal/engine` `ConfigResource.ColorMode`
- *Input*: Mouse mode (`:mouse enable|disable|free`, `:free`) and auto-fire mode (`AutoFireOff`, `AutoFireMain`, `AutoFireBoth`, cycled by `a`) are operator preferences. [Input §7](input-and-modes.md#7-mouse-and-automatic-fire) · `internal/engine` `GameContext.AutoFire`
- *Simulation*: Step mode, `StepFSM` or `StepEvent`, decides whether a run-until stops at the next FSM transition or the next dispatch of a named event. [Runtime §9](runtime.md#9-time-control) · `internal/engine` `StepMode`
- *Physics*: Impulse mode, `ImpulseAdditive` or `ImpulseOverride`, decides whether a collision impulse adds to velocity or replaces it. See also **impulse**. [Generic kinetic: Impulses, mass, immunity, and stun](generic-kinetic.md#impulses-mass-immunity-and-stun) · `pkg/vmath/physics` `ImpulseMode`
- *Content*: An image's render mode, `ascimage.RenderMode`, is quadrant (one Unicode quadrant per 2×2 pixels) or background (one background cell per pixel); a dual `.vifimg` records it. [Content and tools §8](content-assets-and-tools.md#8-image-and-pattern-pipeline) · `pkg/ascimage` `RenderMode`
- *Genetics*: a `StreamingEngine`'s `RefillMode` is deterministic by default, so a seed and operation sequence fix its proposal stream, or `RefillTimeBudget`, bounded by `TickBudget` and letting scheduling change the stream; a mutation's `BoundaryMode` returns an out-of-range value by clamping, reflecting or wrapping. [Genetic README: Determinism](../pkg/genetic/README.md#determinism) · `pkg/genetic` `RefillMode`, `BoundaryMode`

**monitor** (also: monitor region, `MonitorGlobalReset`) — The campaign's background FSM region and the only one started at boot: it spawns and arms the cursor, opens `main`, pays every gold completion, and answers `session.any_defeated` with a new game through its `MonitorGlobalReset` state. See also **region**, **defeat**. [Gameplay §10](gameplay.md#10-embedded-campaign-progression) · [FSM §11](fsm-and-configuration.md#11-authoring-pattern) · `internal/asset/scenario/monitor.toml`

**Moore outputs** — In a behavior machine, the component fields derived from the current state's flags, which the host writes on every step, suspended or not: the swarm's, the quasar's and a data species' `Combat.IsEnraged`, the quasar's `IsShielded`, `Shield.Active` and its ward, a data species' `Shielded` and its ward, its `TeleportLine`, which draws the teleport line, its `ChargeLine`, which pulses the charge line, and its `Landing`, which blinks the landing marker, and a storm circle's `IsInvulnerable` and its mount's `Mount.Armed`, which reads false while the circle is held. See also **enraged**. [Behavior machines §3](machine-design.md#3-hosting-a-machine) · `internal/system` `SwarmSystem.Update`, `QuasarSystem.Update`, `StormSystem.updateCircleAttacks`, `BehaviorSystem.Update`

**motif** (also: ladder, Pattern.Motif) — The idea a melody pattern is one weight of: patterns of one group sharing a `motif` form a ladder from calm to peak, and a tier change draws the playing motif's member at the new tier while a phrase variation draws another motif's, after the melody rests through the fill bar. Every shipped ladder covers all five tiers, so a tier change never leaves its idea. [Audio §7](audio.md#7-music-sequencer) · `pkg/audio` `Pattern.Motif`, `Sequencer.draw`, `Sequencer.planPhrase`

**motion** (also: `MotionOp`, vim motion) — A vim-style cursor movement (`h j k l`, words, paragraphs, find, map positions such as `gg` and `gm`) defined over the glyph grid and clamped to map bounds, which the router resolves to a destination or, after `d`, a range. See also **operator**, **marker**. [Input §3](input-and-modes.md#3-normal-mode-grammar) · [Input: Normal motions](input-and-modes.md#normal-motions) · `internal/input` `MotionOp`

**mount** — two senses.
- *Gameplay*: A weapon carried by a Shared host (`MountComponent`): every instance aims it at the nearest cursor from Shared positions and fires it identically under `MountAuto` or `MountArmed`, with spread seeded from the tick and host; its shots are Player-domain but a pulse, which is Shared, and a cursor hit counts only on that cursor's owner. See also **weapon**, **lane**, **pulse**. [Combat §3](combat.md#3-drivers) · `internal/component` `MountComponent`, `MountTrigger` · `internal/system` `MountSystem`
- *Build and deploy*: A Kubernetes volume mount; a session's init and game containers mount the wad volume read-only, one `subPath` per category. [Fleet deploy §9](kube-docker-deploy.md#9-the-fleet-objects-and-the-shared-volumes) · `tool/vif-router` `wadMounts`

**msg** (also: `fields.msg`) — The first payload key of every diagnostic log record, naming what happened; viewers index and filter on it, and for a status snapshot record it is the metric group. [Logging §2](logging-and-diagnostics.md#2-record-shape) · [Logging §14](logging-and-diagnostics.md#14-call-site-rules) · `internal/vlog`

**music** — What the sequencer plays: patterns (beats, melodies, basses and fills) on the music bus, arranged by the conductor and ducked under sounding sfx. See also **sequencer**, **conductor**, **pattern**, **sfx**. [Audio §7](audio.md#7-music-sequencer) · `pkg/audio` `Sequencer`; `internal/system` `MusicSystem`

**music recording** (also: `-mw`, `-music-wav`) — Recording the music bus alone, before ducking, to `vif-mus-<stamp>.wav` in the music directory (`-mw=DIR`, `paths.music`); muted or paused stretches add nothing. [Audio §3](audio.md#3-backend-detection-and-failover) · [Filesystem layout §5](filesystem-layout.md#5-logs-journals-and-runtime-output) · `internal/app` (audio.go) · `pkg/audio` (wav.go)

**music.toml** — The music bank of patterns the adaptive conductor arranges: the embedded `internal/asset/audio/music.toml`, to which a user `audio/music.toml` or `-config-music` file adds patterns or replaces them by name. See also **pattern**, **conductor**. [Audio §9](audio.md#9-authored-music-and-sound-overrides) · [Content and tools §7](content-assets-and-tools.md#7-embedded-assets) · `internal/asset/audio/music.toml`

**mute** (also: `-mute`, `Ctrl-S`) — two senses.
- *Audio*: Independent sfx and music mute bits that `Ctrl-S` cycles through silence, sfx only, music only and both; every build starts with both unless `-mute` or the player's saved settings say otherwise, and mute is a viewer preference the journal never carries. A sequencer slot also mutes alone, its pattern playing on in time, as soundlab's mixer does. See also **mask**. [Audio §6](audio.md#6-mixer-buses-mute-and-pause) · `internal/system` `AudioSystem` · `pkg/audio` `SetSlotMuted`
- *Diagnostics*: A log handle's mute silences one runtime's records, as for a replay's copies until presented; failures still pass. [Logging: Correlation stamps](logging-and-diagnostics.md#correlation-stamps) · `internal/vlog` `Log.Mute`

## N

**navigation** (also: `NavigationSystem`, `NavigationComponent`, `pkg/navigation`) — Moving species toward their target groups: `NavigationSystem` keeps flow fields and route graphs per target group, and each mover's `NavigationComponent` holds its direct-path flag, flow direction and locked target, which `ResolveMovementTarget` turns into a steering target. See also **flow field**, **group**. [Navigation §1](navigation-design.md#1-structures) · [Navigation §2](navigation-design.md#2-flow-fields) · `internal/system` `NavigationSystem`, `ResolveMovementTarget` · `pkg/navigation`

**netem gate** (also: `VIF_NETEM`) — A Linux-only, opt-in staged test that shapes loopback with `tc netem` over a real socket, walking a baseline and four stages (latency, jitter, loss, bandwidth), to check that corrections stay bounded, delivery continues and play recovers; it skips unless `VIF_NETEM=1`, root and `tc` are present, and shapes every loopback flow on the machine while it runs. [Development §5](development.md#5-validation-and-tests) · [Services and networking §11](services-and-networking.md#11-verification-and-manual-run) · `internal/app` (netem_test.go)

**network badge** (also: session badge) — The status bar's single network badge, chosen by severity: `Migrating [n]`, `Host lost`, `Net: down` (for `StatusNetworkDownDuration`, 10 s, then gone: a loss read once is noise beside a solo game), `Net: wait`, or player count, local slot and round trip with at most one qualifier (`slow!`, `desync`, `loss`, `slow`) on a green, amber or red background. [Multiplayer §7](multi-player.md#7-diagnostics-and-operations) · [Rendering §9](rendering.md#9-ui-and-debug-projections) · `internal/render/renderer` (status_bar.go)

**network.\*** (also: network status keys, `network.state`) — The status key family a session's transport and protocol publish: transport state (`off`, `waiting`, `connected`, `down`), peers, barrier and commit counters, terms and succession, parity and link measures; `NetworkSystem` registers most of them and resets them in `Init`. See also **key**. [Telemetry reference: Network](telemetry-reference.md#network) · [Telemetry reference: Session telemetry](telemetry-reference.md#session-telemetry) · `internal/system` `NetworkSystem`

**NetworkPort** (also: `SocketPort`, `MeshPort`) — The simulation-facing transport interface that `NetworkSystem` sends through and drains at the poll boundary (`Send`, `Broadcast`, `BroadcastExcept`, `Drain`); `SocketPort` implements it over a socket and `MeshPort` over the in-process test mesh. [Services and networking §5](services-and-networking.md#5-capability-injection) · [Services and networking §9](services-and-networking.md#9-poll-boundary-and-receive-lead) · `internal/engine` `NetworkPort` · `pkg/network` `SocketPort`, `MeshPort`

**NetworkResource** (also: `OnCorrection`, `OnSelective`, `OnAuthority`) — The world's network singleton: the port, this participant's identity, the barrier delay, the shared-digest supplier and the session layer's seams (`OnCorrection`, `OnSelective`, `OnAuthority`, `OnDeparture`), which the network system calls under the world lock; nil outside a session. [ECS §5](ecs-and-events.md#5-singleton-resources) · `internal/engine` `NetworkResource`

**NetworkSystem** — This instance's only crossing point to its peers: a manifest-registered dual-domain system that drains received frames and queues the scheduled artifacts due into the tick's body as it opens, and flushes the closed epoch at tick close. It owns fences, epoch windows, its own lead, owner-state sync, parity digests and relaying, and is inert without a network resource. [Services and networking §9](services-and-networking.md#9-poll-boundary-and-receive-lead) · [Package map §6](package-map.md#6-generated-assembly) · `internal/system` `NetworkSystem`

**node filter** — The fleet node's own nftables table, `inet vif`, which decides what reaches the node from its own network. It owns that one table and never flushes the ruleset, because K3s and Docker program their rules into ordinary nftables tables beside it. [Fleet deploy §6](kube-docker-deploy.md#6-the-nodes-own-filter) · `deploy/guest/nftables.conf`

**Normal mode** (also: `ModeNormal`) — The router's home mode, where play starts and to which Escape returns from the other modes: a Vim-like grammar of counts, motions, operators, find and search repeats, actions and macros, with `i`, `v`, `/` and `:` entering Insert, Visual, Search and Command, and `Ctrl-G` or `F10` opening the configuration menu and `F1` help in Overlay. Escape here cancels a pending key sequence or, with none, flashes the ping grid; simulation runs unless separately paused. See also **mode**, **motion**. [Input §2](input-and-modes.md#2-modes) · [Input §3](input-and-modes.md#3-normal-mode-grammar) · `internal/core` `ModeNormal` · `internal/mode` `Router`

**note** (also: journal note, `EventQueue.Note`) — A journal record that replay applies but never dispatches: `EventCursorPredicted` (a D-18 placement) and `EventSessionState` (a session latch change). See also **journal**, **latch**. [Logging: Written worlds and notes](logging-and-diagnostics.md#written-worlds-and-notes) · `internal/event` `EventQueue.Note`

**novlog** (also: `make nolog`) — The build tag that compiles logging out (`vlog.Linked` false): emission becomes a no-op, `:log`, `:t save` and a journal start are refused as unavailable in this build, and status metrics still count. A `wasm` build behaves the same, in a browser's words, and the Windows build carries the tag. See also **build profile**. [Logging §15](logging-and-diagnostics.md#15-build-variants) · [Build profiles §1](multi-platform.md#1-current-build-profiles) · `internal/vlog`

**nugget** (also: `NuggetComponent`, `NuggetSystem`) — A participant's single personal Player-domain collectible, spawned away from its cursor and collected by overlap or the shield or ember ellipse for 10 heat; `Tab` jumps to it, and a beacon periodically converges materialize beams on it. See also **jump**, **materialize**. [Gameplay: Nuggets](gameplay.md#nuggets) · `internal/system` `NuggetSystem` · `internal/component` `NuggetComponent`

## O

**observer** — A `func(from, to, cause)` bound when a behavior-machine graph is built, which hears every commit, retry, start and stop, so a host feeds status counters without the machine package importing `status`. [Behavior machines §3](machine-design.md#3-hosting-a-machine) · `pkg/machine` `Observer`

**off-map cursor** (also: `[map] cursors = false`, `CursorsOffMap`) — A rostered cursor with no cell and nothing a cell carries, only its slot, control kind, peer and holder, which every spawn and arrival makes in a scenario whose map places no cursor. Captures, corrections, handoffs and departures carry it as any cursor; nothing draws, moves, targets or drains it, and its player plays through the keys the scenario captures, each press from its slot. See also **cursor**, **input hook**, **roster**. [FSM reference: Cursor Lifecycle](fsm-reference.md#cursor-lifecycle) · `internal/system` `CursorSystem.rosterOffMap` · `internal/engine` `ConfigResource.CursorsOffMap`

**offer** (also: `SessionOffer`, `MsgJoinOffer`) — The coordinator's answer to a join's seat: the session anchor, host, assigned identity, term, roster, succession chain, authority policy, relay, and the coming capture's tick and size. Its **preview**, naming nobody, came first, and is what the joiner checked its build against before it built a world. See also **join**, **identity**, **anchor**. [Services and networking §7.2](services-and-networking.md#72-join-identity-and-who-decides-it) · [Domain model §4.5](domain-design.md#45-join-and-stream-framing) · `internal/network` `SessionOffer`

**on_enter** (also: `on_exit`, `on_update`) — A state's lifecycle hooks: `on_enter` actions run root to leaf on entry, `on_exit` leaf to root on exit, and `on_update` every tick for every state on the active path. See also **lifecycle**, **transition**. [FSM reference: Lifecycle Hooks](fsm-reference.md#lifecycle-hooks) · [FSM reference: State Definition](fsm-reference.md#state-definition) · `internal/fsm`

**OnWire** (also: `Replicated`, `stampedCrossings`, wire set) — `event.Replicated` says whether every instance's journal must hold a record (a Shared or Bus event, or a Stamped one that targets Shared); `event.OnWire`, narrower, says whether a peer must receive it. A Bus event travels only when its producer used the crossing path, a Stamped one only when it targets Shared and `stampedCrossings` lists it, and a re-derived Shared event, a `Derived` payload or an event a peer produced (`OriginNetwork`) never. See also **event class**, **derived event**. [Domain model D-10](domain-design.md#d-10--event-class-and-event-domain-answer-different-questions) · `internal/event` `OnWire`, `Replicated`

**opening** (also: journal opening, `opens`, own side, `own`) — A journal opened mid-run starts with it: the live shared world written as a `capture` over a fresh boot of the anchor's identity, flagged `opens` with the run's mode, pause, hold, toggled-off systems, the slot its input follows and its own side: the player world, the player's resources, each system's own state, the corpus position, the event tallies, the status cells and the run's **WorldOrder**. Its replay plays on as this participant, and the run, which adopts the result, is left as it was. See also **journal**, **written world**, **PlayerWorldState**, **LocalStateSaver**. [Logging: Opening mid-run](logging-and-diagnostics.md#opening-mid-run) · `internal/app` `scribe.openJournal` · `internal/event` `JournalCapture.Opens`

**operating point** — two senses.
- *Networking*: One peer's current `linkpace.Plan`: cadence, keyframe interval, planned, budget and floor bit rates, and whether the link constrained it or breached the floor. See also **linkpace**, **cadence**. [Services and networking §9](services-and-networking.md#9-poll-boundary-and-receive-lead) · [Domain model D-24](domain-design.md#d-24--cadence-adapts-the-convergence-floor-does-not) · `pkg/linkpace` `Plan`, `Controller.Update`
- *Multiplayer*: The measured cost of a session at given sizes (host and guest CPU, publication rate, hash-only share, egress, heap), the shape to plan scaling by. [Netcode design §3.1](netcode-design.md#31-operating-point-on-the-in-process-mesh)

**operator** — five senses. See also **game reset**, **command**.
- *Control*: The operator's intents, which a game waiting for its press takes as such whoever the run is, a held game's or a join's: what opens or works the command line and the overlays, quits, mutes or lets go of a key, and the status bar press the menu is under; any other key or click is the press that starts or seats. [Input §2](input-and-modes.md#2-modes) · `internal/mode` `Router.Operates` · `internal/app` `App.joinPressed`, `Router.TakeStart`
- *Architecture*: The person running an instance. Operator state (free mouse, auto-fire, speed, telemetry HUD and pins, breakpoints, the mode with its open line or overlay) survives `:new`; `:new!` clears the HUD, pins and breakpoints and returns the three preferences to their startup values, and operator commands run under the world lock. [Runtime §10](runtime.md#10-reset-and-operator-state) · [Runtime: Operator commands hold the lock](runtime.md#operator-commands-hold-the-lock) · `internal/mode` (commands.go)
- *Input*: A vim operator: `d` (delete) with a motion, plus `dd`, `x` and `D`; a deletion publishes one delete request for the motion's range. See also **motion**. [Input §3](input-and-modes.md#3-normal-mode-grammar) · `internal/input` `OperatorOp` · `internal/mode` `OpDelete`
- *FSM*: A comparison `op` in guards (`eq`, `neq`, `gt`, `gte`, `lt`, `lte`); an omitted one compares `eq`, and any other fails the load. [FSM reference: Factory Guards (parameterized)](fsm-reference.md#factory-guards-parameterized) · `internal/fsm/dialect` `Op`
- *AI*: Genetic operators (`Selector`, `Combiner`, `Perturbator`) select, recombine and mutate candidates in `pkg/genetic`. [pkg/genetic: Operators](../pkg/genetic/README.md#operators) · `pkg/genetic`

**orb** (also: `OrbComponent`, weapon orb) — The orbiting Player-domain indicator of each owned weapon kind but the gun, from which that kind's shots leave; its `Kinetic` only mirrors the orbit angle and cursor position. See also **loadout**. [Combat §3](combat.md#3-drivers) · [Generic kinetic §4](generic-kinetic.md#4-other-uses-of-kinetic) · `internal/component` `OrbComponent`

**ordinary crossing** (also: ordinary frame) — A crossing about what happens in a world both instances already hold: a capture contains it when its sequence is within the capture's fence for its source, and a correction repairs any gap. A barrier-bound crossing, which decides what the world is, is judged by tick instead. See also **crossing**, **crossing fence**, **barrier-bound crossing**. [Multiplayer §3.1](multi-player.md#31-ordinary-and-barrier-bound-crossings) · [Multiplayer §3.2](multi-player.md#32-the-capture-boundary) · `internal/system` `NetworkSystem`

**origin** — three senses. See also **event origin**.
- *Diagnostics*: `event.Origin` names an event's producer (`system`, `input`, `macro`, `command`, `network`, `debug`, `session`, `device`); events of every origin but `system` and `device` are journaled (an own crossing whatever its origin), and dispatch never branches on it. [Logging §9](logging-and-diagnostics.md#9-replay-journal) · [ECS §8](ecs-and-events.md#8-event-transport) · `internal/event` `Origin`, `Origin.Journaled`
- *Input*: The map origin is cell (0,0), the map's top-left, which `go` (`motion_origin`) moves to, or to the nearest free cell when it is blocked. [Input: Prefixes, editing, and modes](input-and-modes.md#prefixes-editing-and-modes) · `internal/mode` `MotionOrigin`
- *Build and deploy*: The browser's `Origin` header, which the router's WebSocket route checks; a native client's is the URL's own. [Build profiles: The public route](multi-platform.md#the-public-route) · `tool/vif-router` (ws_proxy.go)

**output limiter** (also: LimiterCeiling, LimiterLookahead) — The mixer's last stage: it follows the peak of the summed buses 64 samples ahead of what it writes and ramps its gain down over that time, holding the output under 0.9 so stacked kicks duck without the step a gain change inside one sample would put on them. [Audio §2](audio.md#2-stream-contract) · `pkg/audio` `Mixer.renderTick`

**overheat** — Heat added beyond the 100 cap, accumulated per cursor: reaching the threshold emits a heat burst and starts the ember phase, special fire spends it before current heat, and a heat penalty clears it. See also **heat**, **ember**. [Gameplay: Heat and ember](gameplay.md#heat-and-ember) · `internal/component` `HeatComponent`

**overlay** (also: Overlay mode, `ModeOverlay`) — three senses. See also **blend mode**.
- *Input*: Overlay mode's centred window, showing telemetry cards, help, about, the profiler report or the configuration menu; it pauses a single-instance game but not a live session. It takes the pointer whatever the mouse setting: a press outside it is Escape, a press on its scrollbar moves the thumb there and a drag carries it, a press picks a menu row or a telemetry card, and the wheel scrolls its view and leaves the selection. [Input §2](input-and-modes.md#2-modes) · [Input §7](input-and-modes.md#7-mouse-and-automatic-fire) · [Rendering §9](rendering.md#9-ui-and-debug-projections) · `internal/core` `OverlayContent`, `OverlayCard` · `internal/engine` `ComputeOverlayGeometry` · `internal/mode` `Router.handleOverlayPoint`, `Router.overlayView` · `terminal/tui` `ScrollThumb`, `ScrollDrag`
- *Rendering*: A background overlay is a deferred color and intensity a renderer such as strobe sets for otherwise untouched cells, applied by the finalizer. [Rendering §3](rendering.md#3-compositor-data-model) · [Rendering §5](rendering.md#5-semantic-masks-and-post-processing) · `internal/render` `RenderBuffer.SetBackgroundOverlay`
- *Rendering*: Overlay is also a blend operation (`BlendOverlay`), used for ember ring and color shaping. [Rendering §4](rendering.md#4-blend-modes) · `internal/render` `BlendOverlay`

**owner-authored** (also: owner-state sync, `MsgStateSync`) — Shared cursor values only the owning instance writes: energy, heat, shield, boost, weapon and combat, `CursorViewComponent` and `PingComponent`.
