They stay out of the manifest hash, correction magnitude and repair, and all but ping travel on the owner-state sync every `NetworkSyncTicks` (6), which the authority commits like an epoch so every instance writes it at one tick. See also **D-13**. [Domain model D-13](domain-design.md#d-13--owner-authored-shared-values-have-one-writer) · [Netcode design §2](netcode-design.md#2-the-correction-pipeline) · `internal/system` `NetworkSystem` · `internal/snapshot` (manifest.go)

## P

**pacing** (also: pace) — four senses. See also **playout lead**, **time control**.
- *Guest*: A guest trims its wall tick interval by up to `NetworkPacePermilleMax` (80 per mille), or steps whole ticks when every epoch of the window is past `NetworkPaceStepTicks` of error, until the authority's epochs land inside its pace band; the simulation step never changes. [Multi-player §3.5](multi-player.md#35-command-frames-the-commit-and-pacing) · `internal/system` `NetworkSystem.observeAuthorityEpoch`, `paceDecision`; `internal/engine` `Scheduler.TakePace`
- *Script*: An authored script runs unpaced when solo and at real time in a session; `-speed` sets its wall pace, and `-speed max` (no wall pacing) is refused with `-host`, `-join` or seated bots. [Runtime §1.1](runtime.md#11-script-pacing-and-presentation) · `internal/app` `scriptPacing`, `ScriptPaceMax`
- *Bot*: A bot graph releases `actions_per_second` intents (default 12) per second of game time. [Bots: Document](bots.md#document) · `internal/bot` `DefaultActionsPerSecond`
- *Render*: `TimeResource.PaceTime`, the pacing clock's instant a tick ran at, from which the glyph renderer measures how far a frame stands past its tick; instance-local, and only renderers read it. See also **parallax**. [Runtime §5](runtime.md#5-simulation-tick-steps) · `internal/engine` `TimeResource.PaceTime`

**page** — four senses.
- *Correction manifest*: A manifest page is one hashed bucket of a section's canonical rows; a section splits into a power-of-two number of pages, enough for about `SnapshotManifestPageRows` (32) rows each up to `SnapshotManifestMaxPages` (64), rows are hashed to pages so consecutive entities spread out, and a mismatching receiver requests only the differing pages. [Netcode design §2](netcode-design.md#2-the-correction-pipeline) · `internal/snapshot` `pageCount`, `rowPage`, `pageHash`
- *Configuration menu*: One screen of the menu: Game, Scenarios, Multiplayer, Settings, Developer or Launch options. [Menu: Pages](config-menu.md#pages) · `internal/mode` `configPages`
- *Browser*: The web page that hosts the WASM build; while it is hidden (`pageHidden`) the program draws nothing but keeps ticking, and repaints once on return; a joined page hidden five minutes leaves and rejoins on return. See **hidden tab**. [Multi-platform: A hidden tab](multi-platform.md#a-hidden-tab) · `internal/app` `pageHidden`
- *Input*: a half-page motion (`H J K L`, `PageUp`, `PageDown`) moves the cursor half the viewport's height or width per count, clamped to the map and backed off a blocked cell; `PageUp` and `PageDown` page an overlay that owns input. [Input: Normal motions](input-and-modes.md#normal-motions) · `internal/mode` `MotionHalfPageUp`, `MotionHalfPageDown`

**parallax** (also: `DriftResource`, `SceneryDepthMix`) — How drifting text reads as depth. Each **drift layer** moves at its level's fraction of the drift speed, and the glyph renderer draws **scenery** where its layer stands at the frame's moment, carried on from its cells by the part-cell and velocity the environment leaves in `DriftResource` each tick, over the time since the tick's `PaceTime` and a tick at most, and an **entering line**'s waiting runes past the edge the same way. The layers draw far to near and typeable text last, so the nearest glyph shows in a cell and lines of two depths interleave where they cross, and in truecolor each scenery layer mixes its type's bright color into the background by `SceneryDepthMix`. See also **drift**, **pacing**. [Rendering §7](rendering.md#7-layer-inventory) · [Gameplay §9](gameplay.md#9-walls-maps-navigation-and-environment) · `internal/render/renderer` `GlyphRenderer` · `internal/engine` `DriftResource.Carry` · `internal/parameter/visual` `SceneryDepthMix`

**parameter** (also: `internal/parameter`, tunables) — The package of gameplay, engine, network, audio and UI tunables, system priorities and the parse of the shipped sound and music banks, with `internal/parameter/visual` for visual tuning; changing a number there changes a mechanic, while changing a TOML transition changes when it is invoked. [Gameplay §12](gameplay.md#12-balance-and-ownership-map) · [Package map §4](package-map.md#4-production-package-responsibilities) · `internal/parameter`

**parity** (also: runtime parity) — Proved by the correction exchange: every manifest compares the Shared surface section by section at the cadence, and a repair names the sections it moved in `snapshot.repair_sections`. The six-tick wire digest is retired. [Domain design §4.3](domain-design.md#43-runtime-parity) · `internal/converge` `applyRepair`

**park** (also: parked session, vacant) — A dedicated host whose roster empties stops its clock at once (lifecycle phase `vacant`, `/health` `clock=paused`), so a guest that dropped returns to the world it left; an unbounded host replaces a world nobody reclaimed within `SessionVacantReset` (1 min) once and stays parked, and a dial releases the park. [Runtime: An empty session parks](runtime.md#an-empty-session-parks) · `internal/app` `App.holdVacant`, `resumeVacant`

**participant** — two senses. See also **peer**, **roster**, **slot**.
- *Identity*: A session member's identity in `1..MaxPlayers+1`, assigned by the coordinator at admission, where 0 means none and the coordinator is 1 (`CoordinatorParticipant`); `network.PeerID` carries it and authorship is never inferred from it. [Multi-player §1](multi-player.md#1-vocabulary-and-boundaries) · `internal/engine` `CoordinatorParticipant`, `NetworkResource.ParticipantID`
- *Count*: An admitted peer bound to a roster slot, that is, a cursor, on the map or an **off-map cursor**; a dedicated host's cursorless coordinator holds an identity but is not counted, and `MaxPlayers` and `SessionOffer.ParticipantCount` count these. [Logging: Naming a peer](logging-and-diagnostics.md#naming-a-peer-counting-participants) · [Domain design §1](domain-design.md#1-domains-and-identity) · `internal/network` `SessionOffer.ParticipantCount`

**particle** (also: `ParticleComponent`, dust, decay, blossom) — A Player-domain moving entity, drawn as a sigil, not a glyph, whose behavior (dust, decay or blossom) selects one row of `ParticleSystem`'s profile table; decay lowers glyph levels, blossom raises them, and dust is what converted glyphs become. It meets walls by their particle bit, passing hidden through rock that lets it pass, and one no anchor holds rides the **cave frame**. [Gameplay §4](gameplay.md#4-glyph-production-and-decay) · `internal/component` `ParticleComponent`, `ParticleBehavior`; `internal/system` `ParticleSystem`

**passability** (also: passable) — Whether an actor may occupy a cell: a point actor needs a wall-free cell, a composite needs its whole footprint to fit (**composite passability**); navigation names the wall and passability grid by a generation, so a cached field or route graph recomputes only when it moves. [Navigation §3](navigation-design.md#3-passability-tiers-and-wall-changes) · [Navigation §3](navigation-design.md#3-passability-tiers-and-wall-changes) · `pkg/navigation` `CompositePassability`; `internal/system` `NavigationSystem`

**paste** (also: `EventPaste`, clipboard) — Text a host hands the run at once: the page's paste and soft keyboard's text, and Android's system keyboard text, committed, composed or its paste button's, and the Paste key, Ctrl-Shift-V and Shift-Insert, which read the clipboard. It comes as one `terminal.EventPaste` the run types a key at a time, a line break as Enter and another control character as nothing, each key let go as it presses where releases are reported, so a paste longer than the host's event queue arrives whole; into the command line it types as keys would. A terminal's paste arrives as the terminal types it. [Multi-platform §8](multi-platform.md#8-adding-another-renderer-or-android-host) · `internal/app` `pasteKeys` · `android/app` `GameView.paste` · `pkg/browser` `Host`

**pattern** — three senses.
- *Image*: A dual-mode `.vifimg` converted to wall cells with an authored anchor offset, which `WallSystem` spawns as one composite wall. [Rendering §10](rendering.md#10-patterns-and-image-derived-assets) · [Content §8](content-assets-and-tools.md#8-image-and-pattern-pipeline) · `internal/pattern` `PatternResult`, `PatternCell`
- *Music*: A step grid of up to 64 steps and 32 tracks with a role, groups and a tier bitmask that place it in the drawn arrangement, and a motif naming the idea it is one weight of, defined in `music.toml` or `BuiltinPatterns`. [Audio §7](audio.md#7-music-sequencer) · `pkg/audio` `Pattern`, `Pattern.Motif`
- *Search*: The literal text typed in Search mode, matched against the map's glyphs and remembered so `n` and `N` repeat it. [Input §5](input-and-modes.md#5-insert-search-and-command-text-editing) · `internal/mode` `PerformSearch`, `RepeatSearch`

**pattern editor** (also: editor, keys, grid, piano roll, take, `n`, `e`) — Soundlab's editor of one pattern, new (`n`, empty and playing along in the first silent slot) or edited (`e`), which its frame and header say, on two tabs: keys, two octaves and a third of a tracker layout drawn as a piano, each note of the key `key` set dotted, whose take is recorded over the pattern's length and written into it as a track a voice through `take`, each note on the degree of the key that sounds it; and grid, the tracks on a step grid under a bar ruler and the playhead, or with `r` one tonal track as a piano roll. Every edit runs a command, so a script can make it; `^D` twice deletes the pattern. See also **soundlab**. [Audio §10](audio.md#10-soundlab) · `internal/soundlab` tui_editor.go, tui_keys.go

**pause** — three senses. See also **time control**.
- *Simulation*: Pause freezes simulation ticks through the pausable clock while input, dispatch and the frame continue. This instance's own pause (`TimeControl.OwnPause`: a menu or the command line solo, as `[play] pause_menu` and `pause_command` allow, away, a breakpoint) is refused in a live session unless every other participant is its own seat, and an arrival ends it; the pause a session takes is the host's **hold**. [Runtime §8](runtime.md#8-pause-semantics) · `internal/engine` `PausableClock`, `SessionState.Pausable`; `internal/system` `MetaSystem`
- *FSM region*: A paused region keeps its state and time but neither updates nor handles events; other regions continue. `:region pause` and the `PauseRegion` action set it. [FSM §5](fsm-and-configuration.md#5-tick-processing) · [FSM: Operator region primitives](fsm-and-configuration.md#operator-region-primitives) · `internal/fsm` `Machine.PauseRegion`
- *Audio*: A mixer output state that ramps master gain over 250 ms and freezes music in place for resume. [Audio §6](audio.md#6-mixer-buses-mute-and-pause) · `pkg/audio` `Mixer`

**payload** (also: payload prototype, payload codec) — The typed struct an event carries: its type is registered per event as a prototype, it is encoded for the journal and the wire by the table-driven codec (`codec.go` over the field tables of `payload_gen.go`: a presence mask, then each set field in declaration order), which `vif-log` decodes with too, and decoded into a fresh prototype value (`NewPayloadStruct`, `DecodePayload`), and hot payloads are pooled, so handlers treat every payload as borrowed. [ECS §9](ecs-and-events.md#9-event-catalog-and-payload-contracts) · `internal/event` `NewPayloadStruct`, `BatchPool`

**payload names** (also: `dialect.Names`, `PayloadNames`, `AuthoredPayload`) — The names an author spells a payload's enum fields by, one list per Go type: a dense enum's (`type = "gun"`), a mask's bits and unions, one name or a list (`block_mask = ["cursor", "kinetic"]`), and a variant's, picked by the field beside it (a gateway's `sub_type` by its `species`); a species field takes the scenario's behavior programs too, and a loot field its loot kinds. Each resolves in the raw table before it decodes, so a reordered enum retargets nothing a scenario reads. A scenario's and a behavior program's `EmitEvent`, `:emit` and a script's event decode through them, and `-schema` lists them under `names`; a number in an enum field fails the load. See also **data species**, **dialect**. [FSM reference: Event Emission](fsm-reference.md#event-emission) · [Behavior machines §5](machine-design.md#5-dialect-kernel) · `internal/fsm/dialect` `Names` · `internal/event` `PayloadNames`, `AuthoredPayload`

**payload_vars** — An `EmitEvent` table that injects FSM variables into fields of the emitted payload at run time, including nested struct fields and pre-populated slice elements by quoted dot paths; a string field takes the value's decimal form in place of each `{}`. See also **pick**. [FSM reference: Event Emission](fsm-reference.md#event-emission) · [FSM reference: Nested payload_vars](fsm-reference.md#nested-payload_vars-dot-path-injection) · [FSM §8](fsm-and-configuration.md#8-actions-and-delayed-work) · `internal/fsm` `ActionConfig.PayloadVars`

**PCG** — The `math/rand/v2` permuted congruential generator used where a stream lives outside `RandResource`: `pkg/genetic`'s engines, the wall system's maze stream and the music sequencer; the genetic and maze streams carry its position, not only its seed, so a restore continues the sequence. [AI and physics §3](ai-physics-and-evolution.md#3-genetic-library) · [AI and physics §7](ai-physics-and-evolution.md#7-maze-generation) · `pkg/genetic`; `internal/system` `WallSystem`

**peer** — four senses. See also **participant**, **link**, **identity**.
- *Network*: One end of a session link, addressed by the participant identity on its far end, so `network.PeerID` names both who and which link and no instance is its own peer; `peers` counts live transport links and `roster` admitted peers, a dedicated host's cursorless coordinator (peer 1, `NoPlayerSlot`) included, which is a peer but not a participant. [Multi-player §1](multi-player.md#1-vocabulary-and-boundaries) · [Logging: Naming a peer](logging-and-diagnostics.md#naming-a-peer-counting-participants) · `pkg/network` `PeerID`, `Peer`, `PeerManager`
- *Role*: `RolePeer` is the transport role that dials the coordinator (or a bot's holder, or a relay); as a session role it is a non-authoring participant with nobody behind it. [Services §7](services-and-networking.md#7-transport-roles-and-lifecycle) · `pkg/network` `RolePeer` · `internal/network` `SessionRole`
- *Peer link*: In a migrate session, a direct link two already-admitted participants open with `MsgConnect`/`MsgAck`, each dialling the first candidate, and a survivor each candidate whose turn has come; it allocates and captures nothing and serves succession. [Multi-player §5.3](multi-player.md#53-reachability-under--authority-migrate) · `internal/network` `PeerDialer`, `PeerGate`
- *Peer cursor*: A cursor another instance simulates, drawn in one color per roster slot just under the local cursor; its shield and ember fields compose at `PeerFieldBlend` (30%) in truecolor. [Rendering: Local and peer cursors](rendering.md#local-and-peer-cursors) · `internal/render/renderer` `PeerCursorRenderer`

**phase** — ten senses.
- *Allocated session*: `lifecycle.Phase` is a dedicated host's life stage: unstarted, waiting, occupied, vacant, draining or expired. [Runtime: The allocated session](runtime.md#the-allocated-session) · `internal/lifecycle` `Phase`
- *Event*: `EventPhase` marks a record derived from Shared state as settled or as this instance's prediction (`PhasePredicted`), which a correction may make it derive again. [Multi-player §3.4](multi-player.md#34-predicted-derivations) · `internal/event` `EventPhase`
- *Throttle*: A cached Shared derivation's recompute age and last inputs, carried in the capture so every instance recomputes on the same tick (D-17). [Domain design D-17](domain-design.md#d-17--throttle-phase-is-state) · `internal/system` `NavigationSystem`, `navGroupPhase`
- *Profiler*: `prof.Phase` is an engine span the profiler times: `tick`, `tick_wait`, `fsm`, `dispatch`, `systems`, `status`, `frame`, `frame_wait`, `render`, `flush`. [Logging §11](logging-and-diagnostics.md#11-profiler) · `internal/prof` `Phase`
- *Drift*: Each glyph level keeps an integer sub-cell phase per axis, advanced by simulated time, so every instance steps a drifting layer on the same tick. [Gameplay §9](gameplay.md#9-walls-maps-navigation-and-environment) · `internal/system` `EnvironmentSystem`
- *Heat*: The ember phase follows an overheat burst, decaying heat periodically and blocking ordinary energy penalties. [Gameplay: Heat and ember](gameplay.md#heat-and-ember) · `internal/system` `HeatSystem`
- *Ray*: `RayPhase` is a ray's warning or firing step; a ray mount cycles through both, a cursor's ray only fires. [Combat §3](combat.md#3-drivers) · `internal/component` `RayPhase`
- *Status bar*: the FSM phase item shows the first unpaused foreground region's active state with the time left before its `StateTimeExceeds` timeout, or its time in state when it declares none (`fsm.state`, `fsm.elapsed`, `fsm.max_duration`); a background region never shows. [Rendering §9](rendering.md#9-ui-and-debug-projections) · `internal/fsm` `Machine.GetActiveRegionTelemetry` · `internal/render/renderer` (status_bar.go)
- *Behavior machines*: the ordered phases of one `Graph.Step`: global edges, suspension, advance and cues, timeout, pre-tick edges, tick and post-tick edges. [Behavior machines §2.3](machine-design.md#23-step) · `pkg/machine` `Graph.Step`
- *Future design*: The generic-kinetic migration phases 0–6. [Generic kinetic §10](generic-kinetic.md#10-migration-plan)

**PhysicalBodyComponent** (also: body profile) — Future design: explicit body metadata, or a profile resolver, carrying mass and motion kind so actors opt into physics deliberately instead of acquiring it from a bare Kinetic. [Generic kinetic §8](generic-kinetic.md#8-feasible-pseudo-system-design)

**pick** (also: `PickConfig`) — An `EmitEvent` table, `pick = { field, var, from }`, that sets one payload field to the entry of `from` its variable's value names, modulo the list's length; each entry decodes at load as that field's value in its payload, names included, so the draw behind it need not know the list. Vanguard picks its raider program and its cave's stone art with it; `payload_vars` sets the other fields after it, and a reconciled action reads its variable as it reads `payload_vars`. See also **payload names**, **payload_vars**. [FSM reference: Event Emission](fsm-reference.md#event-emission) · `internal/fsm` `ActionConfig.Pick`, `PickConfig` · `internal/fsm/std` `EmitEventArgs.Picks`

**pickup bar** — The bar a **rip** opens before the first full one when a note or a hit sounds more than half a step before its line, its beats extrapolated a beat at a time, into the time before the recording when it begins inside that bar (`Rip.Downbeat` is negative then), so a phrase that leads into the first bar is kept; `CompareRips` skips it against a truth without one. [Audio §11](audio.md#11-ripping-a-recording) · `pkg/audio` rip.go `pickup`

**pin** — two senses.
- *Telemetry*: A telemetry card (one registry group) pinned to the HUD; pinning turns the HUD on, `:t unpin` clears pins, `:d prof` pins its own groups, and pins are operator state that survives a plain `:new`. [Input §9](input-and-modes.md#9-command-mode) · [Runtime §10](runtime.md#10-reset-and-operator-state) · `internal/engine` `GameContext.ToggleOverlayPin`; `internal/render/renderer` `PinnedStatsRenderer`
- *Content*: `-f <file>` pins block delivery to one eligible corpus file, cycled forever; pinning fails if the file was rejected or yields no blocks, and the journal anchor records the pin (`ContentPin`). [Content §6](content-assets-and-tools.md#6-block-selection) · `internal/content` `Cursor.Pin`

**ping** — two senses.
- *Cursor*: The cursor's crosshair, selection grid and movement guidance (`PingComponent`); Escape in Normal flashes the grid, its bounds drive Visual mode, and it is owner-authored but never synced because nothing reads a remote copy. [Gameplay §2](gameplay.md#2-cursor-and-player-state) · [Domain design D-13](domain-design.md#d-13--owner-authored-shared-values-have-one-writer) · `internal/component` `PingComponent`
- *WebSocket*: A ping control frame answered by a pong, part of the minimal RFC 6455 surface. [Multi-platform: Where WebSocket is spoken](multi-platform.md#where-websocket-is-spoken) · `pkg/websocket`

**:player** (also: `:player drop`) — Lists every cursor slot, in hex, as `3 player` or `4 bot of 3`; `:player drop <slot>` dismisses another participant with all the bots it holds, or one bot alone, through one `EventParticipantDeparted`. Only the coordinator may drop, and an instance leaves the session itself with `:leave`, or `:q`. See also **departure**, **bot**, **roster**. [Bots: Commands and the menu](bots.md#commands-and-the-menu) · [Input §9](input-and-modes.md#9-command-mode) · `internal/mode` `handlePlayerCommand` · `internal/app` `membership.dropPlayerLocked`

**Player** — three senses. See also **domain**, **Shared**.
- *Domain*: The Player domain (`DomainPlayer`) is state that belongs to one local participant — corpus glyphs, drains, nuggets, weapons, projectiles, loot and presentation effects — which each instance simulates for itself and never reconstructs elsewhere; Player streams and entities stay out of shared captures, and only a journal's opening carries them, for that participant's own replay. [Domain design §1](domain-design.md#1-domains-and-identity) · [D-6](domain-design.md#d-6--presentation-and-personal-effects-are-player-domain) · `internal/core` `DomainPlayer`
- *Resource*: `PlayerResource` indexes the bounded cursor roster (slot to entity, and the slot selected for local input and camera) for hot-path lookup, the cursor components being the truth, and holds the D-18 placement prediction. [ECS §5](ecs-and-events.md#5-singleton-resources) · `internal/engine` `PlayerResource`
- *Metrics*: Per-cursor status keys `player.<slot>.<metric>`; a bare key mirroring the local slot differs per instance, so it must never be a shared FSM guard input. [FSM reference: Player Metrics](fsm-reference.md#player-metrics) · `internal/status` `PlayerInt`, `PlayerBool`

**player range** (also: `min_players`, `max_players`) — How many players, bots counted, a scenario runs with, declared beside its description (undeclared, 1 and `MaxPlayers`). `max_players` caps a session's cursors beside `-players`, the lower holding, at admission and on `:n <scenario>`; `min_players` raises a startup lobby's quorum and refuses a `-players` below it. A min of 0 waits for one until a game with nobody in it can run. See also **-players**, **lobby**. [FSM §3](fsm-and-configuration.md#3-root-schema) · [Runtime §1.2](runtime.md#12-the-dedicated-host) · `internal/resource` `Scenario.MinPlayers`, `Scenario.MaxPlayers` · `internal/app` `membership.playersFit`

**-players** (also: `Config.Participants`, `SessionOffer.Players`) — A host's ceiling on the session's cursors, its own included (2 to `MaxPlayers`; unset means the whole roster), beside the scenario's `max_players`, the lower holding. Every offer carries it, so a guest that takes the session over admits no more. A dedicated host drives no cursor, so there it counts guests from 1; on a scripted or bot-driven host an explicit value is also the party the startup lobby waits for, and with a `-join` site it is the session size requested. `-check`, `-schema` and `-replay` refuse it. See also **lobby**, **roster**, **dedicated host**. [Development: Session](development.md#session) · [Runtime §1.0](runtime.md#10-the-four-session-flags) · [Runtime §1.2](runtime.md#12-the-dedicated-host) · `internal/app` `Config.Participants`, `membership.sessionCapacity`, `membership.lobbyQuorum`

**PlayerWorldState** (also: CapturePlayerWorld, InstallPlayerWorld, PlayerSide) — Every Player-domain component row of a world by store, its placements and the ones a full cell holds out of the spatial index, and the Player allocator's next entity and totals; `InstallPlayerWorld` writes it under the same entity IDs, after the shared world. `PlayerSide` is the resources the player holds beside it: camera, APM and gesture tracking, timed effects, Player streams and the D-18 prediction. Both ride a journal's opening. See also **opening**, **SharedWorldState**. [Logging: Opening mid-run](logging-and-diagnostics.md#opening-mid-run) · `internal/engine` `PlayerWorldState` `PlayerSide`

**playfield** (also: playfield clip) — The screen cells a simulation coordinate can reach: smaller than the game area when the map is smaller than the viewport, and centred in a margin no cell occupies; simulation layers clip to it and screen layers to the whole buffer. [Rendering §2.1](rendering.md#21-the-playfield-clip) · `internal/render` `RenderContext.PlayfieldRect`, `PlayfieldViewportRect`, `RenderPriority.ClipsToPlayfield`

**playout buffer** (also: held correction, hold window) — A correction or manifest for a tick this instance has not reached, within `NetworkHoldTicks`, is held and compared at the close of exactly that tick, the newest arrival replacing a waiting one; one further ahead is adopted at its own tick as a jump, and one behind is projected. See also **projection**. [Netcode design §2.1](netcode-design.md#21-the-playout-buffer) · `internal/converge` `Corrections.TickClosed`

**playout lead** (also: lead, receive lead, own lead, `BarrierDelayTicks`, `network.barrier_delay_ticks`) — How many ticks after production a participant stamps its own crossings to apply: the worst recent age at which the authority committed its epochs, stamped on the committed copies (`WireBatch.Age`), plus a relay tick, held between the min and max bounds, or the default `NetworkBarrierDelayTicks` (3) until a copy comes back; the authority's is one tick. The lead in force is `NetworkResource.BarrierDelayTicks`, reported as `network.barrier_delay_ticks`, and each change is a journaled local `EventPlayoutLead`. Every copy applies the crossing there unless it reaches the authority **late**. See also **apply tick**, **barrier**, **crossing**. [Multi-player §3.5](multi-player.md#35-command-frames-the-commit-and-pacing) · [Services and networking §9](services-and-networking.md#9-poll-boundary-and-receive-lead) · [Domain design §4.1](domain-design.md#41-receive-lead) · `internal/system` `NetworkSystem.ownLead`, `driveOwnLead`

**Point** (also: `vmath.Point`) — An integer grid cell coordinate; `Point.Center` converts it to the precise (float) position of the cell's centre, and `PointAt` converts back with floor. [AI and physics §6](ai-physics-and-evolution.md#6-numeric-model-and-determinism) · `pkg/vmath` `Point`, `Point.Center`

**PointAt** — two senses.
- *Math*: `vmath.PointAt` returns the cell containing a precise position, using floor so negative coordinates map to the right cell. [AI and physics §6](ai-physics-and-evolution.md#6-numeric-model-and-determinism) · `pkg/vmath` `PointAt`
- *Bot*: A bot graph action that aims the pointer at the nearest target of a class, clamped to the cells the view shows, moving the cursor there, clicking with `fire = true` (on a `button`, the one on `key` if named, pressing its key) or aiming away with `away = true`. [Bots: Actions](bots.md#actions) · `internal/bot` `mind.pointAt`, `mind.aimButton`

**pointer placement** (also: pointer move) — A mouse, a finger or a bot pointer places the cursor on every cell it names at once as a local prediction, but only the newest cell crosses, once a tick; cells passed between ticks are never visited, and placements count toward APM by distance travelled. [Input §7](input-and-modes.md#7-mouse-and-automatic-fire) · [Domain design D-18](domain-design.md#d-18--local-prediction-is-private) · `internal/engine` `World.PushPointerMove`, `FlushPointerMove`

**poll boundary** — The point as a tick opens where `NetworkSystem.Receive` drains the port's bounded inbound notifications into the world and queues the artifacts now due for the tick's settle, so socket goroutines never touch `World`, the event queue or component stores. [Services §9](services-and-networking.md#9-poll-boundary-and-receive-lead) · `internal/system` `NetworkSystem.Receive`; `internal/engine` `NetworkPort`

**pool** — four senses.
- *Route adaptation*: The shuffled pool of `RoutePoolDefaultSize` (100) pre-sampled route assignments that spawns pop, refilled after each weight update and once fewer than a quarter remain. [AI and physics §2](ai-physics-and-evolution.md#2-online-route-adaptation) · `internal/engine` `RoutePopulation.Pool`
- *Genetic*: `genetic.Pool` is an engine's scored candidate set, its members sorted by score, best first. [Genetic README: Core types](../pkg/genetic/README.md#core-types) · `pkg/genetic` `Pool`, `PoolStats`
- *Payload*: Hot event payloads — death requests, explosion batches, typed characters, and the flash, particle and fadeout batches of `BatchPool` — are recycled through a `sync.Pool` and returned by their consumer. [ECS §9](ecs-and-events.md#9-event-catalog-and-payload-contracts) · `internal/event` `BatchPool`, `pool.go`
- *Music*: The sequencer builds each group's pool of patterns per tier and role, from which it draws. [Audio §7](audio.md#7-music-sequencer) · `pkg/audio`

**position** — two senses. See also **spatial grid**, **position lattice**.
- *Component*: `PositionComponent` is an entity's integer map cell, the collision and rendering projection of any precise kinetic state. [ECS §3](ecs-and-events.md#3-position-and-spatial-queries) · [Generic kinetic §2](generic-kinetic.md#2-current-representation-and-units) · `internal/component` `PositionComponent`
- *Store*: `engine.Position` is the position store that also maintains the spatial grid, so a move updates both; it answers point, area, wall, line-of-sight and free-space queries, and `PositionBatch` stages moves for `Commit`, `CommitShared` or `CommitForce`. [ECS §3](ecs-and-events.md#3-position-and-spatial-queries) · `internal/engine` `Position`, `PositionBatch`

**position lattice** (also: `(run, tick, boundary)`, stamp) — The `(run, tick, boundary)` stamp every journaled record receives synchronously at queue push: run advances on a game reset, tick when a tick body opens, at boundary 0, and boundary when a settle group closes, the body's own first and then each non-empty between-tick group; a run and a written world open at 1. [Logging: Position lattice](logging-and-diagnostics.md#position-lattice) · `internal/event` `Stamp`

**prediction** — two senses. See also **projection**, **prediction ledger**.
- *Shared*: A guest runs the whole Shared world forward between corrections; `World.PredictsShared` is true in a live session somebody else authors. [Domain design D-11](domain-design.md#d-11--the-host-is-exact-guests-converge) · [Multi-player §3.4](multi-player.md#34-predicted-derivations) · `internal/engine` `World.PredictsShared`, `SessionState.Predicting`
- *Cursor*: cursor prediction, the private queue of cells this participant requested and Shared simulation has not yet announced; see **cursor prediction**. [Domain design D-18](domain-design.md#d-18--local-prediction-is-private) · `internal/engine` `PlayerResource.Predict`, `Reconcile`; `World.CursorCell`

**prediction ledger** (also: predicted death) — While predicting, a Shared `EventSpeciesKilled` is stamped `PhasePredicted` and held by dying entity; an install that proves the entity gone raises `EventSpeciesKillConfirmed` once, which loot and boost consume; an entry the authority still holds a convergence floor later, or never allocated, is dropped as a misprediction, and past `PredictionLedgerMax` (1024) the oldest entry is released unproved. [Multi-player §3.4](multi-player.md#34-predicted-derivations) · `internal/engine` `World.ConfirmPredictedDeaths`, `SettlePredictedDeaths`

**presentation** (also: Presentation, Host, terminalHost) — four senses.
- *Host*: `app.Presentation` is what a presenting run uses of a screen: a service the hub runs, the `render.Surface` frames go to, its size, blocked time, events in the terminal's vocabulary and the pointer mode, and for one the player can leave, `Away`, which pauses a solo run, and for one that draws the configuration menu itself, `menuPresentation` (see **menu snapshot**); `terminalHost` is the terminal as one, the Android app's view another, and `Config.Host` names a platform's own beside its speakers' writer. [Multi-platform §8](multi-platform.md#8-adding-another-renderer-or-android-host) · `internal/app` `presentation.go`, `App.setAway`, `App.pushMenu`; `android/game` `view`
- *Build*: The presentation state `App` embeds is build-specific: the terminal variant owns the presentation and render orchestrator, and the headless variant is empty. [Multi-platform §2](multi-platform.md#2-composition-boundary) · `internal/app` `presentationState`
- *Domain*: Presentation effects — lightning, flash, fadeout, splash, particles, a cursor's pulses and the like — are Player-domain and must never decide a Shared outcome (D-6). [Domain design D-6](domain-design.md#d-6--presentation-and-personal-effects-are-player-domain) · `internal/core` `DomainPlayer`
- *Runtime*: A presented run draws on a terminal (`-watch`, replay, play); a headless run of the same script produces the same world. [Runtime §1.1](runtime.md#11-script-pacing-and-presentation) · `internal/app` `Mode.Presents`

**preview** (also: join preview, `MsgJoinPreview`, `Coordinator.Preview`) — The offer a join's seat would carry, naming nobody, which the coordinator sends once it has asked the dialer's claims: the dialer checks the build on it, fetches the scenario and builds its world before any place is held, and a full session previews only a dial whose claim names a place there or while a player who left holds one. See also **ready period**, **join**. [Services §8](services-and-networking.md#8-wire-frame-and-session-messages) · [Multi-player §5](multi-player.md#5-membership-topology-and-authority-continuity) · `internal/network` `PreviewSession`, `SessionOffer.ValidatePreview` · `internal/app` `membership.previewParticipant`, `membership.relayPreview`

**priority** — three senses.
- *System*: A system's per-tick order, an integer from `internal/parameter/priority.go` (lower runs first, registration order breaking ties), distinct from initialization dependency order. [ECS §1](ecs-and-events.md#1-world-ownership) · [Domain design D-15](domain-design.md#d-15--classification-is-declared-once) · `internal/engine` `System.Priority`; `internal/parameter` `PriorityNetwork`
- *Render*: `RenderPriority` is a renderer's layer order, lower drawing first and ties keeping registration order; each priority declares whether it clips to the playfield. [Rendering §7](rendering.md#7-layer-inventory) · `internal/render` `RenderPriority`
- *FSM transition*: Within a state, declaration order is priority; if no transition passes, lookup bubbles toward the root. [FSM §5](fsm-and-configuration.md#5-tick-processing) · `internal/fsm`

**probe** (also: `-probe`, `/health`, `/metrics`) — four senses.
- *Supervised endpoint*: `-probe <addr>` (`-serve` only) serves `/health`, whose code says only whether to restart and whose body carries readiness, and the status registry as Prometheus text at `/metrics` (`vif_` prefix); the router and the session pod's startup and liveness probes read `/health`. [Services §12](services-and-networking.md#12-the-supervised-runs-endpoint) · `internal/probe` `Server`, `Snapshot`
- *Link*: A link probe (`MsgLinkProbe`, answered by `MsgLinkEcho` carrying the far end's `LinkReport`) measures a link's round trip and delivery inside the transport and never reaches the game. See also **link**. [Services §8](services-and-networking.md#8-wire-frame-and-session-messages) · `pkg/network` `MsgLinkProbe`, `LinkReport`
- *Genetic*: Probe bins stratify a species' first gene across its scout samples (seven for the eye type, one per type), so every type stays under evaluation. [AI and physics §4](ai-physics-and-evolution.md#4-game-genetic-adapter) · `pkg/genetic/registry` `SpeciesConfig.ProbeBins`, `TrackedSpecies.SampleScout`
- *Behavior machines*: A machine state's fallible entry check, asked after a candidate's guard on every entry but a spawn, which may stash results in the context. [Behavior machines §2.2](machine-design.md#22-graph) · [§2.3](machine-design.md#23-step) · `pkg/machine` `State.Probe`

**processTick** — `Scheduler.processTick`, one simulation tick under the world lock: it settles what was pushed since the last tick, a pending pointer placement first (`pre`, or `input`), stamps the tick, queues the due wire artifacts, advances the FSM, runs the systems and settles what the tick applies in one settle (`tick`), then closes the tick's production epoch. [Runtime §5](runtime.md#5-simulation-tick-steps) · `internal/engine` `Scheduler.processTick`

**profile** — seven senses.
- *System*: A `SystemProfile` declares a system's domain (shared, player or dual) and its required and optional dependencies, declared once in the manifest. [Domain design D-15](domain-design.md#d-15--classification-is-declared-once) · `internal/engine` `SystemProfile`; `internal/manifest` `ProfileFor`
- *Balance*: `internal/profile` holds per-kind tuning tables: the combat matrix of attack profiles, contact damage, masses, collision and homing profiles and the code species' drop tables. [Gameplay §12](gameplay.md#12-balance-and-ownership-map) · [Combat §5](combat.md#5-resolution-statuses-and-what-crosses) · `internal/profile` `AttackProfile`; `pkg/vmath/physics` `CollisionProfile`, `HomingProfile`
- *Build*: A build profile is a compile-time capability set (full native, `vif_headless`, `vif_noaudio`, wasm, Windows). See also **build profile**. [Multi-platform §1](multi-platform.md#1-current-build-profiles) · `Makefile`
- *pprof*: A CPU, heap or mutex profile (`vif-*.pprof`) written to the log directory on request (`:d cpu`, `:d heap`, `:d mutex`); `:d trace` writes an execution trace beside them. [Logging §11](logging-and-diagnostics.md#11-profiler) · `internal/prof`
- *Snapshot*: `SnapshotProfile` declares whether a system carries Shared state through a capture (`SnapshotNone` or `SnapshotState`, D-19). [Domain design D-19](domain-design.md#d-19--every-future-affecting-shared-value-is-restorable) · `internal/engine` `SnapshotProfile`
- *Particle*: The particle profile table holds one row per particle behavior, everything that differs between dust, decay and blossom. [Gameplay §4](gameplay.md#4-glyph-production-and-decay) · `internal/system` `particleProfile`
- *Soak*: The soak test profile (smoke with `-short`, default, or full with `VIF_SOAK=full`) scales repetition and step counts. [Development §5](development.md#5-validation-and-tests) · `internal/app` `soakScale`

**profiler** (also: `internal/prof`, `prof` and `proc` groups, `:d prof`) — The in-process profiler times every system update, event handler, renderer and engine phase and samples process CPU, memory, GC and I/O over each `ProfWindow` (1 s), publishing into the activity-gated `prof`, `prof.top` and `proc` registry groups; off, a timed call costs three atomic loads. [Logging §11](logging-and-diagnostics.md#11-profiler) · `internal/prof` `Profiler`, `Phase`

**projection** — two senses. See also **replay**, **playout buffer**.
- *Correction*: Installing a capture that is behind this instance's tick by simulating it forward in a staging world, fed the crossings past each source's fence and the later barrier-bound artifacts, then moving the live world onto the result, so the clock never goes back; `snapshot.projected_ticks` is the distance. [Multi-player §3.3](multi-player.md#33-projection) · `internal/app` `StagedInstall`, `corrector.feedProjectionLocked`
- *Rendering*: A renderer's read-only view of state it does not own, such as charge and teleport lines and the landing marker projecting species state, or the UI and debug layers projecting resources. [Rendering §7](rendering.md#7-layer-inventory) · [Rendering §9](rendering.md#9-ui-and-debug-projections) · `internal/render/renderer`

**proof** (also: proved world) — Evidence that a participant holds the authority's world at a tick: its hash-only answer to a manifest, or, behind a relay, the tick its relay names for it in its own answer (`RelayedLeaf.Proved`), which the authority's floor counts as a direct answer; a relay serves a participant joining through it the first world it has itself proved. [Netcode design §2](netcode-design.md#2-the-correction-pipeline) · [Multi-player §2](multi-player.md#2-current-contract) · `internal/snapshot` `RelayedLeaf`; `internal/converge` `Corrections.ProvedWorld`

**protection** (also: `ProtectionComponent`, protection mask) — Per-entity immunity bits set at creation: `ProtectFromParticle`, `ProtectFromSpecies`, `ProtectFromDelete`, `ProtectFromDeath`, `ProtectFromWall`, `ProtectFromTyping`, or `ProtectAll`, which every cursor carries and which also lets an entity survive a level clear; the mechanic each flag names checks it, while direct `World.DestroyEntity` callers must already know removal is safe. The wall and typing bits are read on glyphs alone, since `ProtectAll` holds them too; `ProtectScenery` sets them with the species and delete bits. See also **scenery**. [ECS §7](ecs-and-events.md#7-destruction-and-protection) · [Gameplay §9](gameplay.md#9-walls-maps-navigation-and-environment) · `internal/component` `ProtectionComponent`, `ProtectionFlags`, `ProtectScenery`

**prototype** — two senses.
- *Event*: The registered payload type of an event, the only type its journal and wire payload bytes are encoded from and decoded into. See also **payload**. [ECS §9](ecs-and-events.md#9-event-catalog-and-payload-contracts) · [Logging: Record and anchor shapes](logging-and-diagnostics.md#record-and-anchor-shapes) · `internal/event` `NewPayloadStruct`, `RegisterType`
- *Behavior machines*: a data species' body, `component.BehaviorPrototype` from its program's `[prototype]`: footprint and header, hit points, combat row, physics (homing and restitution, each `[prototype.physics]` key over the `Behavior*` default), mass, drops, contact, lifetime, its **look** and pattern period, in `Resources.Behaviors` by program. [FSM reference: Behavior Programs](fsm-reference.md#behavior-programs) · `internal/component` `BehaviorPrototype` · `internal/profile` `BehaviorHoming`

**pulse** (also: `PulseComponent`, `PulseSpec`, `PulseStyle`, `PulseSystem`) — The disruptor's delivery, an entity of its own in its firer's domain: a cursor's stays on its orb's cell and strikes species once, crossing its geometry; a mount's is Shared, rides its host, holds while an armed mount is disarmed, and strikes the cursors inside it, the storm green's every tick. It swells from half its radius over `Grow`, striking inside the size it has reached, and draws as a ring, a glow under species, or nothing (`hidden`); a scenario shapes a mount's with `EventMountRequest`'s `pulse` table. [Combat §3](combat.md#3-drivers) · `internal/component` `PulseComponent`, `DeliveryPulse` · `internal/system` `PulseSystem` · `internal/render/renderer` `pulse.go`

**push** — two senses.
- *Events*: The `World.Push*` methods enqueue events with explicit domain handling: `PushLocal` never replicates, `PushCrossing` crosses, `PushEventDomain` stamps a domain, and `PushRecord` republishes a journaled record during replay. See also **crossing**. [Domain design D-7](domain-design.md#d-7--domain-is-explicit-ambient-context) · `internal/engine` `World.PushLocal`, `PushCrossing`, `PushRecord`
- *Log*: `sub="push"` is the trace subsystem that records every push with a short stack, so the producer can be traced without the dispatch flood. [Logging: sub=push](logging-and-diagnostics.md#subpush) · `internal/engine` `World.pushEvent`; `internal/vlog` `ScopePush`

**pursuit resource** (also: PursuitResource, private group) — Navigation's point field toward each cursor, one private group per cursor on every instance, published every tick and computed at its first read, so a player-domain system steering what one cursor owns reads its owner's slot and only that instance pays for the field. [Navigation §5](navigation-design.md#5-target-groups) · [Navigation §7](navigation-design.md#7-loots-seam) · `internal/engine` `PursuitResource`

**pylon** (also: `PylonComponent`, `PylonSystem`) — A stationary ablative Shared composite species with no Kinetic that soaks damage and pushes nearby species away; in the embedded campaign, destroying the tower's pylons resumes `main`. [Gameplay §8](gameplay.md#8-hostile-and-structural-actors) · [Gameplay §10](gameplay.md#10-embedded-campaign-progression) · `internal/component` `PylonComponent`; `internal/system` `PylonSystem`

## Q

**quadrant mode** (also: background mode) — One of the two `pkg/ascimage` conversion modes: a Unicode quadrant glyph represents a 2-by-2 pixel group, where background mode uses one background cell per sampled pixel. [Content §8](content-assets-and-tools.md#8-image-and-pattern-pipeline) · `pkg/ascimage` `ModeQuadrant`, `ModeBackgroundOnly`

**quasar** (also: `QuasarComponent`, `QuasarSystem`) — A 5×3 Shared composite species fused from drains that tracks the cursor, charges and zaps it with lightning when it leaves range, shields while zapping, and absorbs drains; its homing, charging and zapping are a behavior machine, and a stun returns it home and freezes it. [Gameplay §8](gameplay.md#8-hostile-and-structural-actors) · [Behavior machines §4.2](machine-design.md#42-quasar) · `internal/component` `QuasarComponent`; `internal/system` `QuasarSystem`

## R

**RandomVar** — An FSM action that sets a variable to a draw in `[min, max]` that is a pure function of the run's seed, an optional `seed_var` and a required `index_var`, so a sequence is recomputed by index on every instance instead of stored. [FSM reference: Random Draw](fsm-reference.md#random-draw) · [FSM §8](fsm-and-configuration.md#8-actions-and-delayed-work) · `internal/fsm/std` `RandomVarArgs`

**ramp** — two senses. See also **cave**, **drift**.
- *Cave*: `ramp`, the columns a cave closes in over from its centred mouth or its **cave mouth**, and those a **cave opening** widens over. [Gameplay §9](gameplay.md#9-walls-maps-navigation-and-environment) · `pkg/cave` `Config.Ramp`, `Opening.Ramp`
- *Pace*: an integer rate in microcells a second (`MicroRate`) easing linearly to another over a span of nanoseconds, `ease_ms` as authored, which a cave's scroll and each drift layer use; `Cover` gives the exact distance under it, a trapezoid, in rate times nanoseconds with `CellSpan` to a cell, so every instance steps on the same tick. [Gameplay §9](gameplay.md#9-walls-maps-navigation-and-environment) · `pkg/vmath` `Ramp`, `MicroRate`, `CellSpan`

**rank** (also: `RaiderRank1`..`RaiderRank4`, `RaidRank`) — One of vanguard's four raider difficulty levels, a macro every raider type's program invokes with its cave and its look: a rank up has more hit points, a fifth more mass, a costlier touch and a slower chase on a longer lock, alike in every cave. The `ramp` region's `RaidRank` states climb them, three player kills of a rank's types to the next, and the fourth's third to the **boss stage**. See also **tier**, **vanguard**. [Gameplay §10](gameplay.md#10-embedded-campaign-progression) · `wad/scenario/vanguard/macros.toml`

**raw epoch** (also: raw artifact) — A guest's production epoch before the authority commits it; it travels only up, bundled with those of the participants behind the sender, and nobody else applies it, the committed copy coming back down to every participant, its producer included. See also **epoch**, **upstream**. [Multi-player §3.5](multi-player.md#35-command-frames-the-commit-and-pacing) · `internal/system` `NetworkSystem.sendOwn`, `relayRaw`, `sendUp`

**ray** — two senses.
- *Weapon*: A strip of cells held in a `RayComponent`, run to the first wall or map edge: the emitter's is laid every tick from the cursor through its orb, one cell wide to the orb and three past it, and strikes every tick; a ray mount on a Shared host cycles rest, warning and firing, striking every `RayHitInterval` (250 ms). [Combat §3](combat.md#3-drivers) · `internal/component` `RayComponent`, `RayPhase`
- *Geometry*: `vmath.Ray` is a straight strip of cells in any direction, with one half-width up to its knee and another past it. [Combat §3](combat.md#3-drivers) · `pkg/vmath` `Ray`

**reach** — two senses.
- *Navigation*: one Dijkstra from a cell that measures its weighted distance to several goals at once, what a field seeded at each goal would read at the cell; a snake head locks the cursor it reaches soonest from one. [Navigation §6](navigation-design.md#6-the-snake-lock) · `pkg/navigation` `Reach`
- *Multiplayer* (also: reachability, declared address, `-listen`): In a migrate session a guest binds a listening port of its own before answering the offer and declares it, so the coordinator can publish it in the succession chain (`MsgPeerList`), survivors can dial the successor and, once it hosts, new players join it there, and one following a host through a relay dials that host for a link of its own (`Reach.dialHost`, `NetworkHostDialAttempts` tries); `-no-advertise` or a failed bind leaves a leaf. [Multi-player §5.3](multi-player.md#53-reachability-under--authority-migrate) · `internal/converge` `Reach`, `BindAdvertised`

**ready period** (also: `NetworkJoinPressWindow`, `MsgJoinPrepared`, `MsgJoinSeat`, `RefusalUnpressed`) — How long a coordinator keeps a prepared join's handshake for its seat, 5 s, aligned with the gate's own bound: the dialer says it is ready once its world is built on the **preview**, and asks for its place at its player's press. The coordinator's player is told who is ready (`Coordinator.Prepared`). One that runs out ends the handshake with nothing held, and a later press dials again and seats at once (`membership.takeSeat`), reading Ctrl-C between dials. [Services §8](services-and-networking.md#8-wire-frame-and-session-messages) · [Services §10](services-and-networking.md#10-timeouts-limits-and-security) · `internal/parameter` `NetworkJoinPressWindow` · `internal/network` `PendingJoin.Prepared`, `PendingJoin.Seat`, `ErrPressLapsed`

**reboot gate** — The fleet node's reboot procedure: drain the sessions, reboot, check what a reboot can undo (node Ready, swap off, firewall tables, the log mount, every component current), then rerun the first-session check. [Kube deploy §14](kube-docker-deploy.md#14-the-reboot-gate) · `deploy/k3s/session.sh`

**reconcile** — four senses.
- *FSM*: `reconcile = true` marks an immediate, unguarded `ClassLocal` `EmitEvent` in `on_enter` or `on_exit` that a live capture install re-runs for the state paths it crossed, so a persistent local hold follows the imported state. [FSM reference: Reconciled Local Lifecycle](fsm-reference.md#reconciled-local-lifecycle) · [FSM: Installing a captured machine](fsm-and-configuration.md#installing-a-captured-machine) · `internal/fsm` `Machine.ImportReconciled`
- *World install*: `ReconcileSharedWorld` installs a correction by writing only what differs from the live world, bounded by the correction magnitude rather than the world size. [Netcode design §2](netcode-design.md#2-the-correction-pipeline) · `internal/engine` `World.ReconcileSharedWorld`
- *Cursor prediction*: `PlayerResource.Reconcile` settles one announced placement of the local cursor against the D-18 prediction queue, snapping when the authoritative value is not one it predicted. [Domain design D-18](domain-design.md#d-18--local-prediction-is-private) · `internal/engine` `PlayerResource.Reconcile`
- *Router*: At startup the router deletes any session Job left without a valid Service and any Service without a valid Job. [Router README](../tool/vif-router/README.md#vif-router) · `tool/vif-router` `router.reconcile`

**recorder** — two senses.
- *Flight recorder*: `status.Recorder` keeps a fixed-depth ring of per-tick status-registry snapshots and, on a trigger, flushes it as `sub="rec"` records into the session log, or into a standalone `vif-rec-*.jsonl` when none runs. See also **flight recorder**. [Logging §8](logging-and-diagnostics.md#8-flight-recorder) · `internal/status` `Recorder`
- *Journal*: `journal.Recorder` attaches a replay-journal sink to an event queue (`-j`, `:journal start`) and owns attachment, final counters and file drain. See also **journal**. [Logging §9](logging-and-diagnostics.md#9-replay-journal) · `internal/journal` `Recorder`, `Start`

**redial** — A survivor's first step when its own link to its host drops: a person's run dials that host again under its **claim**, the first host at the door it came in by and one that took the session over where the **succession chain** says, and the run the host admits back into its place replaces this one, as `:join` does. The walk waits up to a window for it, and nothing listening, a refusal no retry helps or no place for the run ends it at once. The claim is let go for the dial, its record as the drop left it, and held again if the run stays. See also **succession**. [Multiplayer §5.1](multi-player.md#51-succession) · `internal/app` `membership.redial` · `internal/converge` `Authority.Redialing`, `Authority.RedialAdmitted`, `Authority.RedialRefused`

**region** (also: `:region`, `:r`) — five senses.
- *FSM*: A parallel instance of the state hierarchy with its own active leaf and path, time in state, pause flag and delayed actions, plus enabled and disabled system lists applied while it is active; every region is Shared state, moved in a live session by the authority's committed transitions alone. [FSM §4](fsm-and-configuration.md#4-states-and-hierarchy) · [FSM §10](fsm-and-configuration.md#10-region-lifecycle-and-system-policy) · [Domain design D-20](domain-design.md#d-20--shared-fsm-regions-move-on-the-authoritys-transitions) · `internal/fsm` `RegionConfig`, `RegionState`
- *Region primitive*: One scheduler-owned operation — list, spawn, pause, resume or terminate — issued by `:region` as a single `EventFSMRegionRequest`; unavailable in a live session or replay. [FSM: Operator region primitives](fsm-and-configuration.md#operator-region-primitives) · `internal/event` `EventFSMRegionRequest`
- *Region file*: A file a region's `file` key names, holding only `[states]`, resolved relative to the entry config's directory and unable to climb out of it. [FSM reference: Configuration Resolution](fsm-reference.md#configuration-resolution) · `internal/fsm` `RegionConfig.File`
- *Render*: The void region is the part of the frame outside the playfield; finalization fills the cells nothing drew there with the void color rather than the theme background. [Rendering §3](rendering.md#3-compositor-data-model) · `internal/render` `RenderBuffer.SetVoidRegion`
- *Profiler*: while `:d trace` records, every timed call is an execution-trace region named `<kind> <name>`. See **trace**. [Logging §11](logging-and-diagnostics.md#11-profiler) · `internal/prof` `StartTrace`

**register** (also: macro register) — A macro slot named `a` to `z` that `qa … q` records parsed intents into and `@a` plays; different registers play concurrently, ordered by playback start. See also **macro**. [Input §6](input-and-modes.md#6-macros) · `internal/mode` `MacroManager`

**registry** — four senses. See also **freeze**.
- *Status*: The status registry: typed atomic metric cells under dotted `group.metric` keys, read lock-free, frozen before the first tick and emitted as periodic snapshots. [Logging §6](logging-and-diagnostics.md#6-status-registry-and-periodic-snapshots) · [Telemetry reference](telemetry-reference.md#the-registry) · `internal/status` `Registry`
- *Event*: The generated event registry maps event names to types, payload prototypes and replication classes. [ECS §9](ecs-and-events.md#9-event-catalog-and-payload-contracts) · [Development §3](development.md#3-generated-code) · `internal/event` `InitRegistry`, `RegisterType`
- *Audio*: The sound and pattern name-to-ID registry, frozen at startup. [Audio §5](audio.md#5-sounds-and-sound-effects) · `pkg/audio` `sound_registry.go`
- *Genetic*: The genetic registry manages up to 256 species populations by id. [Genetic README: registry](../pkg/genetic/README.md#registry) · [AI and physics §4](ai-physics-and-evolution.md#4-game-genetic-adapter) · `pkg/genetic/registry` `Registry`

**:relay** (also: Relay for others, `[play] relay`) — Turns relaying for others on or off: on, the run carries the raw artifacts of any participant that reaches it toward the authority, as a survivor cut off from its host needs; off, the default, only those of the participants that joined through it. Saved in `[play]`; a browser cannot relay. See also **relay**. [Multiplayer §5.1](multi-player.md#51-succession) · [Input §9](input-and-modes.md#9-command-mode) · `internal/mode` `handleRelayCommand` · `internal/engine` `GameContext.Relaying`

**relay** (also: `-relay`) — two senses. See also **raw epoch**, **proof**, **upstream**.
- *Role*: A participant with others behind it: it carries their raw artifacts up and the authority's artifacts down, retains captures to answer their selective repairs and reports their proofs upstream, but authors nothing; a guest is the relay of its own bots and, with `-relay <addr>`, of the people who join there, and of anyone else only once its player turns on **:relay**, its own port then taking a dropped guest's rejoin as its `-relay` door would, what it turns away counted in `network.relay_refused`; its door is named in the chain and, published through `vif-router`, reached behind a NAT (Multi-player §5.6); it lets a survivor go as its epoch window closes once that survivor holds a link of its own to the host. [Multi-player §1](multi-player.md#1-vocabulary-and-boundaries) · [Domain design §4.2](domain-design.md#42-mesh-relay) · [Runtime §1.0](runtime.md#10-the-four-session-flags) · `pkg/network` `RoleRelay` · `internal/network` `SessionRole` · `internal/app` `serveRelay`
- *Relayed join*: A guest serves the join of its own bot, on an in-process stream, or of a person at its `-relay` address, and asks the authority on its own link to allocate the identity and cross the arrival with what the joiner reported (`MsgRelayJoin`, answered by `MsgRelayAdmit`); once the guest hosts, its door admits as the host's does. A relayed person holds no holder, keeps its slot when its relay leaves, redialling the relay's door for a window before it walks the chain, and is a leaf unless it passes `-listen`. [Multi-player §5](multi-player.md#5-membership-topology-and-authority-continuity) · [Services §8](services-and-networking.md#8-wire-frame-and-session-messages) · `internal/network` `RelayJoin`, `RelayAdmit`; `internal/app` `seatLink`, `relayAcceptor`

**release surface** (also: nightly, `SHA256SUMS`, wad archive, release README) — What `release.yml` publishes: Linux client and headless, FreeBSD, an experimental Windows zip, the browser bundle, the wad archive, the Android APK and `SHA256SUMS`, plus the source archive on a tag, each archive with a README for its platform (`make release-readme`); nightly replaces the `nightly` prerelease and pushes the headless image. [Packaging §2](packaging.md#2-release-surface) · `.github/workflows/release.yml` · `deploy/package/release-readme.md`

**relevance** — A correction-scheduling measure: how much of what the next correction moves lies near a peer's cursor (within `SnapshotRelevanceRadius`, 24 cells); it changes when a peer is corrected, never which version of Shared state is authoritative. [Domain design D-24](domain-design.md#d-24--cadence-adapts-the-convergence-floor-does-not) · `internal/converge` `relevance.go`

**render buffer** (also: `RenderBuffer`, compositor) — The terminal-cell compositor: parallel per-cell arrays of cells, touched flags, semantic masks, write mask, clip, background overlay and void region, drawn into with blend modes and finalized for the color mode. [Rendering §3](rendering.md#3-compositor-data-model) · `internal/render` `RenderBuffer`

**RenderContext** — The immutable per-frame value every renderer receives: timing, local cursor, layout, viewport, camera, map and screen size, with the coordinate helpers (`MapToViewport`, `ViewportToScreen`, `MapToScreen`, `IsInViewport`, `VisibleMapBounds`). [Rendering §2](rendering.md#2-render-context-and-coordinates) · `internal/render` `RenderContext`

**renderer** (also: `SystemRenderer`, `RenderOrchestrator`) — A read-only projection of ECS state into the render buffer, declared in the manifest but outside the simulation fingerprint; the orchestrator runs visible renderers in priority order under the world lock and finalizes and flushes outside it. [Rendering §1](rendering.md#1-frame-pipeline) · [Rendering §8](rendering.md#8-renderer-responsibilities) · `internal/render` `SystemRenderer`, `RenderOrchestrator`

**replay** (also: `-r`, `-replay`, `:replay`) — three senses. See also **journal**, **checkpoint**.
- *Journal replay*: Re-running a recorded replay journal on a manual clock, injecting records at their lattice positions, installing written worlds and comparing digests; `-r` presents it as `ModeReplay`, `-r -headless` verifies unattended, and `:replay` (`:r`) navigates it. [Runtime: Replay playback](runtime.md#replay-playback) · [Logging §9](logging-and-diagnostics.md#9-replay-journal) · `internal/journal` `ReplayDriver`; `internal/app` `PlayJournal`, `VerifyJournal`
- *Replay suffix*: The bounded set of crossings an instance retains — its own and those it applied from others — so a projection can re-apply them past an installed capture's fences. [Multi-player §3.3](multi-player.md#33-projection) · `internal/system` `NetworkSystem.LocalReplaySuffix`, `RetainedAfter`
- *Replay copy*: A world cannot be rewound, so a replay viewer going back presents another `ModeReplay` App restored from a checkpoint of the run; one fresh copy waits idle for the next move. [Runtime: Replay playback](runtime.md#replay-playback) · `internal/app` `checkpoint`, `rewinder`; `internal/engine` `WorldCopy`

**reset** — two senses. See also **game reset**, **run**, **session restart**.
- *Game reset*: a new game on the same App, started by `:new`, a defeat or a crossed request; see **game reset**. [Runtime §10](runtime.md#10-reset-and-operator-state) · `internal/engine` `Scheduler.executeReset`; `internal/event` `EventGameResetRequest`
- *Vacant reset*: An unbounded dedicated host replaces a parked world nobody reclaimed within `SessionVacantReset` (1 min), once. [Runtime: An empty session parks](runtime.md#an-empty-session-parks) · `internal/parameter` `SessionVacantReset`

**resource** — four senses.
- *ECS*: A singleton held by the World rather than a per-entity component: time, config, game state, player roster, event queue, RNG, targets, route graphs, adaptation, genetics, transient and view state, status, network, audio and more. [ECS §5](ecs-and-events.md#5-singleton-resources) · `internal/engine` `Resource`
- *External input*: `internal/resource` resolves each external input — scenario, keymap, corpus, audio overrides, bot graphs — through one root precedence, and `-check` validates them without starting a runtime. [Filesystem §3](filesystem-layout.md#3-resolution-policy) · `internal/resource` `Options`, `Check`
- *Host capability*: `FileProvider` and `ContentProvider` are the resource capabilities a build supplies, so systems never open host paths themselves. [Multi-platform §2](multi-platform.md#2-composition-boundary) · `internal/engine` `FileProvider`, `ContentProvider`
- *Kubernetes*: A session pod's CPU and memory requests and limits, growing per seated bot (32 MiB, 64m CPU) and bounded by the namespace `ResourceQuota` and `LimitRange`. [Fleet §6](kubernetes-fleet.md#6-resources) · `tool/vif-router` `botMemoryMi`, `botCPUMilli`; `deploy/k3s/10-quota.yaml`

**rest bar** — The last bar of a phrase whose downbeat moves the melody to another motif or every slot to another group: the melody fades out under the fill and the new idea enters alone on the one; a step along the melody's own ladder needs none. See also **motif**, **fill**. [Audio §7](audio.md#7-music-sequencer) · `pkg/audio` `Sequencer.planPhrase`

**retention** — four senses.
- *Correction*: An authority keeps its last `SnapshotManifestRetention` (4) published captures and their indexes, and every other instance the authoritative worlds it installed or proved, so a relay can answer requests naming them and a successor has a baseline to author from. An index stands over the row encoder's rows for that capture and holds per page their indices and the page hash, nothing else per row; the rows a repair reads are materialised on request. [Netcode design §2](netcode-design.md#2-the-correction-pipeline) · [Netcode §4](netcode-design.md#retention-by-reference) · `internal/converge` `retainedCapture`; `internal/snapshot` `newStoreSection`
- *Replay suffix*: The crossings kept for projection, bounded by ticks, records (`SnapshotReplayRecords`, 512) and bytes (256 KiB). [Multi-player §3.3](multi-player.md#33-projection) · `internal/parameter` `SnapshotReplayTicks`, `SnapshotReplayBytes`
- *Log sink*: Every file-writing logger deletes other `*.jsonl` files in its directory older than 24 hours and the oldest first while over budget. [Logging §13](logging-and-diagnostics.md#13-sink-policy) · `internal/vlog`
- *Fleet logs*: The node's cleanup timer, acting only on the tmpfs log mount, keeps each session's two newest rotations, removes an older one once five minutes old, and removes any file untouched for five hours. [Kube deploy §7](kube-docker-deploy.md#7-the-nodes-log-tmpfs) · `deploy/guest/vif-fleet-log-cleanup.py`

**retry state** (also: `StormSetupRetry`, `MainSpawnGoldRetry`, `StSpawnRetry`) — An FSM state a spawn's setup falls to when the spawn was refused, which waits briefly and re-enters the setup to ask again, so the active state's defensive timeout never retires a region whose spawn did not happen. [FSM §11](fsm-and-configuration.md#11-authoring-pattern) · `internal/asset/scenario` `storm.toml`, `main.toml`

**reveal** — A music build-up on a rising tier: the incoming pattern starts from as many tracks as the outgoing one sounded and adds one a bar, so a build-up never starts thinner than what it replaces. [Audio §7](audio.md#7-music-sequencer) · `pkg/audio` `sequencer.go`

**RNG stream** (also: `RandResource.Stream`) — A labelled deterministic generator derived from (session root, domain, label) and inventoried by `RandResource`; captures carry the Shared streams' positions, Player streams stay local, and a conditional draw seeds from the tick and the entities instead (`MixChain`). [Domain design D-8](domain-design.md#d-8--rng-streams-are-domain-separated-and-restorable) · `internal/engine` `RandResource.Stream`, `SaveStreams`; `pkg/vmath` `DeriveSeed`, `MixChain`

**rip** (also: `Rip`, `RipWAV`, `RipSamples`, `RipParams`, `rip`) — The audio engine's estimate of a recording as what the sequencer would play it with: its tempo and bars, its key and each bar's chord root, and per bar the kick, snare and hihat hits, the melody and the bass line as steps of the sixteenth grid, written by `Patterns` as patterns of up to four bars; soundlab's `rip` adds them to the document and sets the transport's tempo and key. `RipParams` holds its thresholds, each in a range and a gene vector, `DefaultRipParams` the hand-set start and `audio/rip.toml` a tuned set; the engine grades one by ripping music it renders of known patterns (`RenderMusic`, `ExpectedRip`, `CompareRips`), which soundlab's `tune` searches over with `pkg/genetic`. Its front end is a seam (`RipFrontEnd`, `RipFrames`): the built-in analysis or **ripnet**. It keeps its transcript (`Rip.Notes`, listed by soundlab's `notes`), which a voice is fitted to by the engine's own renders (`RenderNote`, `MelDistance`; soundlab's `rip` and `fit`), and its bars compress into a dictionary of one-bar patterns and a **song** (`BarDistance`, `Cluster`, `Rip.Song`; soundlab's `compress`), whose render ripped back is the score of the compression; `refine` closes the loop judged by **fidelity**, the render heard beside the recording through one front end, the steps' pitches moved and the voices fitted until a round gains nothing. The front end reads the recording's **tuning** first (`Rip.Tuning`), a **pickup bar** opens before the first full bar when sound leads into it, and a note off the key keeps its pitch as an **accidental** (`Step.Acc`). Soundlab's `render` writes what the slots hold to a WAV, `corpus` the loops a model trains on, clean or augmented, and `bench` grades the analysis and a loaded model on fresh renders. See also **pattern**, **soundlab**, **ripnet**, **song**. [Audio §11](audio.md#11-ripping-a-recording) · [Rip design](rip-design.md) · `pkg/audio` rip.go, song.go, dsp.go; `internal/soundlab` `cmdRip`, fit.go, dictionary.go, tune.go

**ripnet** (also: `pkg/audio/ripnet`, `ripnet.bin`, rip model, front-end model) — The rip's learned front end: a transformer over the log-mel frames `MelFrames` makes, with heads for the onset, the three drums, the sixty-nine pitches, voicing, the wave and the voice genes of the note under each frame, read into the frames the rip's decoders take; `Spec` is its shape, `tool/rip-train` its PyTorch twin and trainer, and the first root's `audio/ripnet.bin` the weights soundlab hears with. See also **rip**, **transformer**, **voice**. [Rip design §3](rip-design.md#3-learning) · `pkg/audio/ripnet`; `tool/rip-train`

**rock field** (also: `RockField`, `RockLook`, `Position.SetRock`, `ShiftRock`, `RockField.Ahead`) — Rock held as a ceiling and a floor row per map column, with one block mask and the absolute index of column 0, its look (a visual, or a **stone art** preset and seed) laid in `CaveResource` beside it, rather than as wall entities: two **surface**s of the position index, the ceiling solid above and the floor below, which every wall query reads before the walls; a cave step shifts both a column left with the entering column appended, and a capture carries the cave record it is laid from, never the rock. The columns the next tick can bring in wait past its right end, blocking nothing, for a frame drawn between ticks. Rock cannot break cell by cell. See also **cave**, **stone art**. [ECS and events §3](ecs-and-events.md#3-position-and-spatial-queries) · `internal/engine` `RockField`, `RockColumn`, `RockLook`, `Position.SetRock`, `Position.ShiftRock` · `pkg/position` `Surface`

**rod** (also: `WeaponRod`) — The lightning weapon kind: each volley from its orb zaps up to one nearest target per charge, each target once, and at its charge cap (10) each hit arcs once to the nearest other target within `ArcRange`; a mounted rod zaps the cursor it aims at. See also **lightning**, **arc**, **weapon**. [Combat §2](combat.md#2-layers) · [Combat §4](combat.md#4-at-the-charge-cap) · `internal/component` `WeaponRod`, `WeaponSpecs` · `internal/system` `WeaponSystem`

**role** — three senses.
- *Network*: `link.Role` is `RoleNone`, `RoleHost`, `RolePeer` or `RoleRelay`; the transport only binds or dials, and `SessionRole` derives the protocol role from whether the instance authors and whether anyone is behind it. [Services §7](services-and-networking.md#7-transport-roles-and-lifecycle) · `pkg/network` `Role` · `internal/network` `SessionRole`
- *Music*: A pattern's role — rhythm, melody or fill — names the sequencer slot it plays in and, with groups and tiers, where it can be drawn. [Audio §7](audio.md#7-music-sequencer) · `pkg/audio` `Role`
- *Kubernetes*: The router's namespace RBAC `Role`, applied with the fleet objects. [Kube deploy §9](kube-docker-deploy.md#9-the-fleet-objects-and-the-shared-volumes) · `deploy/k3s/40-router-rbac.yaml`

**root** — five senses.
- *Manifest*: The manifest root is one hash over the capture's identity (schema, run, session, seed, map size, authority and term) and every section hash, excluding tick-local header values; equal roots mean a hash-only answer, and a repair must reconstruct the authority's root. [Netcode design §2](netcode-design.md#2-the-correction-pipeline) · `internal/snapshot` `CorrectionManifest.Root`
- *FSM*: `Root` is the implicit shared parent state of every state; user actions on it are rejected because they would run once per region. [FSM §4](fsm-and-configuration.md#4-states-and-hierarchy) · `internal/fsm` `StateRoot`
- *Configuration*: A configuration root is a categorized directory tree (scenario, input, audio, content, image, bot) searched in order: `-config-dir`, the user root, the checkout's `wad/`, then the system roots (`VIF_SYSTEM_ROOTS`). See also **config root**. [Filesystem §3](filesystem-layout.md#3-resolution-policy) · `internal/paths` `ConfigRoots`, `FindFile`
- *Seed*: The root seed (`-seed`, 0 draws one) is the run's base seed; with the session counter, which each reset advances, it derives every simulation and content RNG stream. [Domain design D-8](domain-design.md#d-8--rng-streams-are-domain-separated-and-restorable) · `internal/engine` `RandResource.Root`
- *Composite*: A root composite has no `ParentHeader`; the storm and snake roots are container composites. [ECS §6](ecs-and-events.md#6-composite-entities) · `internal/component` `HeaderComponent.ParentHeader`

**roster** — two senses. See also **participant**, **slot**.
- *Session*: The coordinator's list of admitted peers, each a `RosterEntry` of identity, roster slot (or `NoPlayerSlot`) and holder, the cursorless coordinator included; the offer carries it to each joiner, a handoff preserves it and `SameRoster` compares it. [Domain design §1](domain-design.md#1-domains-and-identity) · [Logging: Naming a peer](logging-and-diagnostics.md#naming-a-peer-counting-participants) · [Multi-player §5.2](multi-player.md#52-the-roster-after-a-loss) · `internal/network` `RosterEntry`, `SessionOffer`, `SameRoster`
- *Cursor roster*: The `MaxPlayers` (16) cursor slots in `PlayerResource`, each recording whether this instance or a remote peer drives the cursor and, for a bot, its holder; every world creates cursors from it in slot order, and a reset keeps its membership. [Gameplay §2](gameplay.md#2-cursor-and-player-state) · `internal/engine` `PlayerResource`, `CursorRosterEntry`

**route** — two senses.
- *Navigation*: One distinct near-optimal path from a gateway's source to its target within a route graph, held as waypoints and its own field over its corridor alone; route adaptation picks one per spawn. See also **graph**. [Navigation §8](navigation-design.md#8-route-graphs) · `pkg/navigation` `Route`, `Waypoint`, `CorridorField`
- *Session route*: The frame every join opens with (`MsgSessionRoute`), naming its session or none, so one address serves several sessions and a participant's port tells a join from a peer link; the fleet reaches a session by name on the front-door, direct and browser routes. [Fleet §9](kubernetes-fleet.md#9-the-session-routes) · `internal/network` `MsgSessionRoute`, `ReadSessionRoute`

**route adaptation** (also: `applyEXP3`) — Online route selection per (route graph, eye subtype): multiplicative-weights (Hedge) updates over mean death fitness with a 0.5% floor, despite the `applyEXP3` name, sampled 90% by weight and 10% as uniform scouts into a shuffled pool of 100 assignments; its state is carried in the capture (D-19). [AI and physics §2](ai-physics-and-evolution.md#2-online-route-adaptation) · `internal/system` `AdaptationSystem`, `applyEXP3`; `internal/engine` `AdaptationState`

**vif-router** — three senses.
- *Input*: `mode.Router` turns parsed intents into game actions and mode changes under the world lock, owns search, history, undo, macros, mouse holds and the configuration menu, and drives only the selected local cursor; nothing it reaches may take the world lock again. [Input §1](input-and-modes.md#1-input-pipeline) · [Runtime: Non-reentrant router rule](runtime.md#non-reentrant-router-rule) · `internal/mode` `Router`, `Router.Handle`
- *Events*: `event.Router` dispatches each queued event, single-threaded, to the handlers registered for its type, in registration order. [ECS §8](ecs-and-events.md#8-event-transport) · `internal/event` `Router`, `Handler`
- *Router*: `tcpRouter` (the front door) and `wsRouter` (the browser route) place a dial on its session's pod by the name in its first frame or URL. [Fleet §9](kubernetes-fleet.md#9-the-session-routes) · `tool/vif-router` `tcpRouter`, `wsRouter`

**run** — two senses. See also **reset**, **position lattice**.
- *Game run*: The world between game resets: the run counter advances once per reset and rebases the tick to zero, so every record is stamped (run, tick, boundary) and an artifact naming another run is refused. [Logging: Position lattice](logging-and-diagnostics.md#position-lattice) · [Netcode design §2](netcode-design.md#2-the-correction-pipeline) · `internal/event` `Stamp.Run`, `EventQueue.NextRun`; `internal/converge` `Corrections.followRun`
- *Process run*: One App the `app.Run` loop builds; another scenario (`:n <scenario>`), `:join`, a session restart or a removal by the host replaces it with a fresh one, which `Config.restarted` builds from the command line and what the restart names. The first restart asked for is the one served. [Runtime §10](runtime.md#10-reset-and-operator-state) · [Runtime §1](runtime.md#1-process-modes) · `internal/app` `Run`, `Config.restarted`

**run-until** (also: `:step … fsm|ev`) — A time-control request that runs, at an optional rate, until the next matching FSM region transition or a named event dispatches, then pauses there or restores the previous rate; it trips once and expires after 20,000 ticks. See also **time control**. [Runtime §9](runtime.md#9-time-control) · `internal/engine` `TimeControl`, `BreakState`

**runtime name** — A system's `Name()` string, such as `soft_collision`, equal to its manifest key; `[systems]` lists, region system lists, `:system` and validation use it. [Gameplay §11](gameplay.md#11-system-inventory) · [FSM §10](fsm-and-configuration.md#10-region-lifecycle-and-system-policy) · `internal/engine` `System.Name`; `internal/manifest` `SystemDef.Name`

**runtime report** (also: `sub="race"`, stderr capture) — One block of captured runtime output — a data race, fatal error, panic, summary or other output — that `-dev` finds in the stderr capture file and logs as a `sub="race"` pointer record. [Logging §10](logging-and-diagnostics.md#10-runtime-output-capture) · [sub=race](logging-and-diagnostics.md#subrace) · `internal/core` `RuntimeReport`, `CaptureStderr`

## S

**sandbox** (also: sandbox/) — The tree of standalone prototypes, visual and interaction experiments, and random and render benchmarks, kept outside application assembly and held to narrower validation than `cmd` and `internal`. [Package map §9](package-map.md#9-commands-tools-and-sandboxes) · [Content and tools §10](content-assets-and-tools.md#10-other-repository-tools) · `sandbox/`

**saturated** (also: saturation, Metrics.Saturated, network.link_saturated, spatial.cell_saturations) — two senses.
