- *Networking*: a link is saturated when it, rather than the sender, limited the delivery rate being measured, shown by a backlog standing across two echoes, or by the sender's frames held waiting on it, or the link standing past the bound the sender paces to, longer than two probe intervals (`Sample.Held`) while its round trip stands inflated past its baseline; a trip inflated alone is jitter or another stream's queue, and frames held at the baseline trip are the sender's own bound on a long link. Until then the measured rate is only a floor on capacity. [Services §8](services-and-networking.md#8-wire-frame-and-session-messages) · [Telemetry reference: Network](telemetry-reference.md#network) · `pkg/linkpace` `Metrics.Saturated`
- *Spatial grid*: a cell is saturated when it first holds `MaxEntitiesPerCell` (31) entities; `spatial.cell_saturations` counts those transitions and `spatial.cell_overflows` the insertions dropped past it. [ECS §3](ecs-and-events.md#3-position-and-spatial-queries) · [Telemetry reference: Engine, event and process](telemetry-reference.md#engine-event-and-process) · `internal/engine` `Position.BindTelemetry` · `internal/parameter` `MaxEntitiesPerCell`

**scale** (also: `-scale`, cell scale, zoom, `[presentation] cell`) — three senses.
- *Presentation*: the cell scale, how many screen pixels a cell takes. The desktop window's `-scale n` draws each atlas pixel as an n-pixel block, nearest filtered, for a HiDPI screen; Android sizes a cell 12 dp tall from the density and stretches the grid that fits over the whole view, filtered. Either way the frame is drawn at the atlas's size and enlarged by the GPU, and a pointer maps through it. The zoom is a cell's height in dp (a CSS pixel in the page) that Android's pinch and the page's pinch or Ctrl and the wheel move within one range, 4 to 40 in half steps, never under the atlas on Android; the run keeps it in `vif.toml` as `[presentation] cell`. [Multi-platform §8](multi-platform.md#8-adding-another-renderer-or-android-host) · `pkg/window` `Config.Scale`; `android/game` `cellDP`, `Start`, `Zoom` · `internal/parameter` `CellLeast`, `CellMost`, `CellStep` · `pkg/browser` `Config`
- *Simulation*: the time scale, a rational simulation rate on the ladder 1/8x to 8x that `:speed` selects. See **time scale**. [Runtime §9](runtime.md#9-time-control) · `internal/engine` `TimeScale` `ScaleLadder`
- *Audio*: the musical scale the harmony resolves pattern degrees through (minor, harmonic minor, Phrygian, Dorian, minor pentatonic, major), Phrygian by default. [Audio §7](audio.md#7-music-sequencer) · `pkg/audio` `ScaleID`

**scenario** (also: scenario.toml, -s, resource.Scenario) — The TOML document set a run plays: an entry file (`scenario.toml`) plus its macro and region files, declaring FSM regions and states, the system baseline and the map floor, read whole into a bounded canonical form (at most 64 files and 1 MiB) identified by name and digest. It resolves from `-s`, the configured roots or the embedded main campaign, and `wad/scenario` also ships td, blank, simon, dummy and vanguard. See also **scenario digest**, **scenario transfer**. [FSM and configuration §2](fsm-and-configuration.md#2-scenario-resolution) · [FSM reference: Root config](fsm-reference.md#root-config-scenariotoml) · [Gameplay §10](gameplay.md#10-embedded-campaign-progression) · `internal/resource` `Scenario` `LoadScenario` · `internal/asset/scenario`

**scenario digest** (also: ScenarioDigest, scenario_digest) — The SHA-256 of a scenario's canonical form (entry, macro and region files) and the only scenario field two runs compare: a join and a replay are refused on it, and a joiner whose own digest differs is sent the coordinator's bytes. `-check` prints its short form. [FSM and configuration §2](fsm-and-configuration.md#2-scenario-resolution) · [Services §7.2](services-and-networking.md#72-join-identity-and-who-decides-it) · `internal/resource` `Scenario.Digest`

**scenario transfer** (also: MsgScenarioRequest, MsgScenarioBody, Options.Provided) — A joiner whose roots hold no scenario with the session's digest asks the coordinator for it during the handshake and receives the deflated canonical form in chunks; it verifies the bytes against the digest and holds them in memory as `resource.Options.Provided`, never writing them to disk. See also **scenario digest**. [Services §7.2](services-and-networking.md#72-join-identity-and-who-decides-it) · [FSM and configuration §2](fsm-and-configuration.md#2-scenario-resolution) · `internal/network` `MsgScenarioRequest` `MsgScenarioBody` · `internal/resource` `Options.Provided`

**scenery** (also: `ProtectScenery`, `Position.IsScenery`, `environment.drift_scenery`) — Text placed while a drift started with `scenery` runs, which `GlyphSystem` stamps with `ProtectScenery` for life: arriving rock and a wall's push leave it, it drifts into rock and walls and is not drawn there, and species sweeps, deletes, typing, motions and bots pass it by. Decay and blossom change it, and the gun's tracers and special fire take it, as any glyph; dust and a vortex pass over it in rock or a wall. Typeable glyphs share the map with it and draw over it, and the spawner paces each kind by its own density, counting scenery only where it shows. The renderer draws it where its layer stands between ticks, hazed by depth (**parallax**). Vanguard's drifting text is scenery. See also **drift**, **protection**, **top glyph**. [Gameplay §9](gameplay.md#9-walls-maps-navigation-and-environment) · [Gameplay §4](gameplay.md#4-glyph-production-and-decay) · `internal/component` `ProtectScenery` · `internal/engine` `Position.IsScenery` · `internal/system` `GlyphSystem`, `EnvironmentSystem`

**scheduler** (also: engine.Scheduler, settle sources) — `engine.Scheduler` advances the simulation: the fixed tick loop at the run's tick (`GameUpdateInterval`, 50 ms, by default), each tick's locked body in `processTick` (session latch, the opening settle, the wire artifacts due, FSM, systems, the tick's settle, epoch flush), and the between-tick event loop that settles queued events. See also **settle**. [Runtime §5](runtime.md#5-simulation-tick-steps) · [Runtime §6](runtime.md#6-event-loop-concurrency) · `internal/engine` `Scheduler` `Scheduler.processTick`

**schema** (also: -schema, JournalSchema, snapshot.Schema, ScriptSchema) — four senses.
- *Multiplayer*: the build-identity versions a joiner and the coordinator compare before a world is built: `snapshot.Schema` versions the capture layout and `event.JournalSchema` the journal records; a journal anchor carries the journal schema and a replay checks it. [Services §7.2](services-and-networking.md#72-join-identity-and-who-decides-it) · [Logging §9](logging-and-diagnostics.md#9-replay-journal) · `internal/snapshot` `Schema` · `internal/event` `JournalSchema`
- *FSM*: `vif -schema` prints generated JSON of the known events and their payload fields, the names an enum field takes, the vocabularies of a scenario, a bot graph and a behavior program (each guard and action with its arguments), comparison operators and script-visible config fields, for authoring tools. [FSM and configuration §2](fsm-and-configuration.md#2-scenario-resolution) · [Development §4](development.md#4-cli-reference) · `internal/manifest` `Schema` `SchemaVersion` · `internal/fsm` `Machine.Vocabulary`
- *Scenario*: the root schema is the top-level layout of a scenario entry: `description`, `min_players`, `max_players`, `macro_files`, `macros`, `[systems]`, `[map]`, `[navigation]`, `[behaviors]`, `[regions.*]` and `[states.*]`; any other key fails the read. [FSM and configuration §3](fsm-and-configuration.md#3-root-schema) · [FSM reference: Root config](fsm-reference.md#root-config-scenariotoml) · `internal/resource` `entryKeys`
- *Authored files*: an authored script and an authored TOML content file each name `schema = 1` at their top level, and a file of another version is refused. [Development: Authored headless scripts](development.md#authored-headless-scripts) · [Content and tools §5](content-assets-and-tools.md#5-authored-toml-content) · `internal/journal` `ScriptSchema` · `internal/content` `SchemaVersion`

**scope** (also: log scope, -ls, position.Scope) — two senses. See also **cursor scope**.
- *Diagnostics*: a log scope is one bit of a category mask, orthogonal to level and resolved from each record's `sub` tag (an unmapped `sub` falls into `tap`), so one noisy subsystem can be silenced; `-ls` and `:log scope` set it by name or letter. [Logging §4](logging-and-diagnostics.md#4-scopes) · `internal/vlog` `Scope` `ParseScopes`
- *Spatial grid*: a `position.Scope` (`ScopeShared`, `ScopePrivate`, `ScopeAll`) selects which half of a partitioned cell a query visits, so Shared species target only Shared entities. [Domain model §3](domain-design.md#3-spatial-and-entity-classification) · [ECS §3](ecs-and-events.md#3-position-and-spatial-queries) · `pkg/position` `Scope` · `internal/engine` `EntityClass`

**scout** (also: GAScoutRate, scout rate) — two senses.
- *Genetics*: a gateway spawns a stratified probe genotype instead of an evolved one for `GAScoutRate` (8%) of its eyes, so every eye type stays under evaluation. [AI and evolution §4](ai-physics-and-evolution.md#4-game-genetic-adapter) · `internal/system` `GatewaySystem` · `internal/parameter` `GAScoutRate`
- *Route adaptation*: 10% of each refilled route pool is drawn uniformly across routes rather than by learned weight. [AI and evolution §2](ai-physics-and-evolution.md#2-online-route-adaptation) · `internal/system/adaptation.go`

**scribe** (also: scribeRun) — The type in `internal/app` that keeps a run's journal and reads one back: it opens and closes the journal, writes the world the run stands in and the anchor that names it, verifies an anchor against the run, installs a written world as a replay and reads and restores a rewind's checkpoints, over the world and the journal package rather than a session; it reads the run through `scribeRun` alone (position and settling, the barrier, the twin a journal opens against, the seats' journals and the replacing run). `App` forwards `VerifyAnchor` and what the glue and the membership read of it. See also **corrector**, **membership**, **journal**. [Package map §4](package-map.md#4-production-package-responsibilities) · `internal/app` `scribe`

**script** (also: -script, authored script, ScriptDriver, sparring pair, script/test.sh) — two senses.
- *Runtime*: an authored script is a bounded, versioned TOML schedule of input actions (an intent, text, an ex-command or an event) at explicit (run, tick) positions, run headless by `-script` or presented with `-watch`; with `-host` or `-join` it plays as an ordinary participant, as the sparring pair (`script/sparring-host.toml`, `sparring-guest.toml`) does against a person. [Runtime: Authored headless scripts](runtime.md#authored-headless-scripts) · [Development: A scripted participant](development.md#a-scripted-participant) · `internal/journal` `Script` `ScriptDriver` · `internal/app` `RunScript`
- *Development*: `script/test.sh` holds the end-to-end checks no Go test makes: `all` runs the asserted ones (`bundle`, `scenario`, `follow`, `fleet`, `deploy`) and prints PASS or FAIL, and `botfitness` and `load` measure. [Development: End-to-end checks](development.md#end-to-end-checks) · [End-to-end checks](../script/README.md#end-to-end-checks) · `script/test.sh`

**Seal** (also: World.Seal, Encoder.Seal, seal) — two senses.
- *Engine*: `World.Seal` freezes a world's system set and binds each system's profiler timer before any tick or settle ranges it; `Scheduler.Prepare` calls it, then `Registry.Freeze`, so the system list and the metric set are both closed before the first tick. See also **freeze**. [Runtime §5](runtime.md#5-simulation-tick-steps) · [Runtime §7](runtime.md#7-lock-and-ownership-matrix) · `internal/engine` `World.Seal` `Scheduler.Prepare`
- *Capture*: sealing a capture sets its integrity hash over the header, the whole parts and each store's row hashes; a receiver proves a body against it as it resolves, and the seal is one of the host's per-round costs beside capture and index. [Domain model D-23](domain-design.md#d-23--the-host-world-is-the-correction) · [Netcode design §3.1](netcode-design.md#31-operating-point-on-the-in-process-mesh) · `internal/snapshot` `Encoder.Seal` `Encoder.Verify`

**search grid** (also: SearchGrid) — The grid every navigation Dijkstra runs on: one passability read into a bordered grid with the open directions of each cell precomputed, the corner rule folded in, and a ring of distance buckets in place of a heap; one per passability tier, moved with the walls (`Move`), each cell beside a change remasked once. [Navigation §2](navigation-design.md#2-flow-fields) · `pkg/navigation` `SearchGrid`

**Search mode** (also: `ModeSearch`, `/`) — The mode `/` enters to type a pattern: Enter searches the map's glyphs forward from the cursor, wrapping round, moves the cursor to the match, keeps a matched pattern for `n` and `N`, and returns to Normal, as Escape does without searching; Backspace removes the last character, and simulation continues. See also **mode**. [Input §2](input-and-modes.md#2-modes) · [Input §5](input-and-modes.md#5-insert-search-and-command-text-editing) · `internal/core` `ModeSearch` · `internal/mode` `PerformSearch`, `RepeatSearch`

**seat** (also: bot seat, BotSeat) — A bot playing as its own participant on a separate headless App inside its holder's process, in its holder's screen (a person's terminal as it resizes, else the holder's `-size` or 120x40), logging as instance `seat N`, journaling only when `:journal start <slot>` asks, and never a succession candidate. Every seat joins its holder on an in-process stream (`seatLink`), and a run in no session first opens one with no listener (`openSessionLocked`), which `:host` can open to others, and a run that leaves its session with `:leave` or a fork seats its bots again in the one it goes on in, each into its place; a seat under a person's run ends only when the run halts it (`membership.seatEnds`), and one under any other run also on its own fork or its holder's; an authority whose only peers are its own seats may pause (`SessionState.SeatsOnly`). See also **bot**. [Runtime: Bot seats](runtime.md#bot-seats) · [Bots: Ownership and leaving](bots.md#ownership-and-leaving) · `internal/app/seat.go` · `internal/engine` `BotSeat`

**section** (also: SectionSummary, SectionRequest, manifest section) — One independently hashed part of a capture in the correction manifest: `meta`, `streams`, `systems`, `status`, `fsm`, or one Shared component store prefixed `w.`. Each is partitioned into pages and summarized by hash, page count and row count; a receiver sends its page hashes only for sections whose hash differs, and the authority returns the pages that disagree. See also **manifest**, **page**. [Netcode design §2](netcode-design.md#2-the-correction-pipeline) · [Services §8](services-and-networking.md#8-wire-frame-and-session-messages) · `internal/snapshot` `SectionSummary` `SectionRequest`

**seed** (also: -seed, Config.Seed, CrossingID.Seed, seed_var) — five senses.
- *Simulation*: the run's root seed (`-seed`; zero draws one and logs it), from which, with the RNG session, every RNG stream derives; it is session identity a joiner adopts and a replay checks. See also **RNG stream**. [Domain model D-8](domain-design.md#d-8--rng-streams-are-domain-separated-and-restorable) · [Services §7.2](services-and-networking.md#72-join-identity-and-who-decides-it) · `internal/app` `Config.Seed` · `internal/engine` `RandResource`
- *Multiplayer*: a crossing and anything derived from it draw from a seed mixed from the crossing's identity (`CrossingID.Seed`), not from a shared stream, so every copy draws the same values whatever tick it lands on. [Domain model D-3](domain-design.md#d-3--cross-the-smallest-shared-outcome) · [D-8](domain-design.md#d-8--rng-streams-are-domain-separated-and-restorable) · `internal/event` `CrossingID.Seed`
- *FSM*: `RandomVar`'s optional `seed_var` reseeds a recomputable sequence within a run. [FSM reference: Random draw](fsm-reference.md#random-draw) · `internal/fsm/std`
- *Genetics*: `pkg/genetic`'s `EngineConfig.Seed` fixes an engine's PCG stream (zero is valid, and the package never picks a wall-clock seed); the game derives each species' seed from the run's shared root, so a reset reseeds every population. [AI and physics §3](ai-physics-and-evolution.md#3-genetic-library) · [§4](ai-physics-and-evolution.md#4-game-genetic-adapter) · `pkg/genetic` `EngineConfig.Seed` · `internal/system` `GeneticSystem`
- *Navigation*: a flow field seeds its passable target cells, or for a blocked target the first passable ring within eight cells as virtual targets with axis-weighted gap costs, and its reverse Dijkstra runs from those seeds. [Navigation §2](navigation-design.md#2-flow-fields) · `pkg/navigation` (flowfield.go)

**selective repair** (also: selective correction, selective exchange, CorrectionRequest) — The manifest exchange that corrects a guest: the authority sends a manifest root, an equal root ends in a hash-only acknowledgement, and a mismatch descends into differing sections and pages, which come back as a shard set proving the authority root, applied whole or not at all. A relay serves it from retention or answers unserved, and every refusal falls back to a keyframe. See also **shard**, **manifest**, **correction**. [Netcode design §2](netcode-design.md#2-the-correction-pipeline) · [Domain model D-23](domain-design.md#d-23--the-host-world-is-the-correction) · [Services §8](services-and-networking.md#8-wire-frame-and-session-messages) · `internal/snapshot` `CorrectionRequest` `BuildShardSet` `ApplyShardSet` · `internal/converge/selective.go`

**sequence** (also: Seq, CrossingSeq, jseq) — four senses.
- *Events*: `GameEvent.Seq` is the queue slot assigned at push, ordering concurrent producers within a tick; `CrossingSeq` is a source's own wire sequence for its crossings; an instance's crossing fence for its own source is the contiguous applied prefix, and for another source the highest sequence it applied. [ECS §8](ecs-and-events.md#8-event-transport) · [Multiplayer §3.2](multi-player.md#32-the-capture-boundary) · `internal/event` `GameEvent` `CrossingID`
- *Journal*: `jseq` is the journal's dense record sequence, so a gap means exactly one lost record, and `cseq` numbers its written worlds. [Logging: Record and anchor shapes](logging-and-diagnostics.md#record-and-anchor-shapes) · `internal/event` `JournalRecord` `JournalCapture`
- *Owner sync*: each owner-state sync carries its owner's own counter, so a reordered packet is dropped, and an install leaves it alone. See **sync**. [Domain model D-13](domain-design.md#d-13--owner-authored-shared-values-have-one-writer) · `internal/event` `CursorStatePayload`
- *Gameplay*: a gold sequence is a timed Shared composite of characters typed left to right. See **gold sequence**. [Gameplay: Gold sequences](gameplay.md#gold-sequences)

**sequencer** (also: music sequencer) — The music pattern player: a 16th-note grid in 4/4 bars with four crossfading slots (rhythm, melody, free or phrase fill, bass), tempo clamped to 80–200 BPM, pattern transitions immediate or quantized to the bar, and drawn music chosen by group and tier. See also **conductor**. [Audio §7](audio.md#7-music-sequencer) · `pkg/audio` `Sequencer`

**serve** (also: -serve, ModeServer, Mode.Serves) — `-serve <address>` runs a dedicated host: an authority with no terminal, renderer, audio or cursor of its own, on the real clock. See **dedicated host**. [Runtime §1.2](runtime.md#12-the-dedicated-host) · [Runtime §1.0](runtime.md#10-the-four-session-flags) · `internal/app` `ModeServer` `RunServer` `App.Serve`

**service** (also: service.Hub, NodePort Service) — two senses.
- *Architecture*: a lifecycle-managed I/O boundary (terminal, audio, content, files, network) implementing `Name`, `Dependencies`, `Init`, `Start` and `Stop`; the hub starts services in dependency order, and each may contribute a narrow capability to the world's resources. See also **capability**. [Services §1](services-and-networking.md#1-service-lifecycle-contract) · `internal/service` `Service` `Hub`
- *Fleet*: the per-session Kubernetes NodePort Service the router creates beside the session's Job, through which the direct route reaches the pod. See also **Job**, **vif-router**. [Fleet design §1](kubernetes-fleet.md#1-what-is-deployed) · [Fleet design §9](kubernetes-fleet.md#9-the-session-routes) · `tool/vif-router` `buildService`

**session** (also: `:session`, SessionState, SessionController, RNG session, OriginSession) — six senses. See also **session name**, **session restart**, **session image**.
- *Multiplayer*: the instances sharing one Shared world under one authority, with its roster, authority term, correction stream and identity (seed, RNG session, scenario digest); `-host`, `-serve` or `:host` opens one, `-join` or `:join` enters it, and `:session` reports it. [Multiplayer §1](multi-player.md#1-vocabulary-and-boundaries) · [Services §6](services-and-networking.md#6-operator-session-surface) · `internal/network` `Coordinator` `SessionOffer` · `internal/app/session.go`
- *Simulation*: `engine.SessionState` is the session a world's simulation reads (live, participant, authority, seats only, peer count), latched by `World.LatchSession` as a tick opens and wherever the transport, links or authority move, and journaled when it changes; `engine.SessionController` is the lock-held interface operator commands use to host, join, change scenario and seat bots. [Multiplayer §3.4](multi-player.md#34-predicted-derivations) · [Logging: Written worlds and notes](logging-and-diagnostics.md#written-worlds-and-notes) · `internal/engine` `SessionState` `World.LatchSession` `SessionController`
- *RNG*: the RNG session is a counter that, with the root seed, names the run's streams; it advances once per game reset, and a join and a replay adopt the anchor's. [Domain model D-8](domain-design.md#d-8--rng-streams-are-domain-separated-and-restorable) · [Runtime §10](runtime.md#10-reset-and-operator-state) · `internal/engine` `RandResource.Session` `RandResource.NextSession`
- *Fleet*: an allocated session is one created on somebody's behalf: the router turns `POST /vif/api/sessions` (players, bots, log level, scenario) into a Job, Service and pod running `vif -serve`, bounded by `-first-join`, `-empty` and `-drain`. [Runtime: The allocated session](runtime.md#the-allocated-session) · [Deploy §11](kube-docker-deploy.md#11-the-router) · `tool/vif-router` · `internal/lifecycle`
- *Diagnostics*: the session log is the process's main JSONL log; `session` is also the log subsystem for membership (scope letter `m`), the `-serve` loop's `session summary` record, the operator-state record of an on-demand snapshot, and the `session.*` status keys for defeat latches. [Logging §1](logging-and-diagnostics.md#1-layer-map) · [Logging: sub="session"](logging-and-diagnostics.md#subsession) · [Telemetry reference: Player, session and progression](telemetry-reference.md#player-session-and-progression)
- *Event origin*: `OriginSession` (`origin: session` in the journal) marks a roster or lifecycle observation the session layer raises, and what it settled (kill confirmations, the session state the simulation reads); it is journaled because no other record implies a roster change, and unlike network origin it can still cross. See **event origin**. [Logging §9](logging-and-diagnostics.md#9-replay-journal) · `internal/event` `OriginSession`

**session image** (also: vif-headless image) — The container image every session pod runs: a `scratch` layer holding the static `vif` binary of the `vif_headless` profile, built once with Docker, checked, and imported pinned into K3s. [Deploy §8](kube-docker-deploy.md#8-the-session-image) · [Fleet design §1](kubernetes-fleet.md#1-what-is-deployed) · `deploy/docker/Dockerfile`

**session name** (also: -name, MsgSessionRoute, session route) — The name a session answers to, set with `-name` on a host or server; a dialer sends it first in a `MsgSessionRoute` frame (at most 64 bytes, the `/name` of a `vif://` link), so one address can serve several sessions and the fleet routes on it. [Services §6](services-and-networking.md#6-operator-session-surface) · [Fleet design §9](kubernetes-fleet.md#9-the-session-routes) · `internal/network` `MsgSessionRoute` `MaxSessionName` · `internal/app` `Config.SessionName`

**session restart** (also: MsgSessionRestart) — The authority's notice that the session is rebuilding on another scenario: it broadcasts `MsgSessionRestart` with the address to redial, rebuilds solo, and each guest rebuilds on its own command line and redials through the mid-run gate. Peers accept it only from the participant the current term names. [Runtime §10](runtime.md#10-reset-and-operator-state) · [Services §7.2](services-and-networking.md#72-join-identity-and-who-decides-it) · `internal/network` `MsgSessionRestart`

**session role** (also: `sessionRole`) — What a run is to its session, read from what the session wrote rather than kept beside it: solo, seats (a session of its own with no door: one it opened for its bots, or the one `:leave` or a fork goes on in), host, server (`-serve`), guest, left (a guest whose session has nobody left but its own bots), or successor (a guest that took the term over, hosting at its own port until `:host` opens a door, then a host). `:join`, `:host`, `:leave` and `:session` answer by it from one table: solo and seats take `:join` and `:host` and have nothing to leave, a host and a guest refuse both and may leave, a successor takes `:host` and may leave, a server stays in the session it serves, and a run its session left may join another or leave, but not host. See also **authority**, **term**, **seat**, **succession**. [Multiplayer §1](multi-player.md#1-vocabulary-and-boundaries) · [Services and networking §6](services-and-networking.md#6-operator-session-surface) · `internal/app` `sessionRole`, `membership.sessionRoleLocked`

**settings store** (also: SettingsStore, FileStore, write back, page storage) — Where a run writes `vif.toml`'s `[play]` back, each value as the player changes it, every other line kept: the file it read when that is the player's own (the user root or the `-config-dir` root), else the user root's, created at the first change as a copy of the one read, which it shadows from then on; in a browser, the page's `localStorage`, handed to the Worker at the start. A document the writer could not edit in place is refused, never stored broken. See also **vif.toml**. [Filesystem §3](filesystem-layout.md#3-resolution-policy) · [Multi-platform §7](multi-platform.md#7-external-maps-and-assets) · `internal/paths` `SettingsStore`, `SettingsFor`, `FileStore`, `SavePlay` · `cmd/vif` `pageStore`

**settle** (also: settle pass, settle group, Scheduler.Settle, PhaseSettled) — two senses. See also **group**, **scheduler**.
- *Dispatch*: Dispatching queued events to the FSM and handlers pass after pass until the queue is empty, capped at `EventLoopIterations` (16) passes; each settle is labelled by source (pre, tick, loop, reset, input, settle). A tick's own settle closes its body, group 0, and each non-empty between-tick group (pre, input, the event loop, an injected `Settle`) advances the journal boundary counter, so replay keeps groups apart and knows which records a tick applied. [Runtime §5](runtime.md#5-simulation-tick-steps) · [Runtime §6](runtime.md#6-event-loop-concurrency) · [Logging: Position lattice](logging-and-diagnostics.md#position-lattice) · `internal/engine` `Scheduler.Settle` · `internal/app` `App.Settle`
- *Prediction*: a settled record (`PhaseSettled`, the default phase) states a fact this instance owns rather than its prediction; an install settles the prediction ledger against the authority's world, releasing each death it proves and dropping each misprediction. See also **prediction ledger**, **event phase**. [Multi-player §3.4](multi-player.md#34-predicted-derivations) · `internal/event` `PhaseSettled` · `internal/engine` `World.ConfirmPredictedDeaths`, `World.SettlePredictedDeaths`

**sfx** (also: sound effect, `SfxVolume`, `SfxLevels`, `[play] sfx`) — A sound played on a trigger outside the music, through the sfx bus: `AudioEngine.Play` takes its sound's ID, under two per sound and 12 in all, rapid-fire dampening and the sfx volume, and the music ducks while it sounds. The game's are `parameter.Sfx`, each with a level and a shape; a scenario requests one by its sound's name. See also **sound**, **music**, **audio**. [Audio §5](audio.md#5-sounds-and-sound-effects) · `pkg/audio` `AudioEngine.Play`, `AudioConfig.SfxLevels` · `internal/parameter` `Sfx`, `SfxSet`

**shard** (also: shard set, CorrectionShard, CorrectionShardSet, MsgStateShard) — One repaired page of a selective repair, carrying the page's whole rows and its hash; a shard set is one atomic repair that repeats the manifest header and root and is applied whole or not at all. See **selective repair**. [Netcode design §2](netcode-design.md#2-the-correction-pipeline) · [Services §8](services-and-networking.md#8-wire-frame-and-session-messages) · `internal/snapshot` `CorrectionShard` `CorrectionShardSet`

**Shared** (also: Shared domain, DomainShared, ClassShared, SessionShared) — three senses. See also **domain**.
- *Domain*: the Shared domain is the state every participant observes and that must converge (cursors, shared species, gold, walls, towers, gateways, map state, simulation time, the shared FSM): authoritative on the host, predicted on guests and carried by corrections. [Domain model §1](domain-design.md#1-domains-and-identity) · [Multiplayer §1](multi-player.md#1-vocabulary-and-boundaries) · `internal/core` `DomainShared`
- *Event class*: `ClassShared` events are emitted and consumed by Shared simulation and re-derived identically on every instance, so they are journaled everywhere and never sent. [Domain model D-10](domain-design.md#d-10--event-class-and-event-domain-answer-different-questions) · `internal/event` `ClassShared`
- *Map bounds*: `World.SessionShared` reports a world that is, or reproduces, one shared with another participant (a second cursor, a session transport, or a latch adopted from an anchor); while it holds, map bounds are Shared and a terminal resize moves only the local viewport. [Domain model D-14](domain-design.md#d-14--map-bounds-are-shared-authority) · `internal/engine` `World.SessionShared`

**SharedKey** (also: SimDeniedKey, comparison surface) — `SharedKey` selects the status keys two instances must agree on and a capture carries; `SimDeniedKey` selects the keys a replay does not compare. See **compared surface**. [Telemetry reference: The registry](telemetry-reference.md#the-registry) · [Domain model §5](domain-design.md#5-telemetry-and-snapshot-views) · `internal/snapshot` `SharedKey` `SimDeniedKey`

**SharedStateSaver** (also: SaveShared, LoadShared, SharedStateChecker) — The interface a system with private future-affecting Shared state implements to carry it in a capture as canonical bytes (`SaveShared`, `LoadShared`); `SharedStateChecker.CheckShared` lets it refuse a record before anything is written. The manifest declares such systems with `Snapshot: "state"`. [Domain model D-19](domain-design.md#d-19--every-future-affecting-shared-value-is-restorable) · `internal/engine` `SharedStateSaver` `SharedStateChecker`

**SharedWorldState** (also: CaptureSharedWorld) — Every Shared-domain component row of a world by store, plus the Shared allocator's next entity and its created and destroyed totals; it is the world body of a capture, and its store names are wire-stable section ids. [Domain model D-19](domain-design.md#d-19--every-future-affecting-shared-value-is-restorable) · [Multiplayer §3.2](multi-player.md#32-the-capture-boundary) · `internal/engine` `SharedWorldState` `World.CaptureSharedWorld`

**shed** (also: shedding, -shed-window, `ShedPolicy`, `engine.ticks_dropped`, `network.shed`) — What an authority that cannot keep its own tick does: over `-shed-window`, when its scheduler dropped more than `NetworkShedShare` of the ticks it owed, it dismisses its newest bot, or with none left its newest player, never itself or a Wraith, played or headless and asking nobody; a new run or a change of roster starts the window over. The dismissal carries `Shed`, so every participant reads who went and why and the dropped one says so as it starts a local game. See also **eviction**. [Multi-player §3.5](multi-player.md#35-command-frames-the-commit-and-pacing) · `internal/converge` `Authority.driveShedding`, `ShedPolicy` · `internal/event` `ParticipantDepartedPayload.Shed`

**shield** (also: ShieldComponent, ShieldSystem, IsShielded) — two senses.
- *Cursor*: an ellipse around the cursor, active whenever energy is nonzero, that turns hostile hits and contacts into energy drain instead of heat loss, collects nuggets, supplies Visual mode's bounds, and drains 1% of current energy every second. [Gameplay: Shield](gameplay.md#shield) · [Combat §5](combat.md#5-resolution-statuses-and-what-crosses) · `internal/system` `ShieldSystem` · `internal/component` `ShieldComponent`
- *Species*: a quasar raises its own shield (`ShieldTypeQuasar`) while it zaps, a data species in a state flagged `Shielded`, and a snake head is shielded while its body lives; a shielded species refuses every hit, carried in combat as the ward status. See also **ward**. [Combat §5](combat.md#5-resolution-statuses-and-what-crosses) · `internal/component` `QuasarComponent.IsShielded` `BehaviorComponent.Shielded` `SnakeComponent.IsShielded`

**sigil** (also: SigilComponent) — The visual of a non-typeable moving entity, such as a nugget, drawn by the sigil renderer instead of a glyph, and not shown in rock or a wall, as a glyph there is not. [Rendering §7](rendering.md#7-layer-inventory) · [ECS §4](ecs-and-events.md#4-component-catalog) · `internal/component` `SigilComponent`

**simon** — A shipped scenario in `wad/scenario`: a memory game on four buttons bound to keys 1–4, each with its own note, in which each level replays the sequence one note longer for the player to repeat by key or click. [Gameplay §10](gameplay.md#10-embedded-campaign-progression) · `wad/scenario/simon`

**SimTime** (also: SimEpoch, simulation time) — `SimTime(tick, interval)`, `SimEpoch` (2000-01-01 UTC) plus tick times interval, is the only instant a system may treat as now, so every participant and a replay read the same time at each tick. [Domain model D-21](domain-design.md#d-21--simulation-time-is-tick-derived) · [Runtime §5](runtime.md#5-simulation-tick-steps) · `internal/engine` `SimTime` `SimEpoch`

**SimulatesLocally** (also: World.SimulatesLocally, ResolveOwnedCursor) — The D-2 gate reporting whether this instance owns a cursor's simulation (its control is not remote), so only the owner runs that cursor's weapons, projectiles, drains, nuggets and player effects and writes its owner-authored values. See also **gate**. [Domain model D-2](domain-design.md#d-2--one-instance-simulates-each-player) · `internal/engine` `World.SimulatesLocally` `World.ResolveOwnedCursor`

**sink policy** (also: log rotation, log retention) — The rules every file-writing logger (session log, journal, snapshot, recorder sidecar) applies to its directory: an 8,192-record queue that drops rather than blocks, 50 ms flushes, 64 MB rotation, a 512 MB directory budget, 100 MB minimum free disk and 24-hour retention; a `-log-session-id` fleet process keeps only an 8 MB rotation cap. [Logging §13](logging-and-diagnostics.md#13-sink-policy) · `internal/vlog/vlog.go`

**site** (also: -join site, Request a session) — A router's HTTP(S) origin: `-join` given an `http(s)://` URL, or the menu's request form, posts to its `/vif/api/sessions` and dials the link that comes back, so a restart or rejoin follows that session. The site's own web server fronts the router's API and browser route. See also **vif-router**. [Services §6](services-and-networking.md#6-operator-session-surface) · [Config menu: Multiplayer](config-menu.md#multiplayer) · [Deploy §12](kube-docker-deploy.md#12-the-sites-edge) · `internal/network` `RequestSession`

**-size** — `-size <WxH>` gives a run with no terminal of its own its terminal-equivalent geometry: a headless bot's view (default 120x40) and its seats', or a server's D-14 map latch, which every joiner adopts; a server without it takes its first person's terminal, or 120x40 when it seats bots of its own. [Runtime §1.2](runtime.md#12-the-dedicated-host) · [Bots: Flags](bots.md#flags) · [Development §4](development.md#4-cli-reference) · `cmd/vif`

**slot** (also: roster slot, NoPlayerSlot, TimerSlot, SplashSlot) — six senses.
- *Multiplayer*: a roster slot (0..MaxPlayers-1, shown in hex) names a cursor and binds a peer to it, making it a participant; `NoPlayerSlot` marks a peer that drives none, such as a dedicated host's coordinator, and slot zero is the first cursor, not the local one. [Multiplayer §1](multi-player.md#1-vocabulary-and-boundaries) · [Domain model §1](domain-design.md#1-domains-and-identity) · `internal/component` `CursorComponent.Slot` · `internal/parameter` `NoPlayerSlot`
- *Telemetry*: per-slot metrics register `player.<slot>.<suffix>` for every roster slot, with a bare key mirroring the slot this instance drives. [Telemetry reference: The registry](telemetry-reference.md#the-registry) · `internal/status` `NewPlayerInt`
- *ECS*: a `TimerSlot` is one of an entity's three countdowns (lifetime, deadline, cooldown) that `TimerSystem` ages each tick; a store's dense slot is an index into its packed arrays, and an event's queue slot (`GameEvent.Seq`) orders it at push. [ECS: Timers](ecs-and-events.md#timers) · [ECS §2](ecs-and-events.md#2-entities-and-typed-stores) · `internal/component` `TimerSlot`
- *Combat*: a status slot is one per status kind on `CombatComponent` (stun, burn, ward), never stacked. [Combat §5](combat.md#5-resolution-statuses-and-what-crosses) · `internal/component` `StatusKind`
- *Audio*: a music slot is one of the sequencer's four crossfading layers: 0 rhythm, 1 melody, 2 free or phrase fill, 3 bass, the group's anchor; each holds three players so a transition mid-fade cuts no tail. [Audio §7](audio.md#7-music-sequencer) · `pkg/audio` `MusicSlots`, `slotPlayers`
- *Rendering*: a `SplashSlot` names a splash's purpose (gold timer, typing magnifier) and keeps one splash per purpose. [Rendering §7](rendering.md#7-layer-inventory) · `internal/component` `SplashSlot`

**slow** (also: slow!, slow policy, -slow-window, -slow-late, -slow-bytes) — two senses.
- *Status bar*: qualifiers on the session badge: `slow!` when no feasible cadence meets the keyframe floor, `slow` when the cadence backed off and prediction carries more. [Multiplayer §7](multi-player.md#7-diagnostics-and-operations) · [Domain model §4.3](domain-design.md#43-runtime-parity) · `internal/render/renderer/status_bar.go`
- *Multiplayer*: the slow policy evicts a participant whose epochs reach the authority late at least `-slow-late` times a second of game time over `-slow-window`, on a link carrying at least `-slow-bytes`. See **eviction**. [Multiplayer §3.5](multi-player.md#35-command-frames-the-commit-and-pacing) · `internal/converge` `SlowPolicy`

**snake** (also: SnakeSystem, SnakeHeadComponent, SnakeBodyComponent) — A segmented Shared composite species with a head and trailing body members; it keeps one cursor target and route until the target disappears, becomes unreachable, or another route is over a third shorter. Gateways spawn snakes. [Gameplay §8](gameplay.md#8-hostile-and-structural-actors) · `internal/system` `SnakeSystem` · `internal/component` `SnakeComponent`

**snapshot** (also: MsgStateSnapshot, status snapshot, vif-snap, SnapshotProfile, DetachSnapshot, cursor snapshot) — eleven senses. See also **capture**, **sync**.
- *Multiplayer*: the `internal/snapshot` package holds the Shared-world capture, its manifest and its shard sets, and its counters are the `snapshot.*` status keys; a joiner's capture travels as chunked `MsgStateSnapshot` frames, reassembled up to `MaxSnapshotBytes`. [Services §8](services-and-networking.md#8-wire-frame-and-session-messages) · [Netcode design §2](netcode-design.md#2-the-correction-pipeline) · [Telemetry reference: Snapshot and correction](telemetry-reference.md#snapshot-and-correction) · `internal/snapshot` `SharedCapture` · `pkg/network` `SnapshotAssembly`
- *Owner sync*: an owner-authored cursor snapshot is one `MsgStateSync`, the owner's current values for its cursor; `network.state_applied` counts those applied to remote cursors. See **sync**. [Services §8](services-and-networking.md#8-wire-frame-and-session-messages) · [Telemetry reference: Network](telemetry-reference.md#network) · `internal/network` `MsgStateSync` · `internal/event` `CursorStatePayload`
- *Diagnostics*: the periodic status snapshot writes the status registry as one `sub=stat` record per group every `StatSnapshotTicks` (200) ticks, set with `-lt` or `:log stat`. [Logging §6](logging-and-diagnostics.md#6-status-registry-and-periodic-snapshots) · `internal/status` `Registry.Tick`, `Registry.SetSnapshotInterval`
- *Diagnostics*: the on-demand snapshot is the `vif-snap-<timestamp>.jsonl` file `:t save` writes: the full registry plus context, view, player, world and session records taken under the world lock. [Logging §7](logging-and-diagnostics.md#7-on-demand-snapshot) · `internal/engine` `GameContext.SnapshotContext` · `internal/status` `Registry.Snapshot`
- *Comparison*: three sorted line views of a world digest, the context records and the status registry, read together under the world lock: `App.Snapshot` (everything), `SnapshotSimulation` (no operator surface, for replay) and `SnapshotShared` (Shared only, owner-authored state removed: what instances must agree on). [Domain model §5](domain-design.md#5-telemetry-and-snapshot-views) · [Runtime: Replay comparison boundary](runtime.md#replay-comparison-boundary) · `internal/app` `App.Snapshot` `App.SnapshotShared`
- *ECS*: a system's `SnapshotProfile` (`SnapshotNone` or `SnapshotState`, from the manifest's `Snapshot` field) declares whether it carries private Shared state through `SharedStateSaver`. [Domain model D-19](domain-design.md#d-19--every-future-affecting-shared-value-is-restorable) · `internal/engine` `SnapshotProfile` · `internal/manifest` `SystemDef`
- *Detach*: a slice-holding component (`HeaderComponent`, `GenotypeComponent`, `SnakeBodyComponent`) implements `DetachSnapshot`, returning a copy that shares no storage with the world; `DetachSnapshotValue` applies it wherever a capture or checkpoint reads stores out or writes them back, so a later in-place write cannot change a retained copy. [ECS §4](ecs-and-events.md#4-component-catalog) · `internal/engine` `DetachSnapshotValue` · `internal/component` `HeaderComponent.DetachSnapshot`
- *FSM*: `RegionSnapshot`, `VariableSnapshot` and `DelayedSnapshot` are the parts of an exported `MachineState`, which a capture carries. See also **MachineState**. [FSM and configuration: Installing a captured machine](fsm-and-configuration.md#installing-a-captured-machine) · `internal/fsm` `MachineState` `RegionSnapshot`
- *Rendering*: each drawn frame snapshots its context (game time, local cursor, layout, viewport, camera, map and screen size) in one locked read into a by-value **RenderContext**. [Rendering §2](rendering.md#2-render-context-and-coordinates) · `internal/app` `App.renderContext` · `internal/render` `NewRenderContextFromGame`
- *Deployment*: `probe.Snapshot` (live, ready, reason, detail) is what a supervised run reports at `/health`. [Services §12](services-and-networking.md#12-the-supervised-runs-endpoint) · `internal/probe` `Snapshot`
- *Genetics*: `StreamingEngine.Snapshot` copies a population's scored archive and `Inject` replaces it; `Checkpoint` and `Restore` carry the whole continuation point. [AI and evolution §3](ai-physics-and-evolution.md#3-genetic-library) · `pkg/genetic` `StreamingEngine`

**soak** (also: soakScale, VIF_SOAK, TestReplaySoak) — The seeded replay soak tests in `internal/app`, tiered by `soakScale(short, normal, full)`: `go test -short` is the smoke run, the default profile is what CI runs, and `VIF_SOAK=full`, which no CI workflow sets, runs the wide seed sweep, and a failure reruns alone by its hex seed. See also **fuzzer**. [Development §5](development.md#5-validation-and-tests) · `internal/app/soak_test.go`

**SocketPort** (also: socket port) — The transport's socket implementation of `engine.NetworkPort`: background goroutines read frames into a bounded inbound buffer the network system drains each tick, and the port measures each link with its own probes. See **NetworkPort**. [Services §9](services-and-networking.md#9-poll-boundary-and-receive-lead) · `pkg/network` `SocketPort`

**soft collision** (also: SoftCollisionSystem, CheckSoftCollision, flocking) — Contact between species: a source-by-target matrix of overlap pushes (ellipses, or the Kraken's member footprint) that apply a collision profile as an impulse and open a kinetic-immunity window, plus continuous flocking separation; a Shared target's impulse draws from a stream reseeded by tick and pair. [AI and evolution §5](ai-physics-and-evolution.md#5-physics-primitives) · [Domain model D-8](domain-design.md#d-8--rng-streams-are-domain-separated-and-restorable) · `internal/system` `SoftCollisionSystem` · `pkg/vmath/physics` `CheckSoftCollision`

**solo** (also: solo run) — A run that belongs to no session: nothing corrects its Shared world and no wire artifact reaches its ticks. `:host` makes one the coordinator of a new session. See also **session**. [Runtime §5](runtime.md#5-simulation-tick-steps) · [Services §6](services-and-networking.md#6-operator-session-surface) · `internal/app/session.go`

**sound** (also: SoundDef, sound ID, sounds.toml, SoundShape) — A synthesized one-shot: layers of sources through processor chains and optional buses, validated, registered by name and pre-rendered into variants under the embedder's shape; an sfx plays one, as does each drum of the kit, and `audio/sounds.toml` replaces same-name definitions. See also **sfx**, **drum kit**, **registry**. [Audio §5](audio.md#5-sounds-and-sound-effects) · [Audio §9](audio.md#9-authored-music-and-sound-overrides) · `pkg/audio` `SoundDef` `SoundID`

**song** (also: SongDef, `[[song]]`, timeline, dictionary, `compress`, `song`) — A timeline as data: the patterns each slot plays bar by bar at a tempo and in a key, a `[[song]]` of the pattern document beside the patterns it names (`LoadPatternFile`, `MarshalPatternFile`, `ValidateSong`), resolved to pattern IDs (`Timeline`) that `RenderTimeline` plays offline, the patterns changed at the bar lines. Soundlab's `compress` writes one from a **rip** with its dictionary, the one-bar patterns `<name>_<part>_a`, `_b`, ... that bars within a threshold of one another share, and `song` shows, renders or conducts it through the live sequencer, each bar's patterns scheduled to land on its bar line. The game's conductor places patterns by tier, not by bar; a song is soundlab's form until a level wants a fixed piece. See also **rip**, **pattern**, **soundlab**. [Audio §11](audio.md#11-ripping-a-recording) · [Rip design §2](rip-design.md#2-stages) · `pkg/audio` `SongDef`, `Cluster`, `RenderTimeline`; `internal/soundlab` dictionary.go

**soundlab** (also: cmd/soundlab, internal/soundlab, .slab) — The authoring tool over the game's audio engine and banks: a REPL, a TUI over any terminal and headless `.slab` scripts for validation, apply and revert, audition, sequencing, TOML save and WAV export; a save lands where the next game run reads first. The session, documents, verbs and TUI are `internal/soundlab`, which a host with its own screen and speakers runs in-process; `cmd/soundlab` is its terminal command. [Audio §10](audio.md#10-soundlab) · `internal/soundlab` · `cmd/soundlab`

**spatial grid** (also: Grid, Cell, position index) — The dense map-sized grid of 256-byte cells that indexes entity positions for point and area queries, `pkg/position`'s, a volume of **layer**s one deep on a flat map; a cell holds up to `CellCapacity` (31) entities, Shared first, with the private (Player) half capped at `PrivateBudget` (12); the grid lists its occupied cells, which a checkpoint copy, a clear and the position gauges read in place of every cell. See also **scope**, **saturated**, **blocker**. [ECS §3](ecs-and-events.md#3-position-and-spatial-queries) · [Domain model §3](domain-design.md#3-spatial-and-entity-classification) · [Position](position-package.md) · `pkg/position` `Grid`, `Cell`

**special fire** (also: special attack, fire_special) — The heat-spending attack on Backspace or Space: it converts eligible dark Player glyphs to dust, then consumes all dust into one explosion centre per occupied cell, spending one heat, from overheat first, or nothing when there is no dust to consume; glyphs the blast covers become the next attack's dust (dark ones flash away), and only the centres' geometry crosses. See also **main fire**. [Gameplay §7](gameplay.md#7-weapons-and-attacks) · [Combat §5](combat.md#5-resolution-statuses-and-what-crosses) · `internal/system/weapon.go` `internal/system/explosion.go`

**species** (also: SpeciesType, SpeciesNames) — A hostile or structural actor kind: drain, swarm, quasar, storm, pylon, snake, eye, tower and kraken, each with its own components and system, and the data species a scenario's behavior programs define; drains are Player-domain and the rest Shared, and gateways and bullets are not species. [Gameplay §8](gameplay.md#8-hostile-and-structural-actors) · [Domain model §3](domain-design.md#3-spatial-and-entity-classification) · `internal/component` `SpeciesType` `SpeciesNames`

**speed** (also: -speed, :speed, EventGameSpeed) — The simulation rate a run plays at, chosen from the time-scale ladder with `-speed` or `:speed`; for an authored script or headless bot `-speed` is instead the wall pace, where `max` means unpaced. A live session's is its host's: a joiner adopts it from the offer, the host's `:speed` crosses `EventGameSpeed` so every participant changes at one tick, a guest's is refused, and the authority's per-second budgets count a second of game time. See **time scale**. [Runtime §9](runtime.md#9-time-control) · [Runtime §1.1](runtime.md#11-script-pacing-and-presentation) · [Multi-player §3.6](multi-player.md#36-holding-a-session) · `internal/engine` `TimeScale` · `internal/system` `MetaSystem.handleSpeed` · `internal/mode` `speedRefused`

**spirit** (also: SpiritComponent, SpiritPattern, EventSpiritArrived) — A transient converging effect entity that travels from where it spawned to a target cell over its lifetime along a pattern (spiral, linear, wave, bounce, orbit), raised by the stamped `EventSpiritSpawnRequest`. A spirit whose `Species` names one lands it as it arrives (`EventSpiritArrived`, in the spirit's domain): a drain pair's swarm, on the footprint its spirits fly onto. [Rendering §7](rendering.md#7-layer-inventory) · [Domain model §3](domain-design.md#3-spatial-and-entity-classification) · `internal/component` `SpiritComponent` · `internal/system` `SpiritSystem`

**splash** (also: SplashComponent, SplashSlot, `EventSplashTimerRequest`) — A Player-domain overlay of large bitmap text drawn at an absolute position or anchored to an entity, used for countdowns and the typing magnifier: a countdown stands beside its anchor, a composite header, or without one beside its origin cell, one to each, centred below or above it where it fits, an even width half a cell right, where its digits ink; Android's splash screen, which the system shows while the app loads, is another thing (see **start screen**). [Rendering §7](rendering.md#7-layer-inventory) · [Domain model D-6](domain-design.md#d-6--presentation-and-personal-effects-are-player-domain) · `internal/component` `SplashComponent` · `internal/system` `SplashSystem`

**splice** — three senses.
- *Fleet*: the router copies bytes both ways between a player's connection and the session pod with half-close, ending a direction whose write stalls ten seconds. [Fleet design §9](kubernetes-fleet.md#9-the-session-routes) · `tool/vif-router/tcp_proxy.go` `splice`
- *TOML macros*: an invocation standing as an array element whose expansion is itself an array is spliced into that array. [TOML macros §4](toml-macros.md#4-invocations) · `toml/macro`
- *Correction*: `ApplyShardSet` splices a validated shard set's pages into the receiver's own capture and refuses one whose rebuilt root differs. [Netcode design §2](netcode-design.md#2-the-correction-pipeline) · `internal/snapshot` `ApplyShardSet`

**SSE** (also: server-sent events, /vif/api/logs) — The long-lived HTTP event stream on which the LogWisp node service serves the fleet's session logs from loopback; the router reverse-proxies it at `/vif/api/logs`, and the site's edge must pass it unbuffered. [Deploy §10](kube-docker-deploy.md#10-logwisp-the-node-log-reader) · [Fleet design §1](kubernetes-fleet.md#1-what-is-deployed) · `tool/vif-router/log_proxy.go`

**staging world** (also: StagedInstall, StageShared, staging) — A reusable second App and World on the receiving instance into which a capture is resolved, proved and, when behind, projected before it commits between ticks, so a refusal leaves the live world untouched; it logs as instance `staging`. See also **projection**. [Multiplayer §3.3](multi-player.md#33-projection) · [Domain model D-23](domain-design.md#d-23--the-host-world-is-the-correction) · `internal/app` `StagedInstall` `corrector.StageShared`

**stale** (also: network.stale, network.term_stale, StaleTerm) — two senses.
- *Multiplayer*: a participant is stale when its pacing reads it out of step with the authority's epochs beyond what a trim answers: its last decision stepped, or the latest epoch of the window landed late past `NetworkPaceBandTicks`; an indicator only (the status bar shows `desync <ticks>` from `network.pace_late_ticks`). [Multiplayer §7](multi-player.md#7-diagnostics-and-operations) · [Telemetry reference: Network](telemetry-reference.md#network) · `internal/system` `NetworkSystem.publishStaleness`
- *Authority*: a stale-term artifact belongs to an authority term the session has left and is dropped; `network.term_stale` counts them. See also **term**. [Telemetry reference: Network](telemetry-reference.md#network) · [Multiplayer §7](multi-player.md#7-diagnostics-and-operations) · `internal/snapshot` `Telemetry.StaleTerm`

**stamped** (also: ClassStamped, Stamp, stamp) — three senses. See also **ambient domain**.
- *Event class*: a `ClassStamped` event's replication is decided per event from the domain its producer resolved; it is on the wire only when it targets Shared and `stampedCrossings` lists it, today the direct combat request alone. See also **event class**. [Domain model D-10](domain-design.md#d-10--event-class-and-event-domain-answer-different-questions) · `internal/event` `ClassStamped`
- *Entity domain*: materialize and spirit effects are Stamped entities whose domain is resolved per request. [Domain model §3](domain-design.md#3-spatial-and-entity-classification)
- *Diagnostics*: `event.Stamp` (run, tick, boundary) is a journal record's position in the lattice, and every log record carries the correlation stamps `run` and `tick`. [Logging: Correlation stamps](logging-and-diagnostics.md#correlation-stamps) · [Logging: Position lattice](logging-and-diagnostics.md#position-lattice) · `internal/event` `Stamp`

**star** — The link topology the shipped binary builds: every guest links to the authority at its centre, a guest's bots sit behind it as a two-level tree, and in a migrate session every participant but a seat also links to the first succession candidate; the protocol itself relays over any graph. See also **succession chain**, **relay**. [Multiplayer §5](multi-player.md#5-membership-topology-and-authority-continuity) · [Domain model §7](domain-design.md#7-current-limits)

**start gate** (also: MsgStart, MsgReady, start/ready gate) — The handshake that releases a joiner into the capture that follows (`MsgStart`) and has it confirm the install (`MsgReady`); it waits only on guests still linked, and one unconfirmed after `NetworkJoinReadyTimeout` is disconnected. See also **gate**, **lobby**. [Runtime §1.0](runtime.md#10-the-four-session-flags) · [Runtime: The allocated session](runtime.md#the-allocated-session) · `internal/network` `MsgStart` `MsgReady`

**start screen** (also: StartScreen, `StartScreenRenderer`, Tap to start, Click or press a key to start, touch controls) — What every host opens on, in lixen.com's dark theme: the logo at the centre, the start prompt under it on the site's blue button and a ringed button that opens help. Android's shows "Tap to start" and a `?` that opens the touch controls in a window nearly the screen's size, closed by its ×, Back or a tap outside; Android's splash screen before it wears its page and logo, the logo 128 dp across at the window's centre on both, so a launch shows one screen whose buttons fade in as the splash leaves; the run starts behind it, away and silent, its first game in its start hold and started over at the new size if the screen turns first, and the tap, its start, lifts the screen off it; no key reaches the game before the tap. Every browser page opens on it too, its compiled program held until one tap, click or key press, made at any point of the download or compile; a desktop's asks "Click or press a key to start" and its `?` lists the mouse and keyboard controls. A terminal and the desktop window draw theirs over every row above the status bar while the launch's first game stands in its start hold, or a `-join` launch waits for its press: the prompt on a button, and for the held game "F1 help" at the top right, named by the key help is bound to, which a click opens as that key does; the status bar keeps its line, for `:` and messages. The press that starts the game or seats the join lifts it; later games ask on the status line (see **hold**), and `[play] wait` off opens none. [Multi-platform §8](multi-platform.md#8-adding-another-renderer-or-android-host) · `android/app` `StartScreen`, `MainActivity.play`, `res/values/themes.xml` · `web/index.html` `#start`, `#help` · `web/terminal.js` `begin` · `internal/render/renderer` `StartScreenRenderer` · `internal/app` `Host.StartScreen`

**stat** (also: sub=stat, stat.late, :log stat, `-lt`) — two senses.
- *Diagnostics*: `stat` is the log subsystem and scope (letter `s`) of status-snapshot records, one per metric group, whose period `-lt` and `:log stat` set; an on-demand snapshot's context records travel under it too. [Logging: sub="stat"](logging-and-diagnostics.md#substat) · [Logging §6](logging-and-diagnostics.md#6-status-registry-and-periodic-snapshots) · `internal/vlog` `ScopeStat`
- *Registry*: the `stat.*` keys describe the registry itself: `stat.groups` and `stat.metrics` at freeze, and `stat.late`, which counts lookups of an unknown key after freeze and is a regression when non-zero. [Logging: Freeze](logging-and-diagnostics.md#freeze) · `internal/status` `Registry.Freeze`

**state** (also: [states], FSM state, Snapshot: "state", InputState) — five senses. See also **region**, **MachineState**, **GameState**.
- *FSM*: a node declared under `[states.<Name>]` with a parent (default `Root`) and `on_enter`, `on_update`, `on_exit` and transitions; the loader numbers states by sorted name and compiles root-to-leaf paths for least-common-ancestor transitions. [FSM reference: State definition](fsm-reference.md#state-definition) · [FSM and configuration §4](fsm-and-configuration.md#4-states-and-hierarchy) · `internal/fsm`
- *Manifest*: `Snapshot: "state"` on a system's manifest entry declares private future-affecting Shared state the system carries in a capture. See **SharedStateSaver**. [Domain model D-19](domain-design.md#d-19--every-future-affecting-shared-value-is-restorable) · `internal/manifest` `SystemDef.Snapshot`
- *Species*: a species component's machine state, such as `SwarmState` (chase, lock, charge, teleport, arrive, decelerate), which the swarm's behavior machine steps, or `KrakenState`, which the kraken system steps with its own countdowns. [Behavior machines §4](machine-design.md#4-species-graphs) · `internal/component` `SwarmState`, `KrakenState`
- *Input*: the Normal-mode parser's state (idle, count, character wait, operator wait, `g` prefix, marker color wait, macro register waits); an invalid continuation cancels the pending grammar. [Input §3](input-and-modes.md#3-normal-mode-grammar) · `internal/input` `InputState`
- *Fleet*: an allocated session's `state` is its pod's `/health` body (live, ready, capacity, clock, expiry, guests, bots, phase, tick) as the router returns it in each session row and `deploy/k3s/session.sh state` prints it. [Router: HTTP API](../tool/vif-router/README.md#http-api) · `tool/vif-router` (health.go)

**state directory** (also: XDG_STATE_HOME) — Where runtime output goes: `$XDG_STATE_HOME/vif` when that is an absolute path, else `~/.local/state/vif`, on Linux, the BSDs and the other Unix-like targets (the platform user cache directory on macOS and Windows, `./vif` when no base resolves), with separate `log/`, `journal/` and `music/` streams, overridden by `-l=DIR`, `-j=DIR` and `-mw=DIR`. [Filesystem layout §5](filesystem-layout.md#5-logs-journals-and-runtime-output) · `internal/paths` `DefaultLogDir` `DefaultJournalDir` `DefaultMusicDir`

**status** (also: status registry, status bar, StatusKind) — four senses.
- *Diagnostics*: the status registry is four typed maps of atomic metric cells (`Bools`, `Ints`, `Floats`, `Strings`) under dotted `group.metric` keys, frozen before the first tick and read by FSM guards, overlay cards, snapshots, the recorder and `/metrics`. See also **registry**. [Logging §6](logging-and-diagnostics.md#6-status-registry-and-periodic-snapshots) · [Telemetry reference: The registry](telemetry-reference.md#the-registry) · `internal/status` `Registry`
- *Rendering*: the status bar is the bottom two rows on every host: the status items by priority across the upper one, edge to edge (session badge, time control, FSM phase, energy, damage multiplier, boost, grid state, metrics), and the audio letter, mode label, key cell and typed input or message on the bottom one (a message longer than its room rising onto the items' row while it shows), whose left half Android and the page draw again above a touch keyboard while it covers the screen (`StatusCovered`); on a touch screen three taps anywhere on the bar, or on that copy, raise or drop the keyboard; a status message is a notice, a progress line (what the run waits on, a site's session request or a redial, until it says otherwise) or an alert (`parameter.StatusKind`), each shown for its kind's wall time. [Rendering §9](rendering.md#9-ui-and-debug-projections) · `internal/render/renderer/status_bar.go` · `internal/render` `RenderContext.StatusCovered` · `internal/parameter` `StatusRows`, `StatusKinds` · `android/app` `GameView.barTap` · `web/terminal.js` `barUp`
- *Combat*: a combat status (stun, burn or ward; `component.StatusKind`) is one slot per kind on `CombatComponent` with remaining time, period, magnitude and crediting cursor, aged by combat each tick. [Combat §5](combat.md#5-resolution-statuses-and-what-crosses) · `internal/component` `StatusKind` `Status`
- *Capture*: `status` is the capture section holding the compared status surface. [Netcode design §2](netcode-design.md#2-the-correction-pipeline) · `internal/snapshot` `StatusState`

**StatusToVar** (also: StatusIntCompare, since) — The FSM action that copies a live status integer into a variable as a mark; the `StatusIntCompare` guard's `since` argument then compares the value minus that mark, so a scenario counts kills since its own escalation. [FSM reference: Status registry actions](fsm-reference.md#status-registry-actions) · [FSM and configuration §9](fsm-and-configuration.md#9-guards) · `internal/fsm/std` `StatusToVarArgs`

**step** (also: `:step`, `:st`) — three senses.
- *Time control*: `:step` pauses and grants whole ticks, or runs until the next matching FSM transition or named event; `:step off` disarms it. See also **time control**. [Runtime §9](runtime.md#9-time-control) · `internal/engine` `TimeControl` `StepMode`
- *Drivers*: a caller-driven driver's `Step` advances its run by one unit: a script driver applies the actions due and ticks once, a replay driver ticks once and applies that tick's settle groups, a fuzz driver applies one drawn action, and a bot's driver earns allowance, updates its graph under the world lock, releases intents and ticks. [Runtime: Bot seats](runtime.md#bot-seats) · `internal/journal` `ScriptDriver.Step` `ReplayDriver.Step` `FuzzDriver.Step` · `internal/bot` `Driver.Step`
- *Behavior machines*: `Graph.Step`, one machine update run in fixed phases (global edges, suspend, advance and cues, timeout, pre-tick edges, tick, post-tick edges), each taking at most one transition. [Behavior machines §2.3](machine-design.md#23-step) · `pkg/machine` `Graph.Step`

**stone art** (also: `pattern.Art`, `Art256`, `ArtPreset`, `FaceReach`) — Rock drawn cell by cell from a preset (`none` bare stone, `strata` bands, `cracked` veins, `moss` over upward faces, `crystal` sparse bright points, `masonry` courses of blocks), a seed and the cell: its absolute column and row, its depth from the nearest opening and the side that opening lies on, and its group's size. It is a pure integer function that allocates nothing, so every platform draws the same stone; depth past `FaceReach` draws alike. A **cave**'s `art` names its preset, which vanguard **pick**s by the cave's seed, and the wall renderer keeps each profile column's stone with the columns within `FaceReach` it was read from, drawing again only a column that entered or whose neighbours moved. Every glyph is ASCII or in the console fonts, and on the 256 tier `Art256` draws every colour but a moss or crystal glyph's as the grey of its luma, so a console shows grey rock. See also **rock field**. [Gameplay §9](gameplay.md#9-walls-maps-navigation-and-environment) · `internal/pattern` `Art`, `Art256`, `ArtPreset`, `FaceReach` · `internal/engine` `RockLook` · `internal/render/renderer` `stoneColumns`

**store** (also: Store[T], sparse set, typed store) — two senses.
- *ECS*: `engine.Store[T]` holds one component type as a sparse set: an entity-to-slot index paged by entity ID, a dense entity slice and a dense value slice, with swap removal; `engine.Position` is the exception, mirroring it while keeping the spatial index. [ECS §2](ecs-and-events.md#2-entities-and-typed-stores) · `internal/engine` `Store`
- *Capture*: each Shared component store is one capture section (`w.<store>`), its name a wire-stable section id. [Netcode design §2](netcode-design.md#2-the-correction-pipeline) · `internal/engine` `SharedWorldStoreNames` · `internal/snapshot` `StoreSectionPrefix`

**storm** (also: StormSystem, StormCircleComponent, storm region) — three senses.
- *Gameplay*: a multi-part Shared boss of three circles with 3D orbital dynamics: green pulses an area, red carries a turret it fires in bursts at the nearest cursor, blue materializes swarms in. [Gameplay §8](gameplay.md#8-hostile-and-structural-actors) · `internal/system` `StormSystem` · `internal/component` `StormComponent` `StormCircleComponent`
- *FSM*: the embedded campaign's storm region, entered after three quasar kills; each storm kill doubles the cycle damage multiplier. [Gameplay §10](gameplay.md#10-embedded-campaign-progression) · `internal/asset/scenario/storm.toml`
- *Kinetics*: a storm circle's `Pos3D`/`Vel3D` are authoritative and its 2D `Kinetic` a mirror; before physics `absorbExternalImpulse` folds any 2D velocity change (combat, dust, wind) into `Vel3D`. Future design: the storm bridge, a first-class adapter for that transfer. [Generic kinetic §3](generic-kinetic.md#3-species-kinetic-inventory) · [Generic kinetic §8](generic-kinetic.md#8-feasible-pseudo-system-design) · `internal/system` `StormSystem`

**stream** — six senses.
- *Simulation*: an RNG stream, a labelled deterministic generator per domain. See **RNG stream**. [Domain model D-8](domain-design.md#d-8--rng-streams-are-domain-separated-and-restorable) · `internal/engine` `RandResource.Stream`
- *Networking*: each link is one ordered byte stream (TCP, or WebSocket for a browser) carrying framed messages. [Services §7.3](services-and-networking.md#73-why-a-byte-stream-and-not-udp-or-icmp) · [Services §8](services-and-networking.md#8-wire-frame-and-session-messages) · `pkg/network`
- *Diagnostics*: `journal.Stream` is a loaded journal ordered for replay. [Logging §9](logging-and-diagnostics.md#9-replay-journal) · `internal/journal` `Stream`
- *Combat*: at its charge cap the turret streams bullets between waves, `Stream` of them every `WeaponStreamInterval`. [Combat §4](combat.md#4-at-the-charge-cap) · `internal/component` `WeaponSpec`
- *Audio*: the stream contract is the PCM output every backend writes: 44.1 kHz stereo signed 16-bit, in 50 ms buffers by default. [Audio §2](audio.md#2-stream-contract) · `pkg/audio` `AudioSampleRate`
- *Genetics*: a `StreamingEngine`'s proposal stream: the same `Seed` and operation sequence yield the same stream, which never consults wall time under the default deterministic refill. See **StreamingEngine**. [Genetic README: Determinism](../pkg/genetic/README.md#determinism) · `pkg/genetic` `StreamingEngine`

**StreamingEngine** (also: proposal ring, EvalID, PendingCapacity) — The steady-state genetic engine: a scored descending elite archive, a bounded proposal ring and an open-addressed pending table of evaluation IDs that bounds the evaluations in flight at `PendingCapacity` (default 512) and evicts one only when its window of eight slots holds newer ones; `Engine` is the generational alternative. See also **genetic engine**. [AI and evolution §3](ai-physics-and-evolution.md#3-genetic-library) · [genetic README: StreamingEngine](../pkg/genetic/README.md#streamingengine) · `pkg/genetic` `StreamingEngine`

**strobe** (also: EventStrobeRequest) — A deferred background flash that fills otherwise untouched cells as a post-process; a text console shows it in one step while its envelope is past half its peak. [Rendering §5](rendering.md#5-semantic-masks-and-post-processing) · `internal/render/renderer/strobe.go` · `internal/engine` `StrobeState`

**stun** (also: StatusStun) — A combat status that suppresses movement and is never refreshed while it runs, so a target under fire from two players still leaves it. [Combat §5](combat.md#5-resolution-statuses-and-what-crosses) · `internal/component` `StatusStun`

**sub** (also: sub=, subsystem tag) — The subsystem tag on every log record (app, net, session, admit, converge, journal, domain, system, service, fsm, event, dispatch, push, input, stat, rec, lock, race, crash), which maps to a log scope. See also **scope**. [Logging §5](logging-and-diagnostics.md#5-subsystem-catalog) · [Logging §4](logging-and-diagnostics.md#4-scopes) · `internal/vlog` `ScopeOf`

**succession** (also: successor, succession list, candidate, walk, `SuccessionCandidates`) — Under `-authority migrate`, who authors after the authority is lost. The succession list is the chain's participants in join order less the one lost, one away and a bot, read identically by every survivor with no vote; candidate *k* takes the lost term plus *k* once *k − 1* windows of `NetworkSuccessionWindow` passed and publishes a handoff record, and a survivor follows the first record for the host it followed. A guest whose own link dropped **redial**s its host first; one no record reached past the last turn forks. See also **succession chain**, **handoff**, **relay**, **session role**. [Multiplayer §5.1](multi-player.md#51-succession) · [Services: The authority term and the succession](services-and-networking.md#the-authority-term-and-the-succession) · `internal/network` `SuccessionCandidates` `HandoffRecord` · `internal/converge` `Authority.driveWalk`

**succession chain** (also: SuccessionChain, ChainEntry, MsgPeerList) — The append-only, join-ordered list of participants that declared a listening port, with their addresses, which the coordinator publishes on `MsgPeerList`; every participant but a seat keeps a link to the first candidate, and a participant that declares none is a leaf and never a candidate. [Multiplayer §5.3](multi-player.md#53-reachability-under--authority-migrate) · `internal/network` `SuccessionChain` `ChainEntry`

**surface** — five senses. See also **release surface**.
- *Position*: the position package's word for a boundary no entity holds, a height for each column with the cells on one side of it solid, blocking a mask as a **block** does, laid from its writer's record and shifted a column at a time: the game's **rock field** is two, a ceiling and a floor. [Position §1](position-package.md#1-the-boundary) · `pkg/position` `Surface`, `Side`, `Index.AddSurface`, `Index.ShiftSurface`
- *Rendering*: the three-method sink a finished frame is handed to (color mode, `Flush` of the cell grid, `Sync`): the terminal in a terminal build, and what a graphical or Android host implements to draw the same cells. [Rendering §1](rendering.md#1-frame-pipeline) · `internal/render` `Surface`, `RenderOrchestrator`
- *Comparison*: the compared (shared) surface is the set of status keys and lines two instances, or a run and its replay, are compared on. See **compared surface**. [Domain model §5](domain-design.md#5-telemetry-and-snapshot-views) · `internal/snapshot/surface.go`
- *Operator*: the operator session surface is the flags and commands that host, join and report a session (`-host`, `-join`, `-name`, `:host`, `:join`, `:session`). [Services §6](services-and-networking.md#6-operator-session-surface)
- *Diagnostics*: the logging control surface is the startup flags and runtime `:log` commands that set level, scopes, periods and the recorder. [Logging §12](logging-and-diagnostics.md#12-control-surface)

**survivor** — A participant still in the session after its authority is lost: every survivor freezes its clock, floods a loss notice, **redial**s its host when its own link dropped, then walks the succession list, taking the term in its turn if the session lets it move, follows the first record for the host it followed, and forks past the last turn (once its redial ends under `-authority host`). See also **freeze**, **succession**. [Multiplayer §5.1](multi-player.md#51-succession) · `internal/converge` `Authority.beginSuccession`

**suspend predicate** (also: suspend) — A behavior graph's condition (for example stunned, or no cursor) under which a step stops after its global edges: no advance, cue, timeout or tick, while it still returns the flags the host writes. [Behavior machines §2.3](machine-design.md#23-step) · `pkg/machine` `Builder.Suspend`

**swarm** (also: SwarmSystem, SwarmComponent, SwarmState) — A fast 4x2 Shared composite created from enraged drains that cycles chase, lock, charge, teleport, arrive and decelerate on a behavior machine, may teleport around blocked line of sight and arrive beside whatever took its landing, absorbs drains, and has bounded charges and lifetime. [Gameplay §8](gameplay.md#8-hostile-and-structural-actors) · [Behavior machines §4.1](machine-design.md#41-swarm) · `internal/system` `SwarmSystem` · `internal/component` `SwarmComponent` `SwarmState`

**sync** (also: owner-state sync, MsgStateSync) — The owner-state sync carries one cursor's owner-authored values from its owner every `NetworkSyncTicks`, outside the hashed surface; a correction leaves its sequence alone, so the owner's next sync lands over what the install wrote. See **owner-authored**. [Domain model D-13](domain-design.md#d-13--owner-authored-shared-values-have-one-writer) · [Netcode design §2](netcode-design.md#2-the-correction-pipeline) · `internal/network` `MsgStateSync`

**system** (also: engine.System, SystemDef, system baseline, :system) — two senses.
- *ECS*: a per-tick world updater implementing `Init`, `Priority`, `Name` and `Update`, usually also handling events, declared in the manifest with a domain profile, dependencies and snapshot obligation. See also **domain profile**, **manifest**. [ECS §10](ecs-and-events.md#10-adding-ecs-behavior-safely) · [Gameplay §11](gameplay.md#11-system-inventory) · `internal/engine` `System` · `internal/manifest` `SystemDef`
- *FSM*: the system baseline is the scenario's `[systems]` table (every system but `disabled_systems`, or only `only_systems`), which the running regions' lists narrow, extend and trim; toggles, the operator's `:system` included, travel as `EventMetaSystemCommandRequest`; `cursor`, `camera`, `meta` and `network` never toggle, and no running system is left without a required dependency. [FSM and configuration §10](fsm-and-configuration.md#10-region-lifecycle-and-system-policy) · `internal/fsm/std` · `internal/engine` `World.AllowSystemDisable`

## T

**tap** — four senses. See also **dispatch tap**, **key report**.
- *Input*: a captured key's press where the keyboard reports presses alone: its `EventInputReleased` crosses at once with `lost = true`, and a held key repeats taps. [Input §1](input-and-modes.md#1-input-pipeline) · `internal/mode` `Router.pressInput`
- *Simulation*: a dispatch tap (`Scheduler.SetDispatchTap`) receives every event the scheduler dispatches, before the FSM and systems; a driven bot reads its instance's events through it. [ECS and events §8](ecs-and-events.md#8-event-transport) · [Runtime: bot seats](runtime.md#bot-seats) · `internal/engine` `Scheduler.SetDispatchTap`
- *FSM*: a transition tap is an observer on `Machine.OnTransition` (every transition) or `Machine.OnRegion` (region operations); it feeds transition logs, `:step fsm`, breakpoints and the recorder's FSM trigger, and decides nothing. [FSM: telemetry and transition taps](fsm-and-configuration.md#telemetry-and-transition-taps) · `internal/fsm` `Machine.OnTransition`, `Machine.OnRegion`
- *Logging*: a per-item tap is a trace-level record of one item, such as one dispatched or pushed event (`Log.Detail`); `tap` (letter `t`) is also the log scope that collects any `sub` tag with no mapped scope, so an ad-hoc debugging record stays visible. [Logging §4](logging-and-diagnostics.md#4-scopes) · [Logging §3](logging-and-diagnostics.md#3-levels) · `internal/vlog` `ScopeTap`, `Log.Detail`

**target class** (also: bot target) — The class a bot graph's `PointAt`, `HasTarget` and `OnTarget` name in `target`: `glyph` (typeable text, neither gold nor a composite member), `gold`, `nugget`, `species` (a combat entity other than a cursor or a tower), `threat` (a species, a hostile bullet or a hostile missile), `cursor` (another participant's), `button` (a scenario's button, or the one on `key`, at its cell nearest the cursor; the only one sighted on a map without cursors) or `random` (a cell the view shows, `PointAt` only). Distance counts rows twice, as the terminal draws them. See also **PointAt**, **guard**. [Bots: Targets](bots.md#targets) · `internal/bot` `parseTarget`

**td** (also: tower defense, `wad/scenario/td`) — The standalone 500-by-250 tower-defense scenario: players defend two central towers in a maze against escalating waves. As the largest shipped map it is the heavy case in scaling measurements. See also **tower**.
