[Gameplay §10](gameplay.md#10-embedded-campaign-progression) · [Netcode design §3.1](netcode-design.md#31-operating-point-on-the-in-process-mesh) · `wad/scenario/td`

**telemetry** (also: `:telemetry`, `:t`) — two senses. See also **status**, **overlay**, **snapshot**.
- *Status registry*: the metrics every system, the session layer and the engine publish as status-registry cells, read by the telemetry overlay (`:t`) and its pinned-card HUD, status snapshots, the flight recorder, `vif-log` and the probe's `/metrics`. Overlay cards hold at most 15 metrics, and `/` filters them. [Telemetry reference](telemetry-reference.md#telemetry-reference) · [Input §9](input-and-modes.md#9-command-mode) · [Logging §6](logging-and-diagnostics.md#6-status-registry-and-periodic-snapshots) · `internal/status` `Registry`
- *Correction pipeline*: `snapshot.Telemetry` is what captures, corrections and the selective exchange cost the instance that ran them, published as the `snapshot.*` keys; it is per-instance and outside the compared surface. [Telemetry reference: snapshot and correction](telemetry-reference.md#snapshot-and-correction) · `internal/snapshot` `Telemetry`

**teleport line** (also: `TeleportLine`, `TeleportLineRenderer`) — The line a teleport runs from where its body stands to the centre of the footprint it lands on, filling, holding and receding over the teleport: the swarm's teleport, and a data species' state flagged `TeleportLine`, as vanguard's raiders' `Teleport` is. It is no materialize, whose beams converge from the map's edges on a spawn. See also **charge line**, **landing marker**, **materialize**. [Rendering §7](rendering.md#7-layer-inventory) · [FSM reference: Behavior Programs](fsm-reference.md#behavior-programs) · `internal/render/renderer` `TeleportLineRenderer` · `internal/component` `BehaviorComponent.TeleportLine`

**template** — four senses. See also **30-session.yaml**, **TOML macro**.
- *Fleet*: `deploy/k3s/30-session.yaml` is a template, not an object: the router builds the same Job and Service per session, and `session.sh create` renders the file itself. [Fleet deploy §9](kube-docker-deploy.md#9-the-fleet-objects-and-the-shared-volumes) · `tool/vif-router` `manifest.go`
- *Gameplay*: the cursor template is the heat and energy the boot script declares, applied to every admitted cursor, not only slot zero. [Gameplay §2](gameplay.md#2-cursor-and-player-state) · `internal/engine` `PlayerResource.SetInitialResources`
- *TOML macros*: a macro's body is the template an invocation's arguments are substituted into through `${name}`; an argument's own text is never scanned. [TOML macros §3](toml-macros.md#3-references) · `toml/macro`
- *FSM payloads*: an action's event payload is compiled once at load and shared by every emission, so handlers treat it as immutable; `payload_vars` emits a modified copy. [FSM reference: notes](fsm-reference.md#notes) · `internal/fsm` `Action.Args` · `internal/fsm/std` `ApplyPayloadVars`

**term** — three senses. See also **succession**, **handoff**, **terminal service**.
- *Multiplayer*: the authority term (`AuthorityTerm`) is the session's authority generation. A session opens at `FirstTerm` (1; zero means no session), each successful handoff adds one, and every authoritative artifact carries it: a receiver ignores one from a term it has left and refuses one from a term no handoff record granted. [Multiplayer §1](multi-player.md#1-vocabulary-and-boundaries) · [Services: the authority term](services-and-networking.md#the-authority-term-and-the-succession) · `internal/network` `AuthorityTerm`, `FirstTerm`
- *Telemetry overlay*: a filter term is one space-separated word typed after `/`; every term must occur in a card's group name or one of its metric names. [Input §9](input-and-modes.md#9-command-mode) · `internal/mode` `Router.setOverlayFilter`
- *Code*: the `term` field of `TerminalService` holds the `terminal.Terminal` handle and has nothing to do with the authority term; see **terminal service**. `internal/service` `adapter_terminal.go`

**terminal service** (also: `TerminalService`) — The service that owns the lifecycle of the external `lixenwraith/terminal` module: `Init` creates the terminal, `Start` runs one poll goroutine feeding a 256-event channel with backpressure, and `Stop` finalizes the terminal unless `Retain` keeps it for the process's later runs, which `Release` ends; a panic in the poll loop resets the terminal before the process exits. [Services §4](services-and-networking.md#4-terminal-service) · `internal/service` `TerminalService`

**throttle phase** (also: D-17) — Where a cached Shared derivation stands in its recompute schedule, such as a navigation group's recompute counters on each tier, last targets, route rebuild budget and field refresh. It is captured state, so an install re-derives fields and graphs at the same phase on every instance. See also **D-17**, **flow field**. [Domain D-17](domain-design.md#d-17--throttle-phase-is-state) · [Navigation §4](navigation-design.md#4-throttle-phase-and-laziness) · `internal/system` `navGroupPhase`, `navFieldPhase`

**tick** — four senses. See also **epoch**, **SimTime**, **world lock**.
- *Simulation*: one fixed simulation step of the run's `World.TickInterval`, `GameUpdateInterval` (50 ms) unless a host chose another with `-tick`, which its joiners adopt. `Scheduler.processTick` runs it under the world lock: settle what was pushed since the last tick, stamp the tick, queue the wire artifacts due, update the FSM, run the systems in priority order, settle what the tick applies, then commit the tick and flush its production epoch. Game time is `SimTime(tick)`, never wall time (D-21). [Runtime §5](runtime.md#5-simulation-tick-steps) · [ECS and events §8](ecs-and-events.md#8-event-transport) · [Domain D-21](domain-design.md#d-21--simulation-time-is-tick-derived) · `internal/engine` `Scheduler.processTick`
- *FSM*: `Tick` (`EventNone` in code) is the trigger a region evaluates once per game tick, leaf to root, taking at most one tick transition, usually behind a timing guard such as `StateTimeExceeds`. [FSM §5](fsm-and-configuration.md#5-tick-processing) · `internal/fsm` `Machine`
- *Driven run*: `App.Tick(n)` applies arrived corrections, then advances a headless, replay or script run's `ManualClock` by exactly one interval for each of `n` ticks. [Runtime §3](runtime.md#3-runtime-clocks-and-cadence) · `internal/app` `App.Tick`
- *Pacing*: a tick slip (`engine.tick_slips`) is a missed tick deadline: the scheduler fell more than two intervals behind and dropped the debt rather than catching up. It is process pacing and outside the compared surface. [Telemetry reference](telemetry-reference.md#engine-event-and-process) · `internal/engine` `scheduler.go`

**tier hold** (also: TierRiseHold, TierFallHold, TierFallMargin) — The conductor's hysteresis: a tier holds four seconds after any change before rising, then goes straight to the APM's tier, and falls one tier at a time once the APM has sat 30 under the tier's floor for five seconds. See also **conductor**, **intensity tier**. [Audio §8](audio.md#8-adaptive-game-conductor) · `internal/parameter` `TierTracker`

**tier** — three senses. See also **drop tier**, **conductor**, **vanguard**, **rank**.
- *Music*: an intensity tier (Calm, Normal, Elevated, Intense, Peak) that `TierForAPM` maps from five-second music APM at 60, 140, 220 and 300; the conductor draws patterns for the current tier. [Audio §8](audio.md#8-adaptive-game-conductor) · `internal/parameter` `TierForAPM` · `pkg/audio` `Intensity`
- *Loot*: a drop tier is one roll of a **drop table**, among its ordered entries; a unique tier skips weapons the cursor holds at full charge and any loot it already has on the map. [Gameplay: species loot](gameplay.md#species-loot) · `internal/component` `DropTier`
- *Raiders*: a vanguard cave's eight raider types, two to each **rank**, which the raid climbs to the **boss stage**. [Gameplay §10](gameplay.md#10-embedded-campaign-progression) · `wad/scenario/vanguard/scenario.toml` `RaidRank1`..`RaidRank4`

**time control** (also: `TimeControl`) — One instance's single time surface: clock reads, rate, pause and hold, single steps, run-until requests, standing breakpoints (a `BreakSet`) and the wake signal that cuts short a sleep armed at the old rate. It is instance-local, which is why a live session refuses step and breakpoint commands and takes its rate from its host. See also **time scale**, **pause**, **breakpoint**. [Runtime §9](runtime.md#9-time-control) · [Runtime §8](runtime.md#8-pause-semantics) · `internal/engine` `TimeControl`

**time scale** — two senses. See also **time control**, **token**.
- *Simulation*: `TimeScale` is an exact rational rate `Num/Den` on the ladder 1/8, 1/4, 1/2, 1, 2, 4, 8 (`ScaleLadder`, 1x at `ScaleNormalIndex`), set by `-speed` or `:speed` in play mode (in a session its host's, which every participant takes at one tick); `PausableClock` re-anchors on a change, so time never jumps, and `Wall` is the wall interval a game duration occupies at a rate. [Runtime §9](runtime.md#9-time-control) · `internal/engine` `TimeScale`, `ScaleLadder`
- *Scripts and bots*: for a script or bot run, headless or watched, `-speed` is the run's wall pace rather than a simulation rate, and `max` (`ScriptPaceMax`) removes pacing; `max` is refused with `-host`, `-join` or seated bots. [Runtime §1.1](runtime.md#11-script-pacing-and-presentation) · `internal/app` `scriptPacing`

**TimeInState** — A region's time since its last transition (`RegionState.TimeInState`), which `StateTimeExceeds` reads. It is region-scoped, so a timeout declared on an ancestor restarts whenever a child state changes; action-only and internal transitions leave it alone. [FSM reference: timing caveat](fsm-reference.md#timing-caveat) · [FSM §7](fsm-and-configuration.md#7-transition-semantics) · `internal/fsm` `RegionState`

**timer** (also: `armTimer`, `timerRunning`) — three senses. See also **slot**, **helper file**.
- *ECS*: `TimerComponent` gives an entity three countdowns indexed by `TimerSlot` (`TimerLifetime`, `TimerDeadline`, `TimerCooldown`) that `TimerSystem` ages at the start of each tick; an expired lifetime destroys the entity, while a deadline or cooldown waits for its owner to act. Any system arms a slot with `armTimer`, which attaches the timer on first use, and tests one with `timerRunning`; with the helper files, these are the exception to a system file serving only its own system. [ECS and events: timers](ecs-and-events.md#timers) · `internal/component` `TimerComponent` · `internal/system` `TimerSystem`
- *Profiler*: `prof.Timer` accumulates one module's calls (a system's update, a handler, a renderer or an engine phase) within the profiler's open window. [Logging §11](logging-and-diagnostics.md#11-profiler) · `internal/prof` `Timer`, `Profiler.Timer`
- *Fleet node*: systemd timer units on the node: `vif-fleet-log-cleanup.timer` owns session-log retention and `vif-router-token.timer` rotates the router's ServiceAccount token. [Fleet deploy §7](kube-docker-deploy.md#7-the-nodes-log-tmpfs) · [Fleet deploy §11](kube-docker-deploy.md#11-the-router) · `deploy/guest`

**token** — three senses.
- *Runtime*: the frame token (`frameReady`) the main loop returns to the scheduler after each render, which holds the simulation to one outstanding update per frame; the scheduler waits for it only up to a timeout, and not at all above 1x. [Runtime §3](runtime.md#3-runtime-clocks-and-cadence) · [Runtime §9](runtime.md#9-time-control) · `internal/app` `loop.go` · `internal/engine` `Scheduler.awaitFrame`
- *Fleet*: the router's Kubernetes credential is a short-lived ServiceAccount token in a file, rotated by `vif-router-token.timer` and re-read on every request. [Fleet deploy §11](kube-docker-deploy.md#11-the-router) · `deploy/guest/vif-router-refresh-token.sh`
- *Speed*: a ladder token is one rate as written for `-speed` or `:speed` (`1/8` to `8`), which a journal anchor records exactly. See **time scale**. [Logging: record and anchor shapes](logging-and-diagnostics.md#record-and-anchor-shapes) · `internal/engine` `ParseScale`

**TOML macro** (also: `[macros]`, `macro_files`, `toml/macro`) — A `[macros.<Name>]` table with typed `params`, `defaults`, a `doc` line and a `body`; any table with a `macro` key is an invocation, replaced by the body with its arguments substituted. Expansion runs on the parsed tree before `toml.Decode`, is deterministic, and has no built-in library, so equal scenario bytes expand alike on every peer. Scenarios with their behavior programs, bot graphs and the audio banks use it. See also **macro**. [TOML macros §2](toml-macros.md#2-definitions) · [TOML macros §5](toml-macros.md#5-where-macros-live) · `toml/macro` `ExpandDocument`

**top glyph** (also: `TopGlyphAt`, `TopGlyphIs`, `IsTypeable`) — The one glyph of a cell that is drawn and that typing acts on: `Position.TopGlyphAt` passes over **scenery** and picks a Shared (gold) glyph before a player glyph, then the nearer drift layer, then the lower entity ID; `TopGlyphIs` answers whether a typeable entity is the one drawn, by the same order, and from the cell's length when it is alone; scenery is drawn in its layer's order instead, far to near and under typeable text (**parallax**). Motions, markers and bots look through scenery by `Position.IsTypeable`. [Gameplay §3](gameplay.md#3-typing-rules) · [Rendering §7](rendering.md#7-layer-inventory) · `internal/engine` `Position.TopGlyphAt`, `Position.TopGlyphIs`, `Position.IsTypeable`

**TopoSort** (also: `core.TopoSort`) — The deterministic Kahn topological sort behind both service start order and system dependency checks; it reports a missing dependency or a cycle as a typed error (`UnknownDependencyError`, `CycleError`). [Services §2](services-and-networking.md#2-dependency-ordering-and-rollback) · [ECS and events §1](ecs-and-events.md#1-world-ownership) · `internal/core` `TopoSort`

**tower** (also: `TowerComponent`, `TowerSystem`) — A Shared-domain stationary ablative composite that players defend and eyes attack; it blocks cursor placement and is the objective of tower defense, both the campaign's tower region and the `td` scenario. It owns itself: no player's weapon targets it, and its loss is the region's defeat, nobody's kill and no `kills.*` count. `TowerType` only picks its color. See also **td**. [Gameplay §8](gameplay.md#8-hostile-and-structural-actors) · [Gameplay §10](gameplay.md#10-embedded-campaign-progression) · `internal/component` `TowerComponent`

**trace** — three senses. See also **level**, **vif-cpu**, **tap**.
- *Log level*: `TRACE` (`vlog.LevelTrace`, `-lv trace`) is the most verbose level, for per-item records such as each dispatched or pushed event. [Logging §3](logging-and-diagnostics.md#3-levels) · `internal/vlog` `LevelTrace`
- *Call chain*: the `trace` field of a record written through `Log.Trace` is a `->`-joined call chain, such as the holder of a long world-lock hold. [Logging §2](logging-and-diagnostics.md#2-record-shape) · [Logging §14](logging-and-diagnostics.md#14-call-site-rules) · `internal/vlog` `Log.Trace`
- *Execution trace*: `:d trace [s]` records a Go execution trace to `vif-trace-<time>.out` for `go tool trace`, with one region per timed module. [Logging §11](logging-and-diagnostics.md#11-profiler) · `internal/prof` `StartTrace`

**tracer** (also: `TracerComponent`) — The gun's projectile: a head sweeping its row or column cell by cell with a fading trail, which strikes the first wall or combatant it meets and changes the glyphs it crosses by the cursor's polarity. Its impact chains into a derived lightning attack. See also **weapon**. [Combat §3](combat.md#3-drivers) · [Combat §5](combat.md#5-resolution-statuses-and-what-crosses) · `internal/component` `TracerComponent` · `internal/render/renderer` `TracerRenderer`

**transformer** (also: `pkg/transformer`, weight file, ALiBi) — Inference over sequences of frames in pure Go, float32, no CGO: a linear input, pre-norm attention blocks whose heads lean on nearer frames by a slope per head (ALiBi) within a window, exact-GELU feed-forwards, a final norm and named output heads; `Load` reads the versioned weight file a trainer writes, tensors by name with an FNV trailer, and `Forward` runs it. Standard library only; **ripnet** is its one model. [Rip design §3](rip-design.md#3-learning) · `pkg/transformer`

**transient** — two senses. See also **D-6**, **write mask**.
- *Presentation state*: Player-domain, never-replicated effect state: `TransientResource` holds fixed-capacity explosion centres, `ViewResource` holds grayout, strobe and button echo, and the `transient` system (`TransientSystem`) ages them. [Domain D-6](domain-design.md#d-6--presentation-and-personal-effects-are-player-domain) · [Combat §6](combat.md#6-presentation) · `internal/engine` `TransientResource`, `ViewResource` · `internal/system` `TransientSystem`
- *Rendering*: `MaskTransient` is the write-mask category for projectiles, particles, materialization, lines and sigils. [Rendering §5](rendering.md#5-semantic-masks-and-post-processing) · `internal/parameter/visual` `MaskTransient`

**transition** (also: `Transition`, LCA, least common ancestor) — An FSM rule `{trigger, target, guard, actions, capture_vars}`. Taking it captures payload fields, exits up to the least common ancestor (LCA), commits the target, runs its actions and enters down to the new leaf; a transition to the current leaf is action-only, and an `internal = true` one has no target and keeps state and time. In a live session the authority's machine takes it and every instance applies it as a **transition crossing**. See also **trigger**, **guard**, **TimeInState**. [FSM §7](fsm-and-configuration.md#7-transition-semantics) · [FSM reference: transitions](fsm-reference.md#transitions) · `internal/fsm` `Transition`

**transition crossing** (also: `EventFSMTransition`, `Machine.Steer`, `Machine.Apply`, `FSMTransitionPayload.Then`) — One transition the authority's machine took, crossed as `{region, owner, index, trigger, vars, then}` (the state owning the transition, its index there, what triggered it, the variables it captured, and the unguarded `Tick` hops after its target, folded in so a chain of them costs one lead and lasts no tick) and applied at one tick on every instance, the authority's included, where its actions and the target's on_enter run, hop by hop (D-20). A region with one in flight takes nothing until it lands and parks the events the state it is entering reacts to, replaying them there; an internal transition parks nothing, so every event it counts crosses in order, and an internal `Tick` one holds only its region's `Tick` transitions until it lands. A guest's machine follows these alone, as do a replay of a live session and a projection; a reset's own settle takes its transitions in place on every instance; a solo run takes them in place. Only the authority may cross one. See also **transition**, **crossing**, **playout lead**. [Domain design D-20](domain-design.md#d-20--shared-fsm-regions-move-on-the-authoritys-transitions) · [Netcode design §4](netcode-design.md#4-phase-2) · `internal/fsm` `Machine.Steer`, `Machine.Apply` · `internal/engine` `Scheduler.steerFSM`

**transport** — two senses. See also **wire frame**, **WebSocket**, **crossing**.
- *Networking*: `link.Transport` is the framed byte-stream link layer, over TCP or a WebSocket stream, in host, peer and relay roles; `SocketPort` owns its goroutines and serves the engine's `NetworkPort` a tick-drained inbound queue. There is no UDP. [Services §7](services-and-networking.md#7-transport-roles-and-lifecycle) · [Services §7.3](services-and-networking.md#73-why-a-byte-stream-and-not-udp-or-icmp) · `pkg/network` `Transport`, `SocketPort`
- *Events*: event transport is how `GameEvent`s move: through the lock-free event queue inside an instance, and as crossings handed to `NetworkSystem` through `WireSink` for every other instance. Derived events are never transported (D-5). [ECS and events §8](ecs-and-events.md#8-event-transport) · [Domain §4](domain-design.md#4-transport-and-ordering) · `internal/event` `GameEvent`

**trigger** — five senses. See also **transition**, **flight recorder**, **causal player**, **mount**.
- *FSM*: what a transition reacts to: an event name, or `Tick` once per game tick. Each region keeps a trigger mask of the event types its active path names, so an event nothing reacts to is rejected with one bit test; `Machine.Reacts` answers for every state. Every region is Shared state, moved by the authority's transitions, so a trigger is never a `Local` event (D-20). [FSM §6](fsm-and-configuration.md#6-event-processing) · [Domain D-20](domain-design.md#d-20--shared-fsm-regions-move-on-the-authoritys-transitions) · `internal/fsm` `Machine.Reacts`
- *Flight recorder*: a reason to flush the recorder window: `event.dropped`, `lock.hold`, `race`, `crash`, `manual` (`:log rec flush`), `break`, or `fsm:<region>` once `:log rec fsm on` is set. `Registry.Trigger` only sets a flag; the tick goroutine flushes. [Logging: triggers](logging-and-diagnostics.md#triggers) · `internal/status` `Registry.Trigger`
- *Domain model*: a Shared trigger (D-16) elects one causal cursor deterministically before reading any Player state, and only that cursor's owner emits the crossing. [Domain D-16](domain-design.md#d-16--shared-triggers-elect-one-causal-player)
- *Combat*: `MountTrigger` decides when a mounted weapon fires: `MountAuto` whenever ready with a cursor in range, `MountArmed` only while its host holds `Armed`. [Combat §3](combat.md#3-drivers) · `internal/component` `MountTrigger`
- *Behavior machines*: a behavior program's edge fires on `Timeout`, `Tick` (before the state's tick) or `AfterTick` (after it). [FSM reference: Behavior Programs](fsm-reference.md#behavior-programs)

**truecolor** (also: `-color true`) — The RGB color mode: cells carry 24-bit color, and finalization fills untouched backgrounds and, with occlusion on, dims touched backgrounds under the runes the occlusion mask selects. `-color auto` lets the terminal's capability choose it over 256-color. See also **256-color**. [Rendering §6](rendering.md#6-finalization-and-color-modes) · `internal/render` `RenderBuffer`

**trusted peer** (also: plaintext, unauthenticated links) — The session security model: links are plaintext and unauthenticated by decision (fleet decision D6), so anyone who reaches a game port or the browser route can join. The coordinator checks build and session identity, not who a peer is, and the succession's structural checks bound races, not hostility. See also **D1–D6**, **identity**. [Multiplayer §2](multi-player.md#2-current-contract) · [Services §10](services-and-networking.md#10-timeouts-limits-and-security) · [Fleet deploy §0](kube-docker-deploy.md#0-decisions) · `internal/network` `PeerIdentity`

**tuning** (also: `Rip.Tuning`, `RipFrames.Tuning`, cents) — The cents a recording's pitches sit off equal temperament at A440, read by the **rip**'s front end before anything else: over windows four times the frame, every spectral peak votes its offset from the nearest semitone with its height, and the pitch table is read on the mean offset, so a clip forty cents flat reads as one in tune instead of wobbling between two rows; soundlab's `rip` names it when it is off. [Audio §11](audio.md#11-ripping-a-recording) · `pkg/audio` rip.go `tuning`

**tunnel** (also: reverse tunnel, published door, `DoorTunnel`, `TunnelHub`, `-tunnels`) — A door a run publishes through `vif-router` instead of binding one (`-relay vif://<site>:7777`): the run holds a control connection to the front door, is named from the pods' identifier space and handed the link and a token it resumes the name with, and for each dial placed on that name opens a connection back, matched by a ticket and spliced to the dialer, so a door behind a NAT is reached at the site with the front door's budget, ceiling and refusals. `-tunnels` bounds the doors a router carries and `-doors-per-address` those one address holds; a name is resumed only by the run it was issued to, within `NetworkTunnelResumeWindow` of losing its control connection; each connection placed is accounted for as it closes (`session route closed`). See also **door**, **vif-router**, **front door**. [Multiplayer §5.6](multi-player.md#56-relay-doors-and-the-router) · [Fleet §9](kubernetes-fleet.md#9-the-session-routes) · `internal/network` `TunnelHub`, `DoorTunnel` · `tool/vif-router` `tcpRouter.placeDoor` · `internal/app` `membership.publishDoor`

**turret** (also: `WeaponTurret`) — The bullet weapon kind: on a cursor it fires one spread bullet per charge at the nearest targets and, at its charge cap, streams bullets between waves; as a mount it fires hostile bullets, and storm's red circle carries one. See also **weapon**, **mount**. [Combat §2](combat.md#2-layers) · [Combat §4](combat.md#4-at-the-charge-cap) · `internal/component` `WeaponTurret`

**typing** (also: `TypingSystem`, streak) — Checking an Insert-mode rune against the cursor cell's top glyph. A match grants boost, heat and an energy change by glyph color, removes the glyph and advances the cursor; a miss, or a key on a cell with no glyph or only **scenery**, flashes the cursor, costs 10 heat, ends boost and resets the streak (the best streak is `typing.max_streak`). Composite text must be typed left to right. See also **top glyph**. [Gameplay §3](gameplay.md#3-typing-rules) · `internal/system` `TypingSystem`

## U

**unavailable** (also: unavailable in a browser, in this build, in this run) — The one sentence the command line and the configuration menu give for a feature a host lacks: `<Thing> unavailable in a browser` on js/wasm, `in this build` where a build tag left it out (`novlog`, `vif_noaudio`, `vif_headless`), `in this run` where the build has it and this run does not (silent audio, a driven run's session controls). A disabled row's reason and the command's refusal are the same text, and no message names a package, build tag or system call. [Menu](config-menu.md#menu) · [Logging §15](logging-and-diagnostics.md#15-build-variants) · `internal/mode` `unavailable`, `sentence` · `internal/vlog` `Linked`, `ErrDisabled`

**undo** (also: `u`) — Cursor-motion undo: `u` returns the cursor to an earlier position from a ring of 256 that the mode router keeps; it is session interaction state, never persisted. [Input: prefixes, editing, and modes](input-and-modes.md#prefixes-editing-and-modes) · [Input §5](input-and-modes.md#5-insert-search-and-command-text-editing) · `internal/mode` `Router`

**unserved** (also: `MsgStateUnserved`, `CorrectionUnserved`) — A retention holder's answer that it cannot produce pages for a request: it holds no index for that tick or none that answers the pages, the repair would be too wide, or a relay was asked for a whole world it cannot author. The receiver stops waiting for that repair and takes the next whole world the authority sends. See also **retention**, **relay**. [Netcode design §2](netcode-design.md#2-the-correction-pipeline) · [Services §8](services-and-networking.md#8-wire-frame-and-session-messages) · `internal/snapshot` `CorrectionUnserved` · `internal/network` `MsgStateUnserved`

**update.sh** (also: `deploy/update.sh`) — The one-command node deploy: it brings each component (node filter, Kubernetes objects, wad volume, session image, router, LogWisp's configuration) to the checkout's HEAD and LogWisp's binary to upstream `main`, in dependency order, showing each difference as a diff and skipping what is current; `--diff` previews, changes nothing, and exits 1 if anything differs; a pending image, router or LogWisp update refuses while any fleet object exists. See also **drain**. [Fleet deploy](kube-docker-deploy.md#deploying-the-session-fleet) · [Runbook: Deploy a change](../deploy/runbook.md#deploy-a-change) · `deploy/update.sh`

**upgrade path** (also: gun path, `GunPath`, `GunLevel`, `U`) — A scenario's levels for the gun, set by `EventGunAimRequest`'s `path` in place of its aim: each level names the lanes, up to three, the gun fires along each cardinal; a gun of n charges fires level n and holds a charge per level, up to `GunPathMax` (16). A gun drop or vanguard's `U`, a **loot kind** of its own granting a gun charge, raises it a level; vanguard's nine climb from one shot forward to three each way. See also **gun**, **lane**, **drop table**. [Gameplay §7](gameplay.md#7-weapons-and-attacks) · [Combat §4](combat.md#4-at-the-charge-cap) · `internal/component` `GunLevel`, `WeaponComponent.GunPath` · `internal/system` `WeaponSystem` (`gunVolley`)

**upstream** (also: up link, down link, behind, peer link, link direction) — A participant's one link toward the authority: the authority itself, else the relay it joined through (`NetworkResource.Relay`) or adopted once that was gone, which the first link to return its own epochs committed becomes. The links behind it, its down links, are those that send it raw artifacts or were served a join within the epoch window, every link at the authority; a peer link to the successor is neither and carries only floods. Raw artifacts go up, committed ones and corrections down. See also **relay**, **mesh**. [Multi-player §5](multi-player.md#5-membership-topology-and-authority-continuity) · [Domain design §4.2](domain-design.md#42-mesh-relay) · `internal/system` `NetworkSystem.up`, `behind`, `Downstream`, `adoptRelay` · `pkg/network` `Inbound.Served`

**utilisation** (also: `SnapshotLinkUtilisation`) — The share of a link's measured capacity (0.75) that the correction cadence may spend; the rest is headroom for crossing epochs, owner syncs and digests, which share the link but are outside the cadence model. See also **cadence**, **operating point**. [Domain D-24](domain-design.md#d-24--cadence-adapts-the-convergence-floor-does-not) · [Telemetry reference](telemetry-reference.md#snapshot-and-correction) · `pkg/linkpace` `Bounds.Utilisation` · `internal/parameter` `SnapshotLinkUtilisation`

## V

**vanguard** — A shipped scenario in `wad/scenario`: a side-scroller after Vanguard (1981), whose cursor flies a **jagged cave** scrolling past it at twenty columns a second, a column a tick, a new cave each game behind a **cave mouth** of open map, glyphs drifting left behind the rock as parallax at one, two and four times its pace by depth, 20, 40 and 80 cells a second at first; raiders drawn between the two types of the **rank** the raid has climbed to among the eight of the cave's stone art, its **tier**, data species flying one attack cycle of chase, lock and charge or teleport, spawn at the right edge on a timer, each one a player shoots down rolling the raider **drop table**: a `U` at 50%, which raises the gun a level of the scenario's nine-level **upgrade path**, from one shot forward to three each way, until the gun holds its last, energy at 10%, two a drop at a full gun, and heat at 1%, each chance growing for each kill in a row it misses; four ranks shot down end the cave in a **boss stage**, the Kraken, with twice the last one's hit points each time, after which each cave is of another stone and a fifth faster; and a **crush** or a defeat by heat and energy halts the rock and drift under a red strobe before a new game starts. [Gameplay §10](gameplay.md#10-embedded-campaign-progression) · `wad/scenario/vanguard`

**variant** — three senses. See also **build profile**.
- *Audio*: a sound variant is one of a sound's pre-rendered renderings, normally three, which the mixer rotates to reduce repetition. [Audio §5](audio.md#5-sounds-and-sound-effects) · `pkg/audio` `RenderVariants`
- *Build*: a build variant is one tag combination (full, `novlog`, `vif_noaudio`, `vif_headless`, WASM, Windows); `make verify` compiles each. [Multi-platform §1](multi-platform.md#1-current-build-profiles) · [Logging §15](logging-and-diagnostics.md#15-build-variants) · `Makefile` `verify`
- *Storm*: one row of `circleVariants` per storm circle colour, held in one table instead of per-field switches on circle type: its spawn's cooldown, its attack, the cooldown after one, Active's flags and hooks and the blue's cue, from which `NewStormSystem` builds the colour's behavior machine. [Behavior machines §4.3](machine-design.md#43-storm-circles) · `internal/system` `circleVariants`

**verify** (also: make verify) — two senses. See also **replay**, **digest**, **proof**, **arch-check**.
- *Build*: `make verify` regenerates, runs the race-enabled tests, compiles every build variant (`novlog`, audio-free, headless, WASM, Windows), runs the audio-free and headless test subsets, asserts that the headless and WASM builds import no presentation or audio they must not, and runs vet. [Development §2](development.md#2-common-build-workflow) · [Development §5](development.md#5-validation-and-tests) · `Makefile` `verify`
- *Replay*: verifying a journal (`-replay <file> -headless`, `app.VerifyJournal`) replays it unpresented and flat out, failing at the first world digest it does not reproduce; `App.VerifyAnchor` first proves the loaded configuration is the recorded one. [Logging §9](logging-and-diagnostics.md#9-replay-journal) · [Runtime §1](runtime.md#1-process-modes) · `internal/app` `VerifyJournal`

**version stamp** (also: `version.txt`, `-version`) — The release and commit `vif -version` reports: `git archive` expands `internal/asset/version.txt` to the tag and commit, and a checkout build falls back to Go's VCS stamp, so no ldflags are needed. [Packaging §1](packaging.md#1-shared-guidelines) · [Content §7](content-assets-and-tools.md#7-embedded-assets) · `internal/asset` `Version`

**viewer keys** (also: playback keys) — The fixed keys a replay or a watched script or bot reserves: `SPACE . , + - h j k l 0 q` for pause, step, step back, speed, pan or re-centre, and quit (only pan and quit in a live session). They never become simulation intents and are not in the keymap. See also **replay**, **watched**. [Input §9](input-and-modes.md#9-command-mode) · [Runtime: replay playback](runtime.md#replay-playback) · `internal/app` `play.go`

**viewport** (also: `ViewportWidth`, `FollowCamera`) — The visible area of the map on this instance: it crops a map larger than the terminal or centres a smaller one, and the camera follows the cursor with a dead zone. Map and viewport sizes are separate settings; `MapToViewport`, `ViewportToScreen`, `MapToScreen` and `VisibleMapBounds` convert between map, viewport and screen coordinates. See also **camera**, **map**, **playfield**. [Rendering §2](rendering.md#2-render-context-and-coordinates) · [Gameplay §9](gameplay.md#9-walls-maps-navigation-and-environment) · `internal/engine` `ConfigResource.FollowCamera` · `internal/render` `RenderContext`

**vif-router** — The fleet's node-local control-plane service: it turns website session requests into Kubernetes Jobs and Services (its allocator half), is the TCP front door on 7777, routes a run's `-relay` door published through a reverse tunnel by name as it routes a pod (Multiplayer §5.6), and answers the browser route's WebSocket upgrade behind the site's nginx, which terminates TLS, splicing each socket to its pod. See **vif-router**. [Fleet deploy §11](kube-docker-deploy.md#11-the-router) · [Fleet design §1](kubernetes-fleet.md#1-what-is-deployed) · `tool/vif-router`

**vif-cpu** (also: vif-heap, vif-mutex, vif-trace) — The profile captures written to the log directory: `:d cpu`, `:d heap` and `:d mutex` write `vif-cpu-<time>.pprof`, `vif-heap-<time>.pprof` and `vif-mutex-<time>.pprof` for `go tool pprof`, and `:d trace` writes `vif-trace-<time>.out` for `go tool trace`. See also **profiler**. [Logging §11](logging-and-diagnostics.md#11-profiler) · `internal/prof` `capture.go`

**vif-jrn** (also: `vif-jrn-*.jsonl`) — The replay journal file kind: a dedicated JSONL stream (`-j`, `:journal`) of anchors, journaled events, written worlds and digests in the journal directory, read by `-replay` and `vif-log`. See **journal**. [Logging §9](logging-and-diagnostics.md#9-replay-journal) · [Filesystem §5](filesystem-layout.md#5-logs-journals-and-runtime-output) · `internal/vlog` `vlog.go`

**vif-log** — two senses. See also **vlog**, **vif-jrn**.
- *Tool*: `tool/vif-log` is the terminal viewer that indexes session logs and replay journals, merges several files into one chronological view and filters them incrementally. [Content §10](content-assets-and-tools.md#10-other-repository-tools) · [Package map §9](package-map.md#9-commands-tools-and-sandboxes) · `tool/vif-log`
- *File kind*: `vif-log-<stamp>.jsonl` is a process's session log, asynchronous JSON Lines written through `vlog` into the log directory and rotated at 64 MB (8 MB for a fleet session). [Development §8](development.md#8-structured-logging) · [Logging §2](logging-and-diagnostics.md#2-record-shape) · `internal/vlog` `vlog.go`

**vif-mus** — The music recording file kind: `-mw` (`-music-wav`, `paths.music`) records the music bus alone, before ducking, to `vif-mus-<stamp>.wav` in the music output directory; it needs a running mixer. [Audio §3](audio.md#3-backend-detection-and-failover) · [Filesystem §5](filesystem-layout.md#5-logs-journals-and-runtime-output) · `internal/app` `audio.go`

**vif-rec** (also: sidecar, recorder sidecar, `vif-rec-*.jsonl`) — The flight recorder's sidecar file kind: with no session log running but a log directory configured, a recorder flush opens `vif-rec-<timestamp>.jsonl`, writes the window and closes it; with a session log, windows go there instead. A sidecar carries the session log's envelope and opens beside it in one viewer. See **flight recorder**. [Logging: destination](logging-and-diagnostics.md#destination) · `internal/vlog` `dump.go`

**vif-snap** — The on-demand snapshot file kind: `:t save` writes `vif-snap-<timestamp>.jsonl` to the log directory through a second logger, holding the full registry plus context, view, player, world and session records from one coherent tick. See **snapshot**. [Logging §7](logging-and-diagnostics.md#7-on-demand-snapshot) · `internal/vlog` `Dump`

**vif-stderr** — The runtime output capture file kind: `-dev` redirects fd 2 to `vif-stderr-<timestamp>.log` before the terminal enters the alternate screen, and each complete block later read from it becomes one `sub="race"` record. [Logging §10](logging-and-diagnostics.md#10-runtime-output-capture) · `internal/core` `dev.go`

**vif.toml** (also: settings file, `[paths]`, `[audio]`, `[presentation]`, `[play]`) — The settings document at the top of a configuration root, holding defaults a flag would otherwise repeat: `[paths]` names another root, the log, journal and music directories and the scenario, content and keymap, `[audio] buffer_ms` sets the mixer period, `[presentation] window` opens the desktop window as `-g` does (`maximized` and `fullscreen` the state it opens in; `-cli` wins over all of it) and `cell` is the zoom Android and a browser open at and `tmux_frame_cap` holds the terminal to 24 frames a second inside tmux or screen, and `[play]` is how a run starts (the `sfx` and `music` channels, `sfx_volume`, auto-fire, mouse, pointer following, HUD, relaying, the start wait, the frame-rate cap `max_fps`; both channels on and waiting by default); the zoom and each `[play]` value are written back as the player changes them: to the file the run read when it is in the user root or the `-config-dir` root, else to the user root's, created at the first change from the one read; a browser keeps them in the site's storage. The first file found wins whole, a flag on the command line wins over it for that run alone and is never written, and a key this build does not know is dropped from the document written back with the comments that are its own, every other line and comment kept, and named on the status line (`UnknownKeys`; `-check` and soundlab, which reads only the root and the mixer period, name it and write nothing). [Filesystem §3](filesystem-layout.md#3-resolution-policy) · [Config menu: persistence](config-menu.md#key-binding-and-persistence) · `internal/paths` `Settings`, `Play`, `LoadSettings`, `UnknownKeys`, `DropUnknown`, `SettingsFor`, `SavePlay`, `SaveCell`, `SettingsStore`

**vif://** (also: join link, `tcp://`) — The link scheme a player is handed: `vif://host:port/name` is a TCP join of the named session, so one string is both clickable and pasteable after `-join`. `-join` also takes a bare `host:port`, a `ws(s)://` URL, or an `http(s)://` site that creates a session. [Services §6](services-and-networking.md#6-operator-session-surface) · [Fleet design §9](kubernetes-fleet.md#9-the-session-routes) · `pkg/network` `ParseEndpoint`

**VIF_ROUTER_\*** — The router's settings, kept in `/etc/vif-router/router.env` (installed from `deploy/guest/vif-router.env`) and passed as flags by its systemd unit: among them `IMAGE` (the session image the node last built, written by `update-vif-image.sh`), `WEB_ORIGIN` (the one `Origin` the browser route accepts), `TUNNELS` (doors runs may publish through the router at once), `DOORS_PER_ADDRESS` (those one address may hold), `ROUTE_MAX` (connections per session through the front door and the browser route together), `CLIENT_ADDRESS_HEADER` (the header that keys per-player budgets), `PLAYERS_MAX`, `BOTS_MAX` and `WAD`. [Fleet deploy §11](kube-docker-deploy.md#11-the-router) · [Fleet deploy §12](kube-docker-deploy.md#12-the-sites-edge) · `deploy/guest/vif-router.env`

**VIF_CONFIG_DIR** — The Makefile variable naming where `make install-config` writes a configuration root; it defaults to the user root (`~/.config/vif` on Linux) and can name a staging root instead; `make install` ignores it and writes `$(SYSCONFDIR)/vif`. [Development §2](development.md#2-common-build-workflow) · [Filesystem §4](filesystem-layout.md#4-installation) · `Makefile`

**vif_gui** (also: vif_nogui, -g, -gui, make gui, make cli) — The desktop window's build tags. The window (`cmd/vif/gui.go` setting the run's presentation to a `pkg/window` window, Xlib, EGL and GLES 2 loaded through purego with cgo off) is in every Linux and FreeBSD build and opens on `-g`; `vif_gui` (`make gui`, `bin/vif-gui`) opens it by default and `vif_nogui` (`make cli`, `bin/vif-cli`) leaves it out. Android binds `app.Run` through `gomobile` instead. [Multi-platform §8](multi-platform.md#8-adding-another-renderer-or-android-host) · `pkg/window`; `cmd/vif` `gui.go`

**vif_headless** (also: `make headless`, `vif-headless`, dedicated build) — The dedicated-server build tag: it leaves out the terminal adapter, renderers and audio, produces `bin/vif-headless`, and refuses presenting modes, so its entries are `-serve`, unpresented scripts, `-headless` bots and `-replay -headless` verification. It keeps logging and journals, and the fleet image runs it. See also **build profile**, **headless**. [Multi-platform §1](multi-platform.md#1-current-build-profiles) · [Multi-platform §2](multi-platform.md#2-composition-boundary) · `internal/app` `presentation_headless.go`

**vif_noaudio** (also: audio-free build) — The build tag that removes the audio service, mixer and full audio systems while keeping terminal presentation; generated stand-ins install small event sinks under the same `audio` and `music` names, so events, telemetry and system controls keep working. The Windows build carries it too. [Multi-platform §1](multi-platform.md#1-current-build-profiles) · [Multi-platform §2](multi-platform.md#2-composition-boundary) · `internal/manifest` `build_noaudio_gen.go`

**VIF_SYSTEM_ROOTS** — The environment variable that replaces the system configuration roots (`/usr/local/etc/vif`, `/etc/vif`) with its own list, separated as `PATH` is; set but empty, there are none. Packaging uses it to test a staged install. [Filesystem §3](filesystem-layout.md#3-resolution-policy) · [Packaging §7](packaging.md#7-verifying-a-staged-install) · `internal/paths` `SystemRootsEnv`

**VIF_WRITE_GENERATED** — Set to `1` on `go test ./cmd/vif -run Generated`, it rewrites the committed manual page `doc/vif.6` and the bash, zsh and fish completions from the flag table (`helpSections`) after a flag changes; without it the test fails while they are stale. [Docs README: source-of-truth map](README.md#source-of-truth-map) · [Packaging §1](packaging.md#1-shared-guidelines) · `cmd/vif` `TestGeneratedFilesAreTheHelpTable`, `helpSections`

**.vifimg** (also: dual-mode image, `DualModeImage`) — The image asset file kind: one deflate-compressed file holding both truecolor and xterm-256 cells, with transparency, runes, dimensions, render mode and anchor offset, made by the ascimage tool and installed under a root's `image/` for walls and patterns. See **dual-mode image**. [Content §8](content-assets-and-tools.md#8-image-and-pattern-pipeline) · [Content §9](content-assets-and-tools.md#9-ascimage-tool) · `pkg/ascimage` `DualModeImage`

**VisibilityToggle** — The optional renderer interface (`IsVisible`) that lets a renderer stay registered and be skipped while hidden, as the cursor, peer-cursor, overlay and pinned-card renderers are, without rebuilding the pipeline. [Rendering §1](rendering.md#1-frame-pipeline) · `internal/render` `VisibilityToggle`

**visual mode** (also: `ModeVisual`, `v`) — The mode `v` enters: Normal mode's grammar applied over the shield's ping bounds, so while the shield is active `h j k l` step by the bounds radius and word, find and line motions scan every row the bounds cover; `v` or Escape returns to Normal. See also **mode**. [Input §2](input-and-modes.md#2-modes) · `internal/core` `ModeVisual`

**vlog** (also: `internal/vlog`, `vlog.Log`) — The process's log facade over the asynchronous `lixenwraith/log` JSONL logger: one session sink written through a handle per runtime (`vlog.Log`) that stamps run, tick and instance tag, plus an ungated file per journal and standalone snapshot and recorder files. The `wasm` and `novlog` builds compile it to no-op stubs. [Logging §1](logging-and-diagnostics.md#1-layer-map) · [Logging §15](logging-and-diagnostics.md#15-build-variants) · `internal/vlog` `Log`, `NewLog`

**vmath** (also: `pkg/vmath`) — The float64 numeric and geometry package: named constants (`TwoPi`, `Golden64`, `FNVOffset64`, `FNVPrime64`), scalar and vector math (`Vec3`), LUT trigonometry (`Sin`, `Cos`, `Atan2`), ellipses and arcs, grid traversal and cell rays (`Ray`), integer cells (`Point`, `Point.Center`, `PointAt`), seeded randomness (`FastRand`, `DeriveSeed`, `MixChain`) and the word-at-a-time hash every snapshot hash sums (`Hash64`), fixed across platforms and processes. Its `physics` subpackage holds `Kinetic`. [AI and physics §6](ai-physics-and-evolution.md#6-numeric-model-and-determinism) · `pkg/vmath` · `pkg/vmath/physics`

**vocabulary** — two senses.
- *FSM and bots*: the shared vocabulary is the generic actions and guards `std.Register` installs on any machine (variables, `StateTimeExceeds`, compound, payload, region, status and config guards), so scenarios and bot graphs share them; a bot graph is refused the actions that write the world. `-schema` lists each vocabulary with its arguments. [Bots: shared vocabulary](bots.md#shared-vocabulary) · [FSM reference: actions](fsm-reference.md#actions) · `internal/fsm/std` `Register` · `internal/fsm` `Machine.Vocabulary`
- *Behavior machines*: the game verbs a behavior program compiles against, `system.BehaviorVocabulary` over a `behavior.Vocabulary` registry: guards, probes, effects, sustained verbs and flags, each fixing its own event routing, with the move every tick ends with; `-schema` lists it as `vocabularies.behavior`. See also **behavior program**. [FSM reference: Behavior Programs](fsm-reference.md#behavior-programs) · [Behavior machines §6.2](machine-design.md#62-vocabulary) · `internal/behavior` `Vocabulary` · `internal/system` `BehaviorVocabulary`

**void** — two senses. See also **crossing fence**, **playfield**.
- *Multiplayer*: a void crossing is one the authority received more than `NetworkCommitLateTicks` (20) ticks late: committed as void, it is not relayed and applies only on its producer, at its stamped tick; it closes its source's fence, so the next correction takes it back and its producer stops replaying it (`network.commit_void`). [Multiplayer §3.5](multi-player.md#35-command-frames-the-commit-and-pacing) · `internal/system` `NetworkSystem` · `internal/parameter` `NetworkCommitLateTicks`
- *Rendering*: the void is the part of the frame outside the playfield (the centering margin and gutter cells), repainted with `visual.RgbVoid` after finalization so it reads as out of play rather than as empty map. [Rendering §2.1](rendering.md#21-the-playfield-clip) · [Rendering §6](rendering.md#6-finalization-and-color-modes) · `internal/render` `RenderBuffer.SetVoidRegion`

## W

**voice** (also: VoiceDef, `[[voice]]`, voices.toml, tonal voice) — A tonal instrument as data: a `[[voice]]` of the sound document naming an oscillator stack through a resonant low-pass under a filter envelope into an ADSR, or a string as a delay line or as its partials. The shipped five are `voices.toml`; a document's voice of a shipped name retunes that instrument in place, a new name adds an instrument patterns name as any, and `DefineVoice` hot-swaps one, the next note taking it while a sounding one keeps its own. A voice is also a gene vector (`Genes`, `VoiceFromGenes`: thirty-two parameters in [0, 1] and a wave), the form a **rip** fits one in by the engine's renders of a recording's notes (`RenderNote`, `MelDistance`) and a trained front end hears one in. See also **sound**, **pattern**, **soundlab**, **rip**. [Audio §7](audio.md#7-music-sequencer) · [Audio §11](audio.md#11-ripping-a-recording) · `pkg/audio` `VoiceDef` `RegisterVoice` `DefineVoice`; `internal/asset/audio/voices.toml`; `internal/soundlab` fit.go

**vortex** (also: EventParticleVortex) — A request that turns every Player glyph into particles of the behavior it names and holds all of that behavior, for its duration, in a ring just clear of one button, round at the button's proportions and damped hard, on the vortex orbit of the behavior's profile; `simon` marks the button at fault with a decay vortex. See also **decay**, **dust**, **blossom**. [Gameplay §4](gameplay.md#4-glyph-production-and-decay) · `internal/event` `EventParticleVortex` · `internal/system` `ParticleSystem`

**:w** (also: :weapon, `EventWeaponSetRequest`) — Development weapon set on the invoking cursor: one kind's charges, named by the weapon or its attack, capped at its maximum, 0 taking it, the gun included; a negative count flips the energy's sign first (`EnergySetPayload.Flip`), so the crossing strips every kind but the gun before the set lands. See also **:god**, **loadout**. [Input §9](input-and-modes.md#9-command-mode) · `internal/mode` `handleWeaponCommand` · `internal/system` `WeaponSystem.setWeapon`

**wad** — three senses. See also **config root**, **scenario**.
- *Repository*: `wad/` is the repository's external payload (scenarios, content, images), laid out exactly as an installed configuration root, so `-config-dir wad`, or a binary in the checkout's `bin/`, plays the checkout uninstalled. [Filesystem §1](filesystem-layout.md#1-two-payloads-in-the-repository) · [Filesystem §3](filesystem-layout.md#3-resolution-policy) · `internal/paths` `ConfigRoots`
- *Release*: `make wad-archive` packs that same install as `bin/vif-wad.tar.gz`, which unpacks as `vif/` into the directory that holds a configuration root. [Development §2](development.md#2-common-build-workflow) · [Packaging §2](packaging.md#2-release-surface) · `Makefile` `wad-archive`
- *Fleet*: the node's read-only scenario volume (`/var/db/vif/wad`, the `vif-fleet-wad` volume) holding only `scenario/` and `image/`, mounted at `/wad` in every session pod; `deploy/update.sh wad` swaps it by rename without a rebuild, and the router lists its scenarios per request. [Fleet deploy §9](kube-docker-deploy.md#9-the-fleet-objects-and-the-shared-volumes) · [Filesystem §3](filesystem-layout.md#3-resolution-policy) · `tool/vif-router` `wadMounts`

**wait** (also: Wait to start, `[play] wait`, `:wait`, `-nw`, `-no-wait`) — Whether each game a person plays, started or restarted, waits in the start hold for one press (see **hold**), and a join they start for the press that seats it (see **ready period**): `vif.toml`'s `[play] wait`, on by default, which the Settings page's row and `:wait` change and write back, and `-nw` turns off for one run without writing it. A run nobody plays never waits. [Menu: Settings](config-menu.md#settings) · [Filesystem §3](filesystem-layout.md#3-resolution-policy) · `internal/engine` `GameContext.WaitStart`; `internal/paths` `Play.Wait`

**wall** — two senses.
- *Gameplay*: a Shared grid obstacle (`WallComponent`) with a block mask (`WallBlockCursor`, `WallBlockKinetic`, `WallBlockParticle`, `WallBlockSpawn`) and glyph, background or box-drawing visuals, placed singly, by maze, from a pattern image or as a scrolling **cave**; a new wall displaces what stands on its cell. `Index.Blocks` tests one cell against a mask and `Index.BlockBits` hands a derivation that probes many cells the mask's whole grid of bits, kept to every wall placed, moved or removed (`Position.WallChanged` for a `BlockMask` written in place); the **wall log** names the cells each change can have turned. [Gameplay §9](gameplay.md#9-walls-maps-navigation-and-environment) · [ECS and events §3](ecs-and-events.md#3-position-and-spatial-queries) · `internal/component` `WallComponent`, `WallBlockMask` · `pkg/position` `Index.Blocks`, `Index.BlockBits` · `internal/engine` `Position.WallChanged`
- *Time*: wall time, wall pace and the wall clock mean real elapsed time, as against simulation time, which derives from the tick (D-21); parameters measured in it are tagged `[wall]`. [Runtime §3](runtime.md#3-runtime-clocks-and-cadence) · [Domain D-21](domain-design.md#d-21--simulation-time-is-tick-derived) · `internal/parameter`

**wall log** (also: block log, `Index.BlockChanges`) — The cells whose block answer may have moved since a sequence: both cells of every wall placed, moved, removed or rewritten, and each cell a rock shift crossed. A reader keeping its own copy of `Blocks`, navigation, re-tests only those cells; a change naming no cells (an install's `BlocksChanged`, a resize, a rock field laid) loses the log and the reader re-reads every cell. [ECS and events §3](ecs-and-events.md#3-position-and-spatial-queries) · [Navigation §3](navigation-design.md#3-passability-tiers-and-wall-changes) · `pkg/position` `Index.BlockChanges`, `Index.BlocksChanged`

**ward** (also: `StatusWard`, invulnerability) — The combat status a species sets on itself to refuse every hit's damage and every burn tick; no hit ever writes it. Quasar shields, storm phase, the shielded snake head and a data species in a state flagged `Shielded` refresh it from their own systems. See also **status**. [Combat §5](combat.md#5-resolution-statuses-and-what-crosses) · `internal/component` `StatusWard`

**wasm** (also: `js/wasm`, browser build, `make wasm`) — The browser build (`web/vif.wasm`): it runs in a Web Worker behind a page that draws its cell frames on a WebGL canvas and plays the mixer's sound through an AudioWorklet (`pkg/browser`), uses embedded assets with no configuration-root discovery, keeps `[play]` in the page's storage, has no logging sink, plays solo or joins a session over a `wss://` WebSocket route, and never hosts; what it lacks it calls unavailable in a browser. See also **web/**, **WebSocket**, **unavailable**. [Multi-platform §5](multi-platform.md#5-browser-networking) · [Development: WASM host](development.md#wasm-host) · `pkg/network` `websocket_wasm.go`

**watched** (also: `-watch`, watched bot) — A script or bot run presented on this terminal: `-watch` shows the script, or the first bot in place of a player with any further bots seated beside it. Watching does not change the run, which remains an independent participant, and it reserves the viewer keys. See also **viewer keys**, **seat**. [Bots: flags](bots.md#flags) · [Runtime §1.1](runtime.md#11-script-pacing-and-presentation) · `internal/app` `RunBot`, `RunScript`

**weapon** (also: `WeaponType`, `WeaponSpecs`, loadout) — One of six kinds (gun, rod, launcher, disruptor, turret, emitter), each defined by one `WeaponSpecs` row: delivery, attack family, cooldown, charge cap and cap effect. A cursor's loadout (`WeaponComponent`, owner-authored) holds charges per kind and only its owner fires it; a Shared host carries one mounted weapon. Shots and visuals are Player-domain, and only impacts cross. See also **turret**, **tracer**, **mount**, **charge**. [Combat §1](combat.md#1-the-boundary) · [Combat §2](combat.md#2-layers) · [Gameplay §7](gameplay.md#7-weapons-and-attacks) · `internal/component` `WeaponSpecs`, `WeaponComponent`

**web/** (also: browser launcher) — The deployed browser launcher directory: `index.html` loads `terminal.js`, which draws the grid on a canvas and starts the WASM program in a Worker (`worker.js`, `wasm_exec.js`) and hands it argv from `window.VIF_ARGS` and `arg` query parameters. A site copies it as it stands; `make serve` serves it locally. See also **wasm**, **launch arguments**. [Development: WASM host](development.md#wasm-host) · [Multi-platform §6](multi-platform.md#6-browser-launch-arguments) · `web/terminal.js`

**WebSocket** (also: `ws://`, `wss://`, `pkg/websocket`) — three senses. See also **wasm**, **vif-router**, **transport**.
- *Library*: `pkg/websocket` is a minimal standard-library RFC 6455 byte stream shared by the native client, a host's `ws://` listener and `vif-router`: version-13 upgrade, binary frames only, ping, pong and close, no extensions or subprotocols, masking by role and `TCP_NODELAY` on every socket. [Multi-platform: where WebSocket is spoken](multi-platform.md#where-websocket-is-spoken) · `pkg/websocket` `Conn`, `Dial`
- *Public route*: `wss://<site>/vif/ws/<session>`: the site's nginx terminates TLS, and `vif-router` checks the request, the `Origin`, the join budget and the session's liveness before answering the upgrade and splicing raw TCP to the pod's game port. [Multi-platform: the public route](multi-platform.md#the-public-route) · [Fleet design §9](kubernetes-fleet.md#9-the-session-routes) · `tool/vif-router` `ws_proxy.go`
- *Session listener*: `-host ws://:7777` or `-serve ws://:7777` serves a session over plain `ws` from one binary, through the same accept loop and admission as TCP, and `wss://:7777` with `-cert` and `-key`, or `-selfsign`, serves it under TLS (`link.LoadTLS`, `link.SelfSignedTLS`), which a page served over https can open; a host names several doors at once (`-host :7777,wss://:7778`, `link.Doors`), one session behind them; a terminal joining a `wss://` door trusts the system's roots and takes a self-signed door as it is, encrypted and unauthenticated (`dialTLS`), or what `-ca` names, an issuer's PEM or the certificate's `sha256:` fingerprint (`link.TrustTLS`); the browser build dials a route by wrapping the page's WebSocket as a `net.Conn`. [Multi-platform §5](multi-platform.md#5-browser-networking) · [Services §7](services-and-networking.md#7-transport-roles-and-lifecycle) · `pkg/network` `Listen`

**wind** (also: `EventWindStart`, `EventWindCancel`) — The first environment effect of `EnvironmentSystem`: a map-wide force with a direction and duration that varies slightly each tick from a Shared stream and applies `force / mass` to mobile species (drains, swarm, quasar, eye, snake head, storm circles). Its state is part of the Shared capture. See also **environment**. [Gameplay §9](gameplay.md#9-walls-maps-navigation-and-environment) · [Generic kinetic §6](generic-kinetic.md#6-wind-as-the-first-environment-effect) · `internal/system` `EnvironmentSystem`

**window** — six senses. See also **immunity window**, **epoch**, **flight recorder**.
- *Flight recorder*: the recorder window is the last `RecorderDepthTicks` (200) ticks of full registry samples held in memory, written out when a trigger fires. [Logging §8](logging-and-diagnostics.md#8-flight-recorder) · `internal/status` `Recorder`
- *Combat*: the damage-immunity window (`CombatDamageImmunityDuration`, 150 ms) a hit opens on its target, within which each player and attack family may damage it once (D-3); a separate kinetic-immunity window bounds knockback, and soft collision opens one too (`SoftCollisionImmunityDuration`). [Combat §5](combat.md#5-resolution-statuses-and-what-crosses) · `internal/system` `combat.go` · `internal/parameter` `combat.go`
- *Multiplayer*: the epoch window (`NetworkEpochWindow`, 64) admits a source's late epochs once; the hold window (`NetworkHoldTicks`) is how far ahead of the clock a correction or manifest is held rather than taken at once; the floor window (`SnapshotFloorKeyframeTicks`, 60) is the span a link must carry one whole world in; the succession window (`NetworkSuccessionWindow`, 5 s on the wall, since the session stands frozen through it) is how long a survivor waits for a handoff before continuing locally and the longest a successor waits for its group before resuming; the apply window (`NetworkApplyWindowTicks`) bounds how far ahead an artifact's apply tick may be; `-slow-window` (20 s) is the span the eviction policy measures; `NetworkPaceWindow` is how many authority epochs one pace decision reads; `NetworkAdmitWindow` is the per-address admission budget's span. See **playout buffer**, **convergence floor**, **fork**. [Multiplayer §3.5](multi-player.md#35-command-frames-the-commit-and-pacing) · [Netcode design §2.1](netcode-design.md#21-the-playout-buffer) · [Multiplayer §5.1](multi-player.md#51-succession) · [Services §9](services-and-networking.md#9-poll-boundary-and-receive-lead) · [Services §7.2](services-and-networking.md#72-join-identity-and-who-decides-it) · `internal/parameter` `network.go`
- *Profiler*: `ProfWindow` (one wall second of running game, closed on a tick and never spanning a pause) is the span over which module statistics are averaged. [Logging §11](logging-and-diagnostics.md#11-profiler) · `internal/parameter` `ProfWindow`
- *APM*: `GameState` keeps a 60-second APM and a five-second music APM; the status bar shows the five-second one, which drives music tempo and tier. [Input §8](input-and-modes.md#8-apm-signal) · `internal/engine` `GameState.MusicAPM`, `GameState.CurrentAPM`
- *Fleet*: the first-guest window (`-first-join`, 90 s in the fleet) is how long an allocated session waits for its first guest before it exits, and the empty grace (`-empty`, also 90 s) how long it outlives its last guest, which doubles as the reconnect window; the router counts each address's session creations per first-join window. [Runtime: the allocated session](runtime.md#the-allocated-session) · [Fleet design §2](kubernetes-fleet.md#2-runtime-contract) · [Fleet design §8](kubernetes-fleet.md#8-decisions) · `internal/lifecycle` `Policy.FirstJoin`, `Policy.Empty`

**wire frame** — two senses. See also **transport**, **epoch**.
- *Networking*: one transport message: a fixed 12-byte big-endian header (type, flags, sender sequence, ack, payload length) and up to 65,535 payload bytes; after the handshake each direction of a link is deflate runs of frames, with the snapshot codec's bodies raw between them. Ack is observational, and nothing is retransmitted. [Services §8](services-and-networking.md#8-wire-frame-and-session-messages) · [Domain §4.5](domain-design.md#45-join-and-stream-framing) · `pkg/network` `HeaderSize`, `MaxPayloadSize`, `Message`
- *Events*: `event.WireFrame` is one encoded crossing inside an epoch (event as its registry index, domain, payload in the generated codec, sequence); the journal and the wire share its payload codec, and a `WireBatch` carries one source's frames for one produced tick in the binary envelope. [ECS and events §8](ecs-and-events.md#8-event-transport) · [Multiplayer §3.5](multi-player.md#35-command-frames-the-commit-and-pacing) · `internal/event` `WireFrame`, `WireBatch`

**WireSink** — The interface `NetworkSystem` gives the event queue: `Cross` takes a crossing into the barrier at this participant's playout lead, `Receive` queues the scheduled local and peer artifacts due as a tick opens, for its settle, `CrossingApplied` reports applied sequences, and `Flush` sends the closed production epoch. See also **crossing**, **playout lead**. [Services §9](services-and-networking.md#9-poll-boundary-and-receive-lead) · `internal/event` `WireSink`

**world** — three senses. See also **world lock**, **staging world**, **written world**, **capture**.
- *ECS*: `engine.World` owns one complete simulation: component stores, singleton resources (the event queue among them), the position store and spatial grid, the sealed system list and one entity counter per domain, all under one update mutex. A process can hold several: the live world, a guest's staging world, bot seats and replay copies. [ECS and events §1](ecs-and-events.md#1-world-ownership) · `internal/engine` `World`
- *Multiplayer*: the Shared world is the Shared-domain state every participant simulates and the authority corrects; a whole world is an authoritative capture sent entire, as a keyframe or a join, rather than as a delta or a repair. [Netcode design §2](netcode-design.md#2-the-correction-pipeline) · [Domain D-23](domain-design.md#d-23--the-host-world-is-the-correction) · `internal/snapshot` `SharedCapture`
- *Replay*: `engine.WorldCopy` (`World.CopyOut`, `World.CopyIn`) is a checkpoint copy of every store and occupied grid cell in their own order plus both domains' allocators, so a restored world continues exactly; replay keeps a ring of them for stepping back. [Runtime: replay playback](runtime.md#replay-playback) · `internal/engine` `WorldCopy`

**world lock** (also: lock, update mutex, `UpdateMutex`, `LockHoldWarn`, `sub=lock`) — The `World`'s one update mutex: ticks, between-tick event dispatch, input routing, installs, operator commands and the render pass all hold it, so each sees whole state transitions. Code reached from the mode router already holds it and must never take it again. While hold sampling is on (the `lock` scope at debug, or a running recorder), a hold longer than `LockHoldWarn` (20 ms of wall time, 200 ms in a race build) is logged with its call chain and triggers a `lock.hold` recorder flush. [Runtime §7](runtime.md#7-lock-and-ownership-matrix) · [Runtime: non-reentrant router rule](runtime.md#non-reentrant-router-rule) · [Logging: sub="lock"](logging-and-diagnostics.md#sublock) · `internal/engine` `UpdateMutex`, `LockHoldWarn`

**WorldOrder** (also: CaptureOrder, InstallOrder, CellOrder) — The order a world holds its entities in, store by store and cell by cell where two or more give it one. Systems visit them in it and a re-insertion holds another, so a journal's opening carries it and its replay installs it last, after both domains' rows; one naming other entities than the world holds is refused. See also **opening**, **PlayerWorldState**. [Logging: Opening mid-run](logging-and-diagnostics.md#opening-mid-run) · `internal/engine` `WorldOrder`

**Wraith** (also: `WraithComponent`, away, `RosterEntry.Away`, `WraithTimeout`, `RefusalYielding`) — The cursor of a participant that departed without a **dismissal**: it keeps its entity, slot, owner and holder, holds only its cursor, protection and the cell it stood on, and is drawn there as its slot label in the Wraith field, a pale shield whose glow turns; nothing in the world reaches it and it reaches nothing. Its roster entry reads away, which succession and the corrections owed pass over, `:player` and the menu say so, and a holder's bots wait with it. The authority dismisses it once it has waited `WraithTimeout` (2 min of game time, aged on every instance and carried across a reset), or at once for a dial a full session could not otherwise admit, which is refused to retry in a moment. Its participant takes it back with its **claim**, and nobody drives it until then. A person's **fork** keeps every other player as one in the session it goes on in. See also **departure**. [Multi-player §5.4](multi-player.md#54-wraiths) · `internal/component` `WraithComponent` · `internal/system` `CursorSystem.wraith`, `CursorSystem.Update`, `NetworkSystem.expireWraiths` · `internal/app` `membership.yieldLocked`

**write mask** (also: semantic mask, `SetWriteMask`) — The semantic category a renderer tags its cells with before drawing (`MaskPing`, `MaskGlyph`, `MaskField`, `MaskTransient`, `MaskComposite`, `MaskUI`, `MaskWall`, and `MaskHealthBar` for health bars), so late post-processing such as grayout, pause dim and occlusion can select earlier layers without knowing which renderer drew them. See also **mask**. [Rendering §5](rendering.md#5-semantic-masks-and-post-processing) · `internal/render` `RenderBuffer.SetWriteMask` · `internal/parameter/visual` `mask.go`

**written world** (also: `WrittenDelta`, capture record) — A world this instance wrote rather than simulated (a join's install, each correction after it, the world a host opened its session on, or a mid-run journal's **opening**), journaled as a `capture` record holding a `snapshot.WrittenDelta` against the world held just before and sealed with the written world's integrity hash, with the **barrier** the write left on. Replay rebuilds and installs it at the same place, engaging its own barrier where the write did, so a guest's journal replays without the network. See also **journal**, **capture**. [Logging: written worlds and notes](logging-and-diagnostics.md#written-worlds-and-notes) · `internal/snapshot` `WrittenDelta` · `internal/event` `JournalCapture`

## X

**xterm** — The xterm-256 palette: an index is a palette color in 256-color mode; mapped to a text console's sixteen colors it keeps its hue family, and `.vifimg` files store xterm-256 cells beside truecolor ones. See also **256-color**. [Rendering §6](rendering.md#6-finalization-and-color-modes) · [Content §8](content-assets-and-tools.md#8-image-and-pattern-pipeline) · `internal/render` `console.go`

## Z

**zap** — two senses. See also **lightning**, **quasar**.
- *Quasar*: the quasar's ranged attack: when the cursor leaves its effective range it charges for three seconds and then strikes the cursor with lightning until the cursor returns (`QuasarCharging`, `QuasarZapping` in `QuasarComponent.Machine`, `ZapRadius`). [Gameplay §8](gameplay.md#8-hostile-and-structural-actors) · [Behavior machines §4.2](machine-design.md#42-quasar) · `internal/component` `QuasarComponent`
- *Lightning*: a zap is the lightning bolt an attack draws: a rod hit's leaves its orb, a tracer impact's leaves the cursor, a mounted rod zaps the cursor it aims at, and at the rod's charge cap the zap arcs on from target to target. [Combat §5](combat.md#5-resolution-statuses-and-what-crosses) · [Combat §4](combat.md#4-at-the-charge-cap) · [Combat §2](combat.md#2-layers) · `internal/component` `LightningComponent` · `internal/system` `LightningSystem`
